§ 32.001 COMMISSION ON HUMAN RELATIONS.
   (A)   Created. There shall be and there is created and constituted an
advisory commission of the city, to be known by the name and style of “Fort
Scott Commission on Human Relations”.
(Prior Code, § 2.16.010)
   (B)   Purposes. The Commission on Human Relations shall act in an advisory
capacity, and its purposes shall be as follows:
      (1)   To further amicable relations among the various groups of the
population of the city;
      (2)   To help preserve and further the good name of the city for fair
play and promote even better relations among its people;
      (3)   To open the way for each citizen, regardless of race, creed, color
or national origin, to develop according to his or her abilities without
limitations; and
      (4)   To aid the city and its people in benefitting from the fullest
realization of its human resources.
(Prior Code, § 2.16.020)
   (C)   Members; appointment; terms; vacancy filling; compensation. The
members of the commission on human relations shall be appointed by the City
Manager with the approval of the Board of Commissioners, and shall consist of
nine members who shall be drawn from diverse segments of the public and
selected for their wisdom, ability and broad perspective. Three members shall
be appointed for one-year terms, three members for two-year terms and three
members for three-year terms. All subsequent terms upon expiration of the
original appointments shall be for a three-year term and until their successors
have been appointed and qualified for office. Any vacancy in the membership of
the Commission shall be filled by the appointment of a member, as in the case
of an original appointment, for the unexpired term of a member whose office
shall be vacant. All members shall serve without compensation, but the
Commission may be allowed necessary expenses, as may be authorized.
(Prior Code, § 2.16.030)
   (D)   Officers; appointment and election. Officers of the Commission on
human relations shall be a Chairperson, a Vice-Chairperson and a Secretary. The
City Manager shall appoint the first Chairperson and Vice-Chairperson. The
Secretary shall be elected at the first meeting of the Commission. The
officers, after the first year, shall be elected at the annual election of
officers.
(Prior Code, § 2.16.040)
   (E)   Officers’ duties generally; committees. The Chairperson, Vice-
Chairperson and Secretary of the Commission shall have and perform the duties
commonly associated with their respective titles and as may be determined by
the Commission. The Commission shall be further authorized to appoint and fix
the membership of such number of standing and temporary committees as it may
find expedient for the performance of its duties.
(Prior Code, § 2.16.050)
   (F)   Meetings. The Commission on Human Relations shall meet at least once
each month at such time and place as shall be fixed by the Commission by its
standing rules. Special meetings shall be called by the Chairperson or, in his
or her absence, by the Vice-Chairperson, or by the written request of a
majority of the Commission.
(Prior Code, § 2.16.060)
   (G)   Methods and procedures. The Commission in the attainment of its
objectives shall observe the following methods and procedures:
      (1)   Advise the City Manager and other officers of the city on problems
affecting human and intergroup relations;
      (2)   Make studies, surveys and investigations to provide data as needed
in its work;
      (3)   Recommend such measures as it deems necessary to carry out its
objectives;
      (4)   Consult with and obtain cooperation and coordinated effort on the
part of all agencies which function in the field of human relations in the
city;
      (5)   Utilize the resources of individuals and groups toward the
improvement of intergroup relations;
      (6)   Enlist all potential community forces to extend and make more
secure human opportunities and group practices; and
      (7)   Encourage community support for educational programs and, where
appropriate, legislation designed to reduce tension and eliminate
discriminatory practices arising from group prejudice.
(Prior Code, § 2.16.070)
   (H)   Direction and control. The activities, powers and procedures of the
Commission shall at all times be under the direction and control of the
governing body of the city. The Commission can be terminated at any time by
ordinance duly passed by the governing body.
(Prior Code, § 2.16.080)
(Ord. 2407, passed - -1968)
§ 32.002 PLANNING COMMISSION.
   (A)   Created. There is hereby established in the city a “Fort Scott
Planning Commission” under the authority of K.S.A. 12-744 et seq., for the
purpose of recommending and advising the governing body of the city on matters
pertaining to the planning, zoning and subdivision administration of all legal
territory and property inside the city limits.
   (B)   Name. The name of the Commission shall be the “City of Fort Scott,
Kansas, Planning Commission”.
   (C)   Planning area. The planning area shall be defined as all legal
territory and property within the legal boundaries of the city.
   (D)   Appointment, members, terms, vacancy filling, compensation.
      (1)   Members of the Planning Commission shall be appointed by the
governing body.
      (2)   The Planning Commission shall consist of seven members.
         (a)   Four members shall be residents of the city and reside within
the legal boundaries of the city.
         (b)   Two members shall reside outside the legal boundaries of the
city, but within a three-mile radius of the city’s legal boundaries.
         (c)   One member may reside outside of the legal boundaries of the
city, but within a three-mile radius of the city’s legal boundaries.
      (3)   Members of the Planning Commission shall serve a term of three
years.
         (a)   The expiration of terms shall be staggered so that three members
terms of office shall expire during the same year, and the other four members
terms of office shall expire the following year and continue in this fashion
thereafter.
         (b)   Appointments or terms served by a member of the Planning
Commission made prior to the effective date of this section shall not result in
forfeiture of office due to conflict in residency and members shall continue to
serve for the unexpired term.
      (4)   Vacancies shall be filled by appointment for the unexpired term
only.
      (5)   Board members shall serve without compensation.
   (E)   Removal from office. A member of the Planning Commission, once
appointed, shall not be removed during their term of office, except for
reasonable cause, and only after a hearing is held before the governing body of
the city in which a majority vote is made.
   (F)   Election of Chairperson and Vice-Chairperson. Within 30 days after
passage hereof and on an annual basis, the Board shall meet and organize its
membership, electing two of its members as Chairperson and Vice-Chairperson for
year of their term. The City Clerk and/or a designated representative as
appointed by the City Manager shall serve as the Secretary for the Board. Each
officer shall serve until his or her successor is duly elected and qualified.
   (G)   Bylaws, record-keeping. The Planning Commission shall adopt bylaws,
including rules for the transaction of business, which rules and bylaws shall
be approved by the governing body of the city. The Planning Commission shall
keep a record of its functions and activities, which shall be a public record.
   (H)   Quorum. Four members of the Planning Commission shall constitute a
quorum for the transaction of business at any meeting. No action by the
Planning Commission shall be taken, except by a majority vote of the members
present and voting.
   (I)   Meetings. The Planning Commission shall meet bi-monthly at such time
and place as may be fixed by resolution. Special meetings may be called at any
time by the Chairperson or, in his or her absence, by the Vice-Chairperson. All
meetings shall be open to the public.
   (J)   Powers and duties. The Planning Commission shall have the functions,
duties and powers as provided by K.S.A. 12-744 et seq.
   (K)   Budget and expenditures. In the exercise of its functions, duties and
powers, the Planning Commission shall prepare an annual budget; shall have
authority to make expenditures upon vouchers executed by proper officers of the
Planning Commission from funds appropriated by the governing body of the city,
such funds to be held by the City Treasurer, who is designated as fiscal agent
of the Planning Commission; shall have authority to receive, accept and expend
funds, grants and services from the United States Government or its agencies
and instrumentalities in state and local governments, and from private persons
or organizations.
(Ord. 3762, passed 3-19-2024)
§ 32.003 BOARD OF ZONING APPEALS.
   (A)   Number of members. The city hereby and herewith changes its current
three-member Board of Zoning Appeals to a Board of Zoning Appeals containing
five members.
(Prior Code, § 2.22.010)
   (B)   Residency requirement; term of membership. Four Board members shall be
residents of the city, and one Board member shall be a resident of the area
outside the city limits, to be appointed by the City Commission. None of the
members shall hold any other public office in the city, except one member may
be a member of the Planning Commission. The members shall serve four years with
the expiration staggered so that three members terms of office shall expire
during the same year and the other two members term of office shall expire the
following year and continuing in this fashion thereafter. Vacancies shall be
filled by appointment for the unexpired term only. Board members shall serve
without pay.
(Prior Code, § 2.22.020)
   (C)   Adoption of regulations; meetings. The Board shall annually elect one
member as Chairperson and shall appoint a Secretary. The Board may adopt any
other regulations it deems necessary for it to carry out its function. Meetings
of the Board shall be held at the call of the Chairperson and at such other
times as the Board may determine. The Board shall keep minutes of its
proceedings, showing evidence presented, findings of fact by the Board,
decision of the Board and a vote upon each question.
(Prior Code, § 2.22.030)
(Ord. 2730, passed - -1979; Ord. 3498, passed 3-28-2017)
§ 32.004 OLD FORT GOVERNING BOARD.
   (A)   Created; function. The city hereby creates and officially designates a
committee called the “Old Fort Governing Board”. The function of the Old Fort
Governing Board will be to advise the City Manager and the Board of
Commissioners on all matters referred to it by the City Manager or the Board of
Commissioners with reference to the Fort Scott Historical Site.
(Prior Code, § 2.36.010)
   (B)   Members; appointment; term. The Old Fort Governing Board shall be
composed of 15 members. The members are to be appointed by the Board of
Commissioners. The terms of office for Old Fort Governing Board members will be
two years. One-half of the members will initially be appointed after the
adoption of the ordinance codified in this section, for a one-year term, and
the other one-half will be appointed, after the adoption of the ordinance
codified in this chapter, for a two-year term. This will allow for continuity
on the Board at all times.
(Prior Code, § 2.36.020)
   (C)   Powers and duties. The Old Fort Governing Board shall have the
following specific responsibilities and duties:
      (1)   To solicit and assist in coordination of volunteer activities at
the historic site; and
      (2)   To advise the Board of Commissioners and the City Manager on all
matters referred to the board by the Board of Commissioners and the City
Manager.
(Prior Code, § 2.36.030)
   (D)   Meetings. The Old Fort Governing Board shall meet at least once a
month, at a date and time to be determined by the Governing Board or more
often, if necessary.
(Prior Code, § 2.36.040)
   (E)   Quorum. A quorum of more than 50% of the members on the Governing
Board will be necessary for the conduct of any business before the Board.
(Prior Code, § 2.36.050)
   (F)   Members; attendance requirement; removal when. For a member to
maintain good standing on the Board, he or she must attend more than 50% of all
meetings held by the Board during any given calendar year. If a member fails to
meet this attendance requirement, he or she may be removed from the Board
forthwith by a majority vote of the Governing Board members at any regular
meeting.
(Prior Code, § 2.36.060)
(Ord. 2670, passed - -1977)
§ 32.005 PARKS BOARD.
   (A)   Creation. By this creation of the ordinance codified herein, there is
created a “Fort Scott Parks Board of the City of Fort Scott, Kansas”.
(Prior Code, § 2.40.010)
   (B)   Function. The function of this Board will be to serve as a planning
and advisory group to the City Commission.
(Prior Code, § 2.40.020)
   (C)   Membership; terms. The Board shall consist of seven members, who shall
be appointed by the City Commission. Three members of the Board shall serve for
a two-year term and four members of the Board shall serve for a three-year
term. The City Manager will appoint a staff member to serve as a liaison
between the Advisory Board and City Commission and will attend all Advisory
Board meetings. All members shall be residents of the city. Members of the
Board shall serve without compensation.
(Prior Code, § 2.40.030)
   (D)   Election of Chairperson. The Board shall elect a Chairperson and a
Vice-Chairperson from among its members.
(Prior Code, § 2.40.040)
   (E)   Purpose. The city’s Parks Board shall recommend and make suggestions
to the City Manager and governing body regarding all the parks located within
the city limits. In addition, the Board shall make recommendations and reports
to the governing body on all matters within a reasonable time after such
request is made by the governing body.
(Prior Code, § 2.40.050)
(Ord. 3603, passed 3-15-2022; Ord. 3565, passed 10-6-2020)
§ 32.006 DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT BOARD.
   (A)   Created. The city hereby creates a Downtown Redevelopment Board.
(Prior Code, § 2.44.010)
   (B)   Membership; term. The Board shall be composed of seven members to be
appointed by the City Commission. The first four members appointed shall serve
a one-year term and the next three members appointed shall serve a two-year
term. After the original appointments, all members shall serve two-year terms.
Vacancies shall be filled by appointment for the unexpired term only.
(Prior Code, § 2.44.020)
   (C)   Ex officio members. The City Manager, the city’s National Historic
Site Superintendent, the Chairperson of the Downtown Retail Merchants Committee
and the Executive Director of the Fort Scott Area Chamber of Commerce shall
serve as ex officio Board members with no voting privileges.
(Prior Code, § 2.44.030)
   (D)   Procedure. The Board shall annually elect one member as Chairperson or
Co-Chairperson and shall appoint a Secretary. The Board may adopt any other
regulations it deems necessary for it to carry out its functions, including,
but not limited to, formation of committees. Meetings of the Board shall be
held at the call of the Chairperson and at such other times as the Board may
determine. The Board shall keep minutes of its proceedings, showing evidence
presented, findings of fact of the Board, decisions of the Board and a vote
upon each question.
(Prior Code, § 2.44.040)
   (E)   Purpose. The primary purpose of the Board will be to serve as a
planning and advisory group to the City Commissioners on all matters relating
to the development of Downtown Fort Scott, for the purposes of the Board, will
cover an area which is bordered on the north by the National Historic Site, on
the west by Judson Street on the south by Third Street and on the east by the
U.S. 69 bypass.
(Prior Code, § 2.44.050)
   (F)   Quorum. Four members of the Board shall constitute a quorum for the
transaction of business at any meeting.
(Prior Code, § 2.44.060)
(Ord. 2774, passed - -1980; Ord. 2865, passed - -1983)
§ 32.007 BUSINESS IMPROVEMENT DISTRICT AND ADVISORY BOARD.
   (A)   Boundaries and name of district.
      (1)   A Business Improvement District, as provided for in the Business
Improvement District Act in the state, is created for the Downtown Business
District of Fort Scott and described as follows: Judson Street on the west;
Third Street on the south; Highway 69 on the east; and National Historic Site
and Elm Street on the north, as set out in Exhibit A, attached to the ordinance
codified herein, which is on file in the office of the City Clerk.
      (2)   The name of the district shall be the Downtown Business Improvement
District.
(Prior Code, § 2.46.010)
   (B)   Services to be provided. The Downtown Business Improvement District
shall be created to provide to the businesses in the Improvement District the
services and activities as recommended by the Downtown Business Improvement
District Advisory Board and adopted by the governing body in the city budget.
(Prior Code, § 2.46.020)
   (C)   Methods used to finance services. The activities described in division
(B) above shall be financed through a combination of contributions from the
public sector at large and through a fee assessed by the city to each business,
as defined by the act, in the Business Improvement District according to the
type of business and/or the gross leasable area (floor space) used for
retailing or business activities. Fees shall be set according to the following
scale:
 
Class Description                                          Fee
1     Non-retail                                           $80
2     Retail: per owner and employee                       $70 each
3     Retail: 0 - 1,500 sq. ft of gross leasable area      $80
4     Retail: 1,500 - 5,000 sq. ft. of gross leasable area $140
5     Retail: 5,000 sq. ft. or more of gross leasable area $200
 
(Prior Code, § 2.46.030)
   (D)   Budget.
      (1)   Fees collected through the assessment of businesses in the Downtown
Business Improvement District in accordance with division (C) above shall be
expended for the services recommended by the Business Improvement District
Advisory Board and adopted by the governing body in the city budget.
      (2)   Expenditure of revenues collected from the Business Improvement
District shall be in accordance with the requirements of the Business
Improvement District Act.
(Prior Code, § 2.46.040)
   (E)   Assessment of fees. All fees assessed to businesses in the Improvement
District as provided for in this section shall be collected by the city in two
installments in January and July of each year. Fees collected in January shall
authorize operation of a business for the months of January through June of the
calendar year in which they are collected. If said fees are not collected
during January of said year, then they shall be deemed to be past due. Fees
collected in July shall authorize operation of a business for July through
December. If said fees are not collected during the month of July, then the
fees for the second half of the year shall be deemed to be past due. Fees on
vacant commercial property will be assessed to the property owner based on
gross leasable area available for commercial activities. The fees established
by this section shall remain in effect until it is necessary to adjust the fees
to meet budgetary needs as recommended by the Board and adopted by the
governing body.
(Prior Code, § 2.46.050)
   (F)   Downtown Business Improvement District Advisory Board. The Downtown
Business Improvement District Advisory Board is established in accordance with
the provisions of the Business Improvement District Act. The purpose of the
Advisory Board is to monitor and oversee the provision of services, as
established by the ordinance codified in this section. The Board shall, not
later than May 15 of each year, submit to the governing body a recommended
program of services to be performed or provided within the district during the
coming calendar year and a proposed budget to accomplish these objectives. In
addition, the Board shall submit a proposed schedule of fees for assessment on
the businesses in the district and may recommend consideration of changes in
the boundaries of the district. The assessment per business cannot increase
more than 10% per year without notification of all participating businesses of
a public hearing to consider the increase. There shall be nine members of the
Downtown Business Improvement District Advisory Board, who shall be appointed
by the Mayor with the consent of the governing body.
(Prior Code, § 2.46.060)
   (G)   Term of office.
      (1)   Members appointed to the Board shall be representatives of the
businesses located within the district. Of the members first appointed to the
Advisory Board, six shall be appointed for a term of one year and six shall be
appointed for a term of two years. On the expiration of the term of each Board
member, subsequent terms shall be for a period of two years.
      (2)   Any vacancy occurring in the membership of the Business Improvement
District Advisory Board shall be filled by appointment from the City Commission
for the unexpired term.
(Prior Code, § 2.46.070)
   (H)   Enforcement.
      (1)   The amount of any unpaid fee, the payment of which is required
under this section, shall constitute a debt due to the city. The City Attorney
shall, at the direction of the City Manager, institute civil suit in the name
of the city to recover any such unpaid fee. No civil judgment shall bar or
prevent a criminal prosecution for each and every violation of the ordinance
codified in this section.
      (2)   It shall be unlawful for any person who is the owner or operator of
any business required to pay a fee, pursuant to the ordinance codified in this
section, to continue the operation of said business after the fee has become
past due and remains unpaid.
(Prior Code, § 2.46.080)
(Ord. 2924, passed - -1985; Ord. 2951, passed - -1986) Penalty, see §
32.999
§ 32.008 LAROCHE BASEBALL COMPLEX ADVISORY BOARD.
   (A)   Creation.
      (1)   There is hereby established in the city a LaRoche Baseball Complex
Advisory Board for the purpose of advising the City Manager and City
Commissioners on matters pertaining to the continued improvement of the
baseball complex.
      (2)   The city acknowledges and ratifies the acts of the LaRoche Baseball
Complex as such Board has existed de facto, but not formally created.
(Prior Code, § 2.72.010)
   (B)   Membership; term.
      (1)   (a)   Said Board shall consist of three members who are
representatives of:
            1.   City of Fort Scott;
            2.   USD 234; and
            3.   LaRoche family.
         (b)   Said persons shall be appointed by the governing body and their
term shall be for three years.
      (2)   Within 30 days after passage hereof and on an annual basis, the
Board shall meet and organize its membership, electing one of its members as
Chairperson and one of its members as Vice-Chairperson. Thereafter, said Board
shall meet once each quarter as well as the request of the Mayor or City
Manager or at the request of the majority of the members of said Board, with
the Board establishing its own operating procedures.
(Prior Code, § 2.72.020)
   (C)   Purpose. The LaRoche Baseball Complex Advisory Board shall recommend
and make suggestions to the City Manager and governing body and USD 234
Superintendent regarding the continued growth of, and public improvements to
the baseball complex, including the acquisition of additional property,
construction, reconstruction and maintenance of the baseball complex
facilities, and institution of programs and procedures which will increase
usage of said facilities. In addition, the Board shall make recommendations and
reports to the governing body on all matters within a reasonable time after
such request is made by the governing body.
(Prior Code, § 2.72.030)
   (D)   Control and management. The control and management of the LaRoche
Baseball Complex shall be under the jurisdiction of the City Manager or his or
her designated agent. All employees and officers deemed necessary to operate
the LaRoche Baseball Complex shall be appointed, employed, disciplined,
supervised and terminated in the same manner as other employees and officers of
the city.
(Prior Code, § 2.72.040)
(Ord. 3462, passed 11-3-2015)
§ 32.009 CONVENTION AND VISITORS BUREAU.
   (A)   Created. There is hereby established in the city a Fort Scott
Convention and Visitors Bureau Advisory Board for the purpose of advising the
City Manager and City Commissioners on matters pertaining to continued growth
of tourism in the city and surrounding area.
(Prior Code, § 2.76.010)
   (B)   Membership; term. That said Board shall consist of a minimum of five
and not more than nine who are representatives of the lodging and hospitality
market, restaurants, tourist attractions, one at-large member, one ex officio
member from a tourist attraction in the county, one member representing the
Chamber of Commerce and the Director of Tourism for the city. Said persons
shall be appointed by the governing body and their terms shall be for three
years with no person to serve more than two consecutive terms, hereafter the
approval of this section by the governing body. The expiration dates will be
staggered so that three members terms of office shall expire during the same
year and the other remaining members terms of office shall expire the following
year and continuing in this fashion thereafter. Vacancies shall be filled by
appointment for the unexpired term only. Board members shall serve without pay.
(Prior Code, § 2.76.020)
   (C)   Election of officers. Within 30 days after passage hereof and on an
annual basis, the Board shall meet and organize its membership, electing one of
its members as Chairperson and one of its members as Secretary. Thereafter,
said Board shall meet once each quarter as well as at the request of the Mayor
or City Manager or at the request of the majority of the members of said Board,
with the Board establishing its own operating procedures.
(Prior Code, § 2.76.030)
   (D)   Purpose. The city’s Convention and Visitors Bureau Advisory Board
shall recommend and make suggestions to the City Manager and governing body
regarding tourism, events, grants and advertising, concerning the marketing of
the city by the Tourism Office. In addition, the Board shall make
recommendations and reports to the governing body on all matters within a
reasonable time after such request is made by the governing body.
(Prior Code, § 2.76.040)
   (E)   Control and management. The control and management of the Tourism
Office shall be under the jurisdiction of the City Manager or his or her
designated agent. All employees and officers deemed necessary to operate the
tourism office shall be appointed, employed, disciplined, supervised and
terminated in the same manner as other employees and officers of the city.
(Prior Code, § 2.76.050)
(Ord. 3510, passed 6-6-2017; Ord. 3517, passed 10-3-2017)
§ 32.010 BOURBON COUNTY FOOD ALLIANCE.
   (A)   Duties and responsibilities. The city’s Board of Commissioners
approves the establishment of BCFA to achieve the following:
      (1)   Increase access to nutritious and safe food for all residents;
      (2)   Enhance economic development and entrepreneurial opportunities
related to local food production and consumption;
      (3)   Improve health outcomes;
      (4)   Increase education and awareness of how to use healthy foods,
including locally produced, at home or at businesses and institutions;
      (5)   Bring stakeholders together and provide mechanisms for seeking
stakeholder advice and expertise;
      (6)   Coordinate and collaborate with new and existing programs within
the community and other county and state food policy organizations regionally;
      (7)   Support local producers, processors and distributors of food
products;
      (8)   Engage youth of the community to build healthy life skills; and
      (9)   Enhance the ability of local organizations to impact awareness and
consumption of healthy food options.
(Prior Code, § 2.80.010)
   (B)   Membership.
      (1)   The BCFA shall consists of a minimum of six and not more than ten
members. The Board of Commissioners will appoint three members to the BCFA,
responsible for reporting progress to the Commission. An additional three to
seven members will be nominated by the membership and approved by the Board of
Commissioners. Membership shall represent diverse food-related sectors and
groups such as:
         (a)   Community at-large;
         (b)   Producers;
         (c)   City, county and state officials;
         (d)   Youth;
         (e)   Senior citizens;
         (f)   Community and faith based organization;
         (g)   Restaurants;
         (h)   Food security representatives;
         (i)   Economic development representatives;
         (j)   Health professionals;
         (k)   Agriculture organizations;
         (l)   Schools and institutions;
         (m)   Food retail;
         (n)   Kansas State Research and Extension; and
         (o)   Local Health Department.
      (2)   The city will provide staff support to the BCFA as available.
(Prior Code, § 2.80.020)
   (C)   Meetings. The first meeting for BCFA shall be held on or before 12-30-
2017. Meetings shall be held a minimum of six times per year and minutes shall
be promptly recorded and made available to the public.
(Prior Code, § 2.80.030)
   (D)   Reporting and evaluation. BCFA shall prepare a quarterly report for
the Board of Commissioners on the progress and successes related to the
purpose, duties and responsibilities of the alliance. After one year, the
reporting schedule will be reevaluated. The city or its designee shall evaluate
the BCFA every three years to determine its success in improving the food
environment in the community.
(Prior Code, § 2.80.040)
   (E)   Funding. BCFA may actively seek grant funding for its activities.
(Prior Code, § 2.80.050)
(Ord. 3515, passed 7-18-2017)
§ 32.011 STREET ADVISORY BOARD.
   (A)   Created. There is hereby established in the city a Fort Scott Street
Advisory Board for the purpose of recommending and advising the City Manager
and City Commission on matters pertaining to the streets inside the city
limits.
   (B)   Membership; term. Said Board shall consist of five voting members. At
minimum, three voting members must reside within the city limits and two voting
members who may reside within the three-mile radius of the city’s boundaries.
The City Manager, and/or his, her or their designated representative(s) will
serve as non-voting ex officio. As ex officio, the staff member(s) will act as
liaison(s) between city staff and the Board. Additionally, the City Commission
will appoint one City Commissioner, who will serve as a non-voting ex officio.
The appointed City Commissioner will act as a non-voting liaison between the
City Commission and the Board. Members residing within city limits shall be
appointed by the governing body and their term shall be for three years.
Members residing outside of city limits shall be appointed by the governing
body and their term shall be for two years. Vacancies shall be filled by
appointment for the unexpired term only. Board members shall serve without pay.
   (C)   Election of Chairperson and Vice-Chairperson. Within 30 days after
passage hereof and on an annual basis, the Board shall meet and organize its
membership, electing two of its members as Chairperson and Vice-Chairperson.
Thereafter, said Board shall meet once each quarter, as well as at the request
of the Mayor or City Manager or at the request of the majority of the members
of said Board, with the Board establishing its own operating procedures.
   (D)   Purpose. The city’s Street Advisory Board shall recommend and make
suggestions to the City Manager and governing body regarding streets, street
repairs and brick streets located within the city limits. In addition, the
Board shall make recommendations and reports to the governing body on all
matters within a reasonable time after such request is made by the governing
body.
(Ord. 3760, passed 2-20-2024)
§ 32.012 LAND BANK BOARD OF TRUSTEES.
   (A)   Creation; purpose. The Fort Scott Land Bank is hereby established
pursuant to K.S.A. 12-5901 et seq. The Fort Scott Land Bank is an independent
agency and instrumentality of the city with the primary responsibility and
authority to efficiently acquire, hold, manage, transform and convey surplus
city properties and other abandoned, tax foreclosed or otherwise underutilized
or distressed properties in order to convey these properties for productive
use.
(Prior Code, § 17.14.010)
   (B)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly requires a different
meaning.
      BANK. The Fort Scott Land Bank established pursuant to this section.
      BOARD. The Board of Trustees of the Fort Scott Land Bank.
      CITY. The City of Fort Scott, Kansas.
      GOVERNING BODY. The governing body of the City of Fort Scott.
(Prior Code, § 17.14.020)
   (C)   Appointment; terms; vacancy; compensation; dissolution.
      (1)   There is hereby established a Fort Scott Land Bank Board of
Trustees. The Board shall be composed of five Board members.
         (a)   One Board member shall be a City Commissioner, appointed from
the Board of City Commissioners.
         (b)   The remaining four members shall be appointed by the governing
body of the city and those members shall serve for a term of three years from
the date of appointment by the governing body. Appointments may include the
following professions, which are recommended, but not mandatory: one individual
who represents banking; one individual who represents developers; one
individual who represents real estate; and one individual at large of the
community.
      (2)   Vacancies shall be filled by appointment only and shall serve for a
term as set forth herein.
      (3)   Members of the Board of Trustees shall receive no compensation, but
shall be paid their actual and necessary expenses in carrying out their duties
as members of the Board.
      (4)   The City Manager, and/or his, her or their designated
representative(s), shall provide technical support to the Board of Trustees.
Additional support may be contracted as deemed necessary by the Fort Scott Land
Bank Board of Trustees.
      (5)   The Fort Scott Land Bank Board of Trustees may be dissolved by
ordinance of the governing body of the city, without cause. In such case, all
property of the Land Bank shall be transferred to and held by the city and may
be disposed of as otherwise provided by law.
(Prior Code, § 17.14.030)
   (D)   Officers; organization.
      (1)   The Board shall select, annually, from its membership a
Chairperson, a Vice- Chairperson, a Secretary and a Treasurer. The Treasurer
shall be bonded in such amounts as the governing body may require.
      (2)   The Board may appoint such officers, agents and employees as it may
require for the performance of its duties, and shall determine the
qualifications and duties and fix the compensation of such officers, agents and
employees.
      (3)   The Board shall fix the time and place at which its meetings shall
be held. Meetings shall be held within the city and shall be subject to the
state’s Open Meeting Act, K.S.A. 75-4317 et seq., and amendments thereto.
      (4)   A majority of the Board shall constitute a quorum for the
transaction of business. No action of the Board shall be binding unless taken
at a meeting at which at least a quorum is present.
      (5)   The members of the Board shall be subject lo the provisions of the
laws of the state, which relate to conflicts of interest of city officers and
employees, including, but not limited to, K.S.A. 75-4301 et seq. and amendments
thereto.
      (6)   Subject to the provisions of the state’s Tort Claims Act, K.S.A.
75-6101 et seq. and amendments thereto, if any action at law or equity, or
other legal proceeding, shall be brought against any member of the Board for
any act or omission arising out of the performance of duties as a member of the
Board, such member shall be indemnified in whole and held harmless by the Board
for any judgment or decree entered against such member and, further, shall be
defended at the cost and expense of the bank in any such proceeding.
(Prior Code, § 17.14.040)
   (E)   Powers and duties. The Land Bank Board of Trustees shall have the
following powers and duties:
      (1)   To sue and be sued;
      (2)   To enter into contracts;
      (3)   To appoint and remove staff and provide for the compensation
thereof;
      (4)   To acquire, by purchase, gift or devise, and convey any real
property, including easements and reversionary interest, and personal property,
subject to the provisions of this section and slate law;
      (5)   To rebate all or any portion thereof, the taxes on any property
sold or conveyed by the bank;
      (6)   To exercise any other power which may be delegated to the bank by
the governing body; and
      (7)   To exercise any other incidental power which is necessary to carry
out the purposes of the land bank and state law.
(Prior Code, § 17.14.050)
   (F)   Administration of property. The Board shall assume possession and
control of any acquired property by it under this section or state law and
shall hold and administer such property. In the administration of property, the
Board shall:
      (1)   Manage, maintain and protect or temporarily use for a public
purpose such property in the manner the Board deems appropriate;
      (2)   Compile and maintain a written inventory of all such property. The
inventory shall be available for public inspection and distribution at all
times;
      (3)   Study, analyze and evaluate potential, present and future uses for
such property which would provide for the effective reutilization of such
property;
      (4)   Plan for and use the Board’s best efforts to consummate the sale or
other disposition of such property at such times and upon such terms and
conditions deemed appropriate;
      (5)   Establish and maintain records and accounts reflecting all
transactions, expenditures and revenues in relation to the bank’s activities,
including separate itemizations of all transactions, expenditures and revenues
concerning each individual parcel of property acquired; and
      (6)   No less than 30 days prior to the sale of any property owned by the
bank, publish a notice in the official city newspaper announcing such sale.
(Prior Code, § 17.14.060)
   (G)   Budget; records; report.
      (1)   The bank shall be subject to the provisions of the Cash Basis Law,
K.S.A. 10-1101 et seq. and amendments thereto.
      (2)   The budget of the bank shall be prepared, adopted and published as
provided by law for other political subdivisions of the state. No budget shall
be adopted by the Board until it has been reviewed and approved by the
governing body. If the governing body elects not to ratify the budget, it must
reject the plan in its entirety and remand it back to the Board with specific
recommendations for reconsideration.
      (3)   The Board shall keep accurate accounts of all receipts and
disbursements. The receipts and disbursements of the Board shall be audited
yearly by a certified or licensed public accountant and the report of the audit
shall be included in and become part of the annual report of the Board.
      (4)   All records and accounts shall be subject to public inspection
pursuant to K.S.A. 45-216 et seq. and amendments thereto.
      (5)   Any moneys of the bank which are not immediately required for the
purposes of the bank shall be invested in the manner prescribed by K.S.A. 12-
1675, and amendments thereto.
      (6)   The bank shall make an annual report to the governing body on or
before January 31 of each year, showing receipts and disbursements from all
funds under its control and showing all property transactions occurring in each
year. Such report shall include an inventory of all property held by the bank.
A copy of such inventory shall also be published in the official city newspaper
on or before January 31 of each year.
      (7)   The bank shall be subject to the statutory requirements for the
deposit of public money as provided in K.S.A. 9-1401 et seq. and amendments
thereto.
      (8)   The Board, without competitive bidding, may sell any property
acquired by the board at such times, to such persons, and upon such terms and
conditions, and subject to such restrictions and covenants deemed necessary or
appropriate to assure the property’s effective reutilization.
      (9)   The sale of any real property by the Board, under the provisions of
this section or state law, on which there are delinquent special assessments to
finance public improvements shall be conditioned upon the approval of the
governing body.
      (10)   The Board, for the purpose of land disposition, may consolidate,
assemble or subdivide individual parcels of property acquired by the bank.
      (11)   Until sold or otherwise disposed of by the bank, and except for
special assessments levied by the city to finance public improvements, any
property acquired by the bank shall be exempt from the payment of ad valorem
taxes levied by the state and any other political or taxing subdivision of the
state.
      (12)   Except for special assessments levied by the city to finance
public improvements, when the Board acquires property pursuant to this section
and state law, the County Treasurer shall remove from the tax rolls all taxes,
assessments, charges, penalties and interest that are due and payable on the
property at the time of acquisition by the Board.
      (13)   Property held by the bank shall remain liable for special
assessments levied by the city for public improvements, but no payment thereof
shall be required until such property is sold or otherwise conveyed by the
bank.
      (14)   The governing body may abate part or all of any special
assessments which it has levied on property acquired by the bank, and the bank
and the governing body may enter into agreements related thereto. Any special
assessments that are abated shall be removed from the tax rolls by the county
treasurer as of the effective date of the abatement.
      (15)   Any moneys derived from the sale of property by the bank shall be
retained by the bank for the purposes and operations thereof; provided,
however, that, the Board may use all or part of the proceeds from such sale to
reimburse the city for delinquent special assessments due on such property.
      (16)   The Board may establish separate neighborhood or city advisory
committees consisting of persons living or owning property within the city or
neighborhood. In the case of neighborhood advisory committees, the Board shall
determine the boundaries of each neighborhood. In the absence of a resolution
by the Board providing otherwise, each advisory committee shall consist of not
less than five, nor more than nine, persons, to be appointed by the Board for
two-year, overlapping terms. The Board shall consult with each advisory
committee as needed to review the operations and activities of the bank and to
receive the advices of the members of the advisory committee concerning any
matter which comes before the committees.
(Prior Code, § 17.14.070)
(Ord. 3535, passed 6-19-2018; Ord. 3770, passed 9-3-2024)

specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person convicted of a violation of §
32.007(H) of this chapter shall be fined in a sum no greater than $500. Each

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 33.001 IDLE MONEYS; INVESTMENT.
   (A)   Investments permitted.
      (1)   Temporarily idle moneys of the city not currently needed may be
invested in:
         (a)   Direct obligations of the United States government which mature
in one year from date of purchase and which are guaranteed as to principal by
the United States government;
         (b)   Temporary notes of the city, issued pursuant to K.S.A. 10-123,
as amended;
         (c)   Adequately secured bank certificates of deposit;
         (d)   Insured savings and loan association accounts;
         (e)   No-fund warrants of the city;
         (f)   General obligation bonds of the city; or
         (g)   Adequately secured bank time-deposit, open accounts.
      (2)   The term BANK TIME-DEPOSIT, OPEN ACCOUNT, as used in this section,
means a city bank account which is a bank deposit, other than a time
certificate of deposit, with respect to which there is in force a written
contract between the city and the designated bank, which provides that neither
the whole or any part of the deposit may be withdrawn, by check or otherwise,
prior to:
         (a)   The date of maturity, which shall be no less than 30 days after
the date of deposit; or
         (b)   The expiration of the period of notice, which must be given by
the city, in writing, not less than 30 days in advance of withdrawal.
(Prior Code, § 3.04.010)
   (B)   Procedure. The City Treasurer shall report to the Board of
Commissioners as to the amount of money available for investment and shall
submit recommendations for investment. The Board of Commissioners shall
determine the amount, method and term of any investment and the type of
investment to be made. The provisions of this section shall not apply to any
fund of the city, the investment of which is expressly authorized or limited or
prohibited by statutes of the state.
(Prior Code, § 3.04.020)
   (C)   Interest crediting. The interest or other earnings from investments
made pursuant to this section shall be credited pro rata to the fund or funds
from which the investments were made.
(Prior Code, § 3.04.030)
(Ord. 2457, passed - -1971)
**** FUNDS ****
§ 33.015 EMPLOYEE BENEFITS CONTRIBUTION FUND.
   (A)   The city hereby and herewith creates, under the authority of K.S.A.
12-16,102, an Employee Benefits Contribution Fund to be funded with a tax levy
above and beyond those taxes allowed under the limitations imposed by K.S.A.
12-16,102, inclusive, and amendments thereto.
   (B)   Said fund shall provide for the city’s portion of the following
benefits paid to or on behalf of employees of said city:
      (1)   City’s share of Social Security tax;
      (2)   K.P.E.R.S.;
      (3)   Workers’ compensation insurance;
      (4)   Unemployment compensation contribution to state fund;
      (5)   City’s share of health insurance for employees;
      (6)   Cancer insurance premiums; and
      (7)   The city’s share of contributions to the state’s Police and Fire
Pension Funds.
(Prior Code, § 3.16.010) (Ord. 2731, passed - -1979)
§ 33.016 SOCIAL LIABILITY EXPENSE FUND.
   The city establishes and creates a Special Liability Expense Fund, under the
state’s Tort Claims Act, for the purpose of paying all direct and indirect
costs resulting from the implementation of the state’s Tort Claims Act. Said
costs shall include, but shall not be limited to the cost of providing legal
defense, payment of claims, purchase of insurance for payment of claims under
said act, establishment of a self-insurance fund and all other identifiable
costs associated with the implementation of said Act.
(Prior Code, § 3.20.010) (Ord. 2737, passed - -1979)
§ 33.017 ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT REVOLVING LOAN FUND.
   (A)   Declaration of purpose. It is declared by the city that an Economic
Development Revolving Loan Fund (the “fund”) is established for the purpose of
making available for loans to private firms certain moneys obtained by the
city. The fund shall consist of the principal and interest repaid to the city
by the private firms who have received business financing loans from the city.
Subsequent payments to the fund shall consist of the principal and interest
from future loans made directly by the city, or through future Community
Development Block grant projects. In addition, interest earned by the fund
shall be retained in the fund. Reasonable expenses accruing to the city in
managing the fund may be paid from the fund. Nothing in this section shall
preclude the governing body from contributing moneys to the fund from other
sources.
(Prior Code, § 3.28.010)
   (B)   Budgeting authority. The governing body of the city will be in charge
of the budgeting of the fund.
(Prior Code, § 3.28.020)
   (C)   Applicant screening. The governing body shall screen the applicants of
those applying for assistance from the fund, based upon the criteria and
guidelines for the fund with regard to the application.
(Prior Code, § 3.28.030)
   (D)   Decision-making authority.
      (1)   The decisions regarding which applicants are funded, the terms of
the loans, the rates of interest and other matters shall be made by the
governing body, which decisions shall be based generally upon criteria and
guidelines established.
      (2)   However, for good and sufficient reasons, the governing body may
waive or add to any criteria or guidelines if it is determined to be in the
best interest of the city.
(Prior Code, § 3.28.040)
   (E)   Application process. The following is the process to be followed by an
applicant seeking loans from the fund.
      (1)   Applicants must use the application form, personal financial
statement form and use of loan proceeds form supplied by the city. All forms
can be picked up from the City Clerk and returned to the same upon completion.
      (2)   If the governing body favorably reviews an application, the matter
shall be turned over to the city staff to complete the details on the project.
      (3)   Letters of commitment from private sources or financial
institutions confirming the required private financing, which may be required
in the project, shall be included with the application.
      (4)   Some of the factors to be considered by the governing body in
reviewing the applications shall include, but are not limited to: the number of
full-time and low-to-moderate income permanent jobs created; the ratio of jobs
to the loan amount requested; the percent and/or amount of the available fond
balance requested; and the percent and/or amount of private sector funds put
into the project. Other factors may also be considered.
      (5)   Loans from the funds shall not exceed 20 years.
(Prior Code, § 3.28.050)
   (F)   Interest and payment schedule. Interest on the loan and payback
schedule shall be a matter of negotiation between the city and the applicant.
(Prior Code, § 3.28.060)
(Ord. 2961, passed - -1987)
§ 33.018 EQUIPMENT RESERVE FUND.
   The city establishes and creates an Equipment Reserve Fund, pursuant to
K.S.A. 12-1,117, to assist in budgeting funds and depositing those funds into
an Equipment Reserve Fund in order to minimize the necessity for creating debt
to purchase municipal equipment. Funds may be deposited from the city budget on
an annual basis for the purposes stated herein to assist the city in financing
new and replacement equipment.
(Prior Code, § 3.32.010) (Ord. 3087, passed - -1994)
§ 33.019 CAPITAL IMPROVEMENT FUND.
   (A)   Established. The city, by the power invested in it by K.S.A. 12-1,118,
hereby elects to establish a Capital Improvement Fund for public improvement
and infrastructure needs of the city, as hereinafter provided.
(Prior Code, § 3.36.010)
   (B)   Purpose. The Capital Improvement Funds shall be used in whole or in
part for the funding of any public improvement; and/or acquisition, erection,
expansion, installation, improvement, renovation and replacement of city
facilities and equipment.
(Prior Code, § 3.36.020)
   (C)   Fund transfer. Funds may be transferred to the Capital Improvement
Fund from other city funds lawfully available for improvement purposes to the
Capital Improvements Fund, including moneys in the city’s Federal General
Revenue Sharing Fund and General Fund. Any general property tax specifically
levied for the use of such fund shall be authorized by ordinance adopted under
the provisions of § 5 of Article 12 of the Kansas Constitution.
(Prior Code, § 3.36.030)
   (D)   Public improvements. Moneys in such Capital Improvements Fund may be
used to finance, in whole or in part, any public improvement need, including
the repair, restoration and rehabilitation of existing public facilities, and
may be used for engineering and other advance public improvement plans and
studies with reimbursements made to the fund from bond proceeds, special
assessments or state or federal aid available for the completed project.
(Prior Code, § 3.36.040)
   (E)   Authority. The fund shall be in the custody of the Finance Director of
the city and all moneys in such fund may be invested in accordance with the
provisions of K.S.A. 10-131 and amendments thereto, with interest thereon
credited to such fund.
(Prior Code, § 3.36.050)
   (F)   Report required. Annually, the Finance Director shall submit to the
City Commission a complete and detailed report of the condition of such fund,
which report shall be made a part of the city annual report.
(Prior Code, § 3.36.060)
   (G)   Dissolution of fund. Upon dissolution of the fund, any remaining
balance shall be paid over into the General Fund or such other fund as the
ordinance terminating said fund shall direct.
(Prior Code, § 3.36.070)
(Ord. 3330, passed - -2007)
CHAPTER 34: EMERGENCY TELEPHONE SERVICE
Section
   
34.001   Establishment
   
34.002   Tax imposed
   
34.003   Initial tax rate
   
34.004   Annual tax rate review
§ 34.001 ESTABLISHMENT.
   The governing body of the city finds and determines it to be in the public
interest and for the protection of the public health to provide for the
operation of an emergency telephone service and the levying of an emergency
telephone tax for payment of such service in those portions of the jurisdiction
for which emergency telephone service has been contracted, all as provided by
K.S.A. 12-5301 et seq.
(Prior Code, § 8.28.010) (Ord. 3098, passed - -1994)
§ 34.002 TAX IMPOSED.
   There shall be imposed an emergency telephone tax to provide funds for the
payment of such emergency telephone service in an amount not to exceed $0.75
per month per exchange access line in those portions of the jurisdiction for
which emergency telephone service is or shall be contracted, and such
imposition shall be effective 61 days after the publication of the ordinance
codified in this chapter unless a petition is filed with the county’s Election
Officer signed by not less than 5% of the registered voters of the city. In the
event said petition is filed, the imposition of said tax shall be effective
upon a majority of the voters casting ballots in favor of the installation of
the system.
(Prior Code, § 8.28.020) (Ord. 3098, passed - -1994)
§ 34.003 INITIAL TAX RATE.
   The initial tax rate is established at $0.75 per month per exchange access
line in those portions of the jurisdiction for which emergency telephone
service has been contracted.
(Prior Code, § 8.28.030) (Ord. 3098, passed - -1994)
§ 34.004 ANNUAL TAX RATE REVIEW.
   At least once each calendar year, the governing body shall establish a tax
rate, not to exceed the amount authorized, that together with any surplus
revenues carried forward will produce sufficient revenues to fund the
expenditures authorized by this chapter. Amounts collected in excess of that
necessary within a given year shall be carried forward to subsequent years. The
governing body shall make its determination of such tax rate each subsequent
year no later than September 1 and shall fix the new rate to take effect
commencing with the first billing period of each customer on or before the next
January 1. Immediately upon making its determination and fixing the rate, the
governing body shall publish in its minutes the new rate, and it shall notify
by publication in the official city newspaper, every supplier at least 90 days
before such new rate will become effective.
(Prior Code, § 8.28.040) (Ord. 3098, passed - -1994)
