**** GOLF CARTS ****
§ 71.001 OPERATION REGULATIONS; EQUIPMENT.
   (A)   (1)   Golf carts may be operated upon the public highways, streets,
roads and alleys within the corporate limits of the city; provided, however,
that, no golf cart may be operated upon any public highway, street, road and
alley with a posted speed limit more than Thirty (35) mph.
      (2)   No golf cart shall be operated on any interstate highway, federal
highway or state highway; provided, however, that, the provisions of this
section shall not prohibit a golf cart from crossing a federal or state highway
or a street or highway with a posted speed limit greater than 35 mph.
   (B)   No golf cart shall be operated on any public sidewalk.
   (C)   Every person operating a golf cart on the public highways, streets,
roads and alleys of the city shall be subject to all the duties applicable to
the driver of a vehicle imposed by law.
   (D)   (1)   Golf carts may be operated on any public highway, street, road
or alley as set out in division (A) above.
      (2)   For golf carts to be operated on any public highway, street, road
or alley either before sunrise or after sunset, said golf cart must be equipped
with the following:
         (a)   Headlamps;
         (b)   Tail lamps;
         (c)   Reflectors (rear);
         (d)   Stop lamps; and
         (e)   Turn signals.
(Ord. 3769, passed 8-20-2024) Penalty, see §
71.999
§ 71.002 VALID DRIVER’S LICENSE REQUIRED.
   No person shall operate a golf cart on any public highway, street, road or
alley within the corporate limits of the city unless such person has a valid
driver’s license.
(Ord. 3769, passed 8-20-2024) Penalty, see §
71.999
§ 71.003 DEFINITION.
   A GOLF CART means a motor vehicle that has not less than three wheels in
contact with the ground, an unladen weight of not more than 1,800 pounds, is
designed to be operated at not more than 35 mph and is designed to carry not
more than six people, including the driver.
(Ord. 3769, passed 8-20-2024)
§ 71.004 SLOW-MOVING VEHICLE EMBLEM; DISPLAY.
   (A)   It shall be illegal to operate a golf cart on any public highway,
street, road or alley within the corporate limits of the city unless such
vehicle displays a slow-moving vehicle emblem on the rear of the vehicle.
   (B)   For the purpose of this section, a slow-moving vehicle emblem has the
same meaning as contained in K.S.A. 8-1717 and amendments thereto.
   (C)   The slow-moving vehicle emblem shall be mounted and displayed in
compliance with K.S.A. 8-1717 and amendments thereto.
(Ord. 3769, passed 8-20-2024) Penalty, see §
71.999
§ 71.005 INSURANCE.
   (A)   Every owner of a golf cart shall provide liability coverage in
accordance with § 200 of the Standard Traffic Ordinance, and amendments
thereto, and the state’s Automobile Injury Reparations Act, K.S.A. 40-3101 et
seq. and amendments thereto.
   (B)   All provisions of § 200 of the Standard Traffic Ordinance, and
amendments thereto, shall be applicable to all owners and operators of golf
carts.
(Ord. 3769, passed 8-20-2024) Penalty, see §
71.999
§ 71.006 REGISTRATION AND LICENSE; FEE; APPLICATION; INSPECTION.
   (A)   Before operating any golf cart on any public highway, street, road or
alley within the corporate limits of the city and each calendar year
thereafter, the vehicle shall be registered with the city and a license shall
be obtained and placed on the golf cart. The license fee shall be $50 per
calendar year, payable in advance to the City Clerk. The full amount of the
license fee shall be required regardless of the time of year that the
application is made.
   (B)   Application for registration of a golf cart shall be made by the
owner, or owner’s agent, in the office of the City Clerk. The application shall
be made upon forms provided by the city and each application shall contain the
name of the owner, the owner’s residence address or bona fide place of
business, a brief description of the vehicle to be registered including make,
model and serial number, if applicable. Proof of insurance, as required in §
71.005 of this chapter, shall be furnished, at the time of application for
registration.
   (C)   Prior to the issuance of the registration and license, each applicant
for a golf cart license shall first present such vehicle for an official
inspection. If, upon inspection and completion of the registration application,
such vehicle is found to be in a safe mechanical condition, and upon
establishing proof of insurance and payment of the fees herein provided, a
license shall be issued to the owner who shall attach it to the vehicle. The
license shall be displayed in such a manner as to be clearly visible from the
rear of the vehicle. The license number on the application will be accounted
for and then filed in the Police Department.
   (D)   It is unlawful for any person to willfully or maliciously remove,
destroy, mutilate or alter such licenses during the time in which the same is
operative.
   (E)   The license issued hereunder is not transferable. In the event of sale
or other transfer of ownership of any vehicle license under the provisions of
this section, the existing license and the right to use the numbered license
shall expire and the license shall be removed by the owner. It is unlawful for
any person other than the person to whom the license was originally issued to
have the same in his or her possession.
   (F)   In the event a license is lost or destroyed, the City Clerk, upon
proper showing by the licensee and the payment of a fee of $25, shall issue a
new license in accordance with the provisions of this section.
   (G)   It shall be unlawful for any person to:
      (1)   Operate, or for the owner thereof knowingly to permit the
operation, upon a public street, road, highway or alley within the corporate
limits of the city any golf cart, as defined herein, which is not registered,
and which does not have attached thereto and displayed thereon the license
assigned thereto by the city for the current registration year;
      (2)   Display, cause or permit to be displayed, or to have in possession,
any registration receipt, registration license or registration decal knowing
the same to be fictitious or to have been canceled, revoked, suspended or
altered;
      (3)   Lend to or knowingly permit the use by one not entitled thereto any
registration receipt, registration license plate or registration decal issued
to the person so lending or permitting the use thereof;
      (4)   Remove, conceal, alter, mark or deface the license number plate,
plates or decals, or any other mark of identification upon any golf cart.
Licenses shall be kept clean and placed as required by law to be plainly
visible and legible; and/or
      (5)   Carry or display a registered number plate or plates or
registration decal upon any golf cart not lawfully issued for such vehicle.
(Ord. 3769, passed 8-20-2024) Penalty, see §
71.999
§ 71.007 PROPER SIGNALING.
   If the golf cart is not equipped with headlights, brake lights or
taillights, the operator must give hand signals to indicate he or she is
stopping or turning.
(Ord. 3769, passed 8-20-2024) Penalty, see §
71.999
**** UTILITY TERRAIN VEHICLES ****
§ 71.020 OPERATION REGULATIONS.
   (A)   UTVs may be operated UPON the public highways, streets, roads and
alleys within the corporate limits of the city; provided, however, that, no UTV
may be operated upon any public highway, street, road and alley with a posted
speed limit in excess of 35 mph or upon any interstate highway, federal highway
or state highway; provided, however, that, the provisions of this section shall
not prohibit a UTV from crossing a federal or state highway or a street or
highway with a posted speed limit greater than 30 mph.
   (B)   No UTV shall be operated on any public sidewalk or other area not
specifically designated as a traffic lane.
   (C)   Every person operating a UTV on the public highways, streets, roads
and alleys of the city shall be subject to all of the duties applicable to a
licensed driver or a vehicle imposed by law.
(Ord. 3761, passed 3-19-2024) Penalty, see §
71.999
§ 71.021 VALID DRIVER’S LICENSE REQUIRED.
   No person shall operate a UTV on any public highway, street, road or alley
within the corporate limits of the city unless such person has a valid driver’s
license and is 18 years of age or older.
(Ord. 3761, passed 3-19-2024) Penalty, see §
71.999
§ 71.022 DEFINITION.
   A UTV means any motor vehicle which is not less than 48 inches in width, has
an overall length, including the bumper, of not more than 160 inches, has an
unladen weight, including fuel and fluids, of more than 1,000 pounds, can
exceed 25 mph and originally manufactured. This definition does not include an
“all-terrain vehicle (ATV)”, which is a vehicle designed with a seat to be
straddled and which is not authorized to be on the city streets.
(Ord. 3761, passed 3-19-2024)
§ 71.023 INSURANCE.
   (A)   Every owner of a UTV shall provide liability insurance coverage in
accordance with § 200 of the Standard Traffic Ordinance, and amendments
thereto, and the state’s Automobile Injury Reparations Act, K.S.A. 40-3101 et
seq. and amendments thereto.
   (B)   All provisions of § 200 of the Standard Traffic Ordinance, and
amendments thereto, shall be applicable to all owners and operators of UTVs.
(Ord. 3761, passed 3-19-2024) Penalty, see §
71.999
§ 71.024 REGISTRATION AND LICENSE; FEE; APPLICATION; INSPECTION.
   (A)   Before operating any UTV on a public highway, street, road or alley
within the corporate limits of the city and each calendar year thereafter, the
vehicle shall be registered with the city and a license shall be obtained and
placed on the UTV. The license fee shall be $50 per calendar year (ending on
December 31 of each year), payable in advance to the City Clerk. The full
amount of the license fee shall be required regardless of the time of year that
the application is made.
   (B)   Application for registration of a UTV shall be made by the owner, or
owner’s agent, in the office of the City Clerk. The application shall be made
upon forms provided by the city and each application shall contain the name of
the owner, the owner’s residential address or bona fide place of business, a
brief description of the vehicle to be registered including make, model and
serial number, if applicable. Proof of insurance as required in division (D)
below shall be furnished at the time of application for registration.
   (C)   Prior to the issuance of the registration and license, each applicant
for a UTV license shall first present such vehicle for an official inspection.
If a previous license for the prior year has been issued by the city, then no
such inspection shall be required. An inspection shall be required if the
vehicle has been transferred to a new owner. If, upon inspection and completion
of the registration application, such vehicle is found to be in a safe
mechanical condition, and upon establishing proof of insurance and payment of
the fees herein provided, a license shall be issued to the owner who shall
attach it to the vehicle. The license shall be displayed in such a manner as to
be clearly visible from the rear of the vehicle. The license number on the
application will be maintained and filed at City Hall with a copy provided to
the city’s Police Department.
   (D)   It is unlawful for any person to willfully or maliciously remove,
destroy, mutilate or alter such license during the time in which the same is
operative.
   (E)   The license issued hereunder is not transferable. In the event of sale
or other transfer of ownership of any vehicle license under the provisions of
this section, the existing license and the right to use the numbered license
shall expire and the license shall be removed by the owner. It is unlawful for
any person other than the person to whom the license was originally issued to
have the same in his or her possession.
   (F)   (1)   In the event a license is lost or destroyed, the City Clerk,
upon proper showing by the licensee and the payment of a fee of $25, shall
issue a new license in accordance with the provisions of this section.
      (2)   Before an inspection is done on any UTV to be registered with city,
proof of title registration must be provided. If no title registration can be
provided, the city will not consider a UTV for inspection.
   (G)   It shall be unlawful for any person to:
      (1)   Operate, or for the owner thereof knowingly to permit the
operation, upon a public street, road, highway or alley within the corporate
limits of the city any UTV, as defined herein, which is not registered and
which does not have attached thereto and displayed thereon the license assigned
thereto by the city for the current registration year;
      (2)   Display, cause or permit to be displayed, or to have in possession,
any registration receipt, registration license or registration decal knowing
the same to be fictitious or to have been canceled, revoked, suspended or
altered;
      (3)   Lend to or knowingly permit the use by one not entitled thereto any
registration receipt, registration license plate or registration decal issued
to the person so lending or permitting the use thereof;
      (4)   Remove, conceal, alter, mark or deface the license number plate,
plates or decals, or any other mark of identification upon any UTV. Licenses
shall be kept clean and placed as required by law so as to be plainly visible
and legible; and/or
      (5)   Carry or display a registered number plate or plates or
registration decal upon any UTV not lawfully issued for such vehicle.
(Ord. 3761, passed 3-19-2024) Penalty, see §
71.999
§ 71.025 EQUIPMENT REQUIRED.
   All UTVs shall be equipped with the following before registering with the
city:
   (A)   Head lamps;
   (B)   Tail lamps;
   (C)   Reflectors (rear);
   (D)   Stop lamps;
   (E)   Turn signals;
   (F)   Mirror: left side of vehicle;
   G)   Mirror: right side or inside center;
   (H)   Safety belts and shoulder harnesses; and
   (I)   Mufflers and noise suppressing system.
(Ord. 3761, passed 3-19-2024)
**** BICYCLES ****
§ 71.040 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   BICYCLE. Every device propelled by human power upon which any person may
ride, having two tandem wheels either of which is over 20 inches in diameter.
   STREET. The streets, avenues, alleys and other public grounds of the city,
and shall not include sidewalks.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.010) (Ord. 2357, passed - -1965)
§ 71.041 REGISTRATION; INFORMATION REQUIRED.
   The owner of a bicycle may register the same by giving the following
information to the Chief of Police, who shall keep a record thereof at his or
her office:
   (A)   Name of bicycle;
   (B)   Type of bicycle;
   (C)   Color of bicycle;
   (D)   Serial number of bicycle; and
   (E)   Name and address of owner.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.030) (Ord. 2357, passed - -1965; Ord. 3419, passed 8-21-
2012)
§ 71.042 PLATE OR TAG; AFFIXED TO BICYCLE.
   (A)   The city shall furnish to every owner whose bicycle is registered, one
number plate or tag for each bicycle.
   (B)   The tag or plate shall be displayed on the bicycle to which it is
assigned during the current registration year in such a manner as to be clearly
visible from the rear of the bicycle.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.040) (Ord. 2357, passed - -1965)
§ 71.043 LICENSE FEE.
   The annual licence for a bicycle is issued for each calendar year, or part
thereof; provided that, any bicycle which is registered by a new owner on or
following December 24 of any calendar year may display the number plate or tag
for the ensuing calendar year.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.050) (Ord. 2357, passed - -1965; Ord. 3040, passed - -
1992; Ord. 3419, passed 8-21-2012)
§ 71.044 OPERATION REQUIREMENTS AND PROHIBITIONS.
   (A)   Operation astride other than seat prohibited. A person operating a
bicycle shall not ride other than upon or astride the permanent and regular
seat attached thereto, and no bicycle shall be used to carry more persons at
one time than the number for which it is designed and equipped.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.070)
   (B)   Excessive speed prohibited. No person shall operate a bicycle at a
speed greater than is reasonable and prudent under the conditions then
existing.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.080)
   (C)   Operation on right-hand side of street required. Every person
operating a bicycle upon the streets shall ride as near to the right-hand side
of the street as practicable.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.090)
   (D)   Attachment to moving vehicle prohibited. No person riding upon any
bicycle shall attach the same or himself or herself to any moving vehicle.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.100)
   (E)   More than two abreast prohibited. Persons operating bicycles upon the
streets shall not ride more than two abreast.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.110)
   (F)   One hand required on handlebars. No person operating a bicycle shall
carry any package, bundle or article which prevents the rider from keeping at
least one hand upon the handlebars.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.120)
   (G)   Riding on sidewalks prohibited. No person shall ride a bicycle on the
sidewalks in the city.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.130)
   (H)   Trick or stunt riding on streets prohibited. No person shall do any
trick or stunt riding on a bicycle on a street.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.160)
(Ord. 2357, passed - -1965) Penalty, see §
71.999
§ 71.045 EQUIPMENT REQUIRED.
   (A)   Lighting requirements. Every bicycle operated upon the street or
highway in the nighttime shall be equipped with a lamp on the front exhibiting
a white light visible from a distance of at least 500 feet to the front and
with a lamp on the rear exhibiting a red light visible from a distance of 500
feet to the rear; except that, a red reflector meeting the requirements of this
division (A) may be used in lieu of a rear light.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.140)
   (B)   Proper brakes required. No person shall operate a bicycle without
proper brakes upon any street.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.150)
(Ord. 2357, passed - -1965) Penalty, see §
71.999
§ 71.046 COMPLIANCE WITH TRAFFIC REGULATIONS REQUIRED.
   Any person riding a bicycle in the city shall be subject to and shall comply
with all of the provisions of this traffic code applicable to said rider,
except those provisions which, by their nature, can have no application.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.170) (Ord. 2357, passed - -1965)
§ 71.047 PARENTS NOT TO AUTHORIZE VIOLATIONS.
   The parent of any child and the guardian of any ward shall not authorize or
knowingly permit any such child or ward to violate any of the provisions of
this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.180) (Ord. 2357, passed - -1965)
§ 71.048 ENFORCEMENT AUTHORITY.
   The Chief of Police shall have the power to make additional regulations
necessary to make effective the provisions of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.190) (Ord. 2357, passed - -1965)
§ 71.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   (1)   Unless specifically provided herein, a violation of §§
71.001 through
71.007 of this chapter shall be deemed an ordinance traffic infraction. Upon an
entry of a plea of guilty or no contest or upon being convicted of such
violation, the penalty imposed shall be in accordance with § 201, Standard
Traffic Ordinance, and amendments thereto, or such other similar provision as
the city may then have in effect.
      (2)   Violation of §
71.002 of this chapter is punishable by a fine of not more than $500 or by
imprisonment for not more than six months or by both such fine and
imprisonment.
      (3)   (a)   Any person convicted of a violation of any of the provisions
of §
71.006 of this chapter shall, for the first conviction thereof, be punished by
a fine of not more than $500.
         (b)   A violation of §
71.006(G)(2) of this chapter shall constitute an unclassified misdemeanor
punishable by a fine of not less than $100 and forfeiture of the item. A
mandatory court appearance shall be required of any person violating §
71.006(G)(2) of this chapter.
   (C)   (1)   Unless specifically provided herein, a violation of §§
71.020 through
71.025 of this chapter shall be deemed a traffic infraction. Upon an entry of a
plea of guilty or no contest or upon being convicted of such violation, the
penalty imposed shall be in accordance with § 201, Standard Traffic Ordinance,
and amendments thereto, or such other similar provision as the city may then
have in effect.
      (2)   (a)   Any person convicted of, or a violation of, any of the
provisions of §
71.024 of this chapter shall for the first conviction thereof be punished by a
fine of not more than $500.
         (b)   A violation of §
71.024(G)(2) of this chapter shall constitute an unclassified misdemeanor
punishable by a fine of not less than $100 and forfeiture of the item. A
mandatory court appearance shall be required of any person violating §
71.024(G)(2) of this chapter.
   (D)   Any person or persons who violate any of the provisions of §§
71.040 through
71.048 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of an infraction and, upon
conviction, shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances or by impounding such person’s bicycle or
bicycles, if he or she owns more than one, for a period of not to exceed 30
days, during which period of impoundment such person shall not ride any other
bicycle upon the streets of the city or by any combination thereof. In case of
impoundment, the bicycle shall be surrendered to the Chief of Police.
(Prior Code, § 10.40.200)
(Ord. 2357, passed - -1965; Ord. 3761, passed 3-19-2024; Ord. 3769, passed 8-
20-2024)
CHAPTER 72: RAILROADS
Section
   
72.001   Crossing lights required
   
72.002   Lights; failure to provide; notice; cost
   
72.003   Obstruction of streets prohibited; time limit
   
72.004   Unlawful nuisance
 
   
72.999   Penalty
§ 72.001 CROSSING LIGHTS REQUIRED.
   Every railroad or railway company owning, controlling or operating any line
of railroad or railway extending into or passing through the city, or any
person or receiver appointed by any court who is in possession of or operating
any line of railroad or railway extending into or passing through the city,
shall furnish at their own expense, at such places within the corporate limits
of the city as the railway or railroad crosses the public street or highway,
such light or lights as may be designated and ordered by the City Manager and
Board of Commissioners of the city, by resolution.
(Prior Code, § 10.36.010) (Ord. 1106, passed - -1909) Penalty, see §
72.999
§ 72.002 LIGHTS; FAILURE TO PROVIDE; NOTICE; COST.
   (A)   It shall be the duty of the City Clerk to notify any railroad or
railway company or person or receiver mentioned in §
72.001 of this chapter of the action of the City Manager and Board of
Commissioners designating and ordering lights to be placed at any railroad or
railway crossings, and such notice shall be given by service of a copy thereof
upon the ticket or station agent of any such railroad or railway company at the
city, or the receiver owning or operating such railroad or railway, and such
railroad or railway company, or person or receiver owning or operating such
railroad, who fails or neglects to furnish lights at such railroad or railway
crossings as may be designated by the City Manager and Board of Commissioners
for a period of 30 days after receiving service of notice from the City Clerk
shall forfeit and pay to the city the sum of $25 for each day such company,
person or receiver thereafter fails to comply with this chapter, which sum
shall be recovered by a civil action in any court of competent jurisdiction.
The failure to furnish each and every light ordered by the City Manager and
Board of Commissioners shall constitute a separate forfeit and cause of action,
and each day shall constitute a separate and distinct cause of action, which
may be recovered by the city in a separate cause of action.
(Prior Code, § 10.36.020)
   (B)   If any of the railroads or railways, persons or receivers mentioned in
this chapter fails or neglects to furnish lights at railroad crossings as may
be designated by the City Manager and Board of Commissioners within 30 days
after being notified, as mentioned in division (A) above, the city may furnish
the light and may recover therefor by way of penalty and forfeiture, in
addition to the penalty and forfeiture mentioned in division (A) above, double
the amount of any money paid or obligated to be paid by the city in procuring
and furnishing said lights, which may be recovered in a civil action in any
court of competent jurisdiction. The furnishing of lights by the city for every
three months shall constitute a separate cause of action in favor of the city.
(Prior Code, § 10.36.030)
(Ord. 1106, passed - -1909) Penalty, see §
72.999
§ 72.003 OBSTRUCTION OF STREETS PROHIBITED; TIME LIMIT.
   (A)   It is unlawful for any person, persons, corporation or corporations to
obstruct any street avenue or alley in the city by stopping or standing railway
cars, trains of cars, locomotive or locomotives, in, on or across any street
avenue or alley in the city, or the leaving closed of any crossing gate or
other device designed to prevent or regulate the passage of persons or vehicles
on said street, avenue or alley so as to prevent for more than ten minutes at
one time the passage of persons or vehicles on or along any street, avenue or
alley.
   (B)   The leaving of an opening of less than 30 feet in width in the
traveled portion of a street, avenue or alley shall be deemed to be a violation
of this chapter.
   (C)   Any conductor, engineer braker or firefighter, or any other person or
persons who is operating or assisting in operating any railway car, train of
cars, locomotive or locomotives which is allowed to obstruct any street, avenue
or alley in the city shall, on conviction, be deemed and held to be guilty of a
violation of this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 10.36.040) (Ord. 2164, passed - -1953) Penalty, see §
72.999
§ 72.004 UNLAWFUL NUISANCE.
   (A)   It is unlawful for any person, persons or corporations, within the
city limits, to park with the engine running a locomotive or locomotives within
500 feet of any residence or structure unless the locomotive or locomotives are
parked in a railroad yard, which shall be an area so designated by the railroad
and containing three or more tracks.
   (B)   Any conductor, engineer, braker or firefighter, or any other person or
persons who are operating or assisting in operating any locomotive or
locomotives in such an area, shall, on conviction, be deemed to be guilty of a
violation of this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 10.36.045) (Ord. 3176, passed - -1999) Penalty, see §
72.999
§ 72.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person, persons, corporation or corporations violating any of the
provisions of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 10.36.010)
   (B)   Any person, persons, corporation or corporations violating any portion
of §
72.004 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 10.36.045)
(Ord. 2164, passed - -1953; Ord. 3176, passed - -1999)
CHAPTER 73: TRAFFIC AND PARKING SCHEDULES
Schedule
      
I.   Main trafficways
      
II.   Parking restricted
      
III.   Truck routes
      
IV.   ATV restrictions
SCHEDULE I. MAIN TRAFFICWAYS.
   (A)   Pursuant to the provisions of K.S.A. 12-685, the following described
streets and avenues located in the city, are designated and established as main
trafficways:
      (1)   The following trafficways, known as U.S. Highways 54 and 69:
         (a)   Wall Street from the east city limits to the east line of
National Avenue;
         (b)   National Avenue from the north city limits to the east line of
the intersection of East National Avenue with Fairground Avenue; and
         (c)   Fairground Avenue from the south city limits to the south line
of East National Avenue.
      (2)   Sixth Street from National Street to Horton Street and Horton
Street from Sixth Street to Eighteenth Street, and Ninth Street from Horton
Street to Burke Street.
(Prior Code, § 10.20.010)
   (B)   The primary functions of main trafficways shall be the movement of
through traffic between areas of concentrated activity within the city and
between such areas within the city and traffic facilities outside the city.
(Prior Code, § 10.20.020)
(Ord. 2285, passed - -1962; Ord. 2365, passed - -1966)
SCHEDULE II. PARKING RESTRICTED.
   (A)   Parking restricted generally.
      (1)   Main Street parking. It is unlawful for any vehicle to park or
remain parked on North Main Street between 3rd Street and Old Fort Boulevard
for more than two consecutive hours between 7:00 a.m. and 6:00 p.m., unless the
parking space is posted with a different limit.
(Prior Code, § 10.24.010)
      (2)   Two-hour parking areas. It is unlawful for any person to cause,
allow, permit or suffer any vehicle registered in his or her name or operated
or controlled by him or her to be parked for more than two hours in public
parking areas designated and signed as “two-hour parking areas”.
(Prior Code, § 10.24.020)
      (3)   Public square; prohibited; exceptions. It is unlawful for any
person to park any vehicle upon the public square, also known as Market Square,
in the city, except in the spaces marked off by white lines and designated for
such parking by the authority of the governing body of the city, or to park any
vehicle in the entranceways to the parking spaces so marked off and designated.
(Prior Code, § 10.24.030)
      (4)   Truck tractor and trailer; prohibited where.
         (a)   It is unlawful for any person to park or stand any truck
tractor, trailer or semi-trailer on any street in that area zoned as R/S,
Residential Suburban District, R/1, Single-Family Dwelling District, R/2,
Single-Family Dwelling District, R/3, Two-Family Dwelling District and R/4,
Multiple-Family Dwelling District, pursuant to the zoning and subdivision
regulations adopted in Ord. 2992.
         (b)   It is unlawful for any person to park or stand any trailer or
semi-trailer which has been detached from a truck tractor on any street in the
city.
(Prior Code, § 10.24.040)
      (5)   Buses; permission required. It is unlawful for the driver of any
motor bus used for the conveyance of passengers for hire in any business not
operated wholly within the city, to park any motor bus used in such business on
any paved street in the city for the purpose of receiving and discharging
passengers, without first securing written permission to do so from the Board
of Commissioners of the city, and the written consent of the abutting property
owner before whose property the motor bus is to be parked.
(Prior Code, § 10.24.050)
      (6)   Violation; penalty. In addition to any punishment prescribed in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances, there will be a minimum fine created for
each violation in the amount of $60 for violation of this division (A).
(Prior Code, § 10.24.060)
   (B)   Prohibited parking.
      (1)   Prohibited areas. It is unlawful for any vehicle to be operated in
the city limits, or on city-owned property, on any surface other than a
roadway, parking lot or private drive.
(Prior Code, § 10.44.010)
      (2)   Penalty for violation. Any person violating the provisions of this
division (B) shall, upon conviction, be adjudged guilty of a misdemeanor and
shall be assessed a fine of not less than $250 and no more than $500.
(Prior Code, § 10.44.020)
(Ord. 1800, passed - -1925; Ord. 2220, passed - -1956; Ord. 2585, passed - -
1975; Ord. 2600, passed - -1976; Ord. 2675, passed - -1977; Ord. 2739, passed -
-1979; Ord. 2819, passed - -1982; Ord. 3096, passed - -1994; Ord. 3568, passed
11-3-2020; Ord. 3619, passed 11-1-2022)
SCHEDULE III. TRUCK ROUTES.
   (A)   Vehicles required to follow truck route.
      (1)   From and after the designation and signing of any truck route in
the city:
         (a)   To pick up or deliver cargo, such vehicle may depart from the
truck route(s) by the shortest distance to end from the point of said pickup or
delivery. In the case of multiple pickups or deliveries, said departure shall
take a route which has the shortest cumulative distance not on the truck route
(s); and
         (b)   Every truck tractor, trailer (with or without trailer or semi-
trailer) and every vehicle or trailer having three or more axles shall use and
follow such prescribed route or routes while in the city and shall not use any
residential street or other street, except those designated and marked as truck
routes.
      (2)   Officers of public safety of the city may direct the rerouting of
all such vehicles at their option.
(Prior Code, § 10.48.010)
   (B)   Prohibition of travel on Broadway.
      (1)   Motor homes, fifth-wheel trailers, trucks with a vehicle weight
over 11,400 pounds and recreational vehicles with a similar vehicle weight are
prohibited from travel on Broadway between Wall Street and Charles Street in
the city.
      (2)   This prohibition shall exclude all emergency vehicles (fire, safety
and police), and any utility service vehicles or street maintenance vehicles
owned by the city or by one of its franchise holders.
      (3)   Any person, persons, corporation or corporations violating any
portion of this division (B) shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished as provided in division (F) below.
(Prior Code, § 10.48.011)
   (C)   Truck tractors without trailers; permitted when. A truck tractor
without a trailer will be allowed to depart from a prescribed truck route and
proceed by the most direct route to the residence of the driver, owner or
operator and park said truck tractor on private property in a designated
parking area such as a driveway, garage and the like. Said truck tractor may
also be parked in front of the said residence on the public street; provided,
it can be done without interfering with traffic. Any truck tractor so parked
shall have its engine turned off. The vehicle shall not be left running once it
is parked. No more than one truck tractor can be parked at any single
residence.
(Prior Code, § 10.48.015)
   (D)   Truck tractors without trailers; permitted to deviate from truck route
to commercial business.
      (1)   A truck tractor without a trailer will be allowed to depart from a
prescribed truck route and proceed by the most direct route to a commercial
establishment in the city. Said truck tractor will be allowed to park in front
of said commercial establishment on the public street; providing that, can be
done without interfering with traffic, or in a parking lot provided by the
commercial establishment; provided that, does not interfere with traffic. Any
truck tractor so parked shall have its engine turned off. The vehicle should
not be left running once it is parked.
      (2)   Any person, persons, corporation or corporations violating any
portion of this schedule shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished as provided in division (F) below.
(Prior Code, § 10.48.016)
   (E)   Routes designated. The following streets have been designated as truck
routes for the city:
      (1)   U.S. 69 Bypass extending from the north city limits to the south
city limits:
      (2)   U.S. 54 commencing at the north city limits to the east city limits
by way the Wall Street U.S. 69 Bypass exchange;
      (3)   Third Street west from U.S. 69 Bypass to State Street;
      (4)   State Street north from the intersection of Third and State Streets
to the intersection of Wall and State Streets;
      (5)   Wall Street east from the intersection of State Street and Wall
Street to U.S. 69 Bypass;
      (6)   Humbolt Avenue (Maple Road) as it runs east from the west city
limits to the intersection of National Avenue;
      (7)   Linker Street as it runs from the intersection of Crawford and
Linker Street to National Avenue;
      (8)   National Avenue as it runs from the intersection of Humbolt Avenue/
Market Street and National Avenue north to the city limits;
      (9)   Cooper Street from 23rd Street to 20th Street;
      (10)   23rd Street from U.S. 69 Bypass to Liberty Bell Road;
      (11)   Liberty Bell Road from 23rd Street to Campbell on to Hudson Street
to 69 Highway; and
      (12)   20th Street from U.S. 69 Bypass to Cooper Street.
(Prior Code, § 10.48.020)
   (F)   Penalty for violation. The penalty for violation of this schedule is a
minimum fine of $125 up to and including a maximum fine of $500 per violation,
plus applicable court costs.
(Prior Code, § 10.48.030)
(Ord. 2740, passed - -1979; Ord. 2756, passed - -1980; Ord. 2787, passed - -
1981; Ord. 3262, passed - -2003; Ord. 3293, passed - -2005; Ord. 3318, passed -
-2006; Ord. 3621, passed 12-6-2022; Ord. 3622, passed 12-6-2022; Ord. 3623,
passed 12-6-2022)
SCHEDULE IV. ATV RESTRICTIONS.
   Other than to cross at an appropriately designated intersection or crossing,
it is a violation of this traffic code for a UTV to be operated on any of the
following streets:
   (A)   East Wall Street (U.S. 54 Highway); and
   (B)   U.S. 69 Highway.
(Ord. 3761, passed 3-19-2024)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 90.001 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   ANIMAL. Any and all types of animals, both domesticated and wild, male and
female, singular and plural.
   AT-LARGE. Off the premises of the owner or custodian of the animal or fowl
and not under the immediate control of the owner or custodian.
   FOWL. Any and all fowl, domesticated and wild, male and female, singular and
plural.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.010) (Ord. 1881, passed - -; Ord. 1891, passed - -; Ord.
1908, passed - -; Ord. 2831, passed - -1982)
§ 90.002 ANIMAL NUISANCES; OFFENSIVE PREMISES; DISEASED, DEAD ANIMALS.
   (A)   Offensive premises. Any person, or persons, who owns, keeps or uses
any yard, pen or place on his or her premises, or premises under his or her
control, within the city, in or upon which any animal or fowl may be kept in
such a manner as to become offensive to any persons residing in the vicinity,
or annoying to the public, is declared to be a common nuisance.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.020)
   (B)   Treating diseased animals. It is unlawful for any person to keep or
maintain any building or part thereof, or any place, within 100 feet of any
residence within the corporate limits of the city, where diseased animals are
treated or kept for treatment, and the keeping or maintenance of any building
or part thereof where diseased animals are kept for treatment within 100 feet
of a private residence within the city is declared to be a common nuisance, and
every person who keeps or maintains any such building or part thereof, or any
place, within 100 feet of a private residence within the corporate limits of
the city, where diseased animals are kept for treatment, is declared to be
guilty of keeping and maintaining a common nuisance and shall be deemed guilty
of a misdemeanor.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.040)
   (C)   Dead animals. It is unlawful for any person to place, deposit or leave
in any alley, street, lane or public place, or upon any property within the
corporate limits of the city, any offal or carcass of any dead animal, or any
part thereof, or any other matter which is likely to become offensive or
unwholesome, and the placing, depositing or leaving of any such matter or
substance is declared to be a common nuisance. Any person violating any of the
provisions of this division (C) is declared to be guilty of a misdemeanor.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.050)
(Ord. 1881, passed - -; Ord. 1891, passed - -; Ord. 1908, passed - -; Ord.
2831, passed - -1982) Penalty, see §
90.999
§ 90.003 AT-LARGE ANIMALS AND FOWL.
   No person owning or having in his or her custody animals or fowl shall
permit the same to go at large to the injury or annoyance of others, nor shall
such animals or fowl be permitted at large on the streets or other public ways
of the city. Such action is declared to be a nuisance and dangerous to the
public health and safety.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.030) (Ord. 1881, passed - -; Ord. 1891, passed - -; Ord.
1908, passed - -; Ord. 2831, passed - -1982) Penalty, see §
90.999
**** DOGS ****
§ 90.015 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   KEEPING AND HARBORING. Any person who allows any dog or dogs to habitually
remain or be lodged or fed at his or her home, store, yard, enclosure or place.
   MARSHAL or CITY MARSHAL. The Chief of Police of the city.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.010) (Ord. 1750, passed - -; Ord. 2342, passed - -)
§ 90.016 REGISTRATION; PROCEDURE; EXPIRATION DATE; DEADLINE.
   (A)   Required; procedure. No person shall keep a dog in the city after such
dog has reached the age of six months unless that person complies with the
following regulations. The owner, keeper or harborer of any dog shall cause his
or her name, with the name and description of the dog, to be registered with
the city, proof of rabies vaccination, and shall pay each year to the city,
before any dog is registered a registration fee of $3 per dog that is spayed/
neutered or $5 per dog that is not spayed/neutered. They shall keep upon the
neck of each dog the metallic tag that is furnished by the city with the number
and year of registry to be distinctly marked thereon. The city shall keep a
listing of the registry of dogs when registration is made.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.020)
   (B)   Expiration date. All registrations of dogs in the city as provided in
division (A) above will expire on May 31 in each and every year of the
registration of any such dog.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.030)
   (C)   Deadline. The owners or harborers of any dog or dogs within the city
shall have the whole of the month of April in each year within which to
register such dogs, and no arrests shall be made or penalties attached for
failure to register such dog or dogs until after April 30 of each year.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.060)
(Ord. 1750, passed - -; Ord. 1962, passed - -; Ord. 2342, passed - -; Ord.
3039, passed - -1992; Ord. 3567, passed 11-3-2020) Penalty, see §
90.999
§ 90.017 RUNNING AT-LARGE.
   (A)   It is unlawful for the owner, keeper and harborer of any dog or dogs
within the corporate limits of the city to permit such dog or dogs to run at-
large upon any of the streets, alleys or public grounds of the city, or to run
over or trespass upon the private grounds of other citizens or residents of the
city, and all dogs found running at large in violation of this section shall be
impounded or killed.
   (B)   A dog shall be deemed to be running at-large when not confined on the
premises of the owner, keeper or harborer, within a fenced enclosure or animal
shelter or under the control of a person, whether by lead, cord, leash, rope or
chain; provided, however, that, a dog may be considered confined if on a lead,
rope or chain which is securely fastened or picketed in a manner which is
sufficient to keep the dog on the premises.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.040) (Ord. 1750, passed - -; Ord. 2342, passed - -
) Penalty, see §
90.999
§ 90.018 REGISTRATION, IMPOUNDMENT OR DESTRUCTION; FEES.
   (A)   Capture; authority. The Chief of Police shall employ a suitable person
or persons whose duty it shall be to capture all dogs found running at large in
any public place in the city not having a registered collar in compliance with
§ 90.16(A) of this chapter, and to place them in a pound to be provided for
that purpose. If the owner does not appear in 48 hours after such impounding
and claim and register such dog or dogs, then the Chief of Police shall cause
such dog to be killed; provided that, the provisions of this section shall not
apply to dogs now owned or harbored in the city, unless they be found at large
without any owner or master. It shall be the duty of the City Clerk to report
and pay over to the City Treasurer, once each month, all moneys collected by
him or her for the registration fees, he or she taking the Treasurer’s receipt
therefor.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.050)
   (B)   Chief of Police; duties. It shall be the duty of the Chief of Police
in the month of May in each year, to employ sufficient help to have all dogs
within the city registered, captured or killed before June 1 of each year.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.070)
   (C)   Fees. Fees for dog registration, as provided in §
90.016(C) of this chapter, shall be set as follows. For the registration of a
neutered male dog or a spayed female dog, the fee shall be $3; for the
registration of an unneutered male dog or an unspayed female dog, the
registration fee shall be $5; for catching and impounding of each dog, the fee
shall be $7, to be collected from the person redeeming such dog; for receiving
and keeping each dog from the time delivered to the city until redeemed or
destroyed, the poundkeeper shall receive $3 per day, to be collected and taxed
as costs, from the party redeeming the same; for the killing of each dog, the
person performing such service shall receive a fee of not more than $5, to be
paid by the city. All fees collected under this section are to be turned over
to the City Clerk for deposit in the City’s General Fund.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.080)
(Ord. 1750, passed - -; Ord. 2342, passed - -; Ord. 2761, passed - -1980; Ord.
3039, passed - -1992)
§ 90.019 DOG TAX; REPORT AND PAYMENT TO CLERK.
   It shall be the duty of the Chief of Police to report and pay over to the
City Clerk once each week, all moneys collected by him or her as dog tax, he or
she taking the Clerk’s receipt therefor.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.090) (Ord. 1750, passed - -)
§ 90.020 DESTRUCTION OR IMPOUNDMENT WHEN.
   The Chief of Police, or any person acting under his or her authority, is
authorized and empowered to destroy any and all dogs over the age of six months
upon which the proper tax has not been paid, or upon whose neck there is no
tax-tag or plate, after April 30 of each year, as provided in this chapter, or
to catch any such dogs and deliver them to the city poundkeeper.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.100) (Ord. 1750, passed - -)
§ 90.021 POUNDKEEPER.
   It shall be the duty of the keeper of the city prison, who is designated as
the keeper of the city pound, to prepare a receptacle in which he or she shall
keep all dogs brought to him or her under the operation of the preceding
sections of this chapter, for the space of 48 hours, and if the owner or keeper
of any such dog does not appear within that time and claim and register such
dog and pay all costs that have accrued, then the poundkeeper, or someone
selected for the purpose, shall kill the dog.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.110) (Ord. 1750, passed - -)
§ 90.022 FEMALES RUNNING AT-LARGE.
   (A)   The running at-large of any female dog or dogs during their breeding
season is declared to be and is contrary to the public welfare and order, and
it is unlawful, notwithstanding the payment of any license or dog tax thereon.
   (B)   The Chief of Police or any of his or her employees are authorized to
impound such female dog during its breeding season and to deliver same over to
the city poundkeeper.
   (C)   When so impounded, such female dog and the owner or keeper thereof
shall be subject to the same liabilities, penalties and fees as provided in
this subchapter for unlicensed dogs.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.120) (Ord. 1750, passed - -) Penalty, see §
90.999
§ 90.023 VICIOUS DOGS OR OTHER ANIMALS; KEEPING; DESTRUCTION.
   (A)   Generally. It shall be unlawful to keep, harbor or in any way possess
within the corporate limits of the city:
      (1)   Any warm-blooded, carnivorous or omnivorous, wild or exotic animal
(including, but not limited to, non-human primates, raccoons, skunks, foxes and
wild and exotic cats; but excluding fowl, ferrets and small rodents of
varieties used for laboratory purposes);
      (2)   Any animal having poisonous bites; and/or
      (3)   Any vicious dog which has a tendency or propensity to attack or
otherwise endanger the safety of human beings or domestic animals without
provocation.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.130)
   (B)   Destruction; when. If, upon the hearing provided for in §
90.022 of this chapter, the Municipal Judge decides that a dog is vicious and
liable to bite and attack persons within the corporate limits of the city or
has bitten or attacked any person, the Municipal Judge shall order and direct
that said dog be killed.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.140)
   (C)   Destruction; where. Whenever the Municipal Judge of the city orders
and directs that any dog be killed because of the dog being vicious or having
bitten or attacked persons, the Chief of Police shall kill or cause the dogs to
be killed wherever the same may be found within the corporate limits of the
city.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.150)
(Ord. 1750, passed - -; Ord. 2342, passed - -; Ord. 2959, passed - -1987; Ord.
3567, passed 11-3-2020) Penalty, see §
90.999
§ 90.024 DANGEROUS ANIMALS.
   (A)   Definition.
      (1)   A DANGEROUS ANIMAL, as used in this section, shall mean:
         (a)   Any animal which attacks or bites any human being or domestic
animal or has attacked or bitten a human being or domestic animal;
         (b)   Any animal with a known propensity, tendency or disposition to
attack, cause injury to, or threaten the safety of human beings or domestic
animals;
         (c)   Any animal which in a vicious or threatening manner, approaches
any person in apparent attack upon the person while on the streets, sidewalks
or any public grounds or places, or on private property; or
         (d)   Any animal which because of its size, physical characteristics
and vicious propensities is capable of inflicting serious physical injury or
death on a human being.
      (2)   Notwithstanding anything to the contrary herein, no animal shall be
deemed DANGEROUS if any prescribed conduct listed herein took place at a time
when:
         (a)   The person who was the object of such conduct was committing a
willful trespass or other tort upon the premises of the owner or keeper of the
animal;
         (b)   The person who was the object of such conduct was at the time
provoking, teasing, tormenting, abusing or assaulting the animal;
         (c)   The person who was the object of such conduct was committing or
attempting to commit a crime; or
         (d)   The animal who was the object of such conduct was provoking,
tormenting, abusing or assaulting the animal.
      (3)   Any dog owned by governmental or law enforcement agencies when used
in the service of those agencies, shall be exempt from the requirements of this
section.
   (B)   Notice. In the event that the animal control officer has probable
cause to believe that an animal is dangerous, as defined by division (A) above,
a notice to appear in Municipal Court will be issued to the animal’s owner.
   (C)   Hearing. At the hearing in Municipal Court, the city shall have the
burden of proof to show by clear and convincing evidence that the animal is
dangerous pursuant to division (A) above and that the animal is being kept or
harbored within the city limits. The parties may present relevant evidence at
the hearing as to whether the animal is dangerous and as to the appropriate
disposition of the animal.
   (D)   Determination. At the conclusion of the hearing, the Municipal Judge
shall determine whether the animal is dangerous pursuant to division (A) above.
If a determination is made that the animal is dangerous, the Municipal Judge
shall decide whether:
      (1)   The animal shall be destroyed;
      (2)   The animal shall be removed from the city under appropriate
conditions; or
      (3)   The owner or keeper shall be allowed to keep the animal subject to
the provisions of §
90.023(B) of this chapter.
   (E)   Vicious animal. A VICIOUS ANIMAL is defined as any animal that has
caused or attempted to cause serious injury to any person or domesticated
animal or has maimed or killed any person or domesticated animal. All dangerous
animals shall have for identification: city tags and a microchip implant. The
owner or keeper of the dangerous animal shall be responsible for all costs
associated with the identification requirements. All canines in this category
shall obtain these identifiers upon the animal reaching 120 days of age.
   (F)   Leash and muzzle. No person shall permit a dangerous dog to go outside
its kennel or pen unless such dog is securely leashed with a leash no longer
than four feet in length. No person shall permit a dangerous dog to be kept on
a chain, rope or other type of leash outside its kennel or pen unless a person
is in physical control of the leash. Such dogs may not be leashed to inanimate
objects such as trees, posts, buildings and the like. In addition, all
dangerous dogs on a leash outside the animal’s kennel must be muzzled by a
muzzling device sufficient to prevent such dog from biting persons or other
animals.
   (G)   Confinement. All dangerous dogs shall be securely confined indoors or
in a securely enclosed and locked pen or kennel, except when leashed and
muzzled. Such pen, kennel or structure must have secure sides and a secure top
attached to the sides. All structures used to confine dangerous dogs must be
locked with a key or combination lock when such animals are within the
structure. Such structure must have a secure bottom or floor attached to the
sides of the pen, or the sides of the pen must be embedded in the ground no
less than two feet. All structures erected to house dangerous dogs must comply
with all zoning and building regulations of the city. All such structures must
be adequately lighted and ventilated and kept in a clean and sanitary
condition.
   (H)   Confinement indoors.
      (1)   No dangerous dog may be kept on a porch, patio or in any part of a
house or structure that would allow the dog to exit such building on its own
volition.
      (2)   In addition, no such animal may be kept in a house or structure
when the windows are open or when screen windows or screen doors are the only
obstacle preventing the dog from exiting the structure.
   (I)   Signs; muzzling.
      (1)   A vicious animal shall be kept as required under this code.
      (2)   Dangerous animals shall also have “dangerous animal” signs posted
on the from and rear of the property and if the dangerous animal is being
walked outside the home, it must be muzzled and restrained on a leash of no
longer than four feel in length and of sufficient tensile strength to restrain
the animal.
   (J)   Liability insurance. Dangerous animal owners shall carry no less than
$250,000 liability insurance for each dog deemed dangerous. The owner of said
animal shall file a copy of said certificate of insurance with the city, with
said copy to reflect the amount of coverage and the dates of coverage.
   (K)   Location. No dangerous animal may be maintained within 400 feet of a
school, park or public playground.
   (L)   Impounding.
      (1)   An animal that has been deemed dangerous in Municipal Court, and
that has caused any serious injury to, or killed any person or domesticated
animal shall be impounded by the city.
      (2)   The animal shall then be euthanized and disposed of in a humane
manner by order of the Municipal Court Judge or the Chief of Police and, if no
proof of rabies vaccination is available, the head will be sent for proper
testing all at the owner’s expense.
   (M)   Age limits. No person under the age of 18 shall own or possess any
dangerous animal within the city limits.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.135) (Ord. 3567, passed 11-3-2020) Penalty, see §
90.999
§ 90.025 ANNOYING OR DISTURBING DOGS.
   It is unlawful for any person to keep within the city any dog that is in the
habit of coming out in the street to follow after and bark at persons or
vehicles passing by, or to keep any dog that worries or bites any fowl or
animal, or to keep any dog which by loud, frequent and habitual barking,
howling or yelping annoys or disturbs any neighborhood or person.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.160) (Ord. 1750, passed - -) Penalty, see §
90.999
§ 90.026 COMPLAINTS; FILING.
   It shall be the duty of the Chief of Police and all police officers to file
complaints against all persons keeping or harboring any dog or dogs in
violation of the provisions of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.170) (Ord. 1750, passed - -)
§ 90.027 TAGS; TRANSFER OR REMOVAL PROHIBITED.
   (A)   It is unlawful for any person who is the owner or keeper of any dog in
the city to allow or permit any such dog to wear any other tag or check than
the identical one issued by the Clerk of the city for such dog.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.180)
   (B)   It is unlawful for any person to remove or cause to be removed the
collar, check or tag from any dog in the city without the consent of the owner
or keeper thereof.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.190)
(Ord. 1750, passed - -) Penalty, see §
90.999
§ 90.028 OWNER LIABILITY.
   The owner, keeper or harborer of any dog or dogs which trespass upon and do
injury to public property or to the private property of any other person, and
who fails to restore such injury, shall be liable for all damages caused by
such unlawful trespass of said dog or dogs and, in addition thereto, shall be
subject to the penalties provided for in §
90.999 of this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.200) (Ord. 2342, passed - -)
§ 90.029 IMPOUNDMENT; INTERFERENCE PROHIBITED.
   It is unlawful for any person or persons to obstruct, hinder or prevent the
impounding of any dog running at large contrary to the provisions of this
subchapter, or to break open, destroy or injure the city dog pound, door, gate
or enclosure thereof, or to take or attempt to take therefrom a dog therein
impounded without first paying the fee and costs as provided in this
subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.210) (Ord. 2342, passed - -) Penalty, see §
90.999
§ 90.030 ENFORCEMENT.
   It shall be the duty of the police officers of the city, any person or
persons specially appointed by the Chief of Police and the custodian of the
city dump to enforce this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.220) (Ord. 2342, passed - -)
**** RABIES ****
§ 90.045 KEEPING UNVACCINATED DOG PROHIBITED.
   (A)   The keeping or harboring of any dog within the city that does not have
an active vaccination for rabies is declared to be contrary to the public
welfare and unlawful, notwithstanding the payment of any license or dog tax
that has been or may hereafter be paid.
(Prior Code, § 6.12.010)
   (B)   The Chief of Police or any person acting under his or her authority is
authorized and empowered to destroy any and all dugs which have not been
vaccinated according to the terms of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 6.12.040)
(Ord. 1975, passed - -1936) Penalty, see §
90.999
§ 90.046 VACCINATION; TIME LIMIT; CERTIFICATE REQUIRED.
   (A)   The owner, keeper or harborer of any unvaccinated dog or dogs within
the city is given 15 days from the effective date of the ordinance codified in
this subchapter to have the dog or dogs vaccinated for rabies.
(Prior Code, § 6.12.020)
   (B)   It is made the duty of any owner, keeper or harborer of any dog or
dogs within the city, when having a dog vaccinated for rabies, to obtain a
certificate showing the date of the vaccination and by whom the same was made.
(Prior Code, § 6.12.030)
(Ord. 1975, passed - -1936) Penalty, see §
90.999
§ 90.047 KEEPING AND HARBORING.
   Any person who allows any dog or dogs to habitually remain or be lodged or
fed at his or her home, store, yard, enclosure or place shall be deemed and
considered as keeping and harboring the same within the meaning of this
subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 6.12.050) (Ord. 1975, passed - -1936)
§ 90.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any of the provisions of this chapter is deemed
guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof, shall be penalized as
provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.060)
   (B)   Any person who is convicted in Police Court of violating any provision
of §§
90.015 through
90.030 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and shall be
punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 6.08.230)
   (C)   Any person owning, keeping or harboring any dog in the city in
violation of the provisions of §§ 90.45 through
90.047 of this chapter or who violates any of the provisions of this chapter
shall, upon conviction thereof, be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 6.12.060)
