**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 91.001 WATER FLUORIDATION; FUNDS.
   (A)   Upon receiving the consent and approval of the state’s Board of Health
and until further direction of the governing body, the Water Department is
authorized and directed to provide the means and to proceed with the
introduction of sufficient fluoride to raise the total fluoride concentration
in the finished water to one part of fluoride to every million parts of water
being distributed in the water supply system of the city.
(Prior Code, § 8.08.010)
   (B)   Funds for fluoridation shall be provided from Water Department funds.
(Prior Code, § 8.08.020)
(Ord. 2131, passed - -1952)
§ 91.002 SMOKING IN PUBLIC.
   (A)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly requires a different
meaning. When not inconsistent with the context, words used in the present
tense include the future, words in the plural number include the singular
number, and words in the singular number include the plural number. The word
“shall” is always mandatory.
      CITY. The City of Fort Scott.
      PERSON. Any person, firm, partnership, association, corporation, company
or organization of any kind.
(Prior Code, § 8.32.010)
   (B)   Smoking prohibited in specified places. No person shall smoke or carry
a lighted cigar, cigarette or pipe in:
      (1)   City Hall, that building housing the city administrative offices;
      (2)   Public Safety Building, that building housing the Police and Fire
Departments;
      (3)   Miscellaneous public buildings, all buildings owned and occupied by
city employees; and/or
      (4)   Vehicles, any car, truck or equipment owned by the city carrying
people.
(Prior Code, § 8.32.020)
   (C)   Placarding required. The supervisor, or his or her agent, having
control of premises upon which smoking is prohibited by or under the authority
of this section, shall conspicuously display upon the premises a sign reading:
“SMOKING PROHIBITED BY LAW”.
(Prior Code, § 8.32.030)
   (D)   Violation of sign. No person shall smoke in any properly placarded
place, nor shall any person remove any placard required to be erected by or
under the authority of this section.
(Prior Code, § 8.32.040)
(Ord. 3175, passed - -1999) Penalty, see §
91.999
**** NUISANCES ****
§ 91.015 NUISANCES ENUMERATED.
   It is unlawful for any person to maintain or permit any nuisance within the
city as defined, without limitation, as follows:
   (A)   Filth, excrement, lumber, rocks, dirt, cans, paper, trash, metal or
any other offensive or disagreeable thing or substance thrown or left or
deposited upon any street, avenue, alley, sidewalk, park, public or private
enclosure or lot whether vacant or occupied;
   (B)   All dead animals not removed within 24 hours after death;
   (C)   Any place of structure or substance which emits or causes any
offensive, disagreeable or nauseous odors;
   (D)   All stagnant ponds or pools of water;
   (E)   All grass or weeds or other unsightly vegetation not usually
cultivated or grown for domestic use or to be marketed or for ornamental
purposes;
   (F)   All furniture (except for that specifically constructed and intended
for use outside a dwelling and more commonly referred to as patio furniture.
lawn furniture, picnic tables or chairs and the like), including, but not
limited to, refrigerators, freezers, heating and cooking stoves, kitchen
ranges, ovens, furnaces, laundry and dishwashing machines, dryers, disconnected
air conditioning units, televisions, hot water heaters, trash compactors,
toilet bowls, water closets, sinks, lavatories, tubs and showers, if stored,
placed or set upon the front, side or rear yards, or on any open porch, in any
attached carport, in any freestanding carport, or in any garage or shed that is
without doors to conceal such items;
   (G)   All articles or things whatsoever caused, kept, maintained or
permitted by any person to the injury, annoyance or inconvenience of the public
or of, any neighborhood; and
   (H)   Any fence, structure, thing or substance placed upon or being upon any
street, sidewalk, alley or public ground so as to obstruct the same, except as
permitted by the laws of the city.
(Prior Code, § 8.20.010) (Ord. 3133, passed - -1996; Ord. 3411, passed 4-3-
2012) Penalty, see §
91.999
§ 91.016 PUBLIC OFFICER.
   The City Manager shall designate a public officer to be charged with the
administration and enforcement of this subchapter. Other appropriate personnel
of the city may also be designated to enforce this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 8.20.020) (Ord. 3133, passed - -1996)
§ 91.017 COMPLAINTS; INQUIRY AND INSPECTION.
   The public officer shall make inquiry and inspection of premises upon
receiving a complaint or complaints in writing signed by two or more persons
stating that a nuisance exists and describing the same and where located or is
informed that a nuisance may exist by any Health Official, Chief of Police,
Fire Chief or state social worker. The public officer may make such inquiry and
inspection when he or she observes conditions which appear to constitute a
nuisance. Upon making any inquiry and inspection, the public officer shall make
a written report of findings.
(Prior Code, § 8.20.030) (Ord. 3133, passed - -1996)
§ 91.018 NOTICE; CONTENTS.
   (A)   Any person, corporation, partnership or association found by the
public officer to be in violation of §
91.015 of this chapter shall be served a notice of such violation. The notice
shall be served by one of the three following methods:
      (1)   Personal service upon the person in violation;
      (2)   Service by restricted mail, postage prepaid, return receipt
requested; or
      (3)   In the event the whereabouts of such person are unknown and the
same cannot be ascertained in the exercise of reasonable diligence, an
affidavit to that effect shall be made by the public officer and filed with the
City Clerk, and the serving of the resolution shall be made by publishing the
same once each week for two consecutive weeks in the official city newspaper
and by posting a copy of the resolution on the premises where such condition
exists.
(Prior Code, § 8.20.040)
   (B)   The notice shall state the condition(s) which is (are) in violation of
§
91.015 of this chapter. The notice shall also inform the person, corporation,
partnership or association that:
      (1)   He, she or they shall have ten days from the date of serving the
notice to abate the condition(s) in violation of §
91.015 of this chapter;
      (2)   He, she or they have ten days from the date of serving the notice
to request an extension of time to abate from, the public officer of the matter
as provided by §
91.015 of this chapter; and
      (3)   Failure to abate the condition(s) or to request a hearing within
the time allowed may result in prosecution as provided by §
91.999 of this chapter and/or abatement of the condition(s) by the city as
provided by §
91.019 of this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 8.20.050)
(Ord. 3133, passed - -1996)
§ 91.019 ABATEMENT.
   (A)   In addition to, or as an alternative to prosecution as provided in §
91.999 of this chapter, the public officer may seek to remedy violations of
this section in the following manner. If a person to whom a notice has been
sent pursuant to §
91.018(A) of this chapter has neither alleviated the conditions causing the
alleged violation the public officer may present a resolution to the governing
body for adoption authorizing the public officer or other agents of the city to
abate the conditions causing the violation at the end of ten days after passage
of the resolution. The resolution shall further provide that the costs incurred
by the city shall be charged against the lot or parcel of ground on which the
nuisance was located as provided in §
91.021 of this chapter. A copy of the resolution shall be served upon the
person in violation in one of the following ways:
      (1)   Personal service upon the person in violation;
      (2)   Service by restricted mail, postage prepaid, return receipt
requested; or
      (3)   In the event the whereabouts of such person are unknown and the
same cannot be ascertained in the exercise of reasonable diligence, an
affidavit to that effect shall be made by the public officer and filed with the
City Clerk, and the serving of the resolution shall be made by publishing the
same once each week for two consecutive weeks in the official city newspaper
and by posting a copy of the resolution on the premises where such condition
exists.
   (B)   The city may also initiate court proceedings to force the abatement of
nuisances.
(Prior Code, § 8.20.070) (Ord. 3133, passed - -1996)
§ 91.020 EXTENSION OF TIME TO COMPLY.
   The public officer may grant a reasonable extension of time to comply with
the nuisance abatement prior to issuing a complaint in Municipal Court pursuant
to §
91.999 of this chapter presenting a resolution to the governing body for
abatement, as provided in §
91.019 of this chapter; provided that, a good faith effort is being made to
abating the nuisance. Prior to adoption of the presented resolution, the
governing body may grant a reasonable extension of time to abate the
resolution.
(Prior Code, § 8.20.080) (Ord. 3133, passed - -1996)
§ 91.021 COSTS ASSESSED.
   If the city abates the nuisance pursuant to §
91.019 of this chapter, the cost of abatement shall be charged against the lot
or parcel of ground on which the nuisance was located. The city may also obtain
a judgment against the person or entity to cover the case of abating the
nuisance. The City Clerk shall, at the time of certifying other taxes to the
county clerk, certify the costs as provided in this section. The County Clerk
shall extend the same on the tax roll and it shall be collected by the County
Treasurer and paid to the city as other city taxes are collected and paid.
(Prior Code, § 8.20.090) (Ord. 3133, passed - -1996)
§ 91.022 PUBLIC NUISANCES.
   (A)   A public nuisance is an act or omission that obstructs, damages or
inconveniences the rights of the community. It shall be unlawful for any person
to create or commit a public nuisance in or upon any city park, city street,
city right-of-way, city alleyway, public parking lot, public sidewalk, public
building or any other place of public access unless authorized by the City
Manager in a designated area.
   (B)   Examples of a public nuisance include, but is not limited to:
      (1)   Depositing feces and or urine, be it human or animal;
      (2)   Being noticeably intoxicated due to excessive drug or alcohol use;
      (3)   Making living accommodations or preparations to sleep, sleeping,
erecting a tent or storing belongings, among other activities;
      (4)   Yelling, shouting or screaming in a manner that causes members of
the public alarm or concern;
      (5)   Loud or disruptive noises; and/or
      (6)   Beg or solicit for money or food.
(Prior Code, § 8.20.100) (Ord. 3594, passed 10-19-2021) Penalty, see §
91.999
**** WEED CONTROL ****
§ 91.035 WEEDS TO BE REMOVED.
   It shall be unlawful for any owner, agent, lessee, tenant or other person
occupying or having charge or control of any premises to permit weeds to remain
upon said premises or any area between the property lines of said premises and
the centerline of any adjacent street or alley, including, but not specifically
limited to, sidewalks, streets, alley, easements, right-of-way and all other
areas, public or private. All weeds, as hereinafter defined, are hereby
declared a nuisance and are subject to abatement as hereinafter provided.
(Prior Code, § 8.24.010) (Ord. 3435, passed 1-7-2014) Penalty, see §
91.999
§ 91.036 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   CALENDAR YEAR. The period of time beginning January 1 and ending December 31
of the same year.
   WEEDS. Any of the following:
      (1)   Brush and woody vines shall be classified as weeds;
      (2)   Weeds and indigenous grasses which may attain such large growth as
to become, when dry, a fire menace to adjacent improved property;
      (3)   Weeds which bear or may bear seeds of a downy or wingy nature;
      (4)   Weeds which are located in an area which harbors rats, insects,
animals, reptiles or any other creature which either may or does constitute a
menace to health, public safety or welfare; and
      (5)   Weeds and indigenous grasses on or about residential property
which, because of its height, has a blighting influence on the neighborhood.
Any such weeds and indigenous grasses shall be presumed to be blighting if they
exceed eight inches in height.
(Prior Code, § 8.24.020) (Ord. 3435, passed 1-7-2014)
§ 91.037 PUBLIC OFFICER; NOTICE TO REMOVE.
   (A)   The City Manager shall designate a public officer to be charged with
the administration and enforcement of this subchapter. The public officer or an
authorized assistant shall notify in writing the owner, occupant or agent in
charge of any premises in the city upon which weeds exist in violation of this
subchapter, by mail or by personal service, once per calendar year. Such notice
shall include the following:
      (1)   The owner, occupant or agent in charge of the property is in
violation of the city’s weed control law;
      (2)   The owner, occupant or agent in charge of the property is ordered
to cut the weeds within ten days of the receipt of the notice;
      (3)   The owner, occupant or agent in charge of the property may request
a hearing before the governing body or its designated representative within
five days of the receipt of notice;
      (4)   If the owner, occupant or agent in charge of the property does not
cut the weeds, the city or its authorized agent will cut the weeds and assess
the cost of the cutting, including a reasonable administrative fee, against the
owner, occupant or agent in charge of the property;
      (5)   The owner, occupant or agent in charge of the property will be
given an opportunity to pay the assessment and, if it is not paid, will be
added to the property tax as a special assessment;
      (6)   No further notice shall be given prior to removal of weeds during
the current calendar year; and
      (7)   The public officer should be contacted if there are any questions
regarding the order.
   (B)   If there is a change in the record owner of title to property
subsequent to the giving of notice pursuant to this section, the city may not
recover any costs or levy an assessment for the costs incurred by the cutting
or destruction of weeds on such property unless the new record owner of title
to such property is provided notice as required by this section.
(Prior Code, § 8.24.030) (Ord. 3435, passed 1-7-2014)
§ 91.038 ABATEMENT; ASSESSMENT OF COSTS.
   (A)   Upon the expiration of ten days after receipt of the notice required
by §
91.038 of this chapter, and in the event that the owner, occupant or agent in
charge of the premises shall neglect or fail to comply with the requirements of
§
91.035 of this chapter, the public officer or an authorized assistant shall
cause to be cut, destroyed and/or removed all such weeds and abate the nuisance
created thereby at any time during the current calendar year.
   (B)   The public officer or an authorized assistant shall give notice to the
owner, occupant or agent in charge of the premises by mail of the costs of
abatement of the nuisance. The notice shall state that payment of the costs is
due and payable within 30 days following receipt of the notice. Such costs to
be assessed be a flat fee of $100.
   (C)   If the costs of removal or abatement remain unpaid after 30 days
following receipt of notice, a record of the costs of cutting and destruction
and/or removal shall be certified to the City Clerk who shall cause such costs
to be assessed against the particular lot or piece of land on which such weeds
were so removed, and against such lots or pieces of land in front of or
abutting on such street or alley on which such weeds were so removed. The City
Clerk shall certify the assessment to the County Clerk at the time other
special assessments are certified for spreading on the tax rolls of the county.
(Prior Code, § 8.24.040) (Ord. 3435, passed 1-7-2014; Ord. 3488, passed 12-13-
2016; Ord. 3576, passed 4-6-2021; Ord. 3608, passed 5-3-2022)
§ 91.039 RIGHT OF ENTRY.
   The public officer, and the public officer’s authorized assistants,
employees, contracting agents or other representatives are hereby expressly
authorized to enter upon private property at all reasonable hours for the
purpose of cutting, destroyed and/or removing such weeds in a manner not
inconsistent with this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 8.24.050) (Ord. 3435, passed 1-7-2014)
§ 91.040 UNLAWFUL INTERFERENCE.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person to interfere with or to attempt to
prevent the public officer or the public officer’s authorized representative
from entering upon any such lot or piece of ground or from proceeding with such
cutting and destruction.
   (B)   Such interference shall constitute an ordinance violation.
(Prior Code, § 8.24.060) (Ord. 3435, passed 1-7-2014) Penalty, see §
91.999
§ 91.041 NOXIOUS WEEDS.
   Nothing in this subchapter shall affect or impair the rights of the city
under the provisions of K.S.A. Chapter 2, Article 13, relating to the control
and eradication or certain noxious weeds.
(Prior Code, § 8.24.070) (Ord. 3435, passed 1-7-2014)
§ 91.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to § 10.99 of this code of
ordinances.
   (B)   Any person violating §
91.002 of this chapter is subject to §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 8.32.050)
   (C)   Should the person, corporation, partnership or association fail to
comply with the notice to abate the nuisance or request a hearing, per §§
91.015 through
91.022 of this chapter, the public officer may file a complaint in the
Municipal Court of the city against such person, corporation, partnership or
association and, upon conviction of any violation of provisions of §
91.015 of this chapter, be fined in an amount not to exceed $100 or be
imprisoned not to exceed 30 days or be both fined and imprisoned. Each day
during or on which a violation occurs or continues after notice has been served
shall constitute an additional or separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 8.20.060)
(Ord. 3133, passed - -1996; Ord. 3175, passed - -1999)
CHAPTER 92: FAIR HOUSING
Section
   
92.001   Definitions
   
92.002   Renting or selling; prohibited acts
   
92.003   Loans; prohibited acts
   
92.004   Membership in housing service or organization; prohibited acts
   
92.005   Preference permitted when; conditions
   
92.006   Applicability
 
   
92.999   Penalty
§ 92.001 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   DWELLING. Any building, structure or portion thereof which is occupied as,
or designed or intended for occupancy as, a residence by one or more families,
and any vacant land which is offered for sale or lease for the construction or
location thereon of any such building, structure or portion thereof.
   FAMILY. Includes a single individual.
   PERSON. Includes one or more individuals, corporations, partnerships,
associations, labor organizations, legal representatives, mutual companies,
joint-stock companies, trusts, unincorporated organizations, trustees in
bankruptcy, receivers and fiduciaries.
   TO RENT. Includes to lease, to sublease, to let and otherwise to grant for a
consideration the right to occupy premises not owned by the occupant.
(Prior Code, § 9.06.010) (Ord. 2512, passed - -1973)
§ 92.002 RENTING OR SELLING; PROHIBITED ACTS.
   Except as exempted in this chapter, it is unlawful:
   (A)   To refuse to sell or rent after the making of a bona fide offer, or to
refuse to negotiate for the sale or rental, or otherwise make unavailable or
deny, a dwelling to any person because of race, color, religion, sex or
national origin;
   (B)   To discriminate against any person in the terms, conditions or
privileges of sale or rental of a dwelling, or in the provision of services or
facilities in connection therewith, because of race, color, religion, sex or
national origin;
   (C)   To make, print or publish, or cause to be made, printed or published
any notice, statement or advertisement with respect to the sale or rental of a
dwelling that indicates any preference, limitation or discrimination based on
race, color, religion, sex or national origin, or an intention to make any such
preference, limitation or discrimination;
   (D)   To represent to any person, because of race, color, religion, sex or
national origin, that any dwelling is not available for inspection, sale or
rental when such dwelling is in fact so available; and
   (E)   For profit, to induce or attempt to induce any person to sell or rent
any dwelling by representations regarding the entry or prospective entry into
the neighborhood of a person or persons of a particular race, color, religion,
sex or national origin.
(Prior Code, § 9.06.020) (Ord. 2512, passed - -1973; Ord. 2608, passed - -1976)
Penalty, see §
92.999
§ 92.003 LOANS; PROHIBITED ACTS.
   Except as exempted in this chapter, it is unlawful for any bank, building
and loan association, insurance company or other corporation, association, firm
or enterprise whose business consists in whole or in part in the making of
commercial real estate loans, to deny a loan or other financial assistance to a
person applying therefor for the purpose of purchasing, constructing,
improving, repairing or maintaining a dwelling; or to discriminate against any
person in the fixing of such loan or other financial assistance because of the
race, color, religion, sex or national origin of such person or of any person
associated with him or her in connection with such loan or other financial
assistance or the purposes of such loan or other financial assistance, or of
the present or prospective owners, lessees, tenants or occupants of the
dwelling or dwellings in relation to which such loan or other financial
assistance is to be made or given.
(Prior Code, § 9.06.030) (Ord. 2512, passed - -1973; Ord. 2608, passed - -1976)
Penalty, see §
92.999
§ 92.004 MEMBERSHIP IN HOUSING SERVICE OR ORGANIZATION; PROHIBITED ACTS.
   Except as exempted in this chapter, it is unlawful to deny any person access
to or membership or participation in any multiple-listing service, real estate
brokers’ organization or other service, organization or facility relating to
the business of selling or renting dwellings, or to discriminate against any
person in the terms or conditions of such access, membership or participation,
on account of race, color, religion, sex or national origin.
(Prior Code, § 9.06.040) (Ord. 2512, passed - -1973; Ord. 2608, passed - -1976)
Penalty, see §
92.999
§ 92.005 PREFERENCE PERMITTED WHEN; CONDITIONS.
   Nothing in this chapter shall prohibit a religious organization, association
or society, or any non- profit institution or organization operated, supervised
or controlled by or in conjunction with a religious organization, association
or society, from limiting the sale, rental or occupancy of dwellings which it
owns or operates for other than a commercial purpose to persons of the same
religion, or from giving preference to such persons, unless membership in such
religions is restricted on account of race, color, sex or national origin; nor
shall anything in this chapter prohibit a private club not in fact open to the
public, which as an incident to its primary purpose or purposes provides
lodgings which it owns or operates for other than a commercial purpose, from
giving preference to its members.
(Prior Code, § 9.06.050) (Ord. 2512, passed - -1973; Ord. 2608, passed - -1976)
§ 92.006 APPLICABILITY.
   (A)   Nothing in this chapter shall apply to:
      (1)   Any single-family house sold or rented by an owner; provided that,
such private individual owner does not own more than three such single-family
houses at any one time; provided further that, in the case of the sale of any
such single-family house by a private individual owner not residing in such
house at the time of such sale or who was not the most recent resident of such
house prior to such sale, the exemption granted by this division (A)(1) shall
apply only with respect to one such sale within any 24-month period; provided
further that, such bona fide private individual owner does not own any interest
in, nor is there owned, or reserved on his or her behalf, under any express or
voluntary agreement, title to or any right to all or a portion of the proceeds
from the sale or rental of more than three such single-family houses at any one
time; provided further that, the sale or rental of any such single-family house
shall be excepted from the application of this chapter only if such house is
sold or rented:
         (a)   Without the use in any manner of the sales or rental facilities
or the sales or rental services of any real estate broker, agent or
salesperson, or of such facilities or services of any person in the business of
selling or renting dwellings, or of any employee or agent of any such broker,
agent, salesperson or person; and
         (b)   Without the publication, posting or mailing, after notice, of
any advertisement or written notice in violation of §
92.002 of this chapter, but nothing in this proviso shall prohibit the use of
attorneys, escrow agents, abstractors, title companies and other such
professional assistance as necessary to perfect or transfer the title.
      (2)   Rooms or units in dwellings containing living quarters occupied or
intended to be occupied by not more than four families living independently of
each other, if the owner actually maintains and occupies one of such living
quarters as his or her residence.
   (B)   A person shall be deemed to be in the business of selling or renting
dwellings if:
      (1)   He or she has, within the preceding 12 months, participated as
principal in three or more transactions involving the sale or rental of any
dwelling or any interest therein;
      (2)   He or she has, within the preceding 12 months, participated as
agent, other than in the sale of his or her own personal residence, in
providing sales or rental facilities or sales or rental services in two or more
transactions involving the sale or rental of any dwelling or any interest
therein; or
      (3)   He or she is the owner of any dwelling designed or intended for
occupancy by, or occupied by, five or more families.
(Prior Code, § 9.06.060) (Ord. 2512, passed - -1973)
§ 92.999 PENALTY.
   Any person, firm, association or corporation violating any of the provisions
of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction,
shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 9.06.070) (Ord. 2512, passed - -1973)
CHAPTER 93: INOPERATIVE, ABANDONED VEHICLES
Section
   
93.001   Definitions
   
93.002   Findings
   
93.003   Enclosure required within city
   
93.004   Inapplicability; when
   
93.005   Presumption of inoperability; conditions
   
93.006   Violation; notice; time limit for compliance
 
   
93.999   Penalty
§ 93.001 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   INOPERABLE. A vehicle in a condition of being junked, wrecked, wholly or
partially dismantled, discarded, abandoned or unable to perform the functions
or purposes for which it was originally constructed.
   VEHICLE. Any automobile, truck, tractor or motorcycle which, as originally
built, contained an engine, regardless of whether it contains an engine at any
other time.
(Prior Code, § 10.32.010) (Ord. 2454, passed - -1971)
§ 93.002 FINDINGS.
   The Board of Commissioners of the city finds that junked, wrecked,
dismantled, inoperative, discarded or abandoned vehicles in and upon real
property within the city is a matter affecting the health, safety and general
welfare of the citizens of the city for the following reasons:
   (A)   Such vehicles serve as a breeding ground for flies, mosquitoes, rats
and other insects and rodents;
   (B)   They are a danger to persons, particularly children, because of broken
glass, sharp metal protrusions, insecure mounting on blocks, jacks or supports,
and because they are a ready source of fire and explosions;
   (C)   They encourage pilfering and theft, and constitute a blighting
influence upon the area in which they are located, thereby causing a loss in
property value to surrounding property; and
   (D)   They constitute a fire hazard in that they block access for the fire
equipment to adjacent buildings and structures.
(Prior Code, § 10.32.020) (Ord. 2454, passed - -1971)
§ 93.003 ENCLOSURE REQUIRED WITHIN CITY.
   Except as provided in this chapter, it is unlawful for any person,
partnership, corporation or its agent, either as owner, lessee, tenant or
occupant of any lot or land within the city to park, store or deposit, or
permit to be parked, stored or deposited thereon, an inoperable vehicle, unless
it is enclosed in a garage or other building.
(Prior Code, § 10.32.030) (Ord. 2454, passed - -1971) Penalty, see §
93.999
§ 93.004 INAPPLICABILITY; WHEN.
   The provisions of §
93.003 of this chapter shall not apply to any person, firm or corporation or
its agent who is conducting a business enterprise in compliance with existing
zoning regulations or who places such vehicles behind screening of sufficient
size, strength and density to screen such vehicles from the view of the public
using the streets and sidewalks, and to prohibit ready access to such vehicles
by children; provided, however, that, nothing in this section shall authorize
the maintenance of a public nuisance.
(Prior Code, § 10.32.040) (Ord. 2454, passed - -1971)
§ 93.005 PRESUMPTION OF INOPERABILITY; CONDITIONS.
   Any one of the following conditions shall raise the presumption that a
vehicle is inoperable:
   (A)   Absence of an effective registration plate upon such vehicle;
   (B)   Placement of the vehicle or parts thereof upon jacks, blocks, chains
or other supports; and/or
   (C)   Absence of one or more parts of the vehicle necessary for the lawful
operation of the vehicle upon the streets and highways.
(Prior Code, § 10.32.050) (Ord. 2454, passed - -1971)
§ 93.006 VIOLATION; NOTICE; TIME LIMIT FOR COMPLIANCE.
   Whenever an informal complaint is made to the Chief of Police or notice is
given to such officer of the existence of an apparent violation of this
chapter, he or she shall within ten days thereafter cause to be served upon the
person in possession or the owner of the real property upon which such
inoperable vehicle is located a written notice. Such notice shall inform such
person of the violation and direct that he or she take action within ten days
after receipt of the notice to comply with the provisions of this chapter. In
the event such person fails to comply with the provisions of this chapter
within such time, he or she shall be liable for penalties as provided for in
this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 10.32.060) (Ord. 2454, passed - -1971)
§ 93.999 PENALTY.
   Any person, partnership or corporation violating the provisions of this
chapter shall, upon conviction thereof, be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 10.32.070) (Ord. 2454, passed - -1971)

**** FISHING ON CITY LAKES ****
§ 94.001 CITY LAKES.
   This subchapter shall apply to fishing regulations on Lake Fort Scott, Rock
Creek Lake, Fern Lake, West Lake and Fort Scott Community College Lake.
(Prior Code, § 12.36.005) (Ord. 3037, passed - -1992; Ord. 3073, passed - -
1993)
§ 94.002 SIZE LIMITS.
   At any lake owned by the city, it is unlawful for any person to take,
intentionally injure or have in his or her possession any channel catfish under
18 inches in length, any bass under 15 inches in length or any walleyed pike
under 18 inches in length.
(Prior Code, § 12.36.010) (Ord. 2641, passed - -1977) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.003 CATCH LIMITS.
   (A)   At any lake owned by the city, during each 24-hour period, no person
shall take, or have in his or her possession, more than the catch and size
limits posted for each lake.
(Prior Code, § 12.36.020)
   (B)   No person may catch and retain more than two trout in a 24-hour period
from the Gunn Park Lakes. Persons are specifically prohibited from catching and
releasing trout.
(Prior Code, § 12.36.021)
(Ord. 2641, passed - -1977; Ord. 3018, passed - -1990; Ord. 3029, passed - -
1991; Ord. 3037, passed - -1992) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.004 LICENSE REQUIRED.
   All persons will be required to purchase a fishing license from the city and
pay the current fees in place at the time of purchase thereof for both an
annual fishing license and a short-term fishing license.
(Prior Code, § 12.36.022) (Ord. 3018, passed - -1990; Ord. 3029, passed - -
1991; Ord. 3037, passed - -1992; Ord. 3055, passed - -1992; Ord. 3073, passed -
-1993) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.005 PROHIBITED FISHING.
   All fishing is prohibited in the city rearing pond without written
permission from the city.
(Prior Code, § 12.36.025) (Ord. 3037, passed - -1992) Penalty, see §
94.999
**** GUNN PARK ****
§ 94.020 CAMPING.
   (A)   There shall be designated camping areas created by the City Commission
that shall be posted at Gunn Park. There shall also be established and created
a system allowing for the reservation and use of camping spaces at Gunn Park.
Any camping space may be reserved for a period of 14 days with the permit
issued for the utilization of said space. Reservations can be obtained by
calling the City Office or through a designated representative of the city.
   (B)   There shall be established a fee system which shall be adopted by the
City Commission and provide the authorized fees. All fees must be paid in
advance. Unless a space is reserved and paid, the occupant of that space must
immediately vacate the property upon request.
   (C)   Fees for RV camping spots are:
      (1)   Water and electric sites: $20 per night; and
      (2)   Fees for tent camping (primitive): $5 per night.
(Prior Code, § 12.25.010) (Ord. 3606, passed 4-5-2022)
§ 94.021 SHELTER HOUSES.
   (A)   Reservation system. The shelter house reservation system at Gunn Park
may be utilized from 8:00 a.m. to 9:00 p.m. Shelter houses may be reserved by
contacting City Hall. Shelters are not rented to be slept in overnight.
(Prior Code, § 12.40.010)
   (B)   Priority. All shelter houses will be reserved on a first come-first
served basis. Once a reservation is made, it must be confirmed by payment in
full of the fee for reserving said shelter house within 48 hours after
reservation is made.
(Prior Code, § 12.40.020)
   (C)   Fee schedule.
      (1)   The following fee schedule is established on the shelter houses at
Gunn Park:
 
Shelter houses #1 and #4 (open shelters)                           $20 per day
Shelter house #3 (enclosed shelter, no air conditioning or heat)   $25 per day
Shelter houses #2 and #6 (enclosed with heat and air conditioning) $30 per day
Shelter houses #5 and #7 (enclosed with heat and air conditioning) $35 per day
 
      (2)   All shelters must be paid for prior to use.
(Prior Code, § 12.40.030)
(Ord. 3083, passed - -1993; Ord. 3450, passed 12-16-2014; Ord. 3607, passed 4-
5-2022; Ord. 3753, passed 8-15-2023) Penalty, see §
94.999
**** LAKE FORT SCOTT ****
§ 94.035 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   BOURBON COUNTY RESIDENT. Any person or his spouse or minor son and daughter,
residing at home, who owns any real estate in Bourbon County or at a leased
lake lot at Lake Fort Scott that contains a dwelling thereon.
   MOTORBOAT. Any vessel propelled principally by machinery, but shall not
include a vessel which has a valid marine document issued by the United States
Coast Guard or any federal agency successor thereto.
   OPERATE. To navigate or otherwise use a vessel.
   OWNER. A person, other than a lienholder, having the property in or title to
a vessel.
   PERSON. An individual, partnership, firm, corporation, association or other
entity.
   ROWBOAT. Any vessel propelled exclusively by oars or paddles, and includes
canoes.
   SAILBOAT. Any vessel equipped for propulsion by sail.
   VESSEL. Every description of water-craft other than a seaplane on the water,
used or capable of being used as a means of transportation on water.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.010) (Ord. 2584, passed - -1975; Ord. 2712, passed - -
1979)
§ 94.036 DESIGNATION; NAME.
   All of the land heretofore acquired by and belonging to the city and located
on portions of Sections 14, 15, 22, 23 and 26, Township 26, Range 24, Bourbon
County, Kansas, on which a dam has been erected and a water-storage reservoir
created, be and the same is named, “Lake Fort Scott”, and is referred to in
this subchapter as the “lake”.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.020) (Ord. 2584, passed - -1975)
§ 94.037 EXTENSION OF JURISDICTION AND AUTHORITY.
   The police jurisdiction and the authority of the city is extended to include
the lake, and all ordinances of the city shall apply to acts and conduct of any
person thereon to the same extent as though the lake were located within the
corporate limits of the city.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.030) (Ord. 2584, passed - -1975)
§ 94.038 LIMITS OF CITY LIABILITY.
   The public shall be permitted the lawful, sanitary and orderly use of the
lake, as provided in this subchapter. All persons using the lake do so entirely
at their own risk so far as the city is concerned, and such persons are warned
that the city does not maintain the lake for any particular purpose, nor
represent that it is safe to use vessels thereon.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.040) (Ord. 2584, passed - -1975)
§ 94.039 BOAT LICENSES.
   (A)   Motorboat license; application; fee. A license fee for the operation
of a motorboat shall be paid to the City Clerk on an annual basis. The yearly
license fee for all persons for motorboats of less than 25 horsepower shall be
$15, and for motors of 25 horsepower or more, $25. The amount of horsepower
shall be determined by the manufacturer’s ratings.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.050)
   (B)   License; temporary fee.
      (1)   Persons not desiring to buy an annual license fee shall be
permitted to purchase a temporary license upon the same terms and conditions as
the annual license for a period of two days. The fee for a temporary license
shall be $5 for motors of less than 25 horsepower and $10 for motors of 25
horsepower or more.
      (2)   Persons who are engaged in the business of selling motorboats may
obtain a yearly license which would allow the testing of such motorboats on
Lake Fort Scott between the hours of 9:00 a.m. and 5:30 p.m. The fee for such
license shall be $75 per year.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.060)
   (C)   Rowboat license; application; fee. Persons desiring to operate a
rowboat on the lake shall make application to the City Clerk and obtain a
license therefor and the annual license fee shall be $6.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.070)
   (D)   Sailboat license; application; fee. Persons desiring to operate a
sailboat on the lake shall make application to the City Clerk for a license on
the same form prescribed for the application for a motorboat license. Such
license is required before the operation of any sailboat. The annual license
fee shall be $15.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.080)
   (E)   License expiration; to be aboard vessel. All licenses issued under
this subchapter shall expire on December 31 of each year, regardless of the
date of issuance. The license shall be aboard the vessel at all times when it
is to be operated.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.090)
   (F)   Compliance with state regulation required. It is unlawful for any
person to operate a vessel on the lake in violation of any of the applicable
provisions of K.S.A. Chapter 32, Article 11, or in violation of any of the
applicable rules and regulations adopted by the state’s Department of Wildlife
and Parks pursuant to the provisions of said statute.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.100)
   (G)   Boating; closure; no refund of fees. The lake may be closed to boating
at any time and for any period of time for any reason deemed advisable by the
governing body of the city. The licenses authorized in this subchapter are
conditioned on this provision, and no refund will be made to licensees in the
event of such closure.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.110)
   (H)   Additional regulations; posting. The city reserves the right to make
additional rules and regulations concerning boating on the lake, which shall be
binding upon licensees upon the posting of printed copies of such rules and
regulations at the lake.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.120)
   (I)   Operation limited in certain areas.
      (1)   No person shall operate a motorboat on the lake south of a line
beginning at a point where the west lines of Lots 24 and 25, Block E-2, Lake
Fort Scott Lots adjoin, and extending in a straight line and in a westerly
direction to a point on the west bank of the lake, designated by a marker
installed by the city, for water skiing or any similar activity.
      (2)   In the area of the lake, south of the line designated in division
(I)(1) above, no person shall operate a motorboat or other vessel:
         (a)   Without reducing the speed of the vessel so as to prevent wash
or wake from the vessel causing damage or unnecessary inconvenience to the
occupants of the area or other vessels; and
         (b)   Without reducing the speed of the vessel to five mph or less.
      (3)   No person shall operate a motorboat or other vessel close to
swimming areas, moored boats or vessels engaged in fishing, servicing buoys or
markings or similar activities, without reducing the speed of the vessel so as
to prevent wash or wake from the vessel causing damage or unnecessary
inconvenience to the occupants of the area or other vessels.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.170)
(Ord. 2584, passed - -1975; Ord. 2595, passed - -1975; Ord. 2712, passed - -
1979; Ord. 2835, passed - -1982; Ord. 2956, passed - -1987; Ord. 3054, passed -
-1992; Ord. 3620, passed 11-15-2022) Penalty, see §

§ 94.040 REFUSE OR EXCREMENT DEPOSIT PROHIBITED.
   No person shall empty or throw refuse, garbage, rubbish or waste of any kind
in or on the lake, and no human excrement shall be deposited or discharged into
the lake.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.130) (Ord. 2584, passed - -1975) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.041 FISHING; FEE; COMPLIANCE.
   (A)   Compliance; trot, limb, throw lines. Fishing in compliance with the
laws of the state and with all applicable rules and regulations as established
by the state’s Department of Wildlife and Parks and the city, as the same may
be amended from lime to lime, shall be permitted on Lake Fort Scott. No trot,
limb or throw lines shall be used in any manner.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.140)
   (B)   Yearly frontage fees. That the yearly frontage fee for all lots at
Lake Fort Scott shall be $150 per year to be pro-rated per month for the year
2015 and in 2016 payable in one lump sum due on January 1 of each year on each
assigned lot and every year thereafter.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.150)
   (C)   Closure; no refund of fees. The lake may be closed to fishing at any
time and for any period of time for any reason deemed advisable by the
governing body of the city. The licenses authorized under this subchapter are
conditioned on this provision, and no refund will be made to licensees in the
event of such closure.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.160)
(Ord. 2584, passed - -1975; Ord. 3451, passed 1-20-2015)
§ 94.042 SWIMMING.
   (A)   Swimming is permitted, except where prohibited. All persons swimming
in the lake do so entirely at their own risk so far as the city is concerned.
No one shall swim before the hour of 7:00 a.m. and no one shall swim after the
hour of 9:00 p.m.
   (B)   Swimming at Lake Fort Scott is permitted only within the marked area
of the public beach, within 25 feet of any private dock or within 25 feet of
any boat anchored on the south side of the buoys located at the southern end of
Lake Fort Scott, more commonly referred to as the “fishing area”. Swimming in
these areas is permitted only between the hours of 5:00 a.m. and 9:00 p.m.
Swimming in all other areas and at all other times is prohibited. All persons
swimming in the lake do so entirely at their own risk.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.180) (Ord. 2584, passed - -1975; Ord. 2584, passed - -
1975; Ord. 2765, passed - -1980) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.043 HUNTING; DISCHARGE OF FIREARM.
   (A)   No person shall hunt or discharge firearms of any kind on the lake or
on the land described in this subchapter, except for that area of land and
portion of the lake which has been opened for public hunting and leased to the
state’s Department of Wildlife, Parks and Tourism which is described in Exhibit
A, attached to the ordinance from which this section derives.
   (B)   A person hunting on the afore described land leased to the state’s
Department of Wildlife, Parks and Tourism is permitted to discharge a shotgun
with pellets only or a bow and arrow for archery hunting during those times
that hunting is permitted pursuant to state law.
   (C)   A person hunting water fowl on the aforementioned water area leased to
the state’s Department of Wildlife, Parks and Tourism is permitted to discharge
a shotgun with pellets only during those times that hunting is permitted
pursuant to state law.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.190) (Ord. 2584, passed - -1975; Ord. 3421, passed 10-2-
2012) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.044 DESIGNATED ANCHORAGES.
   Those areas of the lake surface within 75 yards of the shoreline lying south
of the line described in §
94.039(I) of this chapter are declared to be designated anchorages.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.200) (Ord. 2584, passed - -1975)
§ 94.045 CAMPING PRIVILEGES AND FEES.
   (A)   There shall be designated camping areas created by resolutions of the
City Commission that shall be posted at Lake Fort Scott. There shall also be
established and created a system allowing the reservation and use of camping
spaces at Lake Fort Scott. Any camping space may be reserved for a period of
time with a permit issued for the utilization of said space. Reservations can
be obtained through the City Office or through a designated representative of
the city.
   (B)   There shall be established a fee system by resolution which shall be
adopted by the City Commission and provide the authorized fee schedule. All
fees must be paid in advance. Unless a space is reserved and paid, the occupant
of that space must immediately vacate the property upon request.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.201) (Ord. 3325, passed - -2007) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.046 SHORELINE EXTENSIONS.
   No person is authorized to change the shoreline by extending any portion
thereof without prior written approval by the City Commission. Any extension of
the shoreline is limited to 50 feet from the normal waterline at the lake.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.210) (Ord. 2928, passed - -1985) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.047 GLASS CONTAINERS PROHIBITED.
   Glass containers, used for food, beverage or any other substance, are
prohibited in any public use areas, city-owned property or on boats or docks on
the water surface.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.230) (Ord. 2928, passed - -1985) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.048 PROPERTY OWNERSHIP CHANGES; CITY TO BE NOTIFIED.
   All property owners or leaseholders are required to notify the city, in
writing, within 30 days after such time as they have sold, leased or
transferred any ownership interest in their lake property.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.240) (Ord. 2928, passed - -1985) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.049 SEWER DISTRICT.
   There is hereby created a Lake Fort Scott Sewer District at Lake Fort Scott.
All residents are required to hook into this system for their sanitary sewer
needs.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.250) (Ord. 3569, passed 12-1-2020) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.050 FISHING AREA ON SHORELINE.
   The city may place buoys on the lake to establish a protected area between
the buoys and the shoreline. All non-fishing boats and skiers are required to
stay to the lake side of the buoys and shall not travel between the buoys and
the shoreline.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.260) (Ord. 2928, passed - -1985) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.051 DIRECTION OF LAKE TRAVEL.
   All motor-driven boats shall run counterclockwise on the lake.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.270) (Ord. 2928, passed - -1985) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.052 SKIING LIMITATIONS.
   Skiing will not be conducted before sunrise or after sunset.
(Prior Code, § 12.28.280) (Ord. 2928, passed - -1985) Penalty, see §
94.999
**** ROCK CREEK LAKE ****
§ 94.065 FISHING PERMITTED; SET, TROT, LIMB OR THROW LINES PROHIBITED.
   Beginning on the effective date of the ordinance codified in this
subchapter, fishing, in compliance with this subchapter and with the laws of
the state and all applicable rules and regulations as established by the
state’s Department of Wildlife and Parks, as the same may be amended from time
to time, shall be permitted on Rock Creek Lake. No set, trot, limb or throw
lines shall be used in any manner.
(Prior Code, § 12.32.010) (Ord. 2360, passed - -1966) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.066 BOAT LICENSING.
   (A)   The fishing license fee for all persons between the ages of 16 and 65
shall be $5 per year. These regulations shall apply to all persons, except
those who are exempt from license requirements under the laws of the state. All
fishing licenses shall expire on December 31 of each year, regardless of date
of issuance.
(Prior Code, § 12.32.020)
   (B)   A license fee for the operation of a motorboat shall be paid to the
City Clerk on an annual basis. The yearly license fee for all persons for
motorboats of less than 25 horsepower shall be $10 and for motors of 25
horsepower or more, $20. The amount of horsepower shall be determined by the
manufacturer’s ratings.
(Prior Code, § 12.32.050)
   (C)   Persons desiring to operate a rowboat on the lake shall make
application to the City Clerk and obtain a license therefor, and the annual
license fee shall be $5.
(Prior Code, § 12.32.060)
(Ord. 2360, passed - -1966; Ord. 2711, passed - -1979; Ord. 2956, passed - -
1987; Ord. 3055, passed - -1992)
§ 94.067 CLOSURE; NO REFUND OF FEES.
   The lake may be closed to fishing and boating at any time and for any period
of time for any reason deemed advisable by the governing body of the city. The
licenses authorized in this subchapter are conditioned on this provision, and
no refund will be made to licensees in the event of such closure.
(Prior Code, § 12.32.040) (Ord. 2360, passed - -1966)
§ 94.068 CAMPING PRIVILEGES AND FEES.
   (A)   (1)   There shall be designed camping areas created by resolutions of
the City Commission that shall be posted at Rock Creek Lake.
      (2)   There shall also be established and created a system for the use of
camping spaces at Rock Creek Lake.
      (3)   Any camping space may be reserved for a period of time with a
permit issued for the utilization of said space.
      (4)   Reservations can be obtained through the City Office or through a
designated representative of the city.
   (B)   (1)   There shall be established a fee system by resolution which
shall be adopted by the City Commission and provide the authorized fee
schedule.
      (2)   All fees must be paid in advance.
      (3)   Unless a space is reserved and paid, the occupant of that space
must immediately vacate the property upon request.
(Prior Code, § 12.32.061) (Ord. 3326, passed - -2007)
§ 94.069 PROHIBITED ACTS.
   The following acts are prohibited on Rock Creek Lake:
   (A)   The use or operation of any motorboat for purposes other than fishing.
No such boat shall be operated at a speed greater than ten mph;
   (B)   Swimming and bathing;
   (C)   The deposit of rubbish or trash on the banks of or near the lake; and
   (D)   All glass containers, used for food, beverage or any other substance,
are prohibited in any public use areas, city-owned property or on boats or
docks on the water surface.
(Prior Code, § 12.32.070) (Ord. 2134, passed - -1966; Ord. 2927, passed - -
12985; Ord. 2947, passed - -1986) Penalty, see §
94.999
§ 94.070 HUNTING; DISCHARGE OF FIREARM PROHIBITED.

on the land described in this subchapter, except for that area which has been
opened for public hunting and leased to the state’s Department of Wildlife,
Parks and Tourism which is described in Exhibit A, attached to the ordinance

   (B)   A person hunting on the afore described land leased to the state’s

CHAPTER 95: PUBLIC LIBRARY
Section
   
95.001   Failure to return library materials
   
95.002   Fraud in procuring library privileges
   
95.003   Notice of violation
   
95.004   Prosecution; presumption of proof
   
95.005   State law exemption; Library Board
§ 95.001 FAILURE TO RETURN LIBRARY MATERIALS.
   It is unlawful for any person to fail to return any book, newspaper,
magazine, pamphlet, manuscript, article, film or any other property owned by
the Fort Scott Public Library after notice in writing to return the same within
15 days has been given to such person; such notice shall be given after the
expiration of the time which, by the rules of the Library Board, the book or
other library material may be kept.
(Prior Code, § 13.20.010) (Ord. 2820, passed - -1982) Penalty, see §
10.999
§ 95.002 FRAUD IN PROCURING LIBRARY PRIVILEGES.
   It is unlawful for any person to give fictitious name or address at the Fort
Scott Public Library in order to obtain possession or use of any book or any
other property of the library, or to use a library card other than his or her
own to secure without permission of the owner thereof possession of any book or
other property of the library, or to conceal his or her identity or place of
employment or residence in any manner whatsoever in order to secure possession
of any book, library card or any other property of the library.
(Prior Code, § 13.20.020) (Ord. 2820, passed - -1982) Penalty, see §
10.999
§ 95.003 NOTICE OF VIOLATION.
   (A)   For purposes of this chapter, whenever it appears that there has been
a violation of §
95.001 of this chapter, the librarian shall give notice of such alleged
violation to the person or persons responsible therefor as herein provided.
   (B)   Such notice shall:
      (1)   Be in writing;
      (2)   Particularize the violations alleged to exist or have been
committed;
      (3)   Specify that the person to whom the notice is issued shall have 15
days within which to correct the violations particularized; and
      (4)   Be addressed to and served upon the holder of the library card at
the address which is most recent as it appears from such person’s application
for a library card.
   (C)   Such notice shall be deemed properly served upon such holder or owner
of a library card if a copy thereof is served upon him or her personally, or if
a copy thereof is sent by certified mail to the address listed as most recent
on his or her application for a library card.
(Prior Code, § 13.20.030) (Ord. 2820, passed - -1982)
§ 95.004 PROSECUTION; PRESUMPTION OF PROOF.
   (A)   In any prosecution charging a violation of §
95.001 of this chapter, proof that a particular individual was the registered
holder of a particular library card which was used to secure any book or any
other library property shall constitute in evidence a prima facie presumption
that the record owner or holder of such library card was the person who
utilized the same to secure a particular book or any other property of the Fort
Scott Public Library at the point where and at the time when such violation
occurred.
   (B)   The presumption stated in division (A) above shall apply only when the
procedure as prescribed in §
95.003 of this chapter has been followed.
(Prior Code, § 13.20.040) (Ord. 2820, passed - -1982)
§ 95.005 STATE LAW EXEMPTION; LIBRARY BOARD.
   The city, by the power vested in it by the Act, hereby elects to exempt
itself from and make inapplicable to it the provisions of K.S.A. 12-1222, and
does hereby provide the following substitute and additional provisions in place
thereof.
   (A)   Upon the establishment of a city library under this act the acting
head of the city (the “Mayor”) shall appoint, with the approval of the City
Commission, a Library Board for such library, consisting of seven members and
one City Commission member.
      (1)   The Library Board shall consist of seven members who shall be
appointed by the Mayor and with the approval of the City Commission.
      (2)   In addition to these appointed members of the Board, one City
Commission member shall be appointed upon the approval of the City Commission.
Any City Commission member, including the Mayor, shall be eligible for
appointment. The appointed City Commissioner shall be ex officio a member of
the Library Board with the same voting powers as appointed members.
      (3)   No person holding any public or official office in the city, other
than a City Commission member, shall be appointed a member of a Library Board
while holding such office.
   (B)   Upon the expiration of the terms of members first appointed,
succeeding members shall be appointed in like manner for terms of four years.
      (1)   Members of a Library Board holding office at the effective date of
this Act shall continue to hold their offices until April 30 following the
expiration of the terms for which appointed, and on or before May 1 following
the first expiration of a term, a sufficient number shall be appointed by the
Mayor, with the approval of the City Commission, for terms of four years to
constitute a library board of the number of members prescribed by this Act.
      (2)   A member of the City Commission appointed to a Library Board shall
serve a term of one year, without restriction as to consecutive terms allowed.
The City Commission shall annually appoint a member of the City Commission, as
outlined herein, with the approval of the City Commission, on or before
February 1 of each year to serve on the Library Board.
   (C)   Four of the members appointed to a Library Board shall be residents of
the city, and three of the members appointed to a Library Board may reside
within a three-mile radius of the city’s legal boundaries.
      (1)   Appointments or terms served by a member of a Library Board made
prior to the effective date of section shall not retroactively count against
the term limit eligibility of a Library Board member hereunder.
      (2)   No person who has been appointed for two consecutive four-year
terms to a Library Board shall be eligible for further appointment to such
Board until one year after the expiration of the second term.
      (3)   Vacancies occasioned by removal from the municipality or the three-
mile radius of city boundaries for such members, resignation or otherwise,
shall be filled by appointment for the unexpired term.
      (4)   Members of the Library Board shall receive no compensation for
their services as such, but shall be allowed their actual and necessary
expenses in attending meetings and in carrying out their duties as members.
(Charter Ord. 35, passed 3-19-2024)

CHAPTER 110: GENERAL BUSINESS LICENSING
Section
Temporary Businesses
   
110.001   Purpose
   
110.002   Definitions

110.008   Temporary businesses in residential areas
 
   
110.999   Penalty
**** TEMPORARY BUSINESSES ****
§ 110.001 PURPOSE.
   The zoning and subdivision regulations of the city currently prohibit all
temporary businesses in the city. The purpose of this subchapter is to allow
for the operation of said business through a permit system in the city.
(Prior Code, § 5.36.010) (Ord. 2784, passed - -1981)
§ 110.002 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   BUSINESS. Any marketing activity conducted for the sale of goods or services
for a profit.
   TEMPORARY BUSINESS. The sale of goods or services sold by a business being
operated in the city for less than two days out of any seven-day period in a
residential area. All other residential selling for longer than that period is
prohibited unless specifically permitted by other city regulations. In all
other non-residential areas, it shall be the sale of goods or services sold by
a business being operated in the city out of a vehicle, truck, trailer, other
mobile unit, tent, outdoor sales area or any business activity not involving
the use of a permanent structure.
(Prior Code, § 5.36.020) (Ord. 2784, passed - -1981; Ord. 2899, passed - -1985;
Ord. 2963, passed - -1987)
§ 110.003 PERMIT REQUIRED.
   No person, persons or entity shall operate a temporary business within the
city without a permit from the City Clerk, except those temporary businesses
which are controlled by and authorized by the Good Old Days Committee during
the annual City of Fort Scott Good Old Days celebration.
(Prior Code, § 5.36.030) (Ord. 2784, passed - -1981; Ord. 3136, passed - -1996)
Penalty, see §
110.999
§ 110.004 PERMIT APPLICATION.
   (A)   An application for a permit to operate a temporary business under this
subchapter shall be made at the City Clerk’s office upon forms provided by the
city. Such application shall be sworn to and filed with the City Clerk at least
24 hours prior to the time in which the permit applied for shall become
effective.
   (B)   The application herein required shall contain the following
information:
      (1)   The name, address or headquarters of the person applying for the
permit;
      (2)   If the applicant is not an individual, the names and addresses of
the applicant’s principal officers and managers;
      (3)   The name and address of the person or persons who will be in direct
charge of conducting the temporary business;
      (4)   The time within which the temporary business will be operated and
the location of the business in the city; and
      (5)   Any application for permit must also be signed by the party on
whose property the temporary business shall be located.
(Prior Code, § 5.36.040) (Ord. 2784, passed - -1981)
§ 110.005 CITY CLERK’S INVESTIGATION.
   (A)   The City Clerk shall examine the applications filed under this
subchapter for a permit and shall make, or cause to be made, such further
investigation of the application and the applicant as the City Clerk shall deem
necessary.
   (B)   The permit will be issued once the City Clerk determines the following
facts:
      (1)   All of the statements made in the application are true; and
      (2)   The applicant has not engaged in any fraudulent transaction or
enterprise.
(Prior Code, § 5.36.050) (Ord. 2784, passed - -1981)
§ 110.006 PERMIT FEES.
   Before a permit is issued, there shall be paid to the Treasurer of the city
the sum of $25 per day for a permit which allows a temporary business to
operate at one location for two days or less, out of any seven-day period,
within the city on an annual basis. All permits shall be issued on a calendar-
year basis commencing on January 1 of each calendar year. There shall be no pro
rata charge made for a permit covering less than a 12-month period.
(Prior Code, § 5.36.060) (Ord. 2784, passed - -1981; Ord. 2963, passed - -1987;
Ord. 3056, passed - -1992)
§ 110.007 PERMIT NON-TRANSFERABLE.
   Any permit issued under this subchapter shall be non-transferable.
(Prior Code, § 5.36.070) (Ord. 2784, passed - -1981)
§ 110.008 TEMPORARY BUSINESSES IN RESIDENTIAL AREAS.
   Any permit issued under this subchapter for a temporary business to be
conducted in a residential- zoned area will require notification of all
property owners within 200 feet of the proposed location before the permit can
be issued. The notification will be by mail pursuant to a certified list of
property owners prepared by an abstracting company at the expense of the
proposed business operator. Once the property owners have been notified, they
will have five days within which to file a written objection to said business
permit. If no such written objections are received within the five-day
notification period, the Clerk is authorized to issue the permit; provided, the
applicant has met all the other eligibility requirements listed in this
subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 5.36.080) (Ord. 2784, passed - -1981; Ord. 2859, passed - -1983)
§ 110.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person convicted of violating any provision of §§
110.001 through
110.008 of this chapter shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances. Each day of operation in violation of the
provisions of §§
110.001 through
110.008 of this chapter shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 5.36.090) (Ord. 2859, passed - -1983)
