**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 111.001 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   ALCOHOLIC LIQUOR. Includes the following four varieties of liquor, as
defined in this section, and every liquid or solid, patented or not, containing
alcohol, spirits, wine or beer and capable of being consumed as a beverage by a
human being, but shall not include any beer or cereal malt beverage containing
not more than 3.2% of alcohol by weight.
      (1)   ALCOHOL. The product of distillation of any fermented liquid,
whether rectified or diluted, whatever may be the origin thereof, and includes
synthetic ethyl alcohol, but does not include denatured alcohol or wood
alcohol.
      (2)   BEER. When its meaning is not enlarged, modified or limited by
other words, means a beverage containing more than 3.2% of alcohol by weight,
obtained by alcoholic fermentation of an infusion or concoction of barley or
other grain, malt and hops in water, and including, among other things, beer,
ale, stout, lager beer, porter and the like having such alcohol content.
      (3)   SPIRITS. Any beverage which contains alcohol obtained by
distillation, mixed with water or other substance in solution, and includes
brandy, rum, whiskey, gin or other spirituous liquors, and such liquors when
rectified, blended or otherwise mixed with alcohol or other substances.
      (4)   WINE. Any alcoholic beverage obtained by normal alcoholic
fermentation of the juice of sound, ripe grapes, fruit or berries or other
agricultural products, including such beverages containing added alcohol or
spirits, as defined in this section, or containing sugar added for the purpose
of correcting natural deficiencies.
   DISTRIBUTOR. A person importing or causing to be imported into the state, or
purchasing or causing to be purchased within the state, alcoholic liquor for
sale or resale to retailers under the provisions of the state’s Liquor Control
Act.
   MANUFACTURER. Every brewer, fermenter, distiller, rectifier, wine maker,
blender, processor, bottler or person who fills or refills an original package,
and others engaged in brewing, fermenting, distilling, rectifying or bottling
alcoholic liquors, as defined in this section, or beer regardless of its
alcoholic content, under the provisions of the state’s Liquor Control Act:
      (1)   Patent and proprietary medicines and medicinal, antiseptic and
toilet preparations;
      (2)   Flavoring extracts, syrups and food products; and
      (3)   Scientific, industrial and chemical products.
   RETAILER. A person licensed under the provisions of the state’s Liquor
Control Act to sell or offer for sale alcoholic liquor for use and consumption
and not for resale in any form.
   PERSON. Includes any natural person, corporation, partnership or
association.
   ORIGINAL PACKAGE. Any bottle, flask, jug, can, cask, barrel, keg, hogshead
or other receptacle or container whatsoever used, corked or capped, sealed and
labeled by the manufacturer of alcoholic liquor to contain and convey any
alcoholic liquor.
   SALE. Any transfer, exchange or barter in any manner or by any means
whatsoever for a consideration, and includes and means all sales made by any
person, whether principal, proprietor, agent, servant or employee.
   SELL AT RETAIL and SALE AT RETAIL. Sales for use or consumption and not for
resale in any form.
   TO SELL. Includes to solicit or receive an order for, to keep or expose for
sale and to keep with intent to sell.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.010) (Ord. 2082, passed - -1949)
§ 111.002 MANUFACTURE; LICENSE REQUIRED; EXCEPTIONS.
   No person shall manufacture, make, brew or distill any alcoholic liquor
within the corporate limits of the city unless such person is licensed under
the provisions of the state’s Liquor Control Act; provided that, nothing
contained in this section shall apply to or prevent:
   (A)   The making of wine, cider or beer by a person, from fruits, vegetables
or grains, or the products thereof by simple fermentation and without
distillation, if it is made solely for the use of the maker and his or her
family;
   (B)   Any drugstore employing a licensed pharmacist from possessing and
using alcoholic liquor in the compounding of prescriptions of duly licensed
physicians;
   (C)   The manufacture of denatured alcoholic products in accordance with
acts of Congress and regulations promulgated thereunder; and/or
   (D)   The making of wine intended for use and used by any church or
religious organization for sacramental purposes.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.020) (Ord. 2082, passed - -1949)
§ 111.003 RETAIL SALE; LICENSE REQUIRED; TAX LEVIED; POSSESSION.
   (A)   Generally. No alcoholic liquor shall be sold at retail by any person
within the corporate limits of the city unless such person shall be licensed
therefor under the provisions of the state’s Liquor Control Act.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.030)
   (B)   Tax levied.
      (1)   There is levied an annual occupation tax of the sum of $250 on each
retailer of alcoholic liquor, including beer containing more than 3.2% alcohol
by weight, for consumption on the premises in the city, who has a retailer’s
license issued by the state’s Director of Alcoholic Beverage Control, which tax
shall be paid before business is begun under an original state license and
within five days after any renewal of a state license.
      (2)   There is further levied an annual occupation tax in the sum of $300
on each retailer of alcoholic liquor, including beer containing more than 3.2%
alcohol by weight, for consumption off the premises in the city (sales in the
original package only) who has a retailer’s license issued by the state’s
Director of Alcoholic Beverage Control, which tax shall be paid before business
is begun under an original state license and within five days after any renewal
of the state license.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.040)
   (C)   Possession in other than original package prohibited. It is unlawful
for any person to have in his or her possession for sale at retail any bottles,
casks or other containers containing alcoholic liquor, except in original
packages.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.100)
(Ord. 2081, passed - -1949; Ord. 2082, passed - -1949; Ord. 2107, passed - -;
Ord. 2773, passed - -1980) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.004 TAX; LICENSE PRESENTATION; RECEIPT TO BE POSTED.
   (A)   Generally. A holder of a license for the retail sale of alcoholic
liquor by the package in the city, issued by the state’s Director of Alcoholic
Beverage Control, shall present such license when applying to pay the
occupation tax levied in §
111.003(B) of this chapter. The tax shall be received and receipt issued by the
City Treasurer for the period covered by the state license.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.050)
   (B)   Receipt to be posted. Every licensee shall cause the city alcoholic
liquor retailer’s occupation tax receipt to be placed in plain view next to or
below the state license in a conspicuous place on the licensed premises.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.060)
(Ord. 2081, passed - -1949; Ord. 2082, passed - -1949)
§ 111.005 PREMISES AND EMPLOYEE REGULATIONS.
   It is unlawful for any retailer of alcoholic liquor:
   (A)   To permit any person to mix drinks in or on the licensed premises;
   (B)   To employ any person under the age of 21 years in connection with the
operation of such retail establishment; or
   (C)   To employ any person in connection with the operation of such retail
establishment who has been adjudged guilty of a felony.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.080) (Ord. 2082, passed - -1949) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.006 SALE OR DELIVERY PROHIBITED WHEN; MINORS.
   No person shall knowingly sell, give away, dispose of, exchange or deliver
or permit the sale, gift or procuring of any alcoholic liquor to or for any
minor under 21 years of age; and no such minor shall represent that he or she
is of age for the purpose of asking for, purchasing or receiving alcoholic
liquor from any persons, except in cases authorized by law. No person shall
knowingly sell, give away, dispose of, exchange or deliver, or permit the sale,
gift or procuring of any alcoholic liquor to or for any person who is mentally
incompetent, or any person who is physically or mentally incapacitated by the
consumption of liquor.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.090) (Ord. 2082, passed - -1949) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.007 POSSESSION AND TRANSPORTATION; UNLAWFUL WHEN.
   Subject to the following limitations, the possession and transportation of
alcoholic liquor for personal use only shall be legal.
   (A)   It is unlawful for any person to transport, in any vehicle upon a
public highway, street or alley within the corporate limits of the city, any
alcoholic liquor, except in the original package or container which shall not
have been opened and the seal upon which shall not have been broken and from
which the original cap or cork shall not have been removed, unless the opened
package or container be in the locked rear trunk or rear compartment, or any
locked outside compartment which is not accessible to the driver or any other
person in the vehicle while it is in motion.
   (B)   After the Governor has issued a proclamation declaring that the tax
and licensing provisions of the state’s Liquor Control Act are in full force
and operation and being administered by the state’s Director of Alcoholic
Beverage Control, it shall be unlawful for any person to transport or have in
his or her possession for his or her personal use in excess of two quarts of
alcoholic liquor on which the tax imposed by the state’s Liquor Control Act has
not been paid, or on the containers of which each mark and stamp required by
the state’s Liquor Control Act has not been affixed; provided that, nothing
contained in this section shall apply to the possession and transportation of
alcoholic liquor by any manufacturer, wholesaler, distributor, retailer, non-
beverage user or common carrier operating pursuant to the provisions of the
state’s Liquor Control Act.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.110) (Ord. 2082, passed - -1949) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.008 CONSUMPTION; PROHIBITED WHERE; GLASS CONTAINERS IN PUBLIC PLACES.
   (A)   (1)    The governing body hereby authorizes the possession and
consumption of alcoholic liquor in the following image. The city understands
that permitted entities contained in the area where the state, through its
Director of the Division of Alcoholic Beverage Control in accordance with § 1,
Chapter 85, of the 2017 Session Laws of Kansas, and amendments thereto. The
possession and consumption of alcoholic liquor within said area is only
permitted within the hours of 9:00 a.m. and 12:00 a.m., seven days a week.
      (2)    All alcoholic liquor removed from a licensed premises authorized
to sell alcoholic liquor under their current license shall be served in a
container that displays the licensee’s trade name or logo or other identifying
mark that is unique to the licensee.
      (3)   This will allow an open container of alcoholic liquor purchased
within the common consumption area and provided by business appropriately
licensed by the state to dispense alcoholic liquor.
      (4)   No alcoholic liquor may be removed from those business premises
within the common consumption area and allowed to transported within the area.
      (5)   In appropriate conduct shall not be permitted within the boundaries
of the common consumption area and any person engaging in such inappropriate
conduct shall be subject to removal from the common consumption area.
Inappropriate conduct may include, but is not limited to, fighting, use of
profanity or indecent language, harassment, destruction of property, lewd or
lascivious acts, or committing any violation of law or city code.
      (6)   All licensees approved by the ABC to participate in the common
consumption area shall at all times comply with any and all federal, state and
city laws or regulations pertaining to the purchase and sale of alcoholic
liquor.
      (7)   The governing body hereby authorizes the City Manager or his or her
designee to take all reasonably necessary actions, including, but not limited
to, the execution of all documents and instruments to obtain the common area
consumption permit on behalf of the city.
      (8)   Subject to receipt of the permit described in division (A)(2)
above, the governing body hereby authorizes the City Manager or his or her
designee to establish rules and regulations pertaining to the use of activities
within the common consumption area. A copy of the rules and regulations shall
be available at the office of the City Clerk during regular business hours and
on the website maintained by the city. Any violation of such rules and
regulations, any official city signage, state law or regulations or the oral
direction of any city employee or authorized volunteer shall be grounds from
expulsion from the common consumption area and criminal sanctions or any other
remedy available under local, state or federal laws will apply.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.120)
   (B)   It is unlawful for any person to drink, consume or carry alcoholic
liquor or any beverage in a glass container upon any public place which shall
include streets, sidewalks, public parks and public parking areas in the city.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.125)
(Ord. 2082, passed - -1949; Ord. 3071, passed - -1993; Ord. 3091, passed - -
1994; Ord. 3381, passed 6-15-2010; Ord. 3774, passed 10-15-2024) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.009 SALOONS.
   It is unlawful for any person to own, maintain, operate or conduct, either
directly or indirectly, an open saloon within the corporate limits of the city.
For the purpose of this section, OPEN SALOON means any place, public or
private, where alcoholic liquor is sold or offered for sale or kept for sale by
the drink or in any quantity of less than 3.4 fluid ounces, or sold or offered
for sale, or kept for sale for consumption on the premises where sold.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.130) (Ord. 2082, passed - -1949) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.010 APPLICABILITY.
   (A)   Nothing contained in this subchapter shall prevent:
      (1)   The making of wine, cider or beer by a person, from fruits,
vegetables or grains, or the products thereof, by simple fermentation and
without distillation, if it is made solely for the use of the maker and his or
her family;
      (2)   Any duly licensed practicing physician or dentist from possessing
or using alcoholic liquor in the strict practice of his or her profession;
      (3)   Any hospital or other institution caring for sick and diseased
persons from possessing and using alcoholic liquor for the treatment of bona
fide patients of such hospital or institution;
      (4)   Any drugstore employing a licensed pharmacist from possessing and
using alcoholic liquor in the compounding of prescriptions of duly licensed
physicians; and/or
      (5)   The possession and dispensation of wine by an authorized
representative of any church for the purpose of conducting any bona fide rite
of religious ceremony conducted by such church.
   (B)   None of the provisions of this subchapter shall apply:
      (1)   To flavoring extracts, syrups or medicinal, scientific, culinary or
toilet preparations or food products unfit for beverage purposes; and/or
      (2)   To wine intended for use and used by any church or religious
organization for sacramental purposes.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.140) (Ord. 2082, passed - -1949)
§ 111.011 NOT TO REPEAL CERTAIN PROVISIONS.
   Nothing contained in this subchapter shall be construed as repealing any of
the existing ordinances of the city relating to cereal malt beverages and malt
products.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.150) (Ord. 2082, passed - -1949)
**** CEREAL MALT BEVERAGES ****
§ 111.025 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   CEREAL MALT BEVERAGE. Includes any fermented but undistilled liquor brewed
or made from malt or from a mixture of malt and/or malt substitute.
   PERSON. Includes individuals, firms, copartnerships, corporations and
associations.
   PLACE OF BUSINESS. Any place at which cereal malt beverages are sold.
   SALE AT RETAIL and RETAIL SALE. Sales for use or consumption and not for
resale in any form.
   WHOLESALER or DISTRIBUTOR. Individuals, firms, copartnerships, corporations
and associations which sell or offer for sale any beverage referred to in this
subchapter, to persons, copartnerships, corporations and associations
authorized by this subchapter to sell cereal malt beverages at retail.
(Prior Code, § 5.12.010) (Ord. 2550, passed - -1974)
§ 111.026 LICENSE REQUIRED; FEES; APPLICATION; ISSUANCE; NON- TRANSFERABILITY;
SUSPENSION OR REVOCATION.
   (A)   Generally. No person shall sell any cereal malt beverage at retail
without first having secured a license for each place of business which such
person desires to operate within the corporate limits of the city, as provided
in this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 5.12.020)
   (B)   Fees. License fees are prescribed as follows:
      (1)   General retailer for each place of business selling cereal malt
beverages at retail in original and unopened containers only, and not for
consumption on the premises: $60;
      (2)   For each place of business selling cereal malt beverages at retail
in kegs, bottles, cases and containers, including on-premises consumption:
$125; and
      (3)   For each place of business selling cereal malt beverage at retail:
$25 shall be collected for the state.
(Prior Code, § 5.12.030)
   (C)   Application. Any person desiring a license shall make an application
to the governing body of the city and accompany the application by the required
license fee for each place of business for which the person desires the
license. The application shall be verified and written upon a form prepared by
the Attorney General of the state, and shall contain:
      (1)   The name and residence of the applicant and how long he or she has
resided within the state;
      (2)   The particular place for which a license is desired;
      (3)   The name of the owner of the premises upon which the place of
business is located;
      (4)   A statement that the applicant is a citizen of the United States
and not less than 21 years of age, and that he or she has not, within two years
immediately preceding the date of making application, been convicted of a
felony or any crime involving moral turpitude, or been adjudged guilty of
drunkenness or driving a motor vehicle while under the influence of
intoxicating liquor, or the violation of any other intoxicating liquor law of
the state or of the United States; and
      (5)   The name and residence of the person who is to have active charge
of the business.
(Prior Code, § 5.12.040)
   (D)   Issuance. If the application is in proper form and accompanied by cash
in the amount of the license fee, the governing body of the city shall examine
the application and, after examination of the application the governing body,
shall, if it approves the same, issue a license to the applicant; provided, no
license shall be issued to:
      (1)   A person who is not a resident of the county and who has not been a
resident in good faith of the state for at least one year prior to the
application and a resident of the county for at least six months;
      (2)   A person who is not of good character and reputation in the
community in which he or she resides;
      (3)   A person who is not a citizen of the United States;
      (4)   A person who, within two years immediately preceding the date of
making application, has been convicted of a felony or any crime involving moral
turpitude, or been adjudged guilty of drunkenness or driving a motor vehicle
while under the influence of intoxicating liquor, or the violation of any other
intoxicating liquor law of any state or of the United States;
      (5)   A copartnership, unless one of the copartners is a resident of the
county, and unless all the members of such copartnership are otherwise
qualified to obtain a license;
      (6)   A corporation, if any manager, officer or director thereof or any
stockholder owning in the aggregate more than 25% of the stock of such
corporation would be ineligible to receive a license under this chapter for any
reason other than non-residence within the county; and
      (7)   A person whose place of business is conducted by a manager or
agent, unless said manager or agent possesses the same qualifications required
of the licensee.
(Prior Code, § 5.12.050)
   (E)   Granting; deemed non-transferable; to be posted. The journal of the
governing body shall show the action taken, and if the license is granted, the
action of the governing body shall direct the officers charged by law to issue
other licenses under the general licensing power of the city to execute the
license by order of the governing body. The license shall not be transferable.
Among other things, the license shall state that it is not transferable and the
calendar year for which issued. The license shall be kept posted in a
conspicuous place in the place of business.
(Prior Code, § 5.12.060)
   (F)   Suspension or revocation; reissuance when. The governing body of the
city, upon five days’ notice to the persons holding a cereal malt beverage
license, shall suspend or revoke such license for any one of the following
reasons:
      (1)   If a licensee has fraudulently obtained the license by giving false
information in the application therefor;
      (2)   If the licensee has violated any of the provisions of this
subchapter or any other ordinance of the city prescribing rules or regulations
relating to cereal malt beverages, as defined in this subchapter or if the
licensee has violated any law of the state for which violation the city is
authorized by law to suspend or revoke the license;
      (3)   If the licensee has become ineligible to obtain a license in this
subchapter;
      (4)   Drunkenness of the person holding such license or permitting any
intoxicated person to remain in such place;
      (5)   The sale of cereal malt beverages to those under the age of 21
years;
      (6)   Non-payment of any license fees;
      (7)   Permitting any gambling in or upon such premises;
      (8)   Permitting any person to mix drinks with material purchased in the
place of business or brought in for that purpose;
      (9)   The employment of persons under 21 years of age as dispensers or
bartenders. The employment of persons under 18 years of age as servers;
      (10)   The employment of persons who have been adjudged guilty of a
felony or any violation of the intoxicating liquor law; and/or
      (11)   The sale or possession of or for permitting any person to use or
consume alcoholic liquor, as defined by the laws of the state, upon or in said
premises; provided that, the provisions of division (H)(8) above shall not
apply if such place of business or premises are also currently licensed as a
club under a license issued by the state’s Director of Alcoholic Beverage
Control. Within 20 days after the order of the governing body suspending or
revoking any license, the licensee may appeal to the District Court of the
county in the manner now provided by law in appeals from the probate court;
provided that, any appeal taken from an order suspending or revoking any such
license shall not suspend the order of suspension or revocation during the
pendency of any such appeal. In case of the revocation of the license of any
licensee, no new license shall be issued to such person, or any person acting
for or on his or her behalf, for a period of six months thereafter.
(Prior Code, § 5.12.080)
(Ord. 2550, passed - -1974; Ord. 2576, passed - -1975; Ord. 2977, passed - -
1988; Ord. 3025, passed - -1991; Ord. 3059, passed - -1992)
§ 111.027 PREMISES REQUIREMENTS.
   No private rooms or closed booths shall be operated in any place of
business. The place of business shall be open to the public and the police at
all times during business hours. No person under 21 years of age shall be
permitted to buy or drink any such beverages in or about said place of
business.
(Prior Code, § 5.12.070) (Ord. 2550, passed - -1974; Ord. 3140, passed - -1996;
Ord. 3564, passed 8-18-2020) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.028 MANUFACTURER, DISTRIBUTOR OR WHOLESALER; SALE OR INTEREST IN SALE
PROHIBITED.
   No manufacturer, distributor, agent or wholesaler shall directly or
indirectly, sell furnishings, fixtures or equipment on the premises of a place
of business of a licensee authorized under this subchapter to sell cereal malt
beverages at retail; nor shall he or she, directly or indirectly, pay for any
such license, or advance, furnish, lend or give money for payment of such
license, or purchase or become the owner of any note, mortgage or other
evidence of indebtedness of such licensee or any form of security therefor; nor
shall such manufacturer, distributor or wholesaler, directly or indirectly, be
interested in the ownership, conduct or operation of the business of any such
licensee; nor shall any manufacturer, distributor or wholesaler be interested,
directly or indirectly, as an owner or part owner of said premises, or as a
lessee or lessor thereof, in any premises upon which cereal malt beverages are
sold at retail. No manufacturer, distributor or wholesaler shall, directly or
indirectly or through a subsidiary or affiliate, or by any officer, director or
firm of such manufacturer, distributor or wholesaler, furnish, give, lend or
rent any interior decorations other than signs, costing in the aggregate more
than $100 in any one calendar year for use in or about or in connection with
any one establishment on which products of the manufacturer, distributor or
wholesaler are sold. No person engaged in the business of manufacturing,
distributing or wholesaling cereal malt beverages shall, directly or
indirectly, pay for, or advance, furnish or lend money for the payment of any
license for another. Any licensee who shall permit or assent or be a part in
any way to any violation or infringement of the provisions of this section,
shall be deemed guilty of a violation of this subchapter, and any money loaned
contrary to a provision of this subchapter shall not be recovered back, or any
note, mortgage or other evidence of indebtedness, or security, or any lease or
contract obtained or made contrary to this subchapter shall be unenforceable
and void. No wholesaler or distributor shall sell any cereal malt beverage to
any person who has not secured a license as provided for in this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 5.12.090) (Ord. 2550, passed - -1974) Penalty, see §
111.999
**** BEER GARDENS ****
§ 111.040 LIMITED PUBLIC SALE.
   The city has deemed it advisable to allow and license limited public sale at
retail and consumption of cereal malt beverages in specific areas of the public
parks or other places in said city during community-wide celebrations, or
certain other public events. Such sale shall be by responsible persons working
for charitable and non-profit organizations with the proceeds going to said
organizations for which a license will be issued by the city, and shall be
allowed for limited periods of time.
(Prior Code, § 5.14.010) (Ord. 2986, passed - -1989)
§ 111.041 LICENSE REQUIRED; CREATION; ISSUANCE; TAX; REGULATIONS; DURATION;
TRANSFERRAL; LOCATION; APPLICATION; REVOCATION OR SUSPENSION.
   (A)   Generally. There is created a license, to be denominated a beer garden
license. A license will be issued by the city to an individual on behalf of a
non-profit, charitable organization or to the organization itself designating
the responsible person.
(Prior Code, § 5.14.020)
   (B)   Tax. The license tax levied shall be $125.
(Prior Code, § 5.14.030)
   (C)   Regulations. No cereal malt beverage may be sold at retail in any city
park of the city, or on any other public grounds of the city, or anywhere in
the city in an outdoor area, except under a beer garden license. The following
regulations shall apply:
      (1)   Such sale shall be limited to the beer garden; and
      (2)   No cereal malt beverage may be sold in a glass container.
(Prior Code, § 5.14.040)
   (D)   Conditional issuance. No beer garden licenses shall be issued, except
during community-wide public celebrations or holidays.
(Prior Code, § 5.14.050)
   (E)   Duration; frequency of issuance; transferral. The duration of a beer
garden license shall be for one calendar year to cover special events, as
defined in this subchapter. The license shall be picked up 72 hours prior to
the event and returned to the city within 72 hours after the event is
terminated or the next business day. No beer garden license shall be
transferred by the beer garden licensee to whom it was originally issued.
(Prior Code, § 5.14.060)
   (E)   Location designation. A beer garden license shall designate
specifically an area in a city park or, alternatively, other public grounds in
the Central Business District or other public grounds in the city, which area
shall be used for the purposes of the license and which area will hereafter be
referred to as a “beer garden”.
(Prior Code, § 5.14.070)
   (F)   Application and issuance; suspension or revocation. The license
application and issuance requirements shall be consistent with §
111.026(C), (D) and (E) of this chapter. The beer garden license may be
suspended or revoked pursuant to §
111.026(F) of this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 5.14.080)
(Ord. 2986, passed - -1989; Ord. 3061, passed - -1992; Ord. 3388, passed 12-21-
2010) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.042 BOUNDARIES.
   The beer garden shall not exceed 1,000 square feet in area, shall be
contiguous and shall have its boundaries clearly marked.
(Prior Code, § 5.14.090) (Ord. 2986, passed - -1989) Penalty, see §
111.999
§ 111.043 SEVERABILITY.
   Nothing contained in this subchapter shall be construed as modifying,
amending or repealing by implication any provisions of §§
111.025 through
111.028 of this chapter. Nothing contained in this subchapter shall be
construed as modifying, amending or repealing by implication any other
ordinance or regulation, whether city, state or federal, relating to control of
vehicular or pedestrian traffic and parking, zoning or other regulations of
cereal malt beverages.
(Prior Code, § 5.14.100) (Ord. 2986, passed - -1989)
§ 111.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   (1)   Any person who violates any provision of §§
111.001 through
111.011 of this chapter for which a penalty is not otherwise specifically
provided shall, upon conviction of any such violation, be punished as provided
in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.160)
      (2)   Any person violating any of the provisions of §
111.006 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished by a fine of not more than $100 or by
imprisonment for not to exceed 30 days, or by both such fine and imprisonment.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.090)
      (3)   Any person violating §
111.007 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished by a fine of not more than $200, or by
imprisonment for not more than six months, or by both such fine and
imprisonment.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.110)
      (4)   Any person violating the provisions of §
111.008(B) of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.125)
      (5)   Any person violating the provisions of §
111.009 of this chapter shall be punished by a fine of not more than $500 and
by imprisonment for no more than 90 days.
(Prior Code, § 5.08.130)
   (C)   Any person violating any of the provisions of §§
111.025 through
111.028 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 5.12.110)
   (D)   Any person violating any of the provisions of §§
111.040 through
111.042 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 5.14.110)
(Ord. 2081, passed - -1949; Ord. 2082, passed - -1949; Ord. 2550, passed - -
1974; Ord. 2986, passed - -1989; Ord. 3071, passed - -1993; Ord. 3091, passed -
-1994; Ord. 3381, passed 6-15-2010; Ord. 3774, passed 10-15-2024)

**** SOLICITORS, CANVASSERS AND PEDDLERS ****
§ 112.001 DEFINITIONS.
   SOLICITOR, CANVASSER and PEDDLER, as used in this subchapter, mean any
individual whose business is mainly or principally carried on by traveling,
either by foot, wagon, automobiles motor vehicle or any other type of
conveyance, from place to place, from house to house or from street to street,
taking or attempting to take orders for the sale of goods, wares, merchandise
or personal property of any kind whatsoever for present or future delivery, or
for services to be furnished or performed in the future, whether or not such
individual has, carries or exposes for sale a sample of the subject of such
sale or whether he or she is collecting advance payments or not. This
definition shall not include persons selling only to retailers or wholesalers.
(Prior Code, § 5.20.010) (Ord. 2570, passed - -1975)
§ 112.002 LICENSE REQUIRED.
   It is unlawful for any person to engage in the business of a solicitor,
canvasser or peddler, as defined m §
112.001 of this chapter, within the corporate limits of the city, without first
obtaining a license therefor as provided in this subchapter, or to engage in
such business after his or her license has been suspended, revoked or
cancelled, or to fail to surrender such license after the same has been
suspended, revoked or cancelled.
(Prior Code, § 5.20.020) (Ord. 2570, passed - -1975) Penalty, see §
112.999
§ 112.003 LICENSE APPLICATION.
   Any person desiring to apply for a license as a solicitor, canvasser or
peddler under the provisions of this subchapter shall file with the City Clerk
a sworn application, in writing in duplicate, on a form to be furnished by the
City Clerk, which shall give the following information:
   (A)   The name and description of the applicant;
   (B)   The permanent home address and local address of the applicant;
   (C)   A brief description of the nature of the business to be carried on or
the goods or services to be sold and the length of time the applicant has been
engaged in such business;
   (D)   If employed, the name and address of the employer, together with
credentials establishing such relationship;
   (E)   The length of time for which the right to do business is desired;
   (F)   If a vehicle is to be used, a description of the same, together with
the license number or other means of identification;
   (G)   A photograph of the applicant taken within one year prior to the date
of the application, which picture shall be two inches by two inches, showing
the head and shoulder of the applicant in a clear and distinguishing manner.
Fingerprints of the applicant shall be taken by the Police Division and filed
with the application;
   (H)   The names of at least two reliable property owners of the county who
will certify as to the applicant’s good character and business responsibility,
or in lieu thereof, such other evidence as to the good character and business
responsibility of the applicant as will enable an investigator to properly
evaluate his or her character and business responsibility; and
   (I)   A statement as to whether or not the applicant has been convicted of
any crime, misdemeanor or violation of this code or any other municipal
ordinance, giving the nature of the offense, the punishment or penalty
assessed, if any, and the city and state where conviction occurred.
(Prior Code, § 5.20.030) (Ord. 2570, passed - -1975)
§ 112.004 LICENSE FEE.
   At the time of the filing of the application, a fee of $25 shall be paid to
the City Clerk to cover the cost of investigation of the facts stated in the
application; provided that, an application for the renewal of a license, filed
within 30 days after the expiration of a former license held by the applicant,
shall be accompanied by an investigation fee of $5.
(Prior Code, § 5.20.040) (Ord. 2570, passed - -1975; Ord. 3058, passed - -1992)
§ 112.005 LICENSE INVESTIGATION.
   Upon receipt of any application for a solicitor’s, peddler’s or canvasser’s
license, the original thereof shall be referred immediately to the City
Manager, who shall cause an investigation to be made of the facts stated in the
application and of the applicant’s business responsibility and moral character.
If the application states a vehicle is to be used, such motor vehicle shall be
inspected by the police and a report of such investigation shall be filed with
the City Manager. The City Manager shall endorse his or her findings on the
application and return the application to the City Clerk within three business
days after receipt thereof.
(Prior Code, § 5.20.050) (Ord. 2570, passed - -1975)
§ 112.006 LICENSE DISAPPROVAL.
   (A)   If, as a result of the investigation provided for in §
112.005 of this chapter, the applicant’s character or business responsibility
are found to be unsatisfactory or the facts stated in the application are found
to be untrue, or if the vehicle to be used by the applicant is not in good
mechanical condition and does not meet all safety requirements, the City
Manager shall endorse on the application his or her disapproval thereof and the
reasons for disapproval before returning the application to the City Clerk, who
shall then notify the applicant that his or her application is disapproved and
that no license will be issued.
(Prior Code, § 5.20.060)
   (B)   Any person aggrieved by the action of the City Manager or City Clerk
in the denial of an application for a license as a solicitor, canvasser or
peddler shall have the right to appeal to the governing body of the city within
ten days after the denial of such application, by filing with the City Clerk a
written notice of intention to appeal, setting forth fully the grounds for such
appeal. The governing body shall, at the next meeting after the filing of such
appeal, fix a time and place for a hearing thereon and notice of such hearing
shall be given to the appellant, in writing, by mailing such notice to the
appellant at his or her last known address, as shown by his or her application,
at least five days prior to the date set for hearing. The decision of the
governing body on such appeal shall be final and conclusive.
(Prior Code, § 5.20.070)
(Ord. 2570, passed - -1975)
§ 112.007 LICENSE APPROVAL.
   If, as a result of the investigation provided for in §
112.005 of this chapter, the character and business responsibility of the
applicant for a license as a solicitor, canvasser or peddler are found to be
good and the facts stated in the application are found to be true, the City
Manager shall endorse his or her approval upon the application before returning
such application to the City Clerk, who shall then, without payment of any
license fee, immediately issue to the applicant a license to engage in the
business described in the application. The license shall contain the signature
of the City Clerk and shall show the name, address and photograph of the
licensee, the class of license issued, the kind of goods to be sold or services
to be rendered thereunder and the date of issuance and the date of expiration
thereof.
(Prior Code, § 5.20.080) (Ord. 2570, passed - -1975)
§ 112.008 LICENSE EXPIRATION DATE.
   All licenses for solicitors, canvassers or peddlers issued under the
provisions of this subchapter shall expire on December 31 of the year of their
issuance.
(Prior Code, § 5.20.090) (Ord. 2570, passed - -1975)
§ 112.009 LICENSE EXHIBITION REQUIRED.
   Peddlers are required to exhibit their licenses at the request of any
citizen.
(Prior Code, § 5.20.100) (Ord. 2570, passed - -1975) Penalty, see §
112.999
§ 112.010 LICENSE SUSPENSION OR REVOCATION.
   (A)   Upon complaint and evidence of good cause, the City Manager is
authorized to suspend the license of any solicitor, canvasser or peddler issued
under the provisions of this subchapter until the second meeting of the
governing body following such suspension, and the governing body, upon a
hearing of the matter with notice of such hearing mailed to the licensee at his
or her last known address, as shown by his or her license, at least five days
prior to the date set for the hearing, may permanently revoke or cancel such
license for good cause, or terminate the suspension and order the return of the
license; provided that, good cause shall include the following:
      (1)   Any reason for which a license could be refused by the City Clerk,
in the case of an original application;
      (2)   Conviction of the licensee by the Police Court in the city in any
case involving a violation of this code or any other ordinance of the city
relating to or regulating any business, trade, calling or profession carried on
or pursued in the city;
      (3)   Fraud, misrepresentation or any false statement contained in the
application for a license;
      (4)   Fraud, misrepresentation or false statement made in the course of
carrying on the business provided for in the license; and
      (5)   Conducting the business of the licensee in an unlawful manner, or
in such manner as to constitute a breach of the peace or to constitute a menace
to the health, safety or welfare of the inhabitants of the city.
   (B)   Whenever a license is suspended, revoked or cancelled, as provided in
division (A) above, the licensee shall immediately surrender such license to
the City Clerk.
(Prior Code, § 5.20.120) (Ord. 2570, passed - -1975)
§ 112.011 USE OF STREETS; STATIONARY LOCATION PROHIBITED.
   The issuance of a peddler’s license to any person by the City Clerk, as
provided in this subchapter, shall not entitle such person to have any
exclusive right to any location in the public streets, nor shall any such
person be permitted any stationary location, nor shall he or she be permitted
to operate in any congested area where his or her operation might impede or
inconvenience the public. For the purpose of this section, the judgment of a
police officer, exercised in good faith, shall be deemed conclusive as to
whether the area is congested or the public impeded or inconvenienced.
(Prior Code, § 5.20.110) (Ord. 2570, passed - -1975)
**** CHARITABLE SOLICITATIONS ****
§ 112.025 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   CHARITABLE. Includes the words patriotic, philanthropic, social service,
welfare, benevolent, educational, civic or fraternal, either actual or
purported.
   PERSON. Any individual, firm, copartnership, corporation, company,
association, church, religious sect, religious denomination, society,
organization or league.
   RELIGIOUS and RELIGION. Do not mean and include the word “charitable”, as
defined in this section, but shall be given their commonly accepted
definitions.
   SOLICIT and SOLICITATION. The request, directly or indirectly, of money,
credit, property, financial assistance or other thing of value on the plea or
representation that such money, credit, property, financial assistance or other
tiring of value Wi-Fi be used for a charitable or religious purpose as those
purposes are defined in this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 5.32.010) (Ord. 2772, passed - -1980)
§ 112.026 PERMIT REQUIRED.
   (A)   No person shall solicit contributions for any charitable purpose
within the city without a permit from the City Clerk; provided, however, that,
the provisions of this section shall apply to the person in charge of all mass
charitable solicitations to be conducted within the city on a given date for
which volunteers without remuneration for making such solicitations are
involved.
   (B)   Only the person in charge of said activity shall be required to
register under the provisions of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 5.32.020) (Ord. 2772, passed - -1980) Penalty, see §
112.999
§ 112.027 PERMIT APPLICATION.
   (A)   An application for a permit to solicit under this chapter shall be
made to the City Clerk’s office upon forms provided by the city. Such
application shall be sworn to and filed with the City Clerk at least 30 days
prior to the time at which the permit applied for shall become effective;
provided, however, that, the City Clerk may, for good cause shown, allow the
filing of an application less than 30 days prior to the effective date of the
permit applied for.
   (B)   The application required in this section shall contain the following
information:
      (1)   The name, address or headquarters of the person applying for the
permit;
      (2)   If the applicant is not an individual, the names and addresses of
the applicant’s principal officers and managers;
      (3)   The name and address of the person or persons who will be in direct
charge of conducting the solicitation; and
      (4)   The time when the solicitations will be made.
(Prior Code, § 5.32.030) (Ord. 2772, passed - -1980)
§ 112.028 INVESTIGATION OF APPLICANTS.
   The City Clerk shall examine all applications filed under this subchapter
for a charitable solicitation permit and shall make, or cause to be made, such
further investigation of the application and the permit will be issued once the
City Clerk determines the following facts:
   (A)   All of the statements made in the application are true;
   (B)   The control and supervision of the solicitation will be under
responsible and reliable persons; and
   (C)   The applicant has not engaged in any fraudulent transaction or
enterprise.
(Prior Code, § 5.32.040) (Ord. 2772, passed - -1980)
§ 112.029 PERMIT FEE.
   Before a permit is issued, there shall be paid to the Treasurer of the city
the sum of $5 as a permit fee.
(Prior Code, § 5.32.050) (Ord. 2772, passed - -1980; Ord. 3057, passed - -1992)
§ 112.030 PERMIT INFORMATION; TIME LIMITATION.
   Permits issued under this chapter shall bear the name and address of the
person by whom the solicitation is to be made, the number of the permit, the
date issued, the dates within which the permit holder may solicit, and a
statement that the permit does not constitute an endorsement by the city of the
purpose or of the person conducting the solicitation. All permits must be
signed by the City Clerk. No permit may grant the right to solicit longer than
90 days from its date.
(Prior Code, § 5.32.060) (Ord. 2772, passed - -1980)
§ 112.031 PERMIT NON-TRANSFERABLE.
   Any permit issued under this subchapter shall be non-transferable.
(Prior Code, § 5.32.070) (Ord. 2772, passed - -1980)
§ 112.032 AGENTS AND SOLICITORS; CREDENTIALS.
   All persons to whom permits have been issued under this subchapter shall
furnish proper credentials to their agents and solicitors for such
solicitation. Such credentials shall include the name of the permit holder, the
date the permit was issued and the specific time period during which said
solicitor is authorized to solicit on behalf of the permit holder.
(Prior Code, § 5.32.080) (Ord. 2772, passed - -1980)
§ 112.033 REVIEW OF PERMIT DENIAL; REQUEST.
   Within five days after receiving notification that the application for a
permit to solicit under this subchapter has been denied, any applicant may file
a written request for review of the denial with the City Manager’s office. Upon
the filing of such a request, the City Manager shall fix a time and place to
meet with the applicant and, at that meeting, the City Manager will review the
information presented to the City Clerk’s office and shall make a decision as
to whether to uphold the original findings or to issue a permit for
solicitation.
(Prior Code, § 5.32.090) (Ord. 2772, passed - -1980)
§ 112.034 RELIGIOUS SOLICITATIONS.
   (A)   No person shall solicit contributions for any religious purpose within
the city without a certificate from the City Clerk’s office. Application for a
certificate shall be made to the City Clerk upon forms provided by the city.
Such application shall be signed by the applicant and shall contain the
following information:
      (1)   The name and address or headquarters of the person applying for the
permit;
      (2)   If the applicant is not an individual, the names and addresses of
the applicant’s principal officers and managers authorizing the religious
activities;
      (3)   The name and address of the person or persons who will be in direct
charge of conducting the religious activities and solicitations within the
city; and
      (4)   The time when such religious activities and solicitations shall be
made, giving the preferred dates for the beginning and ending of such
activities.
   (B)   (1)   Upon receipt of such application, once the City Clerk is
satisfied that all of the information contained in the application is true and
correct, the City Clerk shall forthwith issue the applicant a certificate of
registration. The certificate shall remain in force and effect for a period of
90 days after the issuance thereof and shall be renewed upon the expiration of
this period upon the filing of a new application as provided for in this
section. Certificates of registration shall bear the name and address of the
person by whom the solicitation is to be made, the number of the certificate,
the date issued and a statement that the certificate does not constitute an
endorsement by the city. All persons to whom certificates have been issued
shall furnish credentials to their agents.
      (2)   No person shall solicit for any religious cause without having such
credentials in his or her possession.
(Prior Code, § 5.32.100) (Ord. 2772, passed - -1980) Penalty, see §
112.999
§ 112.035 PERMIT SUSPENSION; NOTICE.
   Whenever it shall be shown, or whenever the City Clerk has knowledge, that
any person to whom a permit has been issued under this subchapter has violated
any of the provisions of this subchapter, or that any promoter, agent or
solicitor of a permit holder has misrepresented the purpose of the
solicitation, the City Clerk shall immediately suspend the permit and give the
permit holder written notice by registered mail of the termination of the
permit for solicitation.
(Prior Code, § 5.32.110) (Ord. 2772, passed - -1980)
§ 112.036 FRAUDULENT MISREPRESENTATION AND MISSTATEMENTS PROHIBITED.
   No person shall directly or indirectly solicit contributions for any purpose
by misrepresentation of his or her name, occupation, financial condition,
social condition or residence, and no person shall make or perpetrate any other
misstatement, deception or fraud in connection with any solicitation of any
contribution for any purpose in the city, or in any application or report filed
under this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 5.32.120) (Ord. 2772, passed - -1980) Penalty, see §
112.999
§ 112.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person violating any provision of §§
112.001 through
112.011 of this chapter shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction,
shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 5.20.130)
   (C)   Any person convicted of violating any provision of §§
112.025 through
112.036 of this chapter shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances. Each day of operation in violation of the
provisions of §§
112.025 through
112.036 of this chapter shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 5.32.130)
(Ord. 2570, passed - -1975; Ord. 2772, passed - -1980)
CHAPTER 113: TAXICABS
Section
   
113.001   Definitions
   
113.002   License required
   
113.003   License application; contents; display
   
113.004   License issuance
   
113.005   Insurance; safety requirements
   
113.006   License application; display
   
113.007   License fees
   
113.008   License suspension or revocation
   
113.009   Previously licensed taxicab
   
113.010   Lost articles
   
113.011   Off-duty time required
   
113.012   Driver; use of intoxicating liquor; license revocation
   
113.013   Right to regulate
   
113.014   Registration and license required
 
   
113.999   Penalty
§ 113.001 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   PERSON. Any person, firm, association, corporation or business organization.
   STREET. Any street, alley, avenue, boulevard, trafficway, lane, court or
public place in the city.
   TAXICAB. Includes all motorized methods of conveyance carrying passengers
for hire for which public patronage is solicited; provided, however, that,
railroad cars and motorbuses designed and constructed for general
transportation of passengers for hire and possessing a manufacturer’s rated
seating capacity of 12 or more passengers, shall not be considered TAXICABS
within the meaning of this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.010) (Ord. 1950, passed - -1934; Ord. 2911, passed - -1985;
Ord. 2942, passed - -1986)
§ 113.002 LICENSE REQUIRED.
   No person, whether acting as owner, principal, agent, employee, lessee or
licensee, shall operate or permit to be operated any taxicab upon the streets
of the city without first having procured a license therefor, as provided in
this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.020) (Ord. 1950, passed - -1934) Penalty, see §
113.999
§ 113.003 LICENSE APPLICATION; CONTENTS; DISPLAY.
   Each cab shall have proof of the company name inside the vehicle for
identification purposes.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.030) (Ord. 1950, passed - -1934; Ord. 2900, passed - -1985;
Ord. 3473, passed 4-5-2016)
§ 113.004 LICENSE ISSUANCE.
   Each license shall be issued by the City Clerk after application has first
been approved by the City Manager or his or her designee. No license shall be
assigned or transferred.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.040) (Ord. 1950, passed - -1934; Ord. 2856, passed - -1983;
Ord. 3473, passed 4-5-2016)
§ 113.005 INSURANCE; SAFETY REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   Before any license for a taxicab shall be issued, the owner shall file
with the City Clerk a standard insurance policy, issued by an insurance company
licensed to do business in the state and subject to the jurisdiction of the
state’s Insurance Department, providing insurance coverage which meets the
minimum requirements of the state. The policy shall contain all other
conditions required by the laws of the state and shall carry a uniform
endorsement for notice to the city of any changes therein in such form as shall
be specified by the City Attorney.
   (B)   The policy shall provide further that it cannot be cancelled until ten
days’ written notice of such cancellation shall have been filed with the City
Clerk.
   (C)   If such policy is cancelled and the owner of the taxicab fails to
provide, within ten days, another policy of insurance complying with the
provisions thereof, then the license issued for such taxicab shall
automatically become void and of no effect.
   (D)   It is further provided that, before any license for a taxicab is
issued, the vehicle or vehicles shall pass a state motor vehicle inspection.
The inspection shall be performed by any state-approved motor vehicle
inspection station.
   (E)   It is further provided, that before any license for a taxicab is
issued, the vehicle operating under this chapter shall be inspected by an
independent automotive garage to verify that the vehicle is safe for the
transportation of passengers. This inspection will include, but will not be
limited to, the physical integrity of the passenger compartment to make certain
that the doors operate properly, that seat belts are provided and in working
order, that seats are properly anchored in a secure fashion, that the floor
boards of the car are intact and able to withstand the weight of passengers as
well as being sealed to prevent infusion of any outside material into the
passenger compartment and all such reports are to be in writing and filed with
the Codes Enforcement Officer of the city. The city will provide an appropriate
inspection form. Each and every vehicle, which is licensed by the city as a
taxicab, must conduct this inspection every six months after the license is
issued for the vehicle.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.050) (Ord. 1950, passed - -1934; Ord. 2626, passed - -1976;
Ord. 2873, passed - -1984; Ord. 2911, passed - -1985; Ord. 2942, passed - -
1986) Penalty, see §
113.999
§ 113.006 LICENSE APPLICATION; DISPLAY.
   No person shall operate a taxicab, as defined in this chapter, without first
making application, in writing, to the City Clerk for a license to do so.
Applications shall be made by the operator and shall contain his or her full
name, address and telephone number of the operator, his or her state driver’s
license number for the appropriate driver’s license required under K.S.A. 8-234
(B), and the company or corporation that he or she is working for. No person
shall be eligible for a license as a taxicab operator if the person has been
convicted of D.U.I. or placed on diversion for D.U.I. within five years of the
date of the application. No person shall be eligible for an operator’s license
who has been convicted of any violent crime against persons or any theft-
related crimes if the same have occurred within five years of the date of the
application. Licenses shall be issued by the City Clerk after receipt of
application and proof that the applicant has a current, valid, state driver’s
license as is set out in K.S.A. 8-234(B), and has met the other requirements of
this section. Each applicant shall have his or her photograph and fingerprints
taken by the Police Department at the time of the application. All applicants
must be licensed to drive by the state for at least 90 days prior to being
issued a taxi operator’s license. No taxicab operator’s license shall be
assigned or transferred. The taxicab operator’s license shall be displayed on
the interior of the vehicle on or adjacent to the sun visor on the right-hand
side of the vehicle.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.060) (Ord. 1950, passed - -1934; Ord. 2626, passed - -1976;
Ord. 2758, passed - -1980; Ord. 2873, passed - -1984; Ord. 2900, passed - -
1985) Penalty, see §
113.999
§ 113.007 LICENSE FEES.
   The taxicab operator’s license fee will be $5 per year. The taxicab license
will be $35 per year.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.070) (Ord. 1950, passed - -1934; Ord. 2626, passed - -1976;
Ord. 2900, passed - -1985; Ord. 3038, passed - -1992; Ord. 3473, passed 4-5-
2016)
§ 113.008 LICENSE SUSPENSION OR REVOCATION.
   In addition to any other penalty provided by this chapter, the City Manager
may revoke any license issued or granted under the provisions of this chapter,
upon conviction of the licensee of the violation of any ordinance of the city
or of any law of the state or in the event such licensee becomes an unfit
person to be engaged in the business of transporting or conveying persons for
hire.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.080) (Ord. 1950, passed - -1934; Ord. 2856, passed - -1983)
§ 113.009 PREVIOUSLY LICENSED TAXICAB.
   Owners of taxicabs now licensed by the city shall have 15 days from the
effective date of the ordinance codified in this chapter in which to comply
with the provisions of this chapter; provided, however, that, the license fee,
as set out in §
113.007 of this chapter, shall not be required to be paid by said owner to
secure a license under the provisions of this chapter, until the license now
held by such owner has expired.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.140) (Ord. 1950, passed - -1934)
§ 113.010 LOST ARTICLES.
   Whenever any package, article, baggage or goods of any kind are left in a
taxicab, the driver or owner of the taxicab shall, upon the discovery of such
package, article, baggage or goods, deliver same to the officer in charge of
the police station of the city, unless such package, article, baggage or goods
shall sooner be delivered to the owner thereof on the order of such owner.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.090) (Ord. 1950, passed - -1934) Penalty, see §
113.999
§ 113.011 OFF-DUTY TIME REQUIRED.
   No person, either acting as owner, principal, agent, employee, lessee or
licensee, shall require or permit any driver of any taxicab in the city who has
worked as a taxicab driver for 12 consecutive hours, to continue on duty or to
drive said taxicab until he or she has had eight hours off duty.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.100) (Ord. 1950, passed - -1934) Penalty, see §
113.999
§ 113.012 DRIVER; USE OF INTOXICATING LIQUOR; LICENSE REVOCATION.
   In the event any driver of a taxicab is convicted of any violent crime
against persons, any theft-related crimes, of driving a motor vehicle while
under the influence of intoxicating liquor or drugs, is convicted for having
liquor or alcohol or cereal malt beverage in his or her possession while
operating a motor vehicle, or is enrolled in a diversion program while being
charged with driving a motor vehicle while under the influence of intoxicating
liquor or drugs, the license of such taxicab driver will be immediately revoked
and no new license for such taxicab driver shall be issued for a period of five
years from the date of the revocation.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.110) (Ord. 1950, passed - -1934; Ord. 2873, passed - -1984)
§ 113.013 RIGHT TO REGULATE.
   The issuance of any license, as provided in this chapter, shall in no manner
abridge or limit the right of the city to hereafter regulate or control the
operation of taxicabs within the city.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.120) (Ord. 1950, passed - -1934)
§ 113.014 REGISTRATION AND LICENSE REQUIRED.
   (A)   It is the duty of every taxicab driver to register every six months
with the Chief of Police of the city.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.130)
   (B)   No person, whether acting as owner, principal, agent, employee, lessee
or licensee, shall operate or permit to be operated upon the streets of the
city any taxicab without first having procured a license from the City Clerk
which shall be permanently placed in the vehicle of the taxicab. The operator
of the taxicab shall have a license with his or her picture also in his or her
possession at all times.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.150)
(Ord. 1950, passed - -1934; Ord. 2626, passed - -; Ord. 2911, passed - -1985;
Ord. 2942, passed - -1986; Ord. 3473, passed 4-5-2016) Penalty, see §
113.999
§ 113.999 PENALTY.
   Any person convicted of violating any provision of this chapter shall be
punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances. Each day of operation in violation of the
provisions of this chapter shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 5.24.160) (Ord. 1950, passed - -1934; Ord. 2873, passed - -1984;
Ord. 2911, passed - -1985)
CHAPTER 114: AMUSEMENTS AND LEISURE
Section
Flea Markets
   
114.001   Definition
   
114.002   Operator duties; other regulations
   
114.003   External displays of merchandise in areas other than parking lots

Farmers Markets
