**** FLEA MARKETS ****
§ 114.001 DEFINITION.
   The term FLEA MARKET is defined to mean a private enterprise open to the
public and operated and managed year-round or seasonally by any person,
partnership, firm or corporation on private property which provides aisles for
the public and spaces or stalls for rental or lease to other persons,
partnerships, firms and corporations desiring to display new and/or used
merchandise for barter and/or sale to the public. This shall also include the
sale of any used merchandise or any mix of new and used merchandise by the flea
market operator which is sold or bartered to the public at a location other
than inside a building. This shall not include short-term displays of
merchandise of a seasonal nature such as live plants and associated products or
outdoor yard ornaments made of non-corrosive material such as concrete. This
shall also not include persons selling new and used automobiles, farm
implements, lawn tractors or other similar products.
(Prior Code, § 5.48.010) (Ord. 3085, passed - -1993)
§ 114.002 OPERATOR DUTIES; OTHER REGULATIONS.
   It shall be the duty of each operator of a flea market to assure that the
flea market conforms to the definition of a flea market as provided in this
subchapter. Each flea market shall conform to all land use regulations, zoning
regulations, Building Codes, Electrical Codes, Plumbing Codes, Gas and Fire
Codes of the city as the same may be applicable to each flea market. It is
unlawful for any flea market to operate in whole or in part on any parking lot
or on any portion of any parking lot if such parking lot or any portion thereof
is accessible to the vehicles of visitors to the flea market.
(Prior Code, § 5.48.020) (Ord. 3085, passed - -1993) Penalty, see §
114.999
§ 114.003 EXTERNAL DISPLAYS OF MERCHANDISE IN AREAS OTHER THAN PARKING LOTS.
   No merchandise, equipment, inoperable vehicles, vehicle parts or any items
for sale or barter shall be stored or displayed outside a building, except
during daylight hours. The flea market operator may display merchandise during
daylight hours consistent with the operational hours of the business in an
external location other than a parking lot. In no event shall the external
display of merchandise by a flea market operator exceed 20% of the interior
space of his or her business building. At the conclusion of the daylight
external display of merchandise, all merchandise must be returned to the
interior portions of the business building of the flea market operator.
(Prior Code, § 5.48.030) (Ord. 3085, passed - -1993) Penalty, see §
114.999
§ 114.004 LICENSE REQUIRED.
   On an annual basis, every flea market operator must apply for a license. An
application for license shall be submitted to the City Clerk. After a review by
the city staff, if the flea market operator’s property is in an area which is
compatible in use as defined in §
114.002 of this chapter, a license will be issued upon the payment of $25 to
the city.
(Prior Code, § 5.48.040) (Ord. 3085, passed - -1993) Penalty, see §
114.999
**** FARMERS MARKETS ****
§ 114.015 LOCATION.
   The governing body of the city hereby establishes the following parameters
and policy relating to the Fort Scott Farmers Market, located at the Fort Scott
Downtown Pavilion, otherwise known as the “Gathering Square Pavilion”, located
between Old Fort Boulevard and Stanton Street on North National Avenue and
adjacent to the Fort Scott Splash Pad.
(Ord. 3767, passed 6-18-2024)
§ 114.016 DATES, HOURS OF OPERATION.
   (A)   The governing body of the city hereby grants the Fort Scott Farmers
Market and the Fort Scott Farmers Market Organizing Committee the annual right
of first refusal and access to the Gathering Square Pavilion during the
schedule as set forth below, without any applicable reservation or fees
required to be applied by the city.
   (B)   The city shall ensure applicable signage and/or public notice, lawful
clearing and closure of the Pavilion to regular public access is provided prior
to the operational hours of the Farmers Market: from the first weekend in May
to the last weekend in October:
      (1)   All Tuesdays from the hours of 3:00 p.m. to 7:00 p.m.; and
      (2)   All Saturdays from the hours of 6:00 a.m. to 1:00 p.m.
(Ord. 3767, passed 6-18-2024)
§ 114.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person who violates any provision of §§
114.001 through
114.004 of this chapter for which a penalty is not otherwise specifically
provided shall, upon conviction of any such violation, be punished as set out
in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 5.48.050) (Ord. 3085, passed - -1993)
CHAPTER 115: GARBAGE COLLECTION AND TRANSPORTATION
Section
   
115.001   Permit required; exception
   
115.002   Definition
   
115.003   Permit fee
   
115.004   Permit term
   
115.005   Permit application
   
115.006   Permit issuance
   
115.007   Vehicle identification plates
   
115.008   Vehicle requirements
   
115.009   Disposal
   
115.010   Hazardous wastes
 
   
115.999   Penalty
§ 115.001 PERMIT REQUIRED; EXCEPTION.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to collect, disturb or transport any
garbage through, over or across the streets and alleys of the city without
first securing a permit; provided, however, that, this section shall not apply
to persons who collect and transport commercial garbage produced on commercial
properties occupied by them or their employers or to persons who collect and
transport garbage produced from residential properties occupied by them; and,
provided further, however, that, such persons not required to obtain a permit
hereunder shall comply with all other regulations set forth in this chapter for
the transportation of garbage or trash through, over and across the streets and
alleys of the city.
(Prior Code, § 5.40.010) (Ord. 3369, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
115.999
§ 115.002 DEFINITION.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the term GARBAGE is putrescible animal and
vegetable waste resulting from the handling, preparation, cooking and
consumption of food.
(Prior Code, § 5.40.020) (Ord. 3369, passed 9-15-2009)
§ 115.003 PERMIT FEE.
   No permit shall be issued unless the applicant therefor shall pay to the
City Clerk a permit fee in the sum of $50 for pickups and $100 for large trucks
per annum for each vehicle used in the collection and transportation of
garbage.
(Prior Code, § 5.40.030) (Ord. 3369, passed 9-15-2009; Ord. 3449, passed 12-16-
2014)
§ 115.004 PERMIT TERM.
   Each permit shall be effective January 1 of the calendar year it is issued
and shall expire on December 31 of the same calendar year.
(Prior Code, § 5.40.040) (Ord. 3369, passed 9-15-2009; Ord. 3436, passed 1-21-
2014)
§ 115.005 PERMIT APPLICATION.
   Any person desiring to collect or transport garbage in the city shall make
application for such permit to the City Clerk. Such application shall set forth
the name of the applicant, the make and type of vehicle to be operated for such
collection and transportation, the title number of such vehicle and shall be
accompanied by a certificate of inspection and proof of current automobile
liability insurance and approval of such vehicle by the Codes Department of the
city, issued not more than 15 days prior to the date of application. The City
Manager shall investigate any applicant’s qualifications and ability to
collect, transport and dispose of garbage in a satisfactory manner.
(Prior Code, § 5.40.050) (Ord. 3369, passed 9-15-2009)
§ 115.006 PERMIT ISSUANCE.
   Upon approval of the application by the City Manager and the granting of a
permit, the City Clerk shall issue a permit upon the payment of the required
permit fee.
(Prior Code, § 5.40.060) (Ord. 3369, passed 9-15-2009)
§ 115.007 VEHICLE IDENTIFICATION PLATES.
   The City Clerk shall issue a receipt, upon payment of the permit fee,
together with a label for each vehicle. The label shall be conspicuously placed
upon the vehicle in a place and position to be clearly visible and in a
condition to be clearly legible, and such label shall be used only on the
vehicle for which it is issued; provided, however, that, such permit label may
be transferred to another vehicle used by the permit holder in the event the
vehicle for which the label was issued ceases to be used in the collection and
transportation of garbage by the permit holder. The transfer of the label may
be effected only after payment to the City Clerk of a transfer fee of $1, and
after a certificate of inspection and approval of such vehicle by the Codes
Department of the city is furnished to the City Manager.
(Prior Code, § 5.40.070) (Ord. 3369, passed 9-15-2009)
§ 115.008 VEHICLE REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   All vehicles used for the transportation of solid waste shall be
maintained in a safe, clean and sanitary condition, and shall be so
constructed, maintained and operated as to prevent spillage of solid waste
therefrom. All vehicles used for transportation of solid waste which contains
semi-liquid or leach ate shall be constructed of suitable material with water-
tight bodies. All vehicles used for the transportation of solid waste shall
have covers of suitable material with fasteners designed to secure all sides of
the cover to the vehicle, which shall be secured whenever the vehicle is
transporting solid waste, or the entire body shall be enclosed, with only
loading hoppers exposed. No solid waste shall be transported in the loading
hoppers.
   (B)   Any vehicle used by any person or corporation for the collection and
transportation of garbage, other than one being used by a person hauling his or
her own garbage, shall be maintained in good mechanical condition and shall
further comply with the certificate of inspection issued by the codes
department of the city if such certificate is required. Any such vehicle shall
be equipped with an enclosed body designed so as to prevent garbage escaping
therefrom.
   (C)   No person shall, at any time, convey, remove or carry away any garbage
upon any street, alley or public grounds of the city unless the same is
conveyed in a water-tight container, securely covered and in such a manner as
to prevent any odor escaping therefrom.
(Prior Code, § 5.40.080) (Ord. 3369, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
115.999
§ 115.009 DISPOSAL.
   Solid waste shall be deposited at a processing facility or disposal area
complying with all requirements of the state or any applicable law of the state
where the processing facility of disposal area is located, and any applicable
federal law, rule or regulation.
(Prior Code, § 5.40.090) (Ord. 3369, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
115.999
§ 115.010 HAZARDOUS WASTES.
   The administrative authority may classify certain wastes as “hazardous
wastes”, which will require special handling and shall be disposed of only in a
manner acceptable to the administrative authority and which will meet all
local, state and federal regulations.
(Prior Code, § 5.40.100) (Ord. 3369, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
115.999
§ 115.999 PENALTY.
   Any person violating any of the provisions of this chapter shall be deemed
guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction, shall be punished as provided in
§
10.999 of this code of ordinances. If any person shall fail to comply with any
provision of this chapter, the administrative authority may, upon 48 hours’
written notice to such person, revoke and cancel such license to collect or
remove solid waste within the city. In no event will any portion of the license
fee be refunded to the licensee upon revocation of such license.
(Prior Code, § 5.40.110) (Ord. 3369, passed 9-15-2009)
CHAPTER 116: NATURAL GAS WELLS
Section
General Provisions
   
116.001   Definitions
   
116.002   Compliance with statutes and chapter
   
116.003   Exceptions
   
116.004   Drilling prohibited where
   
116.005   Drilling, completion, production and maintenance
   
116.006   Transmitting gas in public way prohibited
Permits; Conditions
   
116.020   Conditions generally
   
116.021   Bond requirements
   
116.022   Insurance requirements
   
116.023   Proof of property ownership
   
116.024   Disposal of materials
   
116.025   Consent of adjoining properties
   
116.026   Application requirements
   
116.027   Fees; denial
   
116.028   Transfer
   
116.029   Revocation conditions
 
   
116.999   Penalty
**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 116.001 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   CITY. The City of Fort Scott, Kansas.
   GAS WELL. Any well drilled, to be drilled or used for the intended or actual
production of natural gas.
   GOVERNING BODY. The governing body of the City of Fort Scott, Kansas.
   OWNER OF FEE SIMPLE INTEREST. The owner, in fee simple, of both the surface
and all minerals in, under and upon the real property upon which any natural
gas well is to be drilled under the terms of any permit as provided by this
chapter.
   PERMIT. A drilling permit, issued or sought to be issued under this chapter,
authorizing the drilling of a natural gas well, as herein defined.
   PERSON. Includes persons, firms, corporations, partnerships or associations.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.010) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982)
§ 116.002 COMPLIANCE WITH STATUTES AND CHAPTER.
   Any gas well hereafter drilled, completed or produced under the terms of
this chapter or any amendment thereto shall, in addition to compliance of the
terms of this chapter, be drilled, completed, produced and operated in
compliance with any state or federal law and in compliance with any applicable
rule or regulation of either.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.020) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982) Penalty, see §
116.999
§ 116.003 EXCEPTIONS.
   Any public building erected or the land used by any department of the city,
county, state or federal government may be used for the drilling, completion or
production of natural gas wells within the city, if the gas well can be
completed in a safe manner.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.030) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982)
§ 116.004 DRILLING PROHIBITED WHERE.
   The drilling, completion or production of natural gas wells within the city
shall be entirely prohibited:
   (A)   At any place within the No. 1 Fire Zone;
   (B)   Within 100 feet of any property line separating properties under
different ownership. unless by joint agreement of the owners of properties on
either side of the property line; or
   (C)   Within 100 feet of any street, alley or utility easement.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.040) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982; Ord. 2902, passed - -1985)
Penalty, see §
116.999
§ 116.005 DRILLING, COMPLETION, PRODUCTION AND MAINTENANCE.
   The drilling, completion, production, maintenance and operation of a gas
well drilled under a permit issued under this chapter shall be conducted in
compliance with the following rules and regulations and any departure therefrom
shall constitute a violation of this chapter.
   (A)   Surface pipe must be run and set in full compliance with the
applicable rules and regulations of the state’s Corporation Commission, and in
any event at a depth of at least ten feet below any freshwater producing
strata.
   (B)   The pipe through which gas is produced must be solidly cemented from
top to bottom on the outside of the pipe and in compliance with the applicable
rules and regulations of the stat’s Corporation Commission.
   (C)   Adequate precautions shall be taken and necessary wellhead safety
devices used at all times during the drilling, completion, production, repair
and maintenance of the well.
   (D)   A portable-type snow fence at least four feet in height, or other
comparable fencing, will be required around all pits at all times during
drilling, swabbing, repair or maintenance operations. All drilling equipment
during said times will be blocked off by the use of signs and temporary
barricades.
   (E)   Upon completion of a gas well, the wellhead equipment shall be
enclosed within a chain or wire mesh fence six feet in height, supported by
steel pipe or posts at least four inches in diameter, set in concrete to a
depth of at least 18 inches, and spaced not to exceed four feet distance from
each other, and all gates forming a part of the fence shall be kept closed and
locked at all times; except, when in active use by applicant, other comparable
enclosures can be used if approved by the Codes Office of the city.
   (F)   The applicant or holder of a permit pursuant to the terms of this
chapter shall deposit in advance with the City Clerk the estimated cost of
relocation or temporary removal of any utility lines or facilities required in
connection with the drilling, completion, maintenance, repair or operation of
any such well.
   (G)   Gas produced from any well may be saved, stored or confined in an
appropriate holding tank or device to confine the same.
   (H)   No gas produced or permitted to escape from any gas well drilled or
operated under the terms of this chapter shall be permitted to escape the
confines of the well, the wellhead or transmission lines into the atmosphere
and no flares or burning of gas shall be permitted.
   (I)   Earthen pits may be allowed during the period of drilling, completion,
repair or maintenance of any gas well. Drilling mud, water, petroleum and all
types of liquid or solid waste may be stored in the pits, but must be removed
from the premises and disposed of in accordance with law, none of the afore-
referenced substances shall be allowed to escape from the earthen pits or other
water- tight tanks or vessels provided.
   (J)   Operations for the drilling, completion and equipping of any well to
be drilled under the terms of this chapter shall be completed within a period
of not to exceed 20 days from the date of commencement thereof, and all
drilling equipment, pulling and swabbing equipment removed on or before the
expirations of such period.
   (K)   Operations with respect to the drilling, completion, equipping,
repairing and maintenance of any well drilling under the terms of this chapter
shall be conducted only between the hours of 8:00 a.m. and 5:00 p.m. on
Mondays, Tuesdays, Wednesdays, Thursdays and Fridays.
   (L)   The Building Inspector of the city and other officers, agents and
employees of the city shall have the right at all times to go upon the
applicant’s premises for the purpose of inspecting any gas well and determining
compliance with or violation of the terms of this chapter.
   (M)   All production equipment, structures and premises surrounding any well
site shall at all times be maintained and kept in a clean, sanitary condition,
free of debris, and equipment not being immediately utilized in connection with
production from said well shall be removed from the property.
   (N)   The holder of any permit authorized hereunder shall renew such permit
on a calendar-year basis and pay an annual fee therefor to the office of the
City Clerk in the sum of $100 and at the same time file insurance certificate
renewals as required by §
116.022 of this chapter.
   (O)   The maximum depth of any well shall be 500 feet below grade.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.150) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982) Penalty, see §
116.999
§ 116.006 TRANSMITTING GAS IN PUBLIC WAY PROHIBITED.
   The use of city streets, city alleys and city dedicated, reserved and
acquired utility easements by any person or owner of a gas well, other than the
city, for the transmission of natural gas, is prohibited.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.160) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982) Penalty, see §
116.999
**** PERMITS; CONDITIONS ****
§ 116.020 CONDITIONS GENERALLY.
   Each of the following §§
116.021 through
116.025 of this chapter shall be a condition precedent to the issuance of a
drilling permit under this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.050) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982)
§ 116.021 BOND REQUIREMENTS.
   The filing by the applicant of a corporate surety bond approved by the City
Clerk, executed by a bonding or surety company authorized to do business in the
state, in the amount of $50,000 payable to the city, conditioned upon the full
and faithful compliance with all of the terms and provisions of this chapter
and the conditions of the permit authorized hereby, and saving the city and
holding it free and harmless of and from all judgments for damages sustained by
the city in the event that any claim for damage or injury is maintained against
the city as a result of the activities of the applicant, his, her or its
agents, servants, employees or persons in privity with applicant in the
drilling, completion or plugging of a gas well.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.060) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982)
§ 116.022 INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   The filing by applicant of a policy or policies of indemnity
insurance, or a certificate of such policy or policies issued by an insurance
company authorized to do business in the state and naming the city as a co-
insured; insuring and providing indemnity for judgments and defense of actions
for injuries, loss or damage for which the applicant, applicant’s agents,
servants and employees or persons in privity with applicant may be liable as
the result of the drilling, completion, operation or maintenance of any gas
well or any structure, apparatus, machinery or facilities appurtenant thereto
or used in connection therewith.
   (B)   (1)   Such insurance coverage shall be in an amount not less than
$500,000 aggregate for injury and property damage, per occurrence.
      (2)   Such insurance policy, policies or certificates thereof shall be
accompanied by a certificate of the insurer that such insurance is in full
force and shall not be cancelled without 30 days’ written notice thereof to the
City Clerk.
      (3)   Such insurance shall be maintained continuously in effect during
the entire period during which the applicant, permittee or persons in privity
with applicant and permittee are conducting drilling, completion, maintenance
or production operations with respect to any gas well drilled under this
chapter.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.070) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982)
§ 116.023 PROOF OF PROPERTY OWNERSHIP.
   The applicant shall have furnished satisfactory evidence that applicant is
the fee simple owner of the surface and minerals in, upon and under the real
estate described in the application and upon which the gas well proposed in the
application is to be drilled.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.080) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982)
§ 116.024 DISPOSAL OF MATERIALS.
   The application shall state and describe the manner and method by which
applicant shall dispose of any liquefied petroleum or liquid waste produced
from the well during the drilling and completion thereof, as well as the
production or maintenance thereof, such disposal to be in accordance and in
compliance with all applicable rules and regulations of the state’s Corporation
Commission, the state’s Department of Health and Environment and this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.090) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982)
§ 116.025 CONSENT OF ADJOINING PROPERTIES.
   The application shall be accompanied by the written consent to the granting
of the application by the owners of all adjoining properties; provided,
however, if such consent cannot be obtained by the applicant, the applicant may
file an application for an exception to this requirement with the Board of
Zoning Appeals and if, after hearing, with notice of such adjoining property
owners, the Board of Zoning Appeals shall determine such consent has been
unreasonably withheld, this consent requirement may be waived by order of the
Board of Zoning Appeals.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.100) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982)
§ 116.026 APPLICATION REQUIREMENTS.
   The application for any permit to drill a gas well under the terms of this
chapter shall contain and include:
   (A)   Satisfactory evidence that the conditions precedent set forth in this
chapter have been complied with;
   (B)   A map or plat of applicant’s property and the precise and definite
location thereon of the well to be drilled pursuant to the application, showing
the ownership boundaries of all land adjoining the applicant’s property and the
proposed drill site, together with evidence of the names of said owners and
their addresses; showing the location of all streets, alleys, public areas,
utility easements and structures adjoining the applicant’s property and the
proposed drill site, and the proposed location of transmission lines from the
proposed drill site to the point or points on applicant’s property where it is
proposed or expected that natural gas produced from the gas well will be
utilized;
   (C)   A statement outlining the applicant’s plans for the handling and
disposal of all drilling fluids, basic sediment, oil field brines, liquid
petroleum, water, other deleterious substances and wastes that may be produced
in connection with the drilling, completion, operation and maintenance of the
proposed well, which plan shall be reasonable, adequate, practical and in
accordance with the highest degree of care of the industry, as well as the
specific terms and conditions of this chapter;
   (D)   A written undertaking of the applicant:
      (1)   In the event a well is either non-productive or abandoned, that
within 60 days after the determination thereof or abandonment thereof, all
tools, equipment and machinery used in connection with the drilling,
completion, production or maintenance of the well shall be removed;
      (2)   The premises will be fully restored to its original condition as
soon as practicable and in no case more than 60 days after such determination;
and
      (3)   The well will be plugged in accordance with rules and regulations
of the state’s Corporation Commission.
   (E)   A written undertaking of the applicant that if such well is productive
and completed, only the tools, machinery, structures and equipment necessary
for the normal daily operation thereof shall be maintained at the well site,
and that the premises surrounding the immediate well site shall be restored to
its original condition as nearly as practicable and in no case more than 60
days after the completion of the well;
   (F)   The affidavit of applicant, or authorized agent for applicant,
evidencing the fact that applicant has given written notice by mail or personal
delivery of applicant’s intention to file an application for a permit to drill
a well to the record owners of all the surface area adjoining the applicant’s
land and the proposed drill site. In case the applicant is unable to locate any
such owner or owners, this notice requirement may be satisfied by the affidavit
of the applicant or applicant’s authorized agent to the effect that applicant
is unable, after diligent inquiry, to locate such owner or owners; and
   (G)   The applicant shall have published in a form prescribed by the City
Clerk, in the official city newspaper, one publication at least five days prior
to the filing of the application, of a notice of applicant’s intent to file an
application for a permit to drill a gas well pursuant to the terms of this
chapter and describing with particularity in the notice the legal description
of applicant’s property upon which such well is proposed to be drilled and the
precise location thereon of the proposed drill site.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.110) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982)
§ 116.027 FEES; DENIAL.
   It is unlawful for any person to commence operations for the drilling,
completion or production of a gas well within the corporate limits of the city
without first having lawfully obtained from the city a permit therefor pursuant
to this chapter and any amendments thereto. The applicant shall file with the
City Clerk an application in writing on a form approved and prescribed by the
City Attorney and City Clerk, and shall at the time of such filing deposit with
the City Clerk a fee of $250, which shall be credited to the General Operation
Fund. If the application is denied and no permit granted upon the application
within 60 days from the date of such filing, $100 of the deposit shall be
returned to the applicant and $150 shall be retained by the City Clerk, as an
application fee to provide for the cost of the processing and investigation of
such application and shall be credited to the above-mentioned fund.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.120) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982) Penalty, see §
116.999
§ 116.028 TRANSFER.
   Any permit issued under the terms of this chapter shall not be assignable to
any person or persons not owning the fee simple title to the surface and
minerals in, under and upon the property for which the permit was issued; and
any permit issued and being utilized shall automatically be transferred to any
subsequent owner of the fee simple interest provided all of the terms and
conditions of this chapter continue to be complied with.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.130) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982)
§ 116.029 REVOCATION CONDITIONS.
   The violation of or breach of any of the terms or conditions of this chapter
or the ceasing to exist of any of the conditions precedent listed in this
chapter, or the breach of any of the terms or conditions of any permit issued
pursuant hereunder. Such revocation shall take place only upon hearing by the
governing body, of which hearing the permittee shall be given at least ten
days’ written notice by mail or personal delivery. Mailing of such notice to
the last known mailing address of a permittee shall satisfy the requirements of
notice made in this section.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.140) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982)
§ 116.999 PENALTY.
   Any person violating any of the provisions of this chapter shall, upon
conviction thereof, be punished pursuant to §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 5.44.170) (Ord. 2834, passed - -1982)
CHAPTER 117: CABLE TELEVISION
Section
   
117.001   Cable television regulations established
§ 117.001 CABLE TELEVISION REGULATIONS ESTABLISHED.
   (A)   The city will follow the FCC rate regulations in its regulation of the
basic service rates and charges of the company and any other cable television
system operating in the city, notwithstanding any different or inconsistent
provisions in the franchise.
   (B)   In connection with such regulation, the city will ensure a reasonable
opportunity for consideration of the views of interested parties.
   (C)   The Mayor, or his or her designee, is authorized to execute on behalf
of the city and file with the FCC such certification forms or other instruments
as are now or may hereafter be required by the FCC rate regulations in order to
enable the city to regulate basic service rates and charges.
(Prior Code, § 5.52.010) (Ord. 3088, passed - -1994)
CHAPTER 118: TOBACCO
Section
   
118.001   Purchase, possession by minors
   
118.002   Selling, furnishing to minors
   
118.003   Defenses
 
   
118.999   Penalty
§ 118.001 PURCHASE, POSSESSION BY MINORS.
   (A)   No person who is under 18 years of age shall purchase or attempt to
purchase cigarettes, electronic cigarettes, liquid nicotine or tobacco
products.
   (B)   No person who is under 18 years of age shall possess or attempt to
possess cigarettes, electronic cigarettes, liquid nicotine or tobacco products.
(Prior Code, § 9.40.010) (Ord. 3139, passed - -1996; Ord. 3548, passed 6-18-
2019) Penalty, see §
118.999
§ 118.002 SELLING, FURNISHING TO MINORS.
   No person shall, directly or indirectly:
   (A)   Sell, give or furnish, or attempt to sell, give or furnish any
cigarettes, electronic cigarettes, liquid nicotine or tobacco products to any
person under 18 years of age: or
   (B)   Purchase or attempt to purchase any cigarettes, electronic cigarettes,
liquid nicotine or tobacco products for any person under 18 years of age.
(Prior Code, § 9.40.020) (Ord. 3139, passed - -1996; Ord. 3548, passed 6-18-
2019) Penalty, see §
118.999
§ 118.003 DEFENSES.
   (A)   In determining the penalty to be imposed for a violation of §
118.002(B) of this chapter by a licensed retail dealer whose employee sold,
furnished or distributed the cigarettes, electronic cigarettes, liquid nicotine
or tobacco products, the court shall consider it to be a mitigating
circumstances if the employee had completed a training program, approved by the
Secretary of Revenue or the Secretary’s designee, in avoiding sale, furnishing
or distributing of cigarettes, electronic cigarettes, liquid nicotine or
tobacco products to persons under the 18 years of age.
   (B)   It shall be a defense to a prosecution under §
118.002(B) of this chapter if:
      (1)   The defendant is a licensed retail dealer or a person authorized by
law to distribute samples;
      (2)   The defendant sold, furnished or distributed the cigarettes,
electronic cigarettes, liquid nicotine or tobacco products to the person under
18 years of age with reasonable cause to believe the person was of legal age to
purchase or receive cigarettes, electronic cigarettes, liquid nicotine or
tobacco products;
      (3)   To purchase or receive the cigarettes, electronic cigarettes,
liquid nicotine or tobacco products, the person under 18 years of age exhibited
to the defendants a driver’s licenses, state non-driver’s identification card
of other official or purporting to establish that the person was of legal age
to purchase or receive cigarettes, electronic cigarettes, liquid nicotine or
tobacco product;
      (4)   The defendant engages in the lawful sale, furnishing or
distribution or cigarettes, electronic cigarettes, liquid nicotine or tobacco
products by mail; and
      (5)   The defendant sold, furnished or distributed the cigarettes,
electronic cigarettes, liquid nicotine or tobacco products to the person by
mail only after the person had provided to the defendant an unsworn
declaration, conforming to K.S.A. 53-601 and amendments thereto, that the
person was 18 or more years of age.
(Prior Code, § 9.40.030) (Ord. 3139, passed - -1996; Ord. 3548, passed 6-18-
2019)
§ 118.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person, individual, partnership, corporation or association who
violates §
118.001 of this chapter is guilty of an ordinance cigarette, electronic
cigarettes, liquid nicotine or tobacco infraction for which the fine shall be
$100.
   (B)   Any person, individual, partnership, corporation or association who
violates §
118.002 of this chapter is guilty of an ordinance violation and, upon
conviction, shall be punished by a minimum fine up to $200.
(Prior Code, § 9.40.040) (Ord. 3139, passed - -1996; Ord. 3548, passed 6-18-
2019)

CHAPTER 130: OFFENSES AGAINST PUBLIC PEACE AND DECENCY
Section
   
130.001   Uniform Public Offense Code incorporated
   
130.002   Expectoration
   
130.003   Noise
   
130.004   Billposting
   
130.005   Firearms; discharging and possession
 
   
130.999   Penalty
§ 130.001 UNIFORM PUBLIC OFFENSE CODE INCORPORATED.
   There is hereby incorporated by reference for the purpose of regulating
public offenses within the corporate limits of the city that certain code known
as the “Uniform Public Offense Code, 41st Edition,” prepared and published in
book form by the League of Kansas Municipalities, Topeka, Kansas. One official
copy of said Uniform Public Offense Code shall be marked or stamped “Official
Copy as Adopted by Ord. 3793”, and to which shall be attached a copy of the
ordinance codified herein and filed with the City Clerk to be open to
inspection and available to the public at all reasonable hours.
(Ord. 3793, passed 1-6-2026)
§ 130.002 EXPECTORATION.
   It is unlawful for any person to spit or expectorate upon the floor, steps
or sides, or any portion thereof, of any streetcar or public conveyance, or
upon any hallway, stairway or steps, or upon any part of a public building, or
upon any sidewalk or crosswalk within the city.
(Prior Code, § 9.12.010) (Ord. 1086, passed - -1909) Penalty, see §
130.999
§ 130.003 NOISE.
   (A)   Excessive noise. It is hereby found:
      (1)   The making and creation of excessive, unnecessary or unusually loud
noises within the limits of the city is a condition which has existed for some
time in certain areas of the city;
      (2)   Excessive, unnecessary or unusually loud noises which are prolonged
and unusual in their time, place and use affect and are a detriment to public
health, comfort, convenience, safety, welfare and prosperity of the residents
of the city; and
      (3)   It is necessary and in the public interest for the provisions and
prohibitions hereinafter contained to be enacted, and it is further declared
that the provisions and prohibitions hereinafter contained and enacted are in
pursuance of and for the purpose of securing and promoting the public health,
comfort, convenience, safety, welfare and prosperity and the peace and quiet of
the city and its inhabitants.
(Prior Code, § 9.16.010)
   (B)   Prohibited acts. No person shall unreasonably make, continue or cause
to be made or continued, any noise disturbance. Non-commercial public speaking
and public assembly activities conducted on any public space or public right-
of-way shall be exempt from the operation of this section. The following acts,
and the causing thereof, are declared to be in violation of this section:
      (1)   Operating, playing or permitting the operation or playing of any
radio, television, phonograph, drum, musical instrument, sound amplifier or
similar device which produces, reproduces or amplifies sound:
         (a)   Between the hours of 11:00 p.m. and 7:00 a.m. the following day
in such a manner as to create a noise disturbance across a real property
boundary;
         (b)   In such a manner as to create a noise disturbance at 50 feet (15
meters) from such device, when operated in or on a motor vehicle on a public
right-of-way or public space or in a boat on public waters;
         (c)   In such a manner as to create a noise disturbance to any person
other than the operator of the device, when operated by any passenger on a
common carrier; and
         (d)   This section shall not apply to non-commercial spoken language
covered under division (B)(2) below.
      (2)   Using or operating loudspeakers/public address systems as follows:
         (a)   For any non-commercial purpose, any loudspeaker, public address
system or similar device between the hours of 11:00 p.m. and 7:00 a.m. the
following day, such that the sound therefrom creates a noise disturbance across
a residential real property boundary;
         (b)   For any commercial purpose, any loudspeakers, public address
system or similar device:
            1.   Such that the sound therefrom creates a noise disturbance
across a real property boundary; and
            2.   Between the hours of 11:00 p.m. and 7:00 a.m. the following
day on a public right-of-way or public space.
      (3)   Offering for sale anything by shouting or outcry within any
residential or commercial area of the city (except by permit issued by the City
Clerk and/or except between the hours of 9:00 a.m. and 7:00 p.m.);
      (4)   Owning, possessing or harboring any animal or bird which frequently
or for continued duration, howls, barks, meows, squawks or makes other sounds
which create a noise disturbance across a residential real property boundary;
      (5)   Loading, unloading, opening, closing or other handling of boxes,
crates, containers, trash, building materials or similar objects between the
hours of 11:00 p.m. and 7:00 a.m. the following day in such a manner as to
cause a noise disturbance across a residential real property boundary;
      (6)   Operating or permitting the operation of any tools or equipment
used in construction, drilling or demolition work:
         (a)   Between the hours of 11:00 p.m. and 7:00 a.m. the following day,
seven days a week, such that the sound therefrom creates a noise disturbance
across a residential real property boundary, except for emergency work of
public service utilities;
         (b)   At any other time such that the sound level at or across a real
property boundary exceeds 65 dba. This division (B)(6) shall not apply to
construction hand tools (to include power tools) that are mobile in nature and
are being used at a field construction site for temporary construction
purposes; and
         (c)   This division (B) shall not apply to the use of domestic power
tools.
      (7)   Repairing, rebuilding, modifying or testing any motor vehicle,
motorcycle or motorboat in such a manner as to cause a noise disturbance across
a residential real property boundary.
(Prior Code, § 9.16.020)
(Ord. 3213, passed - -2001) Penalty, see §
130.999
§ 130.004 BILLPOSTING.
   The posting, sticking, painting, branding or stamping, or causing to be
struck, painted, branded or stamped of any printed, written, painted or other
advertisements, bills, notices, signs or posters upon any pavement, sidewalk or
bridge floor within the city is prohibited and declared unlawful.
(Prior Code, § 9.26.010) (Ord. 1078, passed - -1908) Penalty, see §
130.999
§ 130.005 FIREARMS; DISCHARGING AND POSSESSION.
   (A)   Discharging firearms.
      (1)   Prohibited; exceptions. From and after the effective date of the
ordinance codified in this division (A), the discharge of firearms within the
city, except in defense of person or property, or by duly authorized police or
peace officers or persons duly authorized by the Chief of Police of the city,
in writing, to so discharge the same, is prohibited.
(Prior Code, § 9.46.010)
      (2)   Discharging certain weapons prohibited. From and after the
effective date of the ordinance codified in this division (A), the discharging
of air rifles, slingshots and bows and arrows by any person within the limits
of the city is prohibited.
(Prior Code, § 9.46.020)
   (B)   Possessing firearms prohibited.
      (1)   It is unlawful to possess a firearm on the grounds of or within the
following buildings owned or leased by the city:
         (a)   1 E. 3rd Street, known as City Hall;
         (b)   1869 Indian Road, known as the Fort Scott Municipal Airport
terminal building;
         (c)   723 Scott, known as the Fort Scott Swimming Pool;
         (d)   400 N. Hill, known as the Animal Shelter;
         (e)   1604 S. National, known as the Public Safety Center;
         (f)   201 S. National, known as the Public Library;
         (g)   602 Ivy, known as the Asphalt Plant;
         (h)   1 E. 3rd Street, known as Memorial Auditorium;
         (i)   735 Scott, knows as Buck Run Community Center;
         (j)   601 N. Barbee, known as the Wastewater Treatment Facility;
         (k)   910 Burkel, known as the Water Treatment Plant;
         (l)   2104 Sidney, known as the Public Works Maintenance Building; and
         (m)   Any other city-owned or leased building if said building is
conspicuously marked with signs stating that firearms are prohibited in such
building.
      (2)   This section shall not apply to:
         (a)   Duly authorized law enforcement officers, fire personnel
authorized to carry a firearm pursuant to K.S.A. 31-157 and amendments thereto,
or military personnel when engaged in their duties to the extent permitted by
federal or state law;
         (b)   Public officers or public employees trained and duly licenses in
firearm safety while on the grounds of or within the Fort Scott Municipal
Airport terminal building for the purpose of performing periodic deer harvests;
and/or
         (c)   Attorneys when the firearm is possessed solely for the purpose
of attempting to submit the firearm into evidence or examining the firearm in
the course of a pending court proceeding.
      (3)   For the purposes of this section, a FIREARM is any weapon designed
or having the capacity to propel a projectile by force of an explosion or
combustion that is calculated to or reasonably likely to produce death or
serious bodily injury.
      (4)   Any person who enters the grounds of or a building listed herein
with a firearm in violation of this section shall be required to forfeit the
firearm, either temporarily or permanently, and may be required to leave the
premises regardless of the public nature of the grounds or building.
(Prior Code, § 9.48.010)
(Ord. 2231, passed - -1956; Ord. 3317, passed - -2007) Penalty, see §
130.999
§ 130.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Anyone so offending per §
130.002 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and shall be
punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 9.12.010)
   (C)   Violation of §
130.003 of this chapter is a misdemeanor punishable under §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 9.16.030)
   (D)   Any person or persons violating the prohibitions contained in §
130.004 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 9.26.010)
   (E)   (1)   Any person violating any of the provisions of §
130.005(A) of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 9.46.030)
      (2)   Violation of §
130.005(B) of this chapter shall be punished under §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 9.48.010)
(Ord. 1078, passed - -1908; Ord. 1086, passed - -1909; Ord. 3213, passed - -
2001; Ord. 3317, passed - -2007)
CHAPTER 131: CURFEW
Section
   
131.001   Definition
   
131.002   Hours; exceptions
   
131.003   Breaking curfew; permitting or allowing prohibited
   
131.004   Suspension; procedure
 
   
131.999   Penalty
§ 131.001 DEFINITION.
   As used in this chapter, PERSON means a parent, legal guardian or other
person exercising legal custody of a child.
(Prior Code, § 9.38.010) (Ord. 2417, passed - -1969)
§ 131.002 HOURS; EXCEPTIONS.
   It is unlawful for any child under the age of 18 years to wander, lounge,
loaf, loiter or play in, about or upon any public street, alley, sidewalk,
vacant lot, public place or other place normally accessible to the general
public for public use, whether on foot or in a vehicle or by any other means,
after the hour of 12:01 a.m. and before the hour of 6:00 a.m., unless
accompanied by a parent, legal guardian or other person exercising legal
custody of such child; provided, however, that, such prohibition shall not
apply to those children under the age of 18 years who are en route by the most
direct and accessible route between their homes and authorized places of
employment, nor to those who are en route by the most direct and accessible
route from a place of employment, authorized entertainment or authorized place
of attendance to their residences; provided further that, the term AUTHORIZED,
as used in this section, shall denote prior authorization by a parent, legal
guardian or other person exercising legal custody.
(Prior Code, § 9.38.020) (Ord. 2417, passed - -1969) Penalty, see §
131.999
§ 131.003 BREAKING CURFEW; PERMITTING OR ALLOWING PROHIBITED.
   It is unlawful for any parent, legal guardian or other person lawfully
entitled to the care, custody and control of any child under the age of 18
years to suffer, permit or allow any such child to wander, lounge, loaf, loiter
or play in, about or upon any public street, alley, sidewalk, vacant lot,
public place or other place normally accessible to the general public for
public use, after the hour of 12:01 a.m. and before the hour of 6:00 a.m.,
unless accompanied by a parent, legal guardian or other person lawfully
entitled to the care, custody and control of such child.
(Prior Code, § 9.38.030) (Ord. 2417, passed - -1969) Penalty, see §
131.999
§ 131.004 SUSPENSION; PROCEDURE.
   The governing body of the city, on specific occasions and in response to
written requests submitted not less than three calendar days prior to the
occasion, shall have the authority to suspend, by official proclamation
published in the official city newspaper, the hour of guideline as stated in
this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 9.38.040) (Ord. 2417, passed - -1969)
§ 131.999 PENALTY.
   Any person violating any of the provisions of this chapter shall, upon
conviction, be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 9.38.050) (Ord. 2417, passed - -1969)
CHAPTER 132: LITTERING
Section
   
132.001   Short title
   
132.002   Definitions
   
132.003   Litter in public places
   
132.004   Sweeping litter into gutters
   
132.005   Litter thrown by persons in vehicles
   
132.006   Litter caused by truck loads
   
132.007   Litter in parks
   
132.008   Litter in lakes and ponds
   
132.009   Litter on occupied private property; owner to maintain
   
132.010   Litter on vacant lots
   
132.011   Exemptions
   
132.012   Containers
   
132.013   Collection; license required
   
132.014   Clearing litter from open private property by city
   
132.015   Frequency of pickup
 
   
132.999   Penalty
§ 132.001 SHORT TITLE.
   This chapter shall be known and may be cited as the “Fort Scott Anti-Litter
Ordinance”.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.010) (Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009)
§ 132.002 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   GARBAGE. Putrescible animal and vegetable wastes resulting from the
handling, preparation, cooking and consumption of food.
   LITTER. “Garbage”, “refuse”, and “rubbish”, as defined herein, and all other
waste material which, if thrown or deposited as herein prohibited, tends to
create a danger to public health, safety and welfare.
   NEWSPAPER. Any newspaper of general circulation as defined by general law,
any newspaper duly entered with the Post Office Department of the United
States, in accordance with federal regulation. This shall also conclude any
periodical or current magazine regularly published with not less than four
issues per year and sold to the public.
   PARK. A park, reservation, playground, beach, recreation center or any other
public area in the city, owned or used by the city and devoted to active or
passive recreation.
   PERSON. Any person, firm, partnership, association, corporation, company or
organization of any kind.
   PRIVATE PREMISES. Any dwelling, house, building or other structure, designed
or used either wholly or in part for private residential purposes or commercial
purposes, whether inhabited or temporarily or continuously uninhabited or
vacant, and shall include any yard, grounds, walk, driveway, porch, steps,
vestibule or mailbox belonging or appurtenant to such dwelling, house, building
or other structure.
   PUBLIC PLACE. Any and all streets, sidewalks, boulevards, alleys or other
public ways and any and all public parks, squares, spaces, grounds and
buildings.
   REFUSE. All putrescible and non-putrescible solid wastes (except body
waste), including garbage, rubbish, ashes, street cleanings, dead animals,
abandoned automobiles and solid market and industrial wastes.
   RUBBISH. Non-putrescible solid wastes consisting of both combustible and
non-combustible wastes, such as paper, wrappings, cigarettes, cardboard, tin
cans, yard clippings, leaves, wood, glass, bedding, crockery and similar
materials.
   VEHICLE. Every device in, upon or by which any person or property is or may
be transported or drawn upon a highway, including devices used exclusively upon
stationary rails or tracks.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.020) (Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009)
§ 132.003 LITTER IN PUBLIC PLACES.
   No person shall throw or deposit litter in or upon any street, sidewalk or
other public place within the city, except in public receptacles, in authorized
private receptacles for collection, or in official city dumps.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.030) (Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
132.999
§ 132.004 SWEEPING LITTER INTO GUTTERS.
   No person shall sweep into or deposit in any gutter, street or other public
place within the city the accumulation of litter from any building or lot or
from any public or private sidewalk or driveway. Persons owning or occupying
property shall keep the sidewalk in front of their premises free of litter.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.040) (Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
132.999
§ 132.005 LITTER THROWN BY PERSONS IN VEHICLES.
   No person, while a driver or passenger in a vehicle, shall throw or deposit
litter upon any street or other public place within the city or upon private
property.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.050) (Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
132.999
§ 132.006 LITTER CAUSED BY TRUCK LOADS.
   No person shall drive or move any truck or other vehicle within the city
unless such vehicle is so constructed or loaded as to present any load,
contents or litter from being blown or deposited upon any street, alley or
other public place.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.060) (Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
132.999
§ 132.007 LITTER IN PARKS.
   No person shall throw or deposit litter in any park within the city, except
in public receptacles and in such amounts that the litter will be prevented
from being carried or deposited by the elements upon any part of the park or
upon any street or other public place.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.070) (Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
132.999
§ 132.008 LITTER IN LAKES AND PONDS.
   No person shall throw or deposit litter in any pond, lake, stream, or any
other body of water in a park or elsewhere within the city or upon any property
controlled or owned by the city.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.080) (Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
132.999
§ 132.009 LITTER ON OCCUPIED PRIVATE PROPERTY; OWNER TO MAINTAIN.
   (A)   No person shall throw or deposit litter on any occupied private
property within the city, whether owned by such person or not; except that, the
owner or person in control of private property may maintain authorized private
receptacles for collection in such a manner that litter will be prevented from
being carried or deposited by the elements upon any street, sidewalk or other
public place or upon any private property.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.100)
   (B)   The owner or person in control of any private property shall at all
times maintain the premises free of litter; provided, however, that, this
division (B) shall not prohibit the storage of litter in authorized private
receptacles for collection.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.110)
(Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
132.999
§ 132.010 LITTER ON VACANT LOTS.
   No person shall throw or deposit litter on any open or vacant property
private or public within the city, whether owned by such person or not.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.120) (Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
132.999
§ 132.011 EXEMPTIONS.
   The provisions of this chapter shall not apply to the distribution of mail
by the United States, nor to newspapers; except that, newspapers shall be
placed on private in such a manner as to prevent their being carried or
deposited by the elements upon any street, sidewalk or other public place or
upon private property.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.090) (Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009)
§ 132.012 CONTAINERS.
   (A)   Adequate required. The occupant of every dwelling unit and every
commercial generator producing solid waste within the corporate limits of the
city shall provide sufficient and adequate containers for the storage of all
solid waste ordinarily accumulated at such place during the intervals between
collection; which containers shall be maintained in good repair.
   (B)   Residential solid waste. Residential solid waste shall be stored in
containers of not less than 20 gallons in nominal capacity. Containers shall be
fitted with a lid and shall be properly covered at all times, except when
depositing waste therein or removing the contents thereof. The container shall
be of sturdy construction. Galvanized metal containers, or rubber, fiberglass
or plastic containers which do not become brittle in cold weather may be used.
Disposable solid waste containers with suitable frames or containers as
approved by the administrative authority may also be used for storage of
residential solid waste. The occupant of every dwelling unit and two-family
dwelling units shall keep the solid waste containers and area surrounding them
in a clean, neat and sanitary condition.
   (C)   Commercial and industrial solid waste. Commercial and industrial solid
waste shall be stored in solid waste containers as approved by the
administrative authority. The containers shall be covered at all times, except
when depositing waste therein or removing the contents thereof. The solid waste
container must be placed on a pad of sufficient strength to hold the weight of
a fully loaded container, with all new solid waste container pads to be
screened. The owners of such premises shall keep the solid waste containers and
the area surrounding them in a clean, neat and sanitary condition at all times
and shall maintain the container screening in good repair.
   (D)   Bulky rubbish and construction and demolition waste. Containers used
for bulky rubbish and construction and demolition waste shall be covered at all
times during transportation. The cover may be constructed of metal, canvas,
vinyl, netting or other material approved by the administrative authority. No
person shall place paper, cardboard, light weight plastic or other solid waste
which has the potential for becoming airborne or any material that has the
potential of becoming leachate in an uncovered bulky rubbish container.
   (E)   Unapproved. Containers other than those authorized by divisions (A),
(B), (C) and (D) above shall be collected together along with their contents
and disposed of.
   (F)   Multi-family dwellings. The owners of all multiple-family dwellings
shall purchase or contract with a licensed solid waste collector for an
adequate container to be placed on the site for disposal of tenants’ solid
waste and contract with a licensed solid waste collector for the collection,
transportation, and disposal of such solid waste pursuant to this chapter. The
solid waste container must be placed on a pad of sufficient strength to hold
the weight of a fully loaded container, with all new solid waste container pads
to be screened. The owners of such premises shall keep the solid waste
containers and the area surrounding them in a clean, neat and sanitary
condition at all times and shall maintain the container screening in good
repair.
   (G)   Storage. Residential solid waste containers shall be stored behind the
dwelling unit and upon the private property of the residential premises.
Commercial solid waste containers shall be stored upon private property, unless
the owner shall have been granted written permission from the city to use
public property for such purposes. The storage site shall be well drained and
fully accessible to collection equipment, public health personnel and fire
inspection personnel.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.130) (Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
132.999
§ 132.013 COLLECTION; LICENSE REQUIRED.
   (A)   (1)   Contract for service. The occupant of every dwelling unit and
two-family dwelling units and the owners of every multiple-family dwelling and
two-family dwelling in the city shall contract or otherwise agree with a
licensed solid waste collector to collect all solid waste generated thereby.
The occupant of every dwelling unit and two-family dwelling units may collect,
transport or haul his, her or their own solid waste to a facility licensed by
the state to receive such solid waste; provided that, such solid waste is
transported or hauled at least once every seven days.
      (2)   Commercial and industrial generator. Each commercial and industrial
generator shall provide for the proper collection of solid waste, and that
service shall be maintained by either the owner with vehicles as required by §
115.008 of this code of ordinances, or by contract with a licensed solid waste
collector.
      (3)   Placement. Solid waste containers, as required by this chapter for
the storage of residential solid waste, shall be place at the curb, street edge
or alley for collection. Any solid waste containers or any other solid waste
permitted by the article to be placed at the curb or street edge for collection
shall not be so placed until the regularly scheduled collection day or evening
before.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.140)
   (B)   No person shall collect, transport or haul over any of the streets or
alleys of the city or dispose of any solid waste accumulated in the city
without first receiving a license issued by the city, except this division (B)
shall not be constructed to apply to individuals collecting, transporting or
hauling their own household solid waste and/or recyclable materials, the Street
Department or other departments of the city, nor to tree trimmers or tree
surgeons; provided, however, that, such persons shall comply with all
regulations provided by this chapter for the transportation of solid waste over
the streets and alleys.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.150)
(Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
132.999
§ 132.014 CLEARING LITTER FROM OPEN PRIVATE PROPERTY BY CITY.
   (A)   Notice to remove. The Codes Officer is the authorized agent of the
city is hereby authorized and empowered to notify the owner of any open or
vacant private property within the city or the agent of such owner to properly
dispose of litter located on such owner’s property. Such notice shall be by
registered mail, addressed to said owner at his or her last known address.
   (B)   Action upon non-compliance. Upon the failure, neglect or refusal of
any owner or agent so notified, to properly dispose of litter under §
132.013 of this chapter within ten days after receipt of written notice
provided for in division (A) above, or within ten days after the date of such
notice in the event the same is returned to the city’s Post Office Department
because of its inability to make delivery thereof; provided, the same was
properly authorized and empowered to pay for the disposing of such litter or to
order its disposal by the city.
   (C)   Charge included in tax bill. When the city has effected the removal of
such litter or has paid for its removal, the actual costs thereof shall be
charged to the owner of such property on the next regular tax bill forwarded to
such owner by the city, and said charge shall be due and payable by said owner
at the time of payment of such tax bill.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.160) (Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009)
§ 132.015 FREQUENCY OF PICKUP.
   All litter must be removed from all private premises at least once every
seven days.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.170) (Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009) Penalty, see §
132.999
§ 132.999 PENALTY.
   Any person violating any of the provisions of this chapter shall be deemed
guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction, shall be punished as provided in
§
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 8.12.180) (Ord. 3370, passed 9-15-2009)
CHAPTER 133: DRUGS AND CONTROLLED SUBSTANCES
Section
Methamphetamine Precursor Drugs
   
133.001   Findings
   
133.002   Definitions
   
133.003   Sale prohibited
   
133.004   Exceptions
   
133.005   Prima facie evidence
   
133.006   Reporting theft
 
   
133.999   Penalty
**** METHAMPHETAMINE PRECURSOR DRUGS ****
§ 133.001 FINDINGS.
   The Commission of the city has found the manufacture, transportation,
possession and sale of methamphetamine to be inherently dangerous and that the
chemical precursors of methamphetamine and the byproducts and wastes of
methamphetamine production are inherently dangerous and injurious to the public
health, safety and welfare of the citizens of the city, regulation of the sale
of the chemical precursors to methamphetamine production, such as ephedrine
products and pseudoephedrine products, is necessary to protect the citizens of
the city.
(Prior Code, § 8.34.010) (Ord. 3431, passed 9-17-2013)
§ 133.002 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   EPHEDRINE. All forms of ephedrine, ephedrine hydrochloride and all
combinations of these chemicals and any methamphetamine precursor drug
containing these chemicals.
   METHAMPHETAMINE PRECURSOR DRUG. Any drug or substance used to manufacture
methamphetamine that contains pseudoephedrine or ephedrine.
   PACKAGE. Any number of pills, tablets, capsules, caplets or individual units
of a substance held within a container intended for sale.
   PERSON. Any individual, corporation, partnership, trust, limited liability
company, firm, association or other entity.
   PSEUDOEPHEDRINE. All forms of pseudoephedrine, pseudoephedrine hydrochloride
and all combinations of these chemicals and any methamphetamine precursor drug
containing these chemicals.
   SELL. To knowingly furnish, give away, exchange, transfer, deliver,
surrender or supply, whether for monetary gain or not.
(Prior Code, § 8.34.020) (Ord. 3431, passed 9-17-2013)
§ 133.003 SALE PROHIBITED.
   It shall be illegal for any person to sell, deliver or distribute ephedrine,
pseudoephedrine and pseudoephedrine or ephedrine containing products, their
salts, their optical isomers or salts of their optical isomers except as set
forth in the specific exceptions contained in §
133.004 of this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 8.34.030) (Ord. 3431, passed 9-17-2013) Penalty, see §
133.999
§ 133.004 EXCEPTIONS.
   (A)   Ephedrine, pseudoephedrine and pseudoephedrine or ephedrine containing
products, their salts, their optical isomers or salts of their optical isomers
may be sold by a Kansas-licensed pharmacist after being authorized to do so by
a written prescription from a physician or other healthcare professional
licensed by the State of Kansas or any other state to write prescriptions.
   (B)   Ephedrine, pseudoephedrine and pseudoephedrine or ephedrine containing
products, their salts, their optical isomers or salts of their optical isomers
may be distributed by a licensed physician within the physician’s office, or
any clinic, nursing home or other licensed healthcare facility upon the orders
of a physician or other healthcare professional licensed by the State of Kansas
or any other state to write prescriptions.
   (C)   This section regulating ephedrine, pseudoephedrine or other
methamphetamine precursor drugs shall not apply to the sale of animal feed
containing ephedrine or dietary supplement products containing naturally
occurring or herbal ephedra or extracts of herbal ephedra.
(Prior Code, § 8.34.040) (Ord. 3431, passed 9-17-2013)
§ 133.005 PRIMA FACIE EVIDENCE.
   It shall be prima facie proof that a substance is regulated by this section
if the substance is contained in its original packaging and is labeled as being
or containing ephedrine or pseudoephedrine.
(Prior Code, § 8.34.050) (Ord. 3431, passed 9-17-2013)
§ 133.006 REPORTING THEFT.
   (A)   All thefts, shortages, disappearances, miscounts or other losses of
ephedrine, pseudoephedrine or other methamphetamine precursor drugs shall be
reported to the Police Department of the city within 24 hours of discovery.
   (B)   Any person selling ephedrine, pseudoephedrine or other methamphetamine
precursor drugs shall report any difference between the quantity of the
aforementioned drugs shipped and the quantity received to the Police Department
of the city within 24 hours of discovery.
(Prior Code, § 8.34.060) (Ord. 3431, passed 9-17-2013)
§ 133.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Every act or omission constituting a violation of any of the
provisions of §§
133.001 through
133.006 of this chapter by any agent or employee of any person shall be deemed
and held to be a act of such person, and said person shall be punishable in the
same manner as if said act or omission had been done or omitted by him or her
or it personally; provided, such an act or omission was within the scope of
employment or the scope of authority of such agent or employee. Each such
violation of §§
133.001 through
133.006 of this chapter shall be considered a separate offense. Violation of §§
133.001 through
133.006 of this chapter shall be considered and punished as a Class A
misdemeanor.
(Prior Code, § 8.34.070) (Ord. 3431, passed 9-17-2013)
