§ 150.006 PLUMBING CODE.
   (A)   Adopted. The following documents, three copies of which are on file in
the office of the City Clerk, marked and designated as “2012 International Code
Council Plumbing Code” are adopted as codes of the city and each and all of the
regulations, provisions, penalties, conditions and terms of said codes on file
in the office of the City Clerk are referred to, adopted and made a part of
this section as if fully set out.
(Prior Code, § 15.12.010)
   (B)   Additions, insertions and changes.
      (1)   Administrative changes in the Uniform Plumbing Code:
         (a)   Add to Section 10.3, “new buildings and construction”; and
         (b)   Delete Section 20.1 and add:
         Whenever the term ‘Administrative Authority’ is used in this code it
shall mean the Plumbing Inspector and/or his authorized representative.
         (a) The authorized representative shall be a person or persons so
designated by the City Manager or the City Codes Officer.
         (b) The Codes Department or the Building Inspection Department of the
City of Fort Scott shall be responsible for the enforcement of this code.
      (2)   Addition to Section 20.2(b):
      It shall be the duty of the City Clerk and/or his authorized
representative to maintain an official register of all persons, firms or
corporations lawfully entitled to carry on or engage in the business of
plumbing or gas fitting work to whom a license has been issued in accordance
with Section 4.5 of this plumbing ordinance.
      (3)   Amend Section 20.7, Fee Schedule, as follows:
         PERMIT FEES   
         1.   New residential buildings   $10.00
         2.   New commercial, public or industrial building with total
evaluation of less than $20,000   $20.00
         Over $20,000 will be $20.00 plus $1.00 per $1,000 or fraction thereof
for total evaluation in excess of $20,000.00.
         Items 3 through 15 remain the same as far as obtaining a permit, but
there is no permit fee for any of these items.
      (4)   Addition to Section 30.1:
         (1)    The term “qualified plumber” shall mean an individual who has
satisfactorily demonstrated to the Plumbing Board his qualifications as a
plumber and holds a valid Plumbing License.
         (2)    A Master Plumber is a person who may conduct, carry on or
engage in the business of performing plumbing work as a Plumbing Contractor and
who otherwise is a “qualified plumber.”
         (3)    A Journeyman Plumber is a person who labors at the trade of
plumbing as an employee under the supervision of a Master Plumber and who
otherwise is a “qualified plumber.”
         (4)    A Gas Fitter is a person licensed to install gas piping in
connection with heating and refrigeration work.
(Prior Code, § 15.12.020)
   (C)   Additions, insertions and changes in the Uniform Plumbing Code related
to general regulations.
      (1)   Addition to Section 401:
      ABS, PVC or any plastic pipe shall not be used as drain waste or vent or
supply in #1 Fire zone, except in residential building less than three stories
in height.
      (2)   Amend Section 1004(a):
      Water pipe and fittings shall be of brass copper, cast iron, galvanized
malleable iron, galvanized wrought iron, galvanized steel, lead, CPVC and
Polybutylene (PB) hot and cold water plastic piping, or other approved
materials. Polyethylene (PE) or Polyvinal Chloride (PVC) water pipe
manufactured to recognized standards may be used for cold water systems.
Plastic pipe of any approved type shall not be used any closer than three (3)
feet to the cold water inlet or the hot water outlet of hot water heaters,
tanks, or boilers. Plastic pipe is to be considered to be combustible and
necessary clearance shall be observed and maintained. All material used in the
water supply systems, except valves and similar devices shall be of like
material, except where otherwise approved by the Plumbing Inspector.
      (3)   Cast iron sewer pipe must be used in all open street, alley or
other public land (minimum size four inches).
      (4)   Delete Section 318-2(e) of the Uniform Plumbing Code.
      (5)   Section 410 of the Uniform Plumbing Code, add the following:
      Subsoil drains or rainwater drains shall not be discharged into the
sanitary sewers.
      (6)   Amend Section 501 of the Uniform Plumbing Code:
      Floor drain (where used as such) need not be vented, provided it is
within 25 feet of a 3 inch stack or horizontal drain which has at least a 3
inch diameter vent extension through the roof.
      (7)   Addition to Section 20.14 of the Uniform Plumbing Code:
      The Board of Appeals and the Plumbing Board shall be one and the same.
      CREATION OF PLUMBING BOARD. There is hereby created and established a
Plumbing Board, which will act as a certificate examining agency for the City
Inspection Division. The board shall be known as the “Plumbing Board.” The
Board shall have the duties, powers, and functions prescribed by this Code for
the purpose of giving examinations and determining facts in connection with the
issuance of Certificates and Competency and License.
      The Board shall consist of three (3) members. One member of said Board
shall be a licensed Master Plumber, a second member shall be a licensed
Journeyman Plumber and the third member shall be the Plumbing Inspector, of the
City of Fort Scott and he shall be responsible for its organization and
operation.
      (8)   Amend Section 20.3, Violations and Penalties:
      Any person, firm or corporation, violating any provision of this Code
shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof, shall be
punishable by a fine, not to exceed five hundred dollars ($500) or by
imprisonment not to exceed one year or by both such fine and imprisonment. Each
such person is guilty of a separate offense for each and every day during any
portion of which any violation of any provision of the ordinances of the City
is committed, continued or permitted by such person, and he is punishable
accordingly. No permit presuming to give authority to violate or cancel the
provisions of this Code shall be valid, except insofar as the work or use which
it authorized is lawful. The issuance or granting of a permit or approval of
plans shall not prevent the Plumbing Inspector from thereafter requiring the
correction of errors in said plans and specifications or from preventing the
construction operations being carried on thereunder when in violation of this
Code or of any certificate of approval when issued in error.
      Every permit issued by the Plumbing Inspector under the provisions of
this Code shall expire by limitations and become null and void, if the work
authorized by such permit is not commenced within one hundred twenty (120)
days. Before such work can be recommenced, a new permit shall be first
obtained, and the fee shall be one-half (1/2) the amount required for a new
permit or such work, provided no changes have been made, or will be made, in
the original plans and specifications of such work; and provided further, that
such suspension or abandonment has not exceeded one (1) year.
      (9)   Addition to Section 3182(e):
         1.    All waterline rough-in inspections will be done under pressure.
         2.    All rough inspections of DWV will be done under pressure to the
housetrap or clean out.
      (10)   Addition to Section 406, Sewer Line Taps:
      All sewer line taps will be on 45 degree angle (with exception made by
Plumbing Inspector).
      (11)   Addition to Section 406-5:
      Housetraps or clean outs are mandatory in new plumbing installations.
They shall be within 3 feet of outside of building.
      (12)   Addition to Section 20.4(d):
      No permit shall be issued to any person to do or cause to be done any
plumbing, drainage, or gas fittings work regulated by this code, unless such
person holds a valid unexpired Master Plumbers license as herein required or
gas fitting license.
      EXCEPTION: Any permit required by this Code may be issued to any
unlicensed uncertified person to do any work regulated by this Code on a
dwelling used exclusively for his living purposes, including the usual
accessory buildings, existing or in the process of being newly constructed, in
the event that such person certifies that he is, or is to be, the bona fide
owner of such dwelling and accessory buildings, and that the same are occupied,
or are to be occupied, by said owner, provided that said owner shall personally
purchase all material to be used and shall personally perform all labor in
connection therewith. Further, all such plumbing work performed by such
unlicensed, uncertified person shall be limited to only that work on his own
private property and shall specifically exclude plumbing work from the house
side of the water or gas meter to the main and that work on the building sewer
from the sewer easement line to the public sewer.
      (13)   Amend Section 4.3, Bond Required:
      No plumber’s license shall be issued until the applicant therefor shall
have filed an acceptable surety bond in favor of the City of Fort Scott,
Kansas, in the sum of one thousand dollars (1,000.00). The condition of the
said bond be that if the principal named in the bond shall faithfully comply
with all the provisions of the said No. 2803 as amended, and the model code
enacted thereby, then the bond shall be null and void, but if otherwise, then
in full force and effect.
(Prior Code, § 15.12.030)
   (D)   New sections enacted. The following sections are enacted and adopted
as an addition to the Uniform Plumbing Code, 1979 Edition:
      A.    Section 4.1, APPOINTMENT OF MEMBERS TO PLUMBING BOARD
      The members of the Plumbing Board shall be appointed by the Mayor with
the approval of the City Commission to serve a term of two (2) calendar years
and may be reappointed. The members of the Board shall be persons of good
reputation with at least three (3) years experience in their respective field
and shall be residents of the City of Fort Scott. The members of the Board may
be removed by the Mayor for just cause, upon written notice.
      B.    Section 4.2, DUTIES OF PLUMBING BOARD
      The Plumbing Board shall organize by electing one of its regular members
to preside as president. The Board shall establish rules governing
prerequisites of applicants applying for plumbing examinations and for the
issuance licenses. The Board shall conduct examinations of applicants once each
month, unless no applicants are available for examination. The Plumbing Board
shall be responsible for the examination of applicants for the following
licenses:
      (a)    Master Plumber License
      (b)    Journeyman Plumber License
      (c)    Gas Fitter License
      C.    Section 4.3, EXAMINATION STANDARDS OF PLUMBING BOARD
      The Plumbing Board shall set the examination standards to be followed.
The standards shall be consistent with the purpose of the Plumbing Code, which
is for the protection of the public health, safety and welfare of the people of
the City so that those who are recommended to be certified under the Plumbing
Code are qualified in terms of their skills, knowledge, practical experience
knowledge of pertinent law to supervise or perform the work for which they may
be certified. The Board shall set standards and examine applicants for
Certificates of Competency in the following areas:
      (a)    Applicable technical portions of the Plumbing Code.
      (b)    Related technical knowledge.
      (c)    Related skills.
      (d)    Education.
      (e)    Experience (4 yr. for Master Plumber, 2 yr. for Journeyman Plumber
and 1 yr. for Gas Fitter). Periodic review shall be made to maintain the
standards current with the Plumbing Code and building practices.
      D.    Section 4.4, EXAMINATION AND FILING FEE.
      The Plumbing Board shall, immediately after the appointment of its
members, meet and designate the time and place for the examination of all
applicants desiring to do plumbing work or gas fitting within the city. Every
person wishing to be the holder of a Plumber’s or Gas Fitter license shall file
with the Plumbing Board his request for an examination. A filing fee in the
amount of Ten ($10.00) dollars shall be paid at the time of filing of the
application and all such filing fees shall not be subject to refund in the
event that an applicant fails to pass the examination. In the event an
applicant fails to pass the examination, he may be given two (2) re-
examinations without the payment of an additional filing fee if such
examinations are given within six (6) months after the first examination.
      E.    Section 4.5, CERTIFICATE OF COMPETENCY
      If, after examination, the Plumbing Board is satisfied as to the
competency of the applicant, the Board shall issue a Certificate of Competency
or License for either a Journeyman, Master Plumber, or Gas Fitter to such
applicant, authorizing him to engage in or work ax the business of plumbing or
gas fining. All holders of a Plumber or Gas Fitter License shall within ten
(10) days after the receipt thereof and/or by December 31 of each year
thereafter, annually present the same to the City Clerk and pay an annual
registration fee of Thirty Dollars ($30) for Master Plumber License, Twenty
Dollars ($20) for Journeyman License, and Ten Dollars ($10) for Gas Fitter
License. Out of town contractors shall be issued temporary licenses for one
specified job with the cost of the temporary license being One Hundred Dollars
($100) for Master License and Twenty-five Dollars ($25) for Journeyman License.
The City Clerk shall keep an annual register of all licensed plumbers or gas
fitters. Failure to annually register as herein required shall be grounds for
revocation of the license by the Plumbing Board. Any person whose license has
been revoked must be re-examined before being issued a new license.
      F.    Section 4.6, GAS FITTER LICENSE
      To be issued on passing of gas fitting test, a license to heating and air
conditioning contractors for the installation of gas piping for heating or
refrigeration equipment only.
      G.    Section 4.7, GAS FITTER OR PLUMBER VIOLATION
      Plumbers or Gas Fitters found guilty of two violations of plumbing code
may have his license revoked and have to be re-examined under the Uniform
Plumbing Code.
      H.    Section 4.8, APPRENTICE
      An apprentice must work under the supervision of a Master or Journeyman
Plumber at all times. A licensed Journeyman Plumber may do all work under
limited supervision of a Master Plumber.
(Prior Code, § 15.12.040)

§ 150.020 ADOPTED.
   The following documents, three copies of which are on file in the office of
the City Clerk, marked and designated as “2011 National Electrical Code” are
adopted as codes of the city and each and all of the regulations, provisions,
penalties, conditions and terms of said codes on file in the office of the City
Clerk are referred to, adopted and made a part of this section as if fully set
out in this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.010) (Ord. 2962, passed - -1987; Ord. 3026, passed - -
1991; Ord. 3119, passed - -1995; Ord. 3145, passed - -1997; Ord. 3336, passed -
-2008; Ord. 3508, passed 5-16-2017)
§ 150.021 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   ALTERATIONS AND ADDITIONS. The installation of new wiring for additional
switches, receptacles, light fixtures and permanent attached appliances. The
replacement of existing wiring, service panels, subpanels and service drops.
   APPRENTICE ELECTRICIAN. Any unlicensed person working in the electrical
trade under the direct supervision of a master or journeyman electrician.
   ELECTRICAL WIRING. The installations, repair, connection, alteration or
replacement of wiring in buildings and structures for the use of electrical
current for electrical fixtures, appliances, motors or other apparatus.
   MASTER ELECTRICIAN. A person who holds a legal certificate showing that he
or she is qualified, certified and equipped to properly lay out, plan and make
any installation, repair or maintenance of electrical wiring, appliances,
fixtures, motors and other apparatus requiring electrical current.
   JOURNEYMAN ELECTRICIAN. Any person who holds a legal certificate showing
that he is qualified to do electrical wiring under the limited supervision of a
master electrician.
   NON-RESIDENT. Person or firm that does not have a Fort Scott street or rural
route address or that is not employed by a firm with a street or rural route
address in the city.
   SENIOR JOURNEYMAN. A person who holds a legal certificate showing that he or
she is qualified, certified and equipped to properly layout, plan and make any
installation, repair or maintenance of electrical wiring appliances, fixtures,
motors and other apparatus requiring electrical current in one- and two-family
dwellings only.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.020) (Ord. 2962, passed - -1987)
§ 150.022 BOARD OF ELECTRICAL EXAMINERS OF ELECTRICIANS; MEETINGS.
   (A)   There is created a Board of Electrical Examiners to pass upon the
qualifications of all persons doing electrical work or wiring in the city. The
Board shall consist of seven members and with the Electrical Inspector serving
as an ex officio member, voting only in case of a tie vote. The six members of
the Board were appointed by the governing body from available certified master
and journeyman electricians, with members being non-certified. A certified
electrician may serve a maximum of two consecutive terms. The governing body
initially appointed two members for a three-year term, two members for a two-
year term and two members for a one-year term. Any members appointed hereafter
shall be for a full three-year term.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.030)
   (B)   It shall be the duty of the members of the Board of Examiners under
such rules and regulations as they shall prescribe, to hold meetings of the
Board once each month and in such places as may be fixed by the Board for the
purpose of passing upon the qualifications of parties doing the electric wiring
and electrical work within the city. The Electrical Inspector of the city shall
serve as the Secretary of the Board.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.040)
(Ord. 2962, passed - -1987)
§ 150.023 LICENSE APPLICATION.
   (A)   For any licenses referred to in this subchapter, the applicants should
make application to the Electrical Inspector, in writing, setting out such
information as to their experience at the electrical trade and such other
information as may be required by the rules and regulations of the Board. The
information and application form, as approved by the Board of Examiners, shall
be furnished to the applicant at no charge.
   (B)   The Board shall conduct examinations with regard to practical
knowledge of installation of residential, commercial and industrial wiring and
operation of electrical energy on the part of all applicants. Journeyman
applicants must submit evidence that the applicant has had three years’
practical electrical construction experience of residential, commercial and/or
industrial wiring, or two years’ college (electrical) and/or industrial wiring,
or two years’ trade school and one year of practical electrical construction
experience of residential, commercial and/or industrial wiring. Senior
journeyman applicants must submit evidence that the applicant has had one year
of practical electrical construction experience in residential wiring as a
licensed journeyman. Applicants for masters licenses must have two years as
licensed journeyman.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.050) (Ord. 2962, passed - -1987)
§ 150.024 ELECTRICAL WORK PERMIT.
   (A)   Every person, firm or corporation before entering upon any
construction or erection of any electric light or power wiring (except
community antenna television equipment, cable, powering or ancillary services),
in any building in the city, shall make application to the Electrical Inspector
or his or her designate, for an electrical permit describing the proposed work
before proceeding therewith.
   (B)   Permits and inspections shall be required for all repair work or
alterations to any original installation, if the repair is being made because
of an electrical fire or fire damage or as a result of the alterations,
additions, any new circuits must be added to main service panel or subpanel;
except permits and inspections shall not be required for minor repair work,
such as replacement of defective switches, receptacles, tight fixtures, fuses
or breakers.
   (C)   Permits are required on any inspections required by public utility
company, except temporary service hookups.
   (D)   Permits will be issued in the name of master electricians or master
and firm, senior journeyman (for residential only), in the city meeting all the
requirements set forth in this subchapter. Any permit required by this
subchapter may be issued to any person to do any work regulated by this
subchapter in a single-family dwelling used   for living purposes, including
the usual accessory buildings and quarters, and that the same are occupied by
the owner; provided that, said owner shall personally purchase all materials
and shall personally perform all labor in connection therewith, with the
exception of commercial and industrial type buildings.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.060) (Ord. 2962, passed - -1987)
§ 150.025 ELECTRICAL CONTRACTORS AND ELECTRICIANS LICENSE; APPLICATION;
EXAMINATION; FEES; NON-RESIDENT LICENSES.
   (A)   Generally.
      (1)   It shall be unlawful for any person, firm, copartnership or
corporation to engage in business or the trade or occupation of an electrician
without first securing a license from the city.
      (2)   All persons, firms, co-partnerships or corporations who or which
undertake with or for another to do any electrical wiring or other work for
which a permit is required under the Electrical Code or regulations of the city
for a fixed price, fee, percentage of cost or other compensation or who or
which advertises or otherwise represents to the public to have the capacity or
ability to undertake the installation, alteration or renewal of electric
wiring, lighting or power fixture and electrical apparatus of any nature
(except measuring equipment and devices installed by and remaining the property
of the utility company supplying the service), (except cable television
distribution equipment; cable, powering or ancillary service installed by and
remaining the property of community antenna television company supplying the
service) in or on any building structure, sign or premises within the city
shall for the purposes of this subchapter be required to obtain appropriate
license.
      (3)   A master license shall be issued in the name of the person and firm
so engaging in business upon payment of an annual fee in the amount of $30 and
for successive years upon payment of a renewal fee of $30 each year thereafter.
      (4)   Senior journeyman license shall be issued in the name of the person
so engaging in business upon payment of an annual fee in the amount of $25 and
for successive years upon payment of a renewal fee of $25 each.
      (5)   A journeyman license shall be issued in the name of the person and
firm so engaging in business upon payment of an annual fee in the amount of $20
and for successive years upon payment of a renewal fee of $20 each year
thereafter.
      (6)   Payments for such licenses shall be made payable to the city
through the City Clerk’s office.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.070)
   (B)   License applicant; examination. Every person whether an employee or
employer before engaging in electric wiring and electrical work in the city
shall apply to the Board of Examiners of Electricians and pass an examination
as to the applicant’s competency as an electrician and, if on examination,
applicant is found competent to do electrical wiring and electrical work, the
Board shall issue a certificate of examination to such applicant authorizing
him or her to engage in electrical wiring and electrical work in the city. Such
certificate shall remain in force until revoked or cancelled by the Board of
Examiners, but such certificate shall not be transferable. The fee for
examination of each applicant shall be $10, such fee to be paid to the city
clerk at the time the applicant applies for such examination and shall be
retained by the city whether or not the applicant passes the examination. The
examination shall consist of questions, examples, demonstrations designed to
show the extent of the applicant’s knowledge concerning the detailed provisions
of the city’s Electrical Wiring Code and the National Electrical Code, the
practical experience in electrical work which the applicant has had and the
degree of proficiency which he or she has attained. A grade of 75% out of 100%
as perfect, shall be necessary for the applicant to pass. If the applicant is
rejected, he or she shall not be eligible for reexamination until after the
period of 30 days, then it shall be three months. If the Board is satisfied as
to the competency of the applicant based upon a demonstration of ability by
written test, practical wiring test and vouchers from two master the
retirements practical electrical construction experience (in § 150.022 of this
chapter) may be waived by the Board of Electricians.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.080)
   (C)   Electrician’s license fees.
      (1)   (a)   All licenses issued by virtue of this section shall expire on
December 31 in the year of issuance and each year thereafter.
         (b)   The full amount of the required fee shall be paid at the time of
issuance of the license irrespective of the date of issuance and without
proration.
         (c)   Each license shall be renewable within 30 days before expiration
and, if not so renewed, shall, at the end of such time, be null and void and
not subject to renewal without retesting.
      (2)   Licenses issued hereunder shall not be transferable, and it shall
be unlawful for any person, firm or corporation to allow to permit his, her or
its license to be used by another for the purpose of securing any permit
required by the electrical ordinance of the city.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.090)
   (D)   Non-resident electrician’s licenses. The Board of Electrical Examiners
will not issue a license without an examination to a non-resident master or
journeyman electrician, unless the Board has a reciprocal agreement with a city
in Kansas in which the applicant is currently licensed and has passed a written
examination from that city; or, provided further that, if the Board is
satisfied to the competency of the applicant, a license may be issued.
      (1)   Non-resident master or journeyman electrical licenses will be
issued for specified jobs only. The master license fee will be $100 per job and
the journeyman license fee will be $25 per job.
      (2)   Licenses will expire at the end of the specified job or 365 days,
whichever comes first.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.100)
(Ord. 2962, passed - -1987; Ord. 3052, passed - -1992)
§ 150.026 BOND; INSURANCE.
   In addition to the license fee and before the license is issued, the person,
firm or corporation making application for a master license or senior
journeyman license shall have in effect on file with the City Clerk a surety
bond in favor of the city, in the sum of $1,000 approved by the Board of City
Commissioners and the City Attorney and conditional that the principal in said
bond will comply with all provisions of all ordinances of the city relating to
the construction, erection or installation of wire or other apparatus and
mediums for the transmission of electric current for heat, power or light
purposes and, upon further condition, that the city, or any person, firm or
corporation who deems himself, herself or itself injured or damaged by the
principal’s failure to comply with such ordinances, may sue on such bond to
recover his, her or its damage. All persons holding a master license or senior
journeyman license in the city must be covered by a $1,000 bond either by
individual bond or their names listed on the shop’s or firm’s bond.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.110) (Ord. 2962, passed - -1987)
§ 150.027 ELECTRICAL INSPECTOR; APPOINTMENT; DUTIES; AUTHORITY.
   (A)   The City Manager shall appoint an Electrical Inspector and may appoint
assistant inspectors, when necessary. The Electrical Inspector shall not engage
in the electrical business, either directly or indirectly, in the city while
holding the Office of Electrical Inspector.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.120)
   (B)   (1)   The Electrical Inspector shall keep complete records of all
permits issued, inspections made and certificates issued and all other official
work performed under the provisions of this subchapter. All necessary blanks
and record books shall be provided by the city.
      (2)   The Electrical Inspector or his or her authorized assistant shall
inspect all electric light, heat and power wires, fixtures, appliances
conductors and apparatus hereinafter installed in or upon any buildings, shops,
sheds and other structures in the city and all such installations shall be made
in accordance with the provisions of this subchapter, and he or she shall
enforce or cause to be enforced such provisions of this subchapter and, for
that purpose, he or she shall have and is given special police powers necessary
therefor. He or she shall have the right, during reasonable hours, to enter any
building for the purpose of making any inspection required by this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.130)
   (C)   The Electrical Inspector or any firefighter shall have power to at
once cause removal of all wires, or the turning off all electricity where the
same shall interfere with the work of the Fire Department during the progress
of a fire.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.140)
(Ord. 2962, passed - -1987)
§ 150.028 INSPECTIONS; REQUESTS.
   (A)   The Electrical Inspector or his or her assistant shall inspect all
installations of electric wiring, fixtures and other equipment as required by
this subchapter within 24 hours (not including Saturdays, Sundays or legal
holidays) of the receipt of notice that same is ready for inspection. No worker
shall lath, seal or in any manner conceal any electric wiring until such
inspection shall have been made and the installation approved. It shall be the
duty of the person installing electric wiring, fixtures, appliances and
apparatus to promptly notify the Electrical Inspector when the installation is
ready for inspection. Final inspections are required after all electric wiring,
fixtures, appliances and apparatus are in place.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.150)
   (B)   Upon completion of any work in which a permit has been issued under
the provisions of this subchapter and, from time to time, as may be necessary,
it shall be the duty of the person installing same to notify the Electrical
Inspector after which work shall be inspected promptly as provided herein. If
such work shall be found to comply with all the provisions of this subchapter,
the Electrical Inspector shall then issue a certificate of satisfactory
inspection. No electric current shall be turned on, except for foe purpose of
testing, or temporary lighting while the building is under construction, until
such certificate shall be issued. No electric light or power wires or equipment
shall be covered or concealed until it shall have been inspected and approved
by the Electrical Inspector, and said Inspector shall have the authority to
remove or cause to be removed, any lath, plaster or other finish which may
interfere with the proper inspection thereof.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.160)
(Ord. 2962, passed - -1987)
§ 150.029 DEFECTIVE WIRING.
   In case any work which the Electrical Inspector is required to inspect by
the provisions of this subchapter shall be found not to comply with the
requirements hereof, and to be unsafe and defective, he or she shall at once
notify the party or parties doing such work of such defect, and order him, her
or them to change, rearrange or remove the same and upon the party’s or
parties’ failure or refusal to do so within a reasonable time, such party or
parties shall be deemed guilty of a violation of the provisions of this
subchapter, and each and every day which shall elapse after the expiration of
such reasonable time as the same shall be fixed by the Electrical Inspector
without a compliance with such order, shall be considered a separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.170) (Ord. 2962, passed - -1987)
§ 150.030 ELECTRICAL WORK AND INSTALLATION; RULES.
   When performing electrical work or installation within the city, it is
necessary to comply with the following regulations.
   (A)   The only application of aluminum is service entrance cable No. 2 or
larger.
   (B)   Service disconnect must be at meter can, if more than eight feet of
wire is required from meter to service panel.
   (C)   Expired license is subject to retest.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.180) (Ord. 2962, passed - -1987)
§ 150.031 APPEAL BOARD.
   The Electrical Inspector shall decide all questions not provided for in this
subchapter pertaining to the installation or use of electric wires, appliances
or apparatus, appeal may be had for order or decision of the Electrical
Inspector to the Board of Examiners. The decision of the Board of Examiners
shall be final and binding and no appeal shall be had from any order or
decision of the Electrical Inspector unless the same be taken within 30 days
from date of such order or decision.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.190) (Ord. 2962, passed - -1987)
§ 150.032 LIABILITY.
   Nothing in this subchapter shall, in any way, relieve any electrician from
responsibility for materials he or she furnishes or installs, or impair in any
way his or her liability for failure to use due care in protecting life and
property in the subsequent use of electrical installations made by him or her;
nor shall the city be held as assuming liability by reason of any inspection
authorized herein or certificate issued.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.200) (Ord. 2962, passed - -1987)
§ 150.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to § 10.99 of this code of
ordinances.
   (B)   (1)   Any person, firm or corporation violating any provision of §§
150.020 through
150.032 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction thereof, shall be punishable by a fine, not to exceed $500, or by
imprisonment not to exceed one year, or by both such fine and imprisonment.
Each such person is guilty of a separate offense for each and every day during
any portion of which any violation of any provision of the ordinances of the
city is committed, continued or permitted by such person, and he or she is
punishable accordingly. No permit presuming to give authority to violate or
cancel the provisions of §§
150.020 through
150.032 of this chapter shall be valid, except insofar as the work or use which
it authorized is lawful.
      (2)   The issuance or granting of a permit or approval of plans shall not
prevent the Electrical Inspector from thereafter requiring the correction of
errors in the plans and specifications or from preventing the construction
operations being carried on thereunder when in violation of §§
150.020 through
150.032 of this chapter or of any certificate of approval when issued in error.
      (3)   Every permit issued by the Electrical Inspector under the
provisions of §§
150.020 through
150.032 of this chapter shall expire by limitations and become null and void,
if the work authorized by such permit is not commenced within 120 days. Before
such work can be recommenced, a new permit shall be first obtained, and the fee
shall be one-half the amount required for a permit for such work; provided, no
changes have been made, or will be made, in the original plans and
specifications of such work; and, provided further that, such suspension or
abandonment has not exceeded one year.
      (4)   Any person convicted of two violations of any part of §§
150.020 through
150.032 of this chapter may have his, her or their license revoked by the Board
of Examiners or be required to be retested or both.
(Prior Code, § 15.16.210) (Ord. 2962, passed - -1987)
CHAPTER 151: BUILDING REGULATIONS; CONSTRUCTION
Section
   
151.001   Moving buildings
   
151.002   Dangerous structures
 
   
151.999   Penalty
§ 151.001 MOVING BUILDINGS.
   (A)   Permit required; contents. No person or corporation shall be permitted
to move any house, shanty or building through the streets or alleys of the city
without first having obtained a permit from the Chief of the Fire Department,
which permit shall state the conditions to be complied with in moving such
building and shall prescribe the route to be taken and limit the time for
removal.
(Prior Code, § 15.24.010)
   (B)   Permit issuance; bond required.
      (1)   Before any permit, as provided for under division (A) above, shall
issue, the applicant shall first take out a license as provided for under §
5.04.030 and shall file with the Chief of the Fire Department a written
application, said application to be filed 48 hours before any permit is issued,
and shall designate the particular building to be moved, giving its present
location and the place to which said building is to be moved; provided that,
before said permit shall issue, the applicant shall file with the City Clerk a
good and sufficient bond in the sum of $500, to be approved by the City Clerk,
conditioned among other things that the applicant will pay any and all damages
which may be caused to any property, either public or private, within the city
where such injury or damage is inflicted by the applicant, his or her agent,
employees or workmen; and conditioned also that the party will save and
indemnify and keep harmless the city against all liabilities and will, in all
things, strictly comply with the conditions of his or her permit.
      (2)   Upon the filing of the application and the execution and acceptance
of the bond, the permit shall issue.
(Prior Code, § 15.24.020)
(Ord. 1080, passed - -1908) Penalty, see §
151.999
§ 151.002 DANGEROUS STRUCTURES.
   (A)   Enforcing officer; duties generally. The Chief of the Fire Department
is designated as the enforcing officer of the city and charged with the
administration of the provisions of K.S.A. 12-1750 et seq.
(Prior Code, § 15.28.010)
   (B)   Provisions to be cumulative and supplemental. This section shall be
cumulative of any other ordinance relating to the subject of this section and
shall be supplemental thereto.
(Prior Code, § 15.28.020)
(Ord. 2301, passed - -1963)
§ 151.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person violating any of the provisions of §
151.001 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 15.24.030) (Ord. 1080, passed - -1908)
