Any deviation from this list must be with prior approval of the Planning
Commission:
   (A)   Residential horizontal aluminum lap siding;
   (B)   Residential horizontal vinyl lap siding;
   (C)   Cedar or other wood siding;
   (D)   Wood grain, weather-resistant, pressboard siding;
   (E)   Stucco siding; and
   (F)   Brick or stone siding.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.250) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971; Ord. 2783, passed - -;
Ord. 2906, passed - -1985)
§ 153.028 ROOFING MATERIALS.
   The following roofing materials are approved for usage on manufactured
homes. Any deviation from this list must be with prior approval of the Planning
Commission:
   (A)   Asbestos shingles on a roof pitched according to the design
specifications of the shingles;
   (B)   Fiberglass shingles on a roof pitched according to the design
specifications of the shingles;
   (C)   Shake shingles on a roof pitched according to the design
specifications of the shingles;
   (D)   Asphalt shingles on a roof pitched according to the design
specifications of the shingles; and
   (E)   Tile materials on a roof pitched according to the design
specifications of the materials.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.260) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971; Ord. 2783, passed - -;
Ord. 2906, passed - -1985)
**** PERMANENT PLACEMENT OF MANUFACTURED HOMES ****
§ 153.040 TITLE.
   This subchapter shall be known as the “Standard for the Permanent
Installation of Manufactured Homes”, may be cited as such, and will be referred
to herein as “this standard” or “this subchapter”.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.270) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971; Ord. 2783, passed - -;
Ord. 2906, passed - -1985)
§ 153.041 PURPOSE.
   The purpose of this standard is to provide minimum standards to safeguard
life or limb, health, property and public welfare in the city by regulating and
controlling the design, construction and quality of materials for permanent
foundation systems and building service equipment connections which are
necessary to provide for the permanent installation of manufactured homes.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.280) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971; Ord. 2783, passed - -;
Ord. 2906, passed - -1985)
§ 153.042 SCOPE.
   (A)   The provisions of this standard shall apply to the following:
      (1)   Construction of any foundation and anchorage system provided for
the permanent installation of manufactured homes that are constructed in a
factory and which may be designed either to be towed on their own chassis or be
site-delivered by alternative means; and
      (2)   Connection of the building service equipment which is necessary for
the permanent use of manufactured homes.
   (B)   This standard shall not be applicable to the design and construction
of manufactured homes nor shall it be deemed to authorize either modifications
or additions to manufactured homes where otherwise prohibited. This standard
shall not be construed as relieving the installer of a manufactured home of
responsibility for compliance with any other municipal ordinances, codes and
appropriate regulations, or manufacturer’s instructions for permanent
installation.
   (C)   The scope of this standard is limited to the installation of
permanently sited manufactured homes intended to be used as a permanent
dwelling not located in a manufactured home community (mobile home park).
(Prior Code, § 16.08.290) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971; Ord. 2783, passed - -;
Ord. 2906, passed - -1985)
§ 153.043 DEFINITIONS.
   (A)   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall
apply unless the context clearly requires a different meaning. For the purpose
of this standard, certain abbreviations, terms, phrases, words and their
derivatives shall be construed as specified in this subchapter. Words used in
the singular include the plural and the plural the singular. Words used in the
masculine gender include the feminine, and the feminine the masculine.
   (B)   Where the terms are not defined, they shall have their ordinary
accepted meanings within the context with which they are used. “Webster’s Third
New International Dictionary of the English Language”, Unabridged, copyright
1981, shall be considered as providing ordinary accepted meanings.
      ADD-A-ROOM. A unit of manufactured housing not designed as a part of the
original structure which is constructed to code requirements and is not a
manufactured home. It is not to be considered an accessory structure.
      ANCHORING SYSTEMS. A combination of ties, anchoring equipment and/or
anchoring devices that are designed to resist overturning, uplift and lateral
movement of the manufactured home from wind forces.
      APPROVED. Acceptable to the authority having jurisdiction, by reason of
code compliance, investigation, industry standards or testing by nationally
recognized organizations.
      APPROVED AGENCY. An agency approved by the Building Official which is
regularly engaged in conducting tests or furnishing inspection services.
      AUTHORITY HAVING JURISDICTION. The organization or office responsible for
approving an installation or procedure.
      BUILDING CODE. The Building Code as adopted by the municipality.
      BUILDING OFFICIAL. The officer or other designated authority charged with
the administration and enforcement of this standard, or his or her duly
authorized representative.
      BUILDING SERVICE EQUIPMENT. The plumbing, mechanical or electrical
equipment, including piping, wiring fixtures and other accessories, which
provide sanitation, lighting, heating, ventilation or cooling essential for the
habitable occupancy of the building or structure for its designated use and
occupancy.
      CAP. An insert of concrete, metal or decay resisting wood placed between
the top of the pier and the bottom of a floor beam for leveling purposes, and
may include leveling spacers.
      CODE ENFORCEMENT AGENCY. The agency which is charged with the
responsibility of administering this standard.
      DIAGONAL TIE. A tie intended to primarily resist lateral or shear forces
and which may secondarily resist uplift and overturning forces. DIAGONAL TIES
shall be placed along the main frame.
      DWELLING. One or more habitable rooms which are designed to be primarily
occupied by one family with facilities for living, sleeping, cooking, eating
and sanitation.
      ELECTRICAL CODE. The Electrical Code as adopted by the municipality.
      FOOTING. The portion of the foundation of a structure which spreads and
transmits loads directly to the soil or to bearing piles at a depth below the
established frost line without exceeding the safe bearing capacity of the
supporting soil.
      FOUNDATION SYSTEM. See MANUFACTURED HOME FOUNDATION.
      JURISDICTION. The incorporated area of the municipality, together with
any extraterritorial jurisdiction as may be granted through an
intergovernmental corporation agreement.
      LISTED and LISTING. Equipment and materials published in a listing by an
approved testing agency, which is qualified and equipped for experimental
testing, which maintains an adequate periodic inspection of current productions
and whose listing states that the equipment complies with recognized safety
standards.
      MAIN FRAME. The primary structural component on which the body of the
manufactured home is mounted.
      MANUFACTURED HOME. A dwelling which is fabricated in one or more modules
at a location other than the home site, by assembly-line type production
techniques or by other construction methods unique to an off-site manufacturing
process. Every module shall bear a label certifying that is built in compliance
with the Federal Manufactured Home Construction and Safety Standards. For
mobile homes built prior to 6-15-1976, a label certifying compliance to the
Standard for Mobile Homes, NFPA 501 ANSI 119.1, in effect at the time of
manufacture is required. For the purpose of this document, a “mobile home”
shall be considered a MANUFACTURED HOME. A MANUFACTURED HOME is designed to be
towed on its own chassis or be site-delivered by alternative means.
      MANUFACTURED HOME CONSTRUCTION AND SAFETY STANDARDS. A standard for the
construction, design and performance of a manufactured home. This standard is
set forth in 24 C.F.R. parts 3280, 3282 and 3283 and 42 U.S.C. §§ 5401 et seq.,
as mandated in the United States of America and as administrated by the United
States Department of Housing and Urban Development.
      MANUFACTURED HOME FOUNDATION. A site-built or assembled system of
stabilizing devices which:
         (a)   Is capable of transferring design dead loads and live loads
established by the Building Code, and other required design loads, such as
special site conditions, wind and water site conditions, that are imposed by or
upon the structure, into the underlying soil and/or bedrock without failure and
without exceeding the safe bearing capacity of the supporting soil;
         (b)   In frost-susceptible areas, has its lower surface placed at or
below the frost line or which is otherwise adequately insulated/protected to
prevent frost movement;
         (c)   Is constructed of materials acceptable to the authority having
jurisdiction; and
         (d)   Meets the requirements and standards of MPS 4900.1, 1982
Edition, and MPS 4930.1, 1973 Edition, as published by the U.S. Department of
Housing and Urban Development, as amended.
      MANUFACTURED HOME PERMANENT INSTALLATION. The construction which is
required for the permanent installation of a manufactured home, including
construction of the foundation system and required structural connection of on-
site water, gas, electrical and sewer systems, which are necessary for the
normal operation of the manufactured home.
      MECHANICAL CODE. The adopted Mechanical Code of the municipality.
      MOBILE HOME. A movable or portable dwelling, constructed to be towed on
its own chassis and which is capable of connection to utilities for year-round
occupancy. It can consist of one or more components that can be retracted for
delivery purposes and subsequently expanded for additional capacity, or it can
consist of two or more units separately deliverable, but designed to be joined
onto one integral unit. It shall have been built prior to the enactment of the
National Mobile Home Construction and Safety Standards Act of 1974, which
became effective for all mobile home construction on 6-15-1976.
      OCCUPIED SPACE. The total area horizontally covered by the structure in
its occupied configuration, excluding accessory structures, such as, but not
limited to, garages, patios and porches.
      PERIMETER ENCLOSURE. A perimeter enclosure system which completely
encloses the space between the floor of the home and the ground.
      PIER. The portion of the support system between the footing and the
manufactured home structure exclusive of caps and leveling spacers, but not
including foundation walls.
      PLUMBING CODE. The adopted Plumbing Code of the municipality.
      SHALL. As used in this standard, it is mandatory.
      SKIRTING, FOUNDATION. A type of wainscoting constructed of fire and
weather-resistant material, such as aluminum, asbestos board, treated pressed
wood or other approved materials, enclosing the entire undercarriage of a
manufactured home in its sited configuration.
      STABILIZING DEVICES. Any of the components of the anchoring and support
systems such as piers, footings, foundation walls, ties, anchoring equipment,
anchoring devices and any other equipment which supports the manufactured home
and secures it in its sited position.
      STABILIZING SYSTEM. The combination of the anchoring system and the
support system when properly installed.
      STRUCTURE. That which is built or constructed, an edifice or building of
any kind, or any piece of work artificially built up or composed of parts
joined together in some definite manner.
      TIE. Straps, cables or securing devices used to connect the manufactured
home to anchoring devices.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.300) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971; Ord. 2783, passed - -;
Ord. 2906, passed - -1985)
§ 153.044 DESIGN AND CONSTRUCTION.
   (A)   General standards. Manufactured homes required to be installed onto a
permanent foundation shall be designed and constructed to sustain all vertical
and horizontal loads as specified in this standard. The permanent foundations
shall also be required to transfer any other imposed forces from the
manufactured home to undisturbed ground below the established frost line,
without failure and exceeding the safe bearing capacity of the supporting soil.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.310)
   (B)   Dead and live loads. The foundation system shall be designed and
constructed to sustain all dead and live loads imposed thereon. The dead load
used in the design shall be the actual weight of the manufactured home unit to
be installed. The live load, including wind and uplift and snow loads used in
the design, shall be those set forth in the Building Code for single-family
dwellings. Uplift due to overturning forces shall be adequately resisted by the
anchoring system.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.320)
   (C)   Combined loads. For the purpose of determining stresses, all vertical
design loads, including roof live loads, shall be considered as acting
simultaneously. In computing the effect of wind force in combination with
vertical loads, roof live loads need not be included; except that, where snow
loading is required in the design of the roof, at least 50% of such snow load
shall be considered acting in combination with the wind load.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.330)
   (D)   Stress increases. All allowable stress and soil-bearing values may tie
increased one-third when considering wind forces. No increase will be allowed
for vertical loads acting alone.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.340)
   (E)   Rationality. Any system or method of construction that is used shall
conform to a rational analysis in accordance with well established engineering
principles.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.350)
   (F)   Footings and foundations.
      (1)   When requested by the Building Official, soil bearing capacities at
each building site shall be determined. The Building Official may require that
the determination be made by a professional engineer or architect.
      (2)   Footings and foundations, unless otherwise specifically provided,
shall be constructed of masonry, concrete or treated wood, in conformance with
the Building Code. The footings shall rest on undisturbed soil or engineered
fill at a depth at or below any established frost line, and be capable of
transferring all design loads imposed by or upon the structure into soil or
bedrock without failure, or exceeding the safe bearing capacity of the
supporting soil.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.360)
(Ord. 2458, passed - -1971; Ord. 2783, passed - -; Ord. 2906, passed - -1985)
§ 153.045 FOUNDATIONS.
   (A)   Generally. This section prescribes standards for the proper design and
installation of manufactured home foundations. It further clarifies the
definition of a manufactured home foundation.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.370)
   (B)   Manufactured home foundation system.
      (1)   The manufactured home permanent foundation system constitutes a
permanent, load-bearing support system for the manufactured home. The
manufacturer or owner shall be permitted to design for and specify the
installation of any foundation system which meets the design requirements of
this standard. The foundation system shall be installed as specified in this
standard, or as specified in the Building Code for single-family dwellings, or
be designed by a professional engineer or architect.
      (2)   A pier support system shall consist of footings, piers, caps,
leveling spacers or approved prefabricated load-bearing devices. Foundation
walls which enclose basements shall conform to the Building Code.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.380)
   (C)   Manufactured homes provided with manufacturer’s instructions for
permanent installations. If the manufacturer provides instructions for
permanent installation, which are designed by a professional engineer or
architect, then the instructions must meet all applicable requirements of the
Building Code and this standard. The Building Official may waive compliance
with this standard if a conflict exists between this standard and the
manufacturer’s instructions. These instructions shall remain with the home.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.390)
   (D)   Manufactured homes without manufacturer’s installation instructions.
Homes not provided with manufacturer’s installation instructions shall be
installed onto a foundation system meeting the requirements of this standard or
be designed by a professional engineer or architect. Unless designed by a
professional engineer or architect, spacing of piers shall be as specified in
the table set out in division (G) below with piers installed directly under the
main frame of the manufactured home, and with not more than two feet of the
main frame cantilevered beyond the end piers.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.400)
   (E)   Clearance under homes; minimum. A minimum clearance of 18 inches shall
be maintained beneath the lowest member of the floor support framing system.
Clearance from the bottom of wood floor joists or perimeter joists shall be as
specified in the Building Code.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.410)
   (F)   Access to and ventilation of under-floor areas.
      (1)   Under-floor spaces shall be ventilated with openings as specified
in the Building Code. If combustion air for one or more heat-producing
appliances is taken from within the under-floor spaces, ventilation shall be
adequate to assure for proper appliance operation.
      (2)   Access openings shall be not less than 18 inches in any dimension
and not less than three square feet in area and shall be located so that any
water supply and sewer drain connections located under the manufactured home
are accessible for inspection. Access panels or doors shall not be fastened in
a manner requiring the use of a special tool for removal or opening of the
panel or doors.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.420)
   (G)   Footings. The load-bearing capacity of individual load-bearing piers
and their footings shall be not less than the combined live and dead loads as
specified in this standard. Footings shall be adequate in size to withstand the
contributing live and dead loads of the manufactured home and any other imposed
loads. (See the table set out in this section for suggested typical footing
sizes.) Footings or pier foundations (unless otherwise approved by a registered
professional engineer or architect) shall be placed level on firm, undisturbed
soil or on engineered fill, which is free of grass and organic materials,
compacted to a minimum load-bearing capacity as specified in the table set out
below. Where unusual conditions exist, the spacing of piers and the load-
bearing capacity of the soil shall be determined specifically for such
conditions. Concentrated roof loads may require special consideration.
Continuous footings shall conform to the requirements of the Building Code.

or loads.

(Prior Code, § 16.08.430)
(Ord. 2458, passed - -1971; Ord. 2783, passed - -; Ord. 2906, passed - -1985)
§ 153.046 PIERS.
   (A)   Pier construction.
      (1)   Piers shall be designed and constructed to distribute loads evenly.
Multiple-section homes may have concentrated roof loads which will require
special consideration. Load-bearing piers shall be constructed utilizing one of
the following methods:
         (a)   A prefabricated load-bearing device that is listed and labeled
for the intended use;
         (b)   A pier designed by a registered professional engineer or
architect;
         (c)   A pier constructed with concrete blocks conforming to ASTM 090,
laid with mortar joints or approved structural masonry adhesive. Blocks shall
not lie less than a nominal eight inches in width and 16 inches in length.
Mortar mix shall be Type S or one part portland cement, one-half part hydrated
lime and four parts sand by volume. Lime shall not be used with plastic or
water- proof cement;
         (d)   A pier constructed with non-mortared concrete blocks and a
separate anchoring system, conforming to the following criteria.
            1.   Concrete blocks are to be a nominal eight inches by eight
inches by 16 inches conforming to ASTM C-90 with open cells vertical, stacked
true and plumbed with a maximum horizontal block offset of one-half inch from
the top to the bottom of the pier. The maximum height of the pier shall be 36
inches unless designed by a professional engineer or architect.
            2.   Concrete block shall be stable and firm on the footing with
all bearing surfaces clean and smooth.
            3.   The maximum vertical load shall not exceed 6,400 pounds per
pier.
            4.   Non-mortared piers shall not carry any vertical uplift, moment
or horizontal load.
            5.   Tie-downs and anchors shall be designed to resist all uplift
loads not offset by two-thirds of the manufactured home dead load and all
horizontal loads.
            6.   The width of the main frame I-beam flange shall not be less
than two inches.
            7.   Concrete caps shall be the same size as the supporting
concrete block, and a minimum of four inches thick conforming to ASTM C-145,
and may be used with leveling spacers of steel or decay-resistant wood which
shall be level.
            8.   Pier footings shall be solid concrete bearing on firm
undisturbed soil or engineered fill and conforming to this division (G).
            9.   The top surface of the footing shall be smooth, clean and
level, providing a minimum of 50% direct bearing of concrete block on the top
surface of footing; if the top surface of the footing is not level or smooth, a
layer of mortar shall be applied to provide the proper bearing surface for the
concrete block pier.
      (2)   Caps and leveling spacers may be used for leveling of the
manufactured home. Spacing of piers shall be as specified in the manufacturer’s
installation instructions, if available or the table set out in this division
(G) may be used for the spacing of piers. Where unusual conditions or loads
exist, the spacing of piers and the load-bearing capacity of the soil shall be
determined specifically for such conditions or loads.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.440)
   (B)   Height of piers. Piers shall be constructed utilizing one of the
following methods.
      (1)   Piers 36 inches or less in height can be constructed of concrete
block, placed with open cells in vertical position. Single-stacked block piers
shall be installed with the long dimension at right angles to the main frame
member it supports. Piers shall be topped with a cap.
      (2)   Subject to the limitations of this chapter, piers between 36 inches
and 80 inches in height, and all corner piers over 24 inches high, shall be at
least 16 inches in width with blocks interlocked and fully capped with a
minimum four-inch solid concrete block or equivalent.
      (3)   Subject to other limitations of this standard, piers over 80 inches
in height can be constructed in accordance with the provisions of division (B)
(2) above; provided, the piers shall be filled solid with concrete and
reinforced with two continuous one-half inch diameter steel reinforcing bars
inserted vertically through the open cells of each 16-inch block.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.450)
(Ord. 2458, passed - -1971; Ord. 2783, passed - -; Ord. 2906, passed - -1985)
§ 153.047 ANCHORING INSTALLATIONS.
   (A)   Ground anchors.
      (1)   Every ground anchor shall have specification data showing the soil
classifications for which it qualifies. Anchor selection shall be based on a
determination of the soil classification at the depth at which the anchor will
be installed.
      (2)   Ground anchors, including means for attaching ties, shall be
located to comply with anchoring system instructions provided by the
manufactured home manufacturer or, if there are no instructions, in accordance
with the requirements of this standard.
      (3)   Ground anchors shall be designed and installed to transfer the
anchoring loads to the ground. The load-carrying portion of the ground anchors
shall be installed to the full depth called for by the manufacturer’s
installation directions and shall extend below the established frost line into
undisturbed soil.
      (4)   Manufactured ground anchors shall be listed and installed in
accordance with the terms of their listing and the anchor manufacturer’s
instructions, and shall include means of attachment of this meeting the
requirements of divisions (G) and (H) below. Ground anchor manufacturer’s
instructions shall include the amount of pre-load required, the methods of
adjustment after installation, and the load capacity in various types of soil.
These instructions shall include tensioning adjustments which may be needed to
prevent damage to the manufactured home, particularly damage that can be caused
by frost heave. Each ground anchor shall be marked with the manufacturer’s
identification and listed model identification number which shall be visible
after installation. Instructions shall accompany each listed ground anchor
specifying the types of soil for which the anchor is suitable under the
requirements of this division (A).
      (5)   Each approved ground anchor, when installed, shall be capable of
resisting an allowable working load at least equal to 3,150 pounds in the
direction of the tie, plus a 50% overload (4,725 pounds) without failure.
Failure shall be considered to have occurred when the anchor moves more than
two inches at 4,725 pounds in the direction of the tie installation. Those
ground anchors which are designed to be installed so that loads on the anchor
are other than direct withdrawal shall be designed and installed to resist an
applied design load of 3,150 pounds at 40 to 50 degrees from vertical without
displacing the tie end of the anchor more than four inches horizontally.
Anchors designed for connection of multiple ties shall be capable of resisting
the combined working load and overload consistent with the intent expressed
herein.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.460)
   (B)   Anchoring equipment. Anchoring equipment, when installed as a
permanent installation, shall be capable of resisting all loads as specified
within this standard. When the stabilizing system is designed by a qualified
registered professional engineer or architect, alternative designs may be used
providing the anchoring equipment to be used is capable of withstanding a 50%
overload. All anchoring equipment shall be listed or labeled as being capable
of meeting the requirements of this standard. Anchors, as specified in the
Building Code, may be attached to the main frame of the manufactured home by an
approved three-sixteenths-inch-thick slotted steel plate anchoring device.
Other anchoring devices or methods meeting the requirements of this standard
shall be permitted when approved by the Building Official.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.470)
   (C)   Resistance to weather deterioration. All anchoring equipment exposed
to weathering shall have a resistance to weather deterioration at least
equivalent to that provided by a coating of zinc on steel of not less than
0.625 ounces per square foot on each side of the surface coated, as determined
by ASTM A-90-69. Slit or cut edges of zinc-coated steel strapping do not need
to be zinc coated.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.480)
   (D)   Permanency of connections. Anchoring systems shall be so installed as
to be permanent. Anchoring equipment shall be designed to prevent self-
disconnection with no hook ends used.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.490)
   (E)   Tensioning device design. Tensioning devices, such as turnbuckles or
yoke-type fasteners, shall be ended with clevis or forged or welded eyes.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.500)
   (F)   Footings. If footings are used to transfer the anchoring loads to the
ground, steel rods cast in concrete shall be capable of resisting design loads.
Deadman concrete anchors may be used in place of listed anchors if they meet
the requirements of this standard.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.510)
   (G)   Materials and installation. Steel strapping, cable, chain or other
approved materials shall be used for ties. All ties shall be fastened to ground
anchors and drawn tight with turnbuckles or other adjustable tensioning devices
or devices supplied with the ground anchor. The materials shall be capable of
resisting an allowable working load of 3,150 pounds, with no more than 2%
elongation and shall withstand a 50% overload (4,750 pounds). Ties shall comply
with the weathering requirements of division (C) above. Ties shall connect the
ground anchor and the main structural frame. Ties shall not connect to steel
outrigger beams which fasten to and intersect the main structural frame unless
specifically stated in the manufacturer’s installation instructions. Connection
of cable ties to main frame members shall be five-eighths-inch drop forged
closed eye bolts affixed to the frame member in an approved manner. Cable ends
shall be secured with at least three U-bolt type cable clamps with the “U”
portion of the clamp installed on the short (dead) end of the cable to assure
strength equal to that required by this division (H).
(Prior Code, § 16.08.520)
   (H)   Number of ties. Wood floor support systems shall be fixed to perimeter
foundation walls in accordance with provisions of the Building Code. The
minimum number of ties required per side shall be sufficient to resist the wind
load stated in the Building Code. Ties shall be as evenly spaced as practicable
along the length of the manufactured home with the distance from each end of
the home and the tie nearest that end not exceeding eight feet. When continuous
straps are provided as vertical ties, such ties shall be positioned at rafters
and studs. Where a vertical tie and diagonal tie are located at the same place,
both ties may be connected to a single anchor; provided that, the anchor used
is capable of carrying both loadings. Multi-section manufactured homes require
diagonal ties only. Diagonal ties shall be installed on the exterior main frame
and slope to the exterior at an angle of 40 to 50 degrees from the vertical.
Non-continuous vertical ties shall be placed at the main frame. If sufficient
to meet the wind loads as provided in the Building Code, the minimum number of
ties per side of the manufactured home shall be shown in the table that
follows:
Number of Ties Required Per Side of a Manufactured Home

Source: Proposed ANSI A225 Standard on Manufactured Home Installations.
 
(Prior Code, § 16.08.530)
(Ord. 2458, passed - -1971; Ord. 2783, passed - -; Ord. 2906, passed - -1985)
§ 153.048 PERIMETER ENCLOSURES.
   (A)   Materials. Skirting shall be of materials suitable for exterior
exposure and contact with the ground. If skirting is not used, then materials
used as a permanent perimeter enclosure shall be as for regular foundation
construction as required by the Building Code.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.540)
   (B)   Skirting installation.
      (1)   If used, skirting shall be installed in accordance with the
manufacturer’s installation instructions.
      (2)   It shall be adequately secured to assure stability, to minimize
vibrations and susceptibility to wind damage, and to compensate for possible
frost heave.
      (3)   The entire underfloor area of the home shall be completely enclosed
by a perimeter enclosure, except for the required openings.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.550)
   (C)   Permanent perimeter enclosure installation.
      (1)   Retaining walls, if used as permanent perimeter enclosures, shall
resist the lateral displacement of soil or other materials and shall conform to
the Building Code, as specified for foundation walls.
      (2)   Unless a retaining wall is equivalent to a load-bearing foundation,
it should not be attached to the manufactured home. Retaining walls and
foundation walls should be constructed of treated foundation grade wood,
concrete, masonry or other approved materials or combinations of these
materials, as for foundations as specified in the Building Code.
      (3)   When retaining walls or foundation walls are used as a load-bearing
foundation, the manufactured home shall be anchored to the wall. The entire
underfloor area of the home shall be completely enclosed, except for required
openings. Siding material shall extend below the top of the exterior of the
retaining or foundation wall.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.560)
(Ord. 2458, passed - -1971; Ord. 2783, passed - -; Ord. 2906, passed - -1985)
§ 153.049 UTILITY CONNECTIONS.
   (A)   Generally.
      (1)   Utility connections shall be made to assure that utility systems
permit satisfactory functioning and provide for health and safety under all
conditions of normal use.
      (2)   Manufactured home utility services shall only be connected to the
supply source by means of approved materials.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.570)
   (B)   Federal manufactured home connections.
      (1)   A manufactured home which bears a certification label certifying
that it is constructed in compliance with the Federal Manufactured Home
Construction and Safety Standards shall be connected to the utilities as
required by the Federal Manufactured Home Construction and Safety Standards, §
503, and the following.
         (a)   The gas piping supply system shall be designed for a pressure
not exceeding 14-inch water column (one-half inch psi).
         (b)   When a manufactured home is to be installed in an area where the
water pressure exceeds 80 psi, a pressure-reducing valve should be installed.
         (c)   The installation of a self-contained air-conditioner comfort
cooling appliance shall meet the following requirements.
            1.   The installation on a duct common with an installed heating
appliance shall require the installation of an automatic damper or other means
to prevent the cooled air from passing through the heating appliance unless the
heating appliance is certified or listed for such application and the supply
system is intended for such an application.
            2.   The installation shall prevent the flow of heated air into the
external cooling appliance and its connecting ducts to the manufactured home
supply and return air system during the operation of the heating appliance
installed in the manufactured home, and the installation shall prevent
simultaneous operation of the heating and cooling appliances.
         (d)   The power supply to the manufactured home shall be a permanently
installed circuit, Where a permanent feeder is used, the supply shall be by
means of:
            1.   One mast weatherhead installation installed in accordance with
the Electrical Code and containing four continuous insulated, color-coded,
feeder conductors, one of which shall be an equipment grounding conductor; or
            2.   An approved raceway from the disconnecting means in the
manufactured home with provisions for the attachment of a suitable junction box
or fitting to the raceway on the underside of the manufactured home. The
manufacturer shall provide in his or her written installation instructions, the
proper feeder conductor sizes for the raceway and the size of the junction box
to be used.
      (2)   Where there is a conflict in the above requirements, the Federal
Manufactured Home Construction and Safety Standards shall apply.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.580)
   (C)   Non-federal manufactured home connections. All manufactured homes
bearing a certification label certifying that they are built in compliance with
the Building Code shall be connected to the utilities as follows.
      (1)   Water supply shall be as required by the Plumbing Code.
      (2)   Drainage waste system shall be as required by the Plumbing Code.
      (3)   Electrical connections shall be as required by the Electrical Code.
      (4)   Gas supply connections shall be as required by the National Fuel
Gas Code NFPA 54 or the Standard for the Storage and Handling of Liquified
Petroleum Gasses NFPA 58.
      (5)   Oil supply connections shall be as required by the Installation of
Oil Burning Equipment, NFPA 31.
      (6)   Mechanical equipment and accessories shall be listed and labeled
for the use intended and shall be connected as required by the Mechanical Code.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.590)
   (D)   Inspections. Inspections of manufactured home utility connections
shall be as per §
153.067 of this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.600)
(Ord. 2458, passed - -1971; Ord. 2783, passed - -; Ord. 2906, passed - -1985)

Ord. 2906, passed - -1985)
§ 153.067 INSPECTIONS.
   (A)   All utility connections shall be inspected and approved before the
home is occupied.
   (B)   All sewer and drainage lines shall be filled with water and be capable
of holding water for 15 minutes (filled to maximum height without overflowing
into the home).
   (C)   All water lines shall be capable of holding water at pressure of 80
pounds and to be tested with water and air for 15 minutes.
   (D)   All gas lines shall be capable of holding 20 pounds air pressure for
15 minutes.
   (E)   All material used for utility connections from the home to source
shall meet all local Electrical and Plumbing Codes.
   (F)   No utility company shall render service to a Type III, Type IV or
mobile home without prior approval of the city.
(Prior Code, § 16.08.690) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971; Ord. 2783, passed - -;
Ord. 2906, passed - -1985)
**** MOBILE HOME PARKS ****
§ 153.080 PERMIT; ISSUANCE AND CONDITIONS.
   Construction of a mobile home park within the city shall begin only after a
special use permit has been granted by the City Commission, in compliance with
Chapter 158 of this code of ordinances. No such permit shall be granted,
however, until a development plan for the proposed mobile home park has been
prepared and submitted by the developer to the Planning Commission and found
satisfactory by said Commission, and further submitted to the City Commission
with a recommendation for approval.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.010) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
§ 153.081 PLAN; CONTENT; RECOMMENDATIONS AND CRITERIA; SUBMITTAL.
   (A)   The plan required in §
153.080 of this chapter shall be accurately drawn, at a scale acceptable to the
city’s Code Enforcement Officer, and shall show the following:
      (1)   Proposed street and drive pattern;
      (2)   Proposed mobile home spaces and their approximate dimensions;
      (3)   Any existing streets in or abutting the property;
      (4)   Location and size of parking spaces;
      (5)   Location and size of park and playground area;
      (6)   Screening and landscaping;
      (7)   Legal description of the tract;
      (8)   Name of the developer and the firm preparing the plan; and
      (9)   North point, scale and date.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.020)
   (B)   The Planning Commission shall, upon submission of three copies of the
plan and an application for a special use permit, publish notice and hold a
hearing on the proposal, in conformance with Chapter 158 of this code of
ordinances. The decision of the Commission to recommend approval or denial of
the proposed mobile home park shall be based upon the following criteria.
      (1)   The proposed property will be in harmony, in general, with the
comprehensive plan of the city.
      (2)   Safe and efficient ingress and egress of vehicular and pedestrian
traffic and an adequate level of utility and other services is assured.
      (3)   A safe and healthful living environment will exist for the
occupants of the park.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.030)
   (C)   Upon hearing and consideration of the project, the Commission shall,
within reasonable time, submit its recommendation and an endorsed copy of the
plan to the Board of Commissioners for final action.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.040)
(Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
§ 153.082 PERMIT; EXPIRATION AND REAPPLICATION.
   After 24 months from the date of approval of a special use permit by the
City Commission, if the mobile home park is not improved in accordance with the
plans to the extent that utilities, surfaced drives and occupied mobile home
spaces exist over at least 20% of the area of the approved park, the special
use permit shall expire and further development and additional occupancy of the
park shall be prohibited. All mobile homes shall be removed within six months
of the date of expiration of a special use permit. The owner or his or her
agent may apply for a new special use permit in the case of expiration or
cancellation under procedures set out in
Chapter 158 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.050) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
§ 153.083 MINIMUM SIZE.
   No mobile home park shall have a site smaller than will accommodate 18
mobile homes or comprising less than three acres.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.060) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
§ 153.084 MINIMUM SPACE SIZE; DENSITY.
   Each mobile home space shall have dimensions of at least 45 feet in width
and 90 feet in depth. The accepted density for mobile home parks shall be six
mobile homes per acre, with the maximum density being eight mobile homes per
acre.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.070) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
§ 153.085 STAND AND SKIRT REQUIREMENTS.
   Each mobile home space shall contain a portland cement or asphaltic concrete
stand upon which the mobile home will be situated. Each home shall be equipped
with skirts on all sides, such skirts to be of material harmonious to the
mobile home structure.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.080) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
§ 153.086 STREET AND DRIVE REQUIREMENTS.
   All public streets within the park shall be improved to city standards for
residential streets. All private drives shall be surfaced with at least six
inches of compact stone base, with two inches of hot mix bituminous concrete as
the surface course.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.090) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
§ 153.087 OFF-STREET PARKING.
   At least two parking spaces for each mobile home shall be provided within 60
feet of the mobile home. Such parking spaces shall be off the public street or
private drive, and each shall be not less than nine feet by 20 feet in size,
and shall be surfaced to at least the standard set out in §
153.086 of this chapter for drives.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.100) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
§ 153.088 PARK AND PLAYGROUND SPACE.
   Park and playground space shall be provided for occupants of the mobile home
park on the basis of 300 square feet for each space in the park. Such
playground space shall be separate and aside from the open space provided on
each mobile home space, and shall be equipped and maintained for the use of the
residents of the park.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.110) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
§ 153.089 OPEN SPACE.
   Each mobile home shall be located on the mobile home space so that no part
of one mobile home structure, including canopies, awnings, carports and other
protrusions, is closer than 12 feet to another mobile home, or to the edge of
the surface of a drive. No mobile home shall be located closer than 30 feet to
the boundary line of the mobile home park or to a public street.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.120) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
§ 153.090 UTILITIES.
   All liquid waste shall be disposed of through a sanitary sewer system and
treatment facility, the plans of which shall be approved by the Code
Enforcement Officer prior to construction. All solid wastes shall be disposed
of by accumulation in tight containers, and removed at regular intervals by
methods to be approved by the Code Enforcement Officer. All power and telephone
lines shall be underground, and shall be in compliance with standards of the
utility corporation involved. All water supply and distribution shall be
approved by the Code Enforcement Officer prior to construction. The site of the
park shall be graded so that surface water will not accumulate, but will run
off in a manner that will not adversely affect the residential character of the
park or adjacent property. The proposed drainage system shall be approved by
the Code Enforcement Officer prior to construction of streets or other portions
of the park.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.130) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
§ 153.091 LICENSE; FEE.
   All mobile home parks shall be subject to an annual license fee, which may
be issued by the Board of Commissioners. Compliance with this chapter and all
other codes and ordinances of the city shall be necessary prior to the issuance
or renewal of said license. A license fee of $100 shall be paid by the owner of
the mobile home park upon establishing the park, and the annual renewal of the
license shall be in the amount of $10 for each space occupied on the date of
expiration of the previous license.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.140) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
§ 153.092 APPEARANCE AND MAINTENANCE.
   (A)   Each mobile home park or subdivision shall be operated in a sanitary,
orderly and efficient manner, and shall maintain a neat appearance at all
times. No damaged or deteriorated mobile homes shall be permitted to remain,
and suitable and effective rules for regulating the outside storage of
equipment, the removal of wheels and installation of skirting, the collection
of trash and garbage and the attachment of appurtenances to the mobile homes
shall be continually enforced. A map shall be placed at the main entrance to
the park clearly showing the location of each space and the name and address of
the occupant. Such map shall be illuminated at all times, and be enclosed in a
weather-proof case, within full view of persons entering the park. All drives,
playground area and equipment, lawn and trees, and any recreation or accessory
buildings, shall be maintained at a level at least equal to the average
residential neighborhood in the city.
   (B)   All portions of the mobile home park shall be open and accessible to
fire, police and other emergency and protective vehicles and personnel,
including city, county and state inspectors.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.150) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
§ 153.093 LICENSE AND PERMIT CANCELLATION; OWNER RESPONSIBILITY.
   The owner and/or operator of the mobile home park shall be responsible for
the conformance to all plans, codes and ordinances and any conditions set out
in the special use permit, and any deviation or non-performance, or the
occurrence of any nuisance or unhealthful or unsanitary condition, or the
termination of a park operation for one year or more shall constitute grounds
for the cancellation of a license or special use permit, or refusal to renew a
license on the part of the Board of Commissioners. In addition, the city shall
have all remedial and corrective powers conveyed by statutes of the state.
(Prior Code, § 16.24.160) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
**** MOBILE HOME SUBDIVISIONS ****
§ 153.105 PERMITTED UNDER SUBDIVISION REGULATIONS; GENERAL REQUIREMENTS.
   A subdivision for the purpose of selling individual lots in fee for the
purpose of mobile home sites may be permitted under the subdivision ordinance
of the city. Only double-wide or larger mobile homes or modular homes, or
customary single-family dwellings shall be permitted in a mobile home
subdivision. Such structures shall be located in compliance with the yard and
lot area requirements of the R-1 District, and shall be attached to a permanent
concrete foundation or slab. Off-street parking spaces shall be provided in
compliance with
Chapter 158 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 16.28.010) (Ord. 2458, passed - -1971)
§ 153.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person violating any provision(s) of §
153.005 of this chapter shall be subjected to the penalties prescribed in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 16.16.010)
(Ord. 2458, passed - -1971; Ord. 3094, passed - -1994)

**** FIRE PREVENTION CODE ****
§ 154.015 ADOPTION.
   (A)   The following documents, three copies of which are on file in the
office of the City Clerk, marked and designated as “2012 International Code
Council Fire Code” are adopted as codes of the city.
   (B)   Each and all of the regulations, provisions, penalties, conditions and
terms of said codes on file in the office of the City Clerk are referred to,
adopted and made a part of this section as if fully set out in this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.010) (Ord. 2495, passed - -1972; Ord. 3116, passed - -
1995; Ord. 3144, passed - -1997; Ord. 3339, passed - -2008; Ord. 3505, passed
5-16-2017)
§ 154.016 FIREWORKS DISCHARGE.
   (A)   (1)   It is, therefore, adopted that inside the city limits, only
Class C (common) fireworks are permitted to be discharged within the city
limits without a special events permit. Class C fireworks are permitted to be
sold June 20 to July 7 and discharged June 27 to July 2 from the hours of 10:00
a.m. to 10:00 p.m.; July 3 and July 4 from the hours of 10:00 a.m. to 11:59
p.m.; and on July 5 from the hours of 10:00 a.m. to 10:00 p.m.
      (2)   It is illegal to discharge or ignite fireworks in these
circumstances:
         (a)   Within 1,000 feet of a hospital;
         (b)   Within 50 feet of a location where fireworks are sold;
         (c)   Within 100 feet from any gas pump, filling station or bulk fuel
storage facility;
         (d)   It shall be unlawful for any person to throw, cast or propel
fireworks of any kind in the direction of or into the path of any person or
group of persons, or from, in the direction of or into any vehicle of any kind;
         (e)   Bottle rockets. BOTTLE ROCKET means any pyrotechnical device
which is mounted on a stick or wire, and projects into the air when ignited,
with or without reports, and include any device with the same configuration,
with or without reports, which may be classified as a pipe or trough rocket;
         (f)   Any community college campus or surrounding grounds;
         (g)   Bourbon County Fairgrounds;
         (h)   Any city-owned parks; and/or
         (i)   No discharging of fireworks from 18th and Horton to 23rd and
Horton during the time of the city’s fireworks display.
   (B)   The City Manager or the Fire Chief or his or her designated person has
the authority to alter the dates of this section due to severe dry weather
conditions, or any other special circumstances.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.025) (Ord. 3585, passed 6-22-2021; Ord. 3602, passed 2-15-
2022; Ord. 3781, passed 6-17-2025) Penalty, see §
154.999
§ 154.017 DEFINITIONS.
   (A)   Where the term “corporation counsel” is used in the Fire Prevention
Code, it means the City Attorney for the city.
   (B)   Whenever the word “municipality” is used in the Fire Prevention Code,
it means the City of Fort Scott, Kansas.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.020) (Ord. 2495, passed - -1972)
§ 154.018 BONFIRES AND RUBBISH FIRES.
   Subsection 28.1(b) of Section 15.020.030, Fire Prevention Code, of the Fort
Scott Municipal Code shall be amended to read as follows: bonfires or rubbish
fires:
   Section 28.1(b). Location Restricted.
   No person shall kindle or maintain any bon fire or rubbish fire or authorize
any such fire to be kindled or maintained on any private land unless:
      1. The location is more than 50 feet from any structure and adequate
provision is made to prevent fire from spreading to within 50 feet of any
structure, or
      2. The fire is contained in an approved waste burner located safely and
not less than 25 feet from any structure.
   In all instances, a written permit must be obtained from the Fort Scott Fire
Department before any burning is allowed.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.030) (Ord. 2605, passed - -1976; Ord. 3438, passed 5-6-
2014)
§ 154.019 BUREAU OF FIRE PREVENTION.
   (A)   The Fire Prevention Code shall be enforced by the Bureau of Fire
Prevention in the Fire Department of the city, which is established and which
shall be operated under the supervision of the Chief of the Fire Department.
   (B)   (1)   The Chief in charge of the Bureau of Fire Prevention shall be
appointed by the governing body of the city on the basis of examination to
determine his or her qualifications.
      (2)   His or her appointment shall continue during good behavior and
satisfactory service, and he or she shall not be removed from office, except
for cause after public trial.
   (C)   (1)   The Chief of the Fire Department may detail such members of the
Fire Department as inspectors as shall from time to time be necessary.
      (2)   The Chief of the Fire Department shall recommend to the governing
body of the city the employment of technical inspectors, who, when such
authorization is made, shall be selected through an examination to determine
their fitness for the position.
      (3)   The examination shall be open to members and non-members of the
Fire Department, and appointments made after examination shall be for an
indefinite term, with removal only for cause.
   (D)   (1)   A report of the Bureau of Fire Prevention shall be made annually
and transmitted to the Chief executive officer of the municipality.
      (2)   It shall contain all proceedings under the Fire Prevention Code,
with such statistics as the Chief of the Fire Department may wish to include
therein.
      (3)   The Chief of the Fire Department shall also recommend any
amendments to the code which, in his or her judgment, are desirable.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.040) (Ord. 2495, passed - -1972)
§ 154.020 STORAGE OF EXPLOSIVES AND BLASTING AGENTS PROHIBITED; EXCEPTION.
   The limits referred to in § 12.5b of the Fire Prevention Code, in which
storage of explosives and blasting agents is prohibited, are established as
follows: at all places within the limits of the city, except within the
specially constructed magazine located at 15 Stanton Street, Fort Scott,
Kansas.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.050) (Ord. 2495, passed - -1972)
§ 154.021 STORAGE OF FLAMMABLE LIQUIDS IN OUTSIDE ABOVEGROUND TANKS PROHIBITED.
   (A)   The limits referred to in § 16.22a of the Fire Prevention Code, in
which storage of flammable liquids in outside aboveground tanks is prohibited,
are established as all places within the limits of the city, except the
following: beginning at the south right-of-way line of the St. Louis and San
Francisco Railroad tracks, thence south on the west right-of-way line of North
Judson Street to the north right-of-way line of West Pine Street, west on Pine
Street to the east right-of-way line of North Crawford Street north said right-
of-way line to the said St. Louis and San Francisco Railroad tracks, thence
east on the south right-of-way line of the said tracks to the place of
beginning; beginning at the south right-of-way line of West Wall Street, thence
south on the west right-of-way line of South National Avenue to the north
right-of-way fine of West First Street, thence west on said right-of-way line
to the east right-of-way line of North Judson Street, thence north on said
right-of-way line to the south right-of-way line of West Wall Street, thence
east on said right-of-way line to the place of beginning; beginning on the
south right-of-way line of the St. Louis and San Francisco Railroad tracks,
thence south and southwesterly on the west right-of-way line of North Hill
Street to the north right-of-way line of East Oak Street, thence west on the
said right-of-way line to the east right-of-way line of North Wilson Street,
thence north on said right-of-way line to the south right-of-way line of the
said railroad tracks, thence east on the said right-of-way line of the said
railroad tracks to the point of beginning; beginning at the south right-of-way
line of East Third Street, thence south on the west right-of-way line of South
Margrave Street to the north right-of-way line of East Fourth Street, thence
west on said right-of-way line to the east right-of-way line of North Hill
Street, thence north on the said right-of-way line to the south right-of-way
line of East Third Street, thence east on said right-of-way line to the place
of beginning.
   (B)   The limits referred to in § 16.61 of the Fire Prevention Code, in
which new bulk plants for flammable or combustible liquids are prohibited, are
established as the same as those found in division (A) above.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.060) (Ord. 2495, passed - -1972) Penalty, see §
154.999
§ 154.022 BULK STORAGE OF LIQUEFIED PETROLEUM GAS PROHIBITED.
   The limits referred to in § 21.6a of the Fire Prevention Code, in which bulk
storage of liquefied petroleum gas is restricted, are established as follows:
the bulk storage of liquefied petroleum gas in the city limits of the city is
prohibited.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.070) (Ord. 2495, passed - -1972) Penalty, see §
154.999
§ 154.023 TRANSPORTATION OF EXPLOSIVES AND BLASTING AGENTS; VEHICLE ROUTES.
   The routes referred to in § 12.7m of the Fire Prevention Code for vehicles
transporting explosives and blasting agents are established as follows: all
vehicles proceeding through the city in a northerly or southerly direction must
follow the U.S. Highway 69 bypass; all vehicles proceeding through the city in
an easterly or westerly direction must follow U.S. Highway 54.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.080) (Ord. 2495, passed - -1972) Penalty, see §
154.999
§ 154.024 TRANSPORTATION OF HAZARDOUS CHEMICALS OR DANGEROUS ARTICLES; VEHICLE
ROUTES.
   The routes referred to in § 20.14 of the Fire Prevention Code, for vehicles
transporting hazardous chemicals and other dangerous articles, are established
as follows: all vehicles proceeding through the city in a northerly or
southerly direction must follow the U.S. Highway 69 bypass; all vehicles
proceeding through the city in an easterly or westerly direction must follow
U.S. 54.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.090) (Ord. 2495, passed - -1972)
§ 154.025 FIRE LANES ESTABLISHED.
   The fire lanes referred to in § 28.16 of the Fire Prevention Code are
established as follows: beginning at the northeast corner of the Fort Scott
Senior High School Building, thence east to the west right-of-way line of Scott
Avenue, thence south on the said right-of-way line a distance of 150 feet,
thence west to the said Fort Scott Senior High School Building, thence north
along the east wall of the said Fort Scott Senior High School building to the
place of beginning.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.100) (Ord. 2495, passed - -1972)
§ 154.026 MODIFICATION; PROCEDURE.
   The Chief of the Bureau of Fire Prevention shall have power to modify any of
the provisions of the Fire Prevention Code upon application, in writing, by the
owner or lessee, or his or her duly authorized agent, carrying out the strict
letter of the code; provided that, the spirit of the code shall be observed,
public safety secured and substantial justice done. The particulars of such
modification when granted or allowed and the decision of the Chief of the
Bureau of Fire Prevention thereon shall be entered upon the records of the
Department, and a signed copy shall be furnished the applicant.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.110) (Ord. 2495, passed - -1972)
§ 154.027 RIGHT OF APPEAL; TIME LIMIT.
   Whenever the Chief of the Bureau of Fire Prevention shall disapprove an
application or refuse to grant a permit applied for, or when it is claimed that
the provisions of the code do not apply or that the true intent and meaning of
the code has been misconstrued or wrongly interpreted, the applicant may appeal
from the decision of the Chief of the Bureau of the Fire Prevention to the
governing body of the city within 30 days from the date of the decision
appealed.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.120) (Ord. 2495, passed - -1972)
§ 154.028 PERMIT; WHEN REQUIRED; LIST; POSTING.
   The governing body of the city, the Chief of the Fire Department and the
Chief of the Bureau of Fire Prevention shall act as a committee to determine
and specify, after giving affected persons an opportunity to be heard, any new
materials, processes or occupancies which shall require permits, in addition to
those now enumerated in the code. The Chief of the Bureau of Fire Prevention
shall post such list in a conspicuous place in his or her office and distribute
copies thereof to interested persons.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.130) (Ord. 2495, passed - -1972)
**** FIRE AND EXPLOSION DAMAGED STRUCTURES ****
§ 154.040 REGULATION ESTABLISHED; INSURANCE COMPANY REQUIREMENTS.
   The city is authorized to utilize the procedures established by K.S.A. 40-
3901 et seq., whereby no insurance company shall pay a claim of a named insured
for loss or damage to any building or other structure located within the city,
arising out of any fire, explosion or windstorm where the amount recoverable
for the loss or damage to the building or other structure, under all policies,
is in excess of 75% of the face value of the policy covering such building or
other insured structure, unless there is compliance with the following
procedures.
   (A)   When final settlement on a covered claim has been agreed to or arrived
at between the named insured or insureds and the company or companies, and such
final settlement exceeds 75% of the face value of the policy covering any
building or other insured structure, and when all amounts due the holder of a
first real estate mortgage against the building or other structure, pursuant to
the terms of the policy and endorsement thereto, shall have been paid, the
insurance company or companies shall execute a draft payable to the City Clerk,
in an amount equal to 15% of the proceeds of the covered claim payment, unless
the Codes Enforcement Officer of the city has issued a certificate to the
insurance company or companies that the insured has removed the damaged
building or other structure, as well as all associated debris, or repaired,
rebuilt or otherwise made the premises safe and secure.
   (B)   Such transfer of funds shall be on a pro rata basis by all companies
insuring the building or other structure. Policy proceeds remaining after the
transfer to the city shall be disbursed in accordance with the policy terms.
   (C)   Upon the transfer of the funds as required by division (A) above, the
insurance company shall provide the city with the name and address of the named
insured or insureds, the total insurance coverage applicable to said building
or other structure, and the amount of the final settlement agreed to or arrived
at between the insurance company or companies and the insured or insureds,
whereupon the city’s Codes Officer shall contact the named insured or insureds
by certified mail notifying them that said insurance proceeds have been
received by the city and apprise them of the procedures to be followed under
this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 15.22.010) (Ord. 2989, passed - -1989; Ord. 3157, passed - -
1998)
§ 154.041 FIRE INSURANCE PROCEEDS FUND.
   The City Treasurer is authorized and shall create a fund to be known as the
“Insurance Proceeds Fund”. All moneys received by the city, as provided for by
this chapter, shall be placed in such fund and deposited in an interest-bearing
account.
(Prior Code, § 15.22.020) (Ord. 2989, passed - -1989; Ord. 3157, passed - -
1998)
§ 154.042 PROCEDURE UPON RECEIPT OF INSURANCE PROCEEDS.
   (A)   Within 20 days of the receipt of said moneys, the Codes Enforcement
Officer of the city shall determine, after prior investigation, whether the
city shall instigate proceedings under the provisions of K.S.A. 12-1750 et
seq., as amended.
   (B)   If the city has determined that proceedings under K.S.A. 12-1750 et
seq., shall be initiated, it will do so immediately, but no later than 30 days
after receipt of said moneys by the city.
   (C)   Upon notification to the City Treasurer by the city’s Codes
Enforcement Officer that no proceedings shall be initiated under K.S.A. 12-1750
et seq., as amended, the city shall return all such moneys received, plus
accrued interest, to the insured or insureds as identified in the communication
from the insurance company or companies. Such return shall be accomplished
within 30 days of the receipt of said moneys from the insurance company or
companies.
(Prior Code, § 15.22.030) (Ord. 2989, passed - -1989)
§ 154.043 PAYMENT OF EXCESS PROCEEDS.
   If the city has proceeded under the provisions of K.S.A. 12-1750 et seq., as
amended, all moneys in excess of that which is ultimately necessary to comply
with said provisions for the removal of the building or structure, less salvage
value, if any, shall be paid to the insured.
(Prior Code, § 15.22.040) (Ord. 2989, passed - -1989)
§ 154.044 PAYMENTS MADE FROM FUND; LIEN.
   (A)   If the city determines with regard to a building or other structure
damaged by fire, explosion or windstorm, that it is necessary to act under
K.S.A. 12-1750 et seq., as amended, any proceeds received by the city under
authority of §
154.040(A) of this chapter relating to that building or other structure shall
be used to reimburse the city for any expenses incurred by the city in
proceeding under K.S.A. 12-1750 et seq., as amended.
   (B)   Upon reimbursement from the insurance proceeds, the city shall
immediately effect the release of the lien resulting therefrom.
   (C)   Should the expenses incurred by the city exceed the insurance proceeds
paid over to the city under §
154.040(A) of this chapter, the city shall establish a new lien as authorized
by K.S.A. 12-1750 et seq., as amended, in an amount equal to such excess
expenses incurred.
(Prior Code, § 15.22.050) (Ord. 2989, passed - -1989; Ord. 3157, passed - -
1998)
§ 154.045 CITY CLERK TO NOTIFY INSURANCE COMMISSIONER.
   The City Clerk is authorized and shall notify the Commissioner of Insurance
of the state within 14 days of the adoption of the ordinance codified in this
subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 15.22.060) (Ord. 2989, passed - -1989; Ord. 3157, passed - -
1998)
§ 154.046 APPLICATION.
   This subchapter shall apply to fire, explosion or windstorm damage to
buildings or other structures.
(Prior Code, § 15.22.070) (Ord. 2989, passed - -1989; Ord. 3157, passed - -
1998)
§ 154.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   (1)   (a)   Any person who violates any of the provisions of the code
adopted in §§
154.015 through
154.028 of this chapter or fails to comply therewith, or who violates or fails
to comply with any order made thereunder, or who builds in violation of any
detailed statement of specifications or plans submitted and approved thereunder
or any certificate or permit issued thereunder, and from which no appeal has
been taken, or who fails to by the governing body of the city or by a court of
competent jurisdiction within the time fixed in §§
154.015 through
154.028 of this chapter, shall severally for each and every such violation and
non-compliance, respectively, be guilty of a misdemeanor, punishable as
provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances. The imposition of one penalty for any
violation shall not excuse the violation or permit it to continue, and all such
persons shall be required to correct or remedy such violations or defects
within a reasonable time. When not otherwise specified, each ten days that
prohibited conditions are maintained shall constitute a separate offense.
         (b)   The application of the penalty in division (B)(1) above shall
not be held to prevent the enforced removal of prohibited conditions.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.140)
      (2)   Violation of §
154.016 of this chapter is punishable under §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 15.20.025)
(Ord. 2495, passed - -1972; Ord. 3585, passed 6-22-2021; Ord. 3602, passed 2-
15-2022; Ord. 3781, passed 6-17-2025)
