**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 155.001 DISPLAY OF MERCHANDISE.
   It is unlawful for any person, firm, association or corporation to display
or cause to be displayed merchandise in such a manner that the merchandise, or
any part thereof, projects over or across any sidewalk in the city.
(Prior Code, § 12.16.010) (Ord. 1944, passed - -1933) Penalty, see §
155.999
§ 155.002 OBSTRUCTION OF STREETS AND SIDEWALKS.
   It is unlawful for any person or persons to place on the sidewalks or in any
of the streets or alleys of the city any papers, boxes, rubbish, trash, dirt,
filth or any substance or thing that will render the sidewalks, streets or
alleys unclean. The sidewalks and alleys and spaces, back of buildings
bordering on the alleys, must be kept free and clear of any papers, boxes,
rubbish, trash or substance of any kind, and the sidewalks in front of all
business houses shall be kept by their occupants free and clear from
obstructions, filth and dirt.
(Prior Code, § 8.16.010) (Ord. 1282, passed - -1912) Penalty, see §
155.999
**** SIDEWALKS ****
§ 155.015 CONSTRUCTION; REQUIRED; RESOLUTION; NOTICE; PUBLICATION; TIME LIMIT;
BID PROCEDURE; SPECIFICATIONS.
   (A)   The Board of Commissioners of the city may, by resolution passed at
any meeting of the Board, require the building of any new sidewalk upon the
petition of 25 or more taxpayers residing in the ward in which the sidewalk is
proposed to be laid, in which resolution shall be specified the kind, the width
and the length of the sidewalk to be built, and also the time in which the same
shall be built, which shall not be later than 30 days after the passage and
publication of such resolution. Immediately upon the passage of the resolution
by the Board of Commissioners ordering any sidewalk to be built, it shall be
the duty of the Clerk to cause the publication of the resolution, also the
publication of a notice stating the name of the street or streets upon which
the sidewalk or walks are to be constructed, and the legal description of the
lots or pieces of ground upon which they abut, which resolution and notice
shall be published in two issues of the official city paper.
(Prior Code, § 12.04.010)
   (B)   (1)   It shall be the duty of the owner or owners of any property
abutting upon any sidewalk ordered to be built to build the sidewalk in
accordance with the resolution mentioned in division (A) above. If the sidewalk
is not constructed within 30 days, unless longer time is granted by the Board
of Commissioners, then it shall be and is made the duty of any Board of
Commissioners to proceed to build the sidewalk. A detailed estimate of the cost
shall be made, under oath, by the City Engineer and submitted to the Board of
Commissioners.
      (2)   Sealed proposals for construction of the sidewalk shall be invited
by an advertisement published by the City Clerk in the official city paper. The
notice shall state the time and place at which the bids will be opened, and the
Board of Commissioners shall let the work by contract to the lowest responsible
bidder if the bid does not exceed the estimate.
(Prior Code, § 12.04.020)
   (C)   All sidewalks constructed within the city shall be of cement mixed in
accordance with specifications prepared by or approved by the Engineer. It is
unlawful for any person to put down or cause to be put down within the city any
sidewalk composed in whole or part of wood or any other combustible material;
or to put down or cause to be put down any limestone flagging sidewalk or stone
or vitrified brick sidewalk without first having obtained the consent of the
Board of Commissioners given at a regular meeting of the Board and entered in
the minutes of such meeting.
(Prior Code, § 12.04.040)
(Ord. 1755, passed - -1924) Penalty, see §
155.999
§ 155.016 CONDEMNATION; REPAIR; PROCEDURE; COST ASSESSMENT.
   The Board of Commissioners may, at any time, by resolution condemn any
portion of any sidewalk whenever, in its judgment, it is deemed necessary, and
provide for the construction of a new sidewalk in accordance with the
provisions of this subchapter, or may provide for the removal of any sidewalk
without providing for any new sidewalk in its stead. No formality shall be
required to authorize the repairing of the sidewalk, but the City Engineer,
after giving five days’ notice to the owner or his or her agent, if known, of
the necessity therefor, and without notice if the lot or piece of land is
unoccupied, may make all necessary repairs at any time, keeping account of
costs thereof and reporting same to the Board of Commissioners. The city shall
levy a special assessment against the lots or pieces of land abutting on the
sidewalk so repaired for the cost of repairs together with a penalty of 20% of
the amount thereof.
(Prior Code, § 12.04.030) (Ord. 1755, passed - -1924)
§ 155.017 COST; SPECIAL ASSESSMENT.
   To pay for constructing or repairing any sidewalk within the city, special
assessment shall be levied on all lots and pieces of land abutting on any such
sidewalk, according to the frontage feet thereof.
(Prior Code, § 12.04.050) (Ord. 1755, passed - -1924)
§ 155.018 REPAIR AND CLEANING; OWNER RESPONSIBILITY.
   The owners and occupants of any real estate within the corporate limits of
the city are severally required to keep in good order and repair any sidewalk
or gutter adjoining or abutting any real estate so owned or occupied, and any
such owners and occupants are also required to keep such sidewalks and gutters
clean and free from ice, snow and other substances.
(Prior Code, § 12.04.060) (Ord. 798, passed - -1901) Penalty, see §
155.999
**** ENCROACHMENTS, OBSTRUCTIONS AND ENCLOSURES ****
§ 155.030 PROHIBITED ACTS.
   It is unlawful for any person or persons, to encroach upon, obstruct or
enclose, any of the streets, alleys, avenues and public grounds of the city, or
any part or portion of any of the streets, avenues, alleys or public grounds,
by fencing in or by building or erecting structures, houses, fences, walls,
stairs, staircases or porches upon, in or over the same.
(Prior Code, § 12.08.010) (Ord. 644, passed - -1891) Penalty, see §
155.999
§ 155.031 CONTINUANCE PROHIBITED; OWNER RESPONSIBILITY.
   It is unlawful for any person or persons now owning property within the city
encroaching upon, obstructing or enclosing in the manner mentioned in §
155.030 of this chapter, any of the streets, alleys, avenues or public grounds,
or any part or portion of the same, to permit said encroachments, obstructions
or enclosures to continue after the expiration of the time mentioned in the
notice provided for in this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 12.08.020) (Ord. 644, passed - -1891) Penalty, see §
155.999
§ 155.032 NOTICE; TIME LIMIT FOR REMOVAL; SERVICE PROCEDURE.
   (A)   Any person now or hereafter owning property encroaching upon,
obstructing or enclosing any of the streets, alleys, avenues and public
grounds, or any part of the same in the city m the manner described in §
155.030 of this chapter, shall be notified, in writing by the Codes Officer to
remove, abate and discontinue said encroachments, obstructions or enclosures
within 15 days from the time the notice is served upon them.
(Prior Code, § 12.08.030)
   (B)   (1)   The notice provided for in division (A) above shall be served by
the Codes Officer by leaving a copy thereof with the owner of the property, or
at the owner’s usual place of residence. The codes officer shall endorse upon
the original of the notice the time and manner of serving the same, and the
notice shall be filed in the office of the City Clerk and shall be deemed and
taken as prime facie evidence that service of the notice was made as set forth
in this section.
      (2)   When the person or persons to be served with the notice are non-
residents of the city, the Codes Officer shall make publication service by
publishing the notice for three consecutive days in the official paper of the
city, and the publication shall be deemed of the same force and effect as if
personal service had been made.
(Prior Code, § 12.08.040)
(Ord. 644, passed - -1891)
§ 155.033 REMOVAL BY CITY; PROCEDURE; COST ASSESSMENT.
   It shall be the duty of the Codes Officer if, at the expiration of 15 days,
the encroachments, obstructions or enclosures have not been removed, abated and
discontinued, to remove, abate and discontinue the encroachments, obstructions
and enclosures, or cause the same to be done, at the expense of the city. The
Codes Officer shall certify to the City Clerk the names of the owners of the
property, together with the descriptions of their property adjacent to the
encroachments, obstructions and enclosures, and the cost of removing, abating
or discontinuing the same, and the cost shall be assessed upon and against the
owners of the adjacent property each year by the City Manager and the Board of
Commissioners of the city, and the same shall be certified to the County Clerk
to be placed upon the tax roll and collected as other special assessments.
(Prior Code, § 12.08.050) (Ord. 644, passed - -1891)
**** EXCAVATIONS ****
§ 155.045 PERMIT REQUIRED; APPLICATION; EXCEPTIONS; BOND REQUIRED; CONDITIONS;
EXCEPTIONS; CONTENTS; FEES.
   (A)   It is unlawful for any unauthorized person, firm or corporation to
remove, destroy, cut or encumber any sidewalk, alley, curb, gutter or any
improved surface of any street or alley, or to make any opening or excavation
therein or in the area known as the parking, between the right-of-way of the
street and the adjacent private property line, or in any public parking lot, or
to lay any pipes or wires in the city, unless and until a permit has been
issued by the city authorizing such work. The permit shall be issued upon
application to the city-showing the necessity for such work and the nature of
the work proposed to be done, and upon the approval thereof by the city;
provided that, the private property owners adjacent to the above-mentioned
public property shall not be required to obtain a permit for the purpose of
planting trees, shrubs or flowers on the public property.
(Prior Code, § 12.12.010)
   (B)   No person, firm or corporation desiring to perform any excavation work
in the public property referred to in division (A) above shall be issued a
permit for such work until that person, firm or corporation has first filed
with the City Clerk a surety bond in the amount of $2,000 in favor of the city.
The condition of the bond shall be that said person, firm or corporation will
hold the city harmless from all liability arising from any work performed by
that person, firm or corporation, pursuant to the permit referred to in
division (A) above, and shall comply with the provisions of this subchapter in
every respect; provided that, all persons, firms or corporations granted a
franchise by the city or who have filed a bond with the City Clerk pursuant to
the terms of another ordinance of the city are exempt from the provisions of
this section.
(Prior Code, § 12.12.020)
   (C)   Any permit issued for making any excavation, opening or cut in any
street, curb, gutter, alley or public parking lot, including the parking and
sidewalks therein, shall specify the location of such opening, the approximate
depth and area of the authorized excavation or cutting, and the curb, gutter,
sidewalk or other improvement to be cut or removed and rebuilt or replaced by
the permittee within a period to be limited by the permit.
(Prior Code, § 12.12.030)
   (D)   Individual applicants for such permit, provided that said work is
approved by the city, will be issued a permit at no cost; provided, however,
that, all persons, firms or corporations granted a franchise by the city are
exempt from the provisions of this section.
(Prior Code, § 12.12.040)
(Ord. 2563, passed - -1975; Ord. 3041, passed - -1992; Ord. 3489, passed 12-13-
2016) Penalty, see §
155.999
§ 155.046 SAFETY REQUIREMENTS.
   It is unlawful for any person, firm or corporation to leave any excavation
open or uncovered, or to leave any building materials, vehicle or like
obstruction in any street, alley, sidewalk, curb, gutter, public parking lot or
public way in the city during the course of any improvement, unless the same is
guarded at all times by barricades and unless any such work or obstructions are
guarded with lights sufficient in number and so placed as to warn and guard
vehicles and pedestrians from injury or danger. Emergency services shall be
notified of all such obstructions.
(Prior Code, § 12.12.050) (Ord. 2563, passed - -1975) Penalty, see §
155.999
§ 155.047 REPAIR AND REPLACEMENT; PERMIT HOLDER RESPONSIBILITY.
   If any street, pavement, public parking lot, sidewalk or curb and gutter is
damaged by any person operating under a permit issued under the provisions of
this subchapter, the permit holder shall repair or replace the same at his or
her own expense, and he or she shall be liable on his or her bond for the
satisfactory repair of the same. If the total amount of the bond is inadequate
to make satisfactory repairs or pay damages which may have been sustained, the
permit holder shall be liable for any additional amount required to make the
repairs or pay damages. The permit holder shall under no circumstances be
considered as an agent of the city. All work or repairs shall be done under the
supervision of the Public Works Director. If repairs are not made, or if
unsatisfactorily made, the city may complete the same and charge the costs to
the permit holder. The permit holder shall further be subject to punishment
under the criminal provisions of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 12.12.060) (Ord. 2563, passed - -1975) Penalty, see §
155.999
§ 155.048 RESTORATION TO ORIGINAL CONDITION; MAINTENANCE.
   It shall be the duty of any person, firm or corporation making an excavation
in any of the improved or unimproved streets, alleys or other public grounds of
the city to restore such excavation to its original condition within 14 days
from the commencement of work or the date the permit was issued (whichever
shall first occur), and to maintain the same for a period of six months
following the replacement of the excavated materials. If restoration is not
completed within the allowed 14-day time period, the same person, firm or
corporation will be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and upon conviction, will be
assessed a penalty of $150 per day for each day that restoration is not
completed. If the site is not properly maintained for six months following
replacement of excavated materials and following notice of the same, a penalty
of $150 per day may be assessed for each day that the maintenance is not
completed.
(Prior Code, § 12.12.090) (Ord. 2563, passed - -1975; Ord. 3489, passed 12-13-
2016) Penalty, see §
155.999
§ 155.049 PUBLIC WORKS DIRECTOR; SUPERVISION AND INSPECTION DUTIES.
   The Public Works Director, or any other city official so designated by the
City Manager, shall be responsible for monitoring and ensuring that all
excavations, openings and cuts in the streets, alleys, sidewalks, curbs and
gutters of the city, are in accordance with the provisions of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 12.12.100) (Ord. 2563, passed - -1975)
**** POLES, CABLES AND WIRES ****
§ 155.060 OVER STREETS; MINIMUM HEIGHT; EXCEPTIONS.
   Any and all persons or corporations using the streets, avenues or alleys of
the city for the purpose of placing, locating and maintaining cables or wires
in, over, along or across the streets, avenues or alleys of the city shall
place, locate and maintain such cables or wires, at the point where the cables
or wires cross any street, avenue or alley, at least 25 feet above the surface
of the street, avenue or alley; provided that, this provision shall not in any
way be construed to affect the trolley wire of any street railway company
operating cars and maintaining trolley wires upon and along any of the streets,
avenues or alleys of the city; provided further that, whenever any person, firm
or corporation may have a good and lawful reason for passing under any cable or
wires, as mentioned above, which cable or wires may be lower than 25 feet above
the surface of the street, avenue and alley over which the same may pass, the
cable or wires shall be raised at the expense of the person, firm or
corporation maintaining the cable or wires, upon the person desiring to have
the cable or wires raised notifying the person, firm or corporation owning or
maintaining the cable or wires, of his or her desire.
(Prior Code, § 12.20.010) (Ord. 1036, passed - -1907) Penalty, see §
155.999
§ 155.061 ERECTION; PERMIT REQUIRED; APPLICATION; CONTENTS.
   It is unlawful for any company or corporation to erect any telegraph or
telephone pole or poles, or string any wires thereon within the city, without
first filing with the codes officer a written application stating the intended
location of each pole, the kind and height thereof and the height at which
wires are to be strung, and receiving from the codes officer a permit to erect
the same.
(Prior Code, § 12.20.020) (Ord. 821, passed - -1902) Penalty, see §
155.999
§ 155.062 PERMIT; ISSUANCE.
   (A)   It shall be the duty of the Codes Officer, upon the filing of any
application for a permit, to inspect the location or locations applied for, and
if, in his or her judgment, such location or locations are suitable and proper,
and the kind and height of poles suitable, and the height at which wires are to
be placed sufficient, he or she shall issue to such company or corporation a
permit.
   (B)   If, in his or her judgment, the interests of the public will be better
protected by other locations, or a different kind of poles, or different
construction, he or she will issue his or her permit accordingly, keeping in
view proper protection to drainage, shade trees, ingress and egress to and from
property and the rights and interests of the public generally.
(Prior Code, § 12.20.030) (Ord. 821, passed - -1902)
§ 155.063 BOARD OF COMMISSIONERS; DECISION.
   If a company or corporation is not satisfied with the locations or kind of
poles or other items of construction designated by the Codes Officer, such
company or corporation may notify the City Manager, who shall convene the Board
of Commissioners without unnecessary delay, and the Board of Commissioners
shall by resolution designate the locations of the poles, the kind and height
thereof and the height at which the wires shall be strung upon the line or part
of line desired to be erected.
(Prior Code, § 12.20.040) (Ord. 821, passed - -1902)
§ 155.064 COMPLAINT; PROCEDURE; CITY ACTION.
   (A)   Any person owning or occupying any property abutting upon any street
or alley along which any telegraph or telephone line extends who feels that his
or her rights have been infringed upon by the location, character or condition
of the line, may call upon the Codes Officer to inspect the same and if, in his
or her opinion, any changes should be made in the location or construction of
the line, including its poles, wires and all fixtures and appliances, then he
or she shall notify the company or corporation owning the line, or its agents,
designating the changes desired. If the company does not within three days
commence to make such alterations and finish the same within reasonable time,
he or she shall notify the City Manager and Board of Commissioners and also
notify the company or its agents to appear before the Board of Commissioners at
a date to be named in the notice, when an opportunity will be given the company
or its agents to be heard regarding such alterations.
   (B)   After the hearing, the Board of Commissioners may by resolution direct
such alterations to be made in the kind and location of poles, and in the
height at which the wires shall run, and any such changes in construction as
may seem to the Board of Commissioners proper. It shall be the duty of the
corporation to proceed at once to make such alterations.
(Prior Code, § 12.20.050) (Ord. 821, passed - -1902)
§ 155.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person, firm, association or corporation violating any of the
provisions of §
155.001 of this chapter shall be adjudged guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 12.16.020)
   (C)   Any person or persons who violate the provisions of §
155.002 of this chapter shall, upon conviction, be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 8.16.020)
   (D)   Any person who puts down or causes to be put down within the city any
sidewalk in violation of the provisions and requirements of §§
155.015 through
155.018 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances. Such sidewalk may be considered an
obstruction in the street and may be removed by the city at any time at the
cost of the owner of the property.
(Prior Code, § 12.04.070)
   (E)   Any person or persons who violate the provisions of §§
155.030 through
155.033 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 12.08.060)
   (F)   Any person, firm or corporation making any excavation, opening or cuts
in the streets, alleys, sidewalks, curbs, gutters or other public grounds of
the city without first complying with the provisions of §§
155.045 through
155.049 of this chapter, or who violates any of the provisions of §§
155.045 through
155.049 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon
conviction, shall be punished in the amount of $150 for each count and may be
assessed the costs, labor and/or expenses required to restore the property as
set out in §§
155.045 through
155.049 of this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 12.12.120)
   (G)   Any company or corporation erecting or having erected any telephone or
telegraph pole or poles, or stringing any wire or wires in violation of the
provisions of §§
155.060 through
155.064 of this chapter, or maintaining such pole or poles or wire or wires at
any point or points from which they have been ordered to be removed by
resolution of the Board of Commissioners, shall be subject to a penalty of $5
per day, payable to the city for each pole for each and every day such pole or
poles are permitted to stand; provided that, in case any pole or poles are
ordered to be removed, the company shall have five days from the passage of
such resolution before the penalty shall be attached. All penalties are to be
recovered by civil action in any court having competent jurisdiction, and, in
addition to such penalties, every agent, employee or other person who erects or
who shall in any manner aid or assist in the erection or maintenance of any
such telephone or telegraph pole or poles, or the stringing of any wire or
wires contrary to the provisions of §§
155.060 through
155.064 of this chapter shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction,
shall be punished as provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 12.20.060)
(Ord. 644, passed - -1891; Ord. 798, passed - -1901; Ord. 821, passed - -1902;
Ord. 1282, passed - -1912; Ord. 1755, passed - -1924; Ord. 1944, passed - -

**** STREET TREES ****
§ 156.001 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   PARK TREES. Trees, shrubs, bushes and all other woody vegetation in public
parks having individual names, and in all areas owned by the city to which the
public has free access as a park.
   STREET TREES. Trees, shrubs, bushes and all other woody vegetation on land
lying between property lines on either side of all streets, avenues or ways
within the city.
(Prior Code, § 12.24.010) (Ord. 2527, passed - -1974)
§ 156.002 PERMITTED SPECIES.
   (A)   The following list constitutes the official street tree species list
for the city.
   (B)   No species other than those included in this list may be planted as
street trees without permission from the city’s Park Commission:
Small Trees Medium Trees Large Trees

Flowering Crabapple (sp.) Honeylocust                   Bur Oak
Paper Mulberry            Pin Oak                       English Oak

Hawthorn (sp.)            Red Mulberry (male)           American Sycamore

                                                      (Cottonless)
                                                      Basswood
 
(Prior Code, § 12.24.020) (Ord. 2527, passed - -1974) Penalty, see §
156.999
§ 156.003 SPACING REQUIREMENTS.
   The spacing of street trees shall be in accordance with the three species
size classes listed in §
156.002 of this chapter, and no trees may be planted closer together than the
following:
   (A)   Small trees: 30 feet;
   (B)   Medium trees: 40 feet; and
   (C)   Large trees: 50 feet.
(Prior Code, § 12.24.030) (Ord. 2527, passed - -1974) Penalty, see §
156.999
§ 156.004 DISTANCE FROM CURB AND SIDEWALK.
   The distance trees may be planted from curbs or curblines and sidewalks
shall be in accordance with the three species size classes listed in §
156.002 of this chapter, and no trees may be planted closer than the following
to any curb or sidewalk:
   (A)   Small trees: two feet;
   (B)   Medium trees: three feet; and
   (C)   Large trees: four feet.
(Prior Code, § 12.24.040) (Ord. 2527, passed - -1974) Penalty, see §
156.999
§ 156.005 DISTANCE FROM STREET CORNERS AND FIREPLUGS.
   No street tree shall be planted closer than 20 feet to any street corner,
measured from the point of nearest-intersecting curbs or curblines. No street
tree shall be planted closer than ten feet to any fireplug.
(Prior Code, § 12.24.050) (Ord. 2527, passed - -1974) Penalty, see §
156.999
§ 156.006 DISTANCE FROM UTILITIES.
   No street trees other than those species listed as small trees in §
156.002 of this chapter may be planted under or within ten lateral feet of any
overhead utility wire, or over or within five lateral feet of any underground
waterline, sewer line, transmission line or other utility.
(Prior Code, § 12.24.060) (Ord. 2527, passed - -1974) Penalty, see §
156.999
§ 156.007 CARE AND MAINTENANCE.
   (A)   The owners and occupants of any real estate within the corporate
limits of the city are severally required to prune, maintain and remove any and
all plants, shrubs or trees as may be necessary to ensure safety. Said owner or
occupant may be compelled to prune, remove or maintain said trees and shrubs by
the Codes Officer of the city when it is deemed said trees and shrubs are
diseased, dead, damaged or decaying to such an extent that they pose a safety
hazard. The owners or occupiers of said property will be given a written notice
from the Codes Officer and shall have ten days after said notice to effectuate
said repair, removal or maintenance of said plants, trees and shrubs or the
city may take appropriate legal action.
   (B)   The city shall have the right to plant, prune, maintain and remove,
plants, trees and shrubs within the lines of all streets, alleys, avenues,
lanes, squares and public grounds, as may be necessary to ensure the safety or
to preserve the symmetry and beauty of such public grounds. In the event the
city is required to fulfill the owners’ or occupiers’ responsibilities under
this section the cost associated with providing said service shall be borne by
the owners and occupiers of the property, shall be assessed against the real
estate, shall be taxed against said real estate and shall be collected by the
city in any appropriate means necessary.
(Prior Code, § 12.24.070) (Ord. 2527, passed - -1974; Ord. 3137, passed - -
1996) Penalty, see §
156.999
§ 156.008 OBSTRUCTION OF STREET LAMP, INTERSECTION OR TRAFFIC-CONTROL DEVICE
PROHIBITED.
   Every owner of any tree overhanging any street or right-of-way within the
city shall prune the branches so that the branches do not obstruct the light
from any street lamp or obstruct the view of any street intersection, and so
that there is a clear space of eight feet above the surface of the street or
right-of-way. Such owners shall remove all dead, diseased or dangerous trees or
broken or decayed limbs which constitute a menace to the safety of the public.
The city shall have the right to prune any tree or shrub on private property
when it interferes with the proper spread of light along the street from a
streetlight, or interferes with visibility of any traffic-control device or
sign.
(Prior Code, § 12.24.080) (Ord. 2527, passed - -1974) Penalty, see §
156.999
§ 156.009 DEAD, DISEASED TREES; REMOVAL.
   The city shall have the right to the removal of any dead or diseased trees
on public or private property within the city when such trees constitute a
hazard to life and property, or harbor insects or disease which constitutes a
potential threat to other trees within the city. Removal shall be done by
owners at their own expense within 60 days after the date of service of the
notice. In the event of the failure of owners to comply with such provisions,
the city shall have the authority to remove such trees and charge the cost of
removal on the owner’s property tax notice.
(Prior Code, § 12.24.090) (Ord. 2527, passed - -1974; Ord. 3598, passed 12-7-
2021) Penalty, see §
156.999
§ 156.010 PARK COMMISSION; INTERFERENCE PROHIBITED.
   (A)   It is unlawful for any person to prevent, delay or interfere with the
city’s Park Commission, or any of its agents or servants, while engaging in and
about the planting, cultivating, mulching, pruning, spraying or removing of any
street trees, park trees or trees on private grounds, as authorized in this
subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 12.24.100)
   (B)   The Board of Commissioners shall have the right to review the conduct,
acts and decisions of the city’s Park Commission. Any person may appeal from
any ruling or order of the city’s Park Commission to the Board of
Commissioners, who shall hear the matter and make a final decision.
(Prior Code, § 12.24.120)
(Ord. 2527, passed - -1974) Penalty, see §
156.999
§ 156.011 ARBORIST; LICENSE; FEE; INSURANCE REQUIRED.
   It is unlawful for any person or firm to engage in the business or
occupation of trimming, pruning, treating or removing street or park trees
within the city without first applying for and procuring a license. The license
fee shall be $30 annually, in advance; provided, however, that, no license
shall be required of any public service company or city employee doing such
work in the pursuit of their public service endeavors. Before any license is
issued, each applicant shall first file evidence of possession of liability
insurance in the minimum amounts of $25,000 for bodily injury and $10,000
property damage indemnifying the city and any person injured or damaged
resulting from the pursuit of such endeavors as described in this section.
(Prior Code, § 12.24.110) (Ord. 2527, passed - -1974; Ord. 3042, passed - -
1992) Penalty, see §
156.999
§ 156.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person violating any provision of §§
156.001 through
156.011 of this chapter shall, upon conviction or plea of guilty be punished as
provided in §
10.999 of this code of ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 12.24.130) (Ord. 2527, passed - -1974)
CHAPTER 157: AIRPORT REGULATIONS
Section
   
157.001   Short title
   
157.002   Definitions
   
157.003   Airport Airspace Protection Commission; zones established
   
157.004   Airport Zone height limitations
   
157.005   Construction requirements; supplemental notice
   
157.006   Use restrictions
   
157.007   Non-conforming uses
   
157.008   Permits
   
157.009   Administration and enforcement
   
157.010   Appeals
   
157.011   Airport Board of Appeals; review
   
157.012   Judicial review
   
157.013   Conflicting regulations
 
   
157.999   Penalty
§ 157.001 SHORT TITLE.
   This chapter shall be known and may be cited as the “Fort Scott Municipal
Airport Height and Hazard Regulations”.
(Prior Code, § 8.36.010) (Ord. 3415, passed 7-17-2012)
§ 157.002 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   ABANDONMENT. Any item which has ceased to be used for its designed and
intended purpose. The factors used in determining whether or not an item has
been abandoned include, but are not limited to, the following:
      (1)   Present operability and functional utility of the item;
      (2)   The date of last effective use of the item;
      (3)   The condition of disrepair or damage;
      (4)   The last time an effort was made to repair or rehabilitate the
item;
      (5)   The status of registration or licensing of the item;
      (6)   The age and degree of obsolescence;
      (7)   The cost of rehabilitation or repair of the item when compared to
its market value; or
      (8)   The nature of the area and location of the item.
   AIRPORT. The Fort Scott Municipal Airport.
   AIRPORT AIRSPACE PROTECTION COMMISSION. The Commission appointed by the city
and Bourbon County and Crawford County, comprised of two members of the Fort
Scott Airport Advisory Board, the City Zoning Administrator and one appointee
each from the two counties, to function as the temporary Airport Zoning
Commission for the purposes of hosting a public hearing and recommending to the
City Commission adoption of height and hazard regulations.
   AIRPORT BOARD OF APPEALS (BOARD OF APPEALS). The Appeals Board to consider
appeals from the enforcement of these regulations, to be appointed by the city
and Bourbon County and Crawford County, comprised of two members of the Fort
Scott Airport Advisory Board, the City Zoning Administrator and one appointee
each from the two counties.
   AIRPORT ELEVATION. The highest point of the airport’s usable landing area
measured in feet above sea level (918 feet above mean sea level for FSK).
   AIRPORT HAZARD. Any structure or tree or use of land which obstructs the
airspace required for the flight of aircraft in landing or taking-off or
permanently raises the published or planned approach minimums at any airport or
is otherwise hazardous to such landing or taking-off of aircraft.
   AIRPORT HAZARD AREA. Also referred to as the AIRPORT AIRSPACE PROTECTION
AREA. Any area of land or water upon which an airport hazard might be
established, including any which may permanently raise the published or planned
approach minimums of the airport, if not prevented as provided in this chapter
and as depicted on the “Airport Hazard Area Map” adopted by and made a part of
this chapter; and including the FAA part 77, Civil Airport Imaginary Surfaces,
which consist of the horizontal surface, conical surface, primary surface,
approach surface, transitional surface and precision approach surfaces; and the
terminal instrument procedures (TERPS) surfaces and the vertically guided
approach surfaces (VGAS) for the airport as referenced on the State Department
of Transportation’s (KDOT) “Kansas Airspace Awareness Tool”, at http:/
www.ksdot.org/airspacetool.
   AIRPORT HAZARD AREA MAP. The map depicting the airspace airport airspace
protection area and attached to the ordinance from which this chapter derives
as Exhibit A, and made a part hereof.
   AIRPORT LAYOUT PLAN (ALP). A plan adopted by the City Commission that
depicts existing airport facilities and proposed developments as determined
from the airport planners’ review of the aviation activity forecasts, facility
requirements and alternatives analysis.
   AIRPORT MANAGER or MANAGER. The administrative staff assigned by the city to
manage the Fort Scott Municipal Airport and to administer these regulations.
   APPROACH MINIMUMS. The minimum ceiling or visibility under which an aircraft
may be landed with the use of a published approach procedure. It also means
planned non-precision or precision instrument approach minimums so indicated on
an approved airport layout plan or any other planning document.
   APPROACH SURFACE. A surface longitudinally centered on the extended runway
centerline, extending outward and upward from the end of the primary surface
and at the same slope as the approach zone height limitation slope set forth in
§
157.004 of this chapter. In plan, the perimeter of the APPROACH SURFACE
coincides with the perimeter of the approach zone.
   CITY COMMISSION. The City Commission for the City of Fort Scott Kansas.
   CONICAL SURFACE. A surface extending outward and upward from the periphery
of the horizontal surface at a slope of 20 feet horizontally to each foot
vertically (20:1) for a horizontal distance of 4,000 feet.
   CONSTRUCT A MUNICIPAL SOLID WASTE LANDFILL (MSWLF). To excavate or grade
land, or raise structures, to prepare a municipal solid waste landfill as
permitted by the appropriate regulatory or permitting authority.
   FAA. The Federal Aviation Administration.
   FSK. The three-letter identifier assigned by the Federal Aviation
Administration to the public-use airport owned and operated by the city, as a
“location identifier” for airport navigation aid, weather station and manned
air traffic control facility in air traffic control, telecommunications,
computer programming, weather reports and related services.
   FORT SCOTT AIRPORT (FORT SCOTT MUNICIPAL AIRPORT FSK). The public-use
airport owned and operated by the City of Fort Scott, Kansas.
   HAZARD TO AIR NAVIGATION. An obstruction determined to have a substantial
adverse effect on the safe and efficient utilization of the navigable airspace.
   HAZARDOUS WILDLIFE. Species of wildlife (birds, mammals, reptiles),
including feral animals and domesticated animals not under control, that are
associated with aircraft strike problems, are capable of causing structural
damage to airport facilities, or act as attractants to other wildlife that pose
a strike hazard.
   HEIGHT. For the purpose of determining the height limits in all zones set
forth in this chapter and shown on the Fort Scott Airport Hazard Area Map, the
datum shall be mean HEIGHTS as measured from the elevations of FSK Runway 18-36
unless otherwise specified.
   HORIZONTAL SURFACE. A horizontal plane 150 feet above the established
airport elevation, the perimeter of which in plan coincides with the perimeter
of the horizontal zone.
   LARGER THAN UTILITY RUNWAY. A runway that is constructed for and intended to
be used by propeller driven aircraft of greater than 12,500 pounds maximum
gross weight and jet powered aircraft (FSK Runway 18-36).
   NON-CONFORMING USE. Any pre-existing structure, object of natural growth or
use of land which is inconsistent with the provisions of this chapter or an
amendment thereto.
   OBSTRUCTION. Any structure, growth, or other object, including a mobile
object, which exceeds a limiting height set forth in §
157.004 of this chapter.
   PERSON. An individual, firm, partnership, corporation, company, association
joint stock association or government entity; includes a trustee, a receiver,
an assignee or a similar representative of any of them.
   PRIMARY SURFACE. A surface longitudinally centered on a runway. When the
runway has a specially prepared hard surface, the PRIMARY SURFACE extends 200
feet beyond each end of that runway. The width of the PRIMARY SURFACE is set
forth in §
157.003 of this chapter. The elevation of any point on the PRIMARY SURFACE is
the same as the elevation of the nearest point on the runway centerline.
   RUNWAY. A defined area on an airport prepared for landing and taking-off of
aircraft along its length.
   RUNWAY, NON-PRECISION INSTRUMENT APPROACH. A runway having an existing
instrument approach procedure utilizing air navigation facilities with only
horizontal guidance, or area type navigation equipment, for which a straight-in
non-precision instrument approach procedure has been approved or planned (FSK
Runway 18-36 existing).
   RUNWAY, PRECISION INSTRUMENT APPROACH or INSTRUMENT APPROACH PROCEDURE
(TAP). A series of predetermined maneuvers for the orderly transfer of an
aircraft under instrument flight conditions from the beginning of the initial
approach to a landing, or to a point from which a landing may be made visually
(FSK Runway 18-36 ultimate).
   SOLID WASTE. Any garbage, or refuse, sludge from a wastewater treatment
plant, water supply treatment plant or air pollution control facility and other
discarded material, including solid, liquid, semi-solid or contained gaseous
material resulting from industrial, commercial, mining and agricultural
operations, and from community activities, but does not include solid or
dissolved materials in domestic sewage, or solid or dissolved materials in
irrigation return flows or industrial discharges that are point sources subject
to permit under PDFS regulations.
   STRUCTURE. An object, including a mobile object, constructed or installed by
humans, including but without limitation, buildings, towers, cranes,
smokestacks, earth formation and overhead transmission lines.
   TERMINAL INSTRUMENT PROCEDURES (TERPS). Surfaces that are constructed from
the electronic signals transmitted by ground-based and satellite-based air
navigation electronic equipment, which are the instrument procedures that
aircraft pilots use to fly between airports and land on runways.
   TRANSITIONAL SURFACES. These surfaces extend outward at ninety degree angles
to the runway centerline and the runway centerline extended at a slope of seven
feet horizontally for each foot vertically (7:1) from the sides of the primary
and approach surfaces to where they intersect the horizontal and conical
surfaces. TRANSITIONAL SURFACES for those portions of the precision approach
surfaces, which project through and beyond the limits of the conical surface,
extend a distance of 5,000 feet measured horizontally from the edge of the
approach surface and at 90-degree angles to the extended runway centerline.
   TREE. Any object of natural growth.
   VERTICALLY GUIDED APPROACH SURFACES (VGAS). Satellite-based approach
surfaces which are established to protect instrument approach procedures (IAP)
that provide vertical guidance and lower approach minima. Examples of landings
systems protected by VGAS include instrument landing system (ILS) based
approaches and GPS based procedures utilizing a wide area augmentation system
(WAAS) with localizer performance with vertical guidance (LPV); and which can
improve airport capacity when ground based systems are out of service, and
provide accurate, reliable access to more airports previously not served by
precision approaches.
   WILDLIFE ATTRACTANTS. Any human-made structure, land-use practice or human-
made or natural geographic feature that can attract or sustain hazardous
wildlife within the airport hazard area. These ATTRACTANTS can include
architectural features, landscaping, waste disposal sites, wastewater treatment
facilities, agricultural or aquaculture activities, surface mining or wetlands.
   ZONES: APPROACH, TRANSITIONAL, HORIZONTAL, CONICAL, VGAS AND TERPS. The
zones that are set forth in §
157.003 of this chapter.
   ZONING ADMINISTRATOR. The staff person assigned by the city to administer
the city zoning regulations.
(Prior Code, § 8.36.020) (Ord. 3415, passed 7-17-2012)
Editor’s note:
   Exhibit A referenced in this section is not set out herein, but is on file
in the office of the City Clerk.
§ 157.003 AIRPORT AIRSPACE PROTECTION COMMISSION; ZONES ESTABLISHED.
   (A)   Prior to establishing the zones that are set forth in this chapter,
there shall be submitted to the City Commission reports and recommendations by
the Fort Scott Airport Airspace Protection Commission (hereafter referred to as
the “Commission”), which Commission shall:
      (1)   Recommend the boundaries of the various zones that are set forth;
      (2)   Recommend the height and hazard regulations to be adopted therefor;
      (3)   Make a preliminary report which considers:
         (a)   The character of the flying operations currently conducted and
expected to be conducted at the airport;
         (b)   The nature of the terrain within the airport hazard area;
         (c)   The character of the neighborhood in and surrounding the airport
hazard area; and
         (d)   The uses to which the property to be regulated is put and
adaptable.
      (4)   Hold a public hearing on the recommended regulations and submit a
final report and recommendations to the City Commission.
   (B)   The airport height and hazard regulations shall apply to the airport
hazard areas as recommended by the airspace protection commission and adopted
by the City Commission. The airport hazard area, wherein the height and hazard
regulations apply, include all of the land area as defined in these
regulations, generally lying beneath the approach surfaces, transitional
surfaces, horizontal surfaces, conical surfaces and any other territory
surrounding the Fort Scott Airport divided into zones, including the
established and ultimate minimum approach surfaces and precision approaches, as
depicted on the Fort Scott Airport Hazard Area Map Exhibit A (which is based on
the KDOT “Kansas Airspace Awareness Tool” at http:/www.ksdot.org/airspacetool),
and Exhibit B (which is based on the FSK Airport Airspace Drawing, existing and
ultimate, as adopted in the FSK ALP), both of which are made a part hereof and
attached to the ordinance from which this chapter derives. The airport hazard
area map exhibits are provided to the public as an attachment to the ordinance
from which this chapter derives for illustrative purposes only in order to
generally show where these regulations apply; and they are not to be relied
upon as regulatory tools. For administrative purposes, when applying these
regulations on a case-by-case basis, the standard FAA procedures for
determining compliance of proposed structures with height and hazard standards
should be relied upon as the starting point for interpreting the applicability
of these regulations.
   (C)   An area located in more than one of the following zones is considered
to be only in the zone with the more restrictive height limitation. The various
zones are hereby established and defined as follows.
      (1)   FAR part 77, Surfaces, sub-part C, Civil Airport Imaginary Surfaces
Zones. The horizontal surface, conical surface, primary surface, approach
surface and transitional surface as designated by the FAA.
         (a)   Runway Larger than Utility with a Visibility Minimum Greater
than 3/4- Mile Non-Precision Instrument Approach Zone (34:1). The inner edge of
this approach zone coincides with the width of the primary surface and is 500
feet wide. The approach zone expands outward uniformly to a width of 3,500 feet
at a horizontal distance of 10,000 feet from the primary surface. Its
centerline is the continuation of the centerline of the runway (FSK Runway 18-
36, existing).
         (b)   Runway Larger Than Utility With a Visibility Minimum Less Than
3/4-Mile Precision Instrument Approach Zone (50:1). The inner edge of this
approach zone coincides with the width of the primary surface and is 1,000 feet
wide. The approach zone expands outward uniformly to a width of 16,000 feet at
a horizontal distance of 50,000 feet from the primary surface. Its centerline
is the continuation of the centerline of the runway (FSK Runway 18-36,
ultimate).
         (c)   Transitional Zone. The transitional zones are the areas beneath
the transitional surfaces.
         (d)   Horizontal Zone. The horizontal zone is established by swinging
arcs of 5,000 feet radii for all runways designated utility or visual and
10,000 feet for all others from the center of each end of the primary surface
of each runway and connecting the adjacent arcs by drawing lines tangent to
those arcs. The horizontal zone does not include the approach and transitional
zones.
         (e)   Conical Zone. The conical zone is established as the area that
commences at the periphery of the horizontal zone and extends outward therefrom
a horizontal distance of 4,000 feet.
      (2)   Runway Approach Minimum Zones. The approach zones to the runways,
the minimum ceiling or visibility under which an aircraft may be landed with
the use of a published approach procedure; and the planned non-precision or
precision instrument approach minimums so indicated on the approved airport
layout plan (ALP) or the airport hazard area map, which depicts the terminal
instrument procedures (TERPS) surfaces and the vertically guided approach
surfaces (VGAS) for the airport as referenced on the State Department of
Transportation’s (KDOT) “Kansas Airspace Awareness Tool” at http:/
www.ksdot.org/airspacetool.
      (3)   Terminal Instrument Procedures Surface (TERPS) Zones. Can extend
ten nautical miles from a runway, constructed from the electronic signals
transmitted by ground and space based air navigation electronic equipment,
which instrument procedures aircraft pilots use to fly between airports and
land on runways.
      (4)   Vertically Guided Approach Surfaces (VGAS) Zones. Provide lower
minima for approach procedures that do not rely on ground based navigational
systems, including instrument landing system (ILS), to improve airport capacity
when ground based systems are out of service; for better access to runways with
terrain or airspace constraints using curved RNAV legs and narrower protected
surfaces; and for improved safety by eliminating circling maneuvers and
providing laterally and vertically guided approaches not available through
conventional ground-based navigational aid (NAVAID) procedures or through
existing area navigation (RNAV) procedures.
(Prior Code, § 8.36.030) (Ord. 3415, passed 7-17-2012)
§ 157.004 AIRPORT ZONE HEIGHT LIMITATIONS.
   (A)   Except as otherwise provided in this chapter, no structure shall be
erected, altered or maintained, and no tree shall be allowed to grow in any
zone created by this chapter to a height in excess of the applicable height
herein established for such zone.
   (B)   Such applicable height limitations are hereby established for each of
the zones in question as follows.
      (1)   FAR part 77, Surfaces, sub-part C, Civil Airport Imaginary Surfaces
Zones. The horizontal surface, conical surface, primary surface, approach
surface and transitional surface as designated by the FAA.
         (a)   Runway Larger Than Utility With a Visibility Minimum Greater
Than 3/4- Mile Non-Precision Instrument Approach Zone. Slopes 34 feet outward
for each foot upward (34:1) beginning at the end of and at the same elevation
as the primary surface and extending to a horizontal distance of 10,000 feet
along the extended centerlines of each runway (FSK Runway 18-36, existing).
         (b)   Runway Larger Than Utility With a Visibility Minimum Less Than
3/4-Mile Precision Instrument Approach Zone. Slopes 50 feet outward for each
foot upward (50:1) beginning at the end of and at the same elevation as the
primary surface and extending to a horizontal distance of 50,000 feet along the
extended centerlines of each runway (FSK Runway 18-36, ultimate).
         (c)   Transitional Zones. Slope seven feet outward for each foot
upward (7:1) beginning at the sides of and at the same elevation as the primary
surface and the approach surface, and extending to a height of 100 feet above
the elevations each runway. In addition to the foregoing, there are established
height limits sloping seven feet outward for each foot upward beginning at the
sides of and at the same elevation as the approach surface of each runway, and
extending to where they intersect the conical surface. Where the precision
instrument runway approach zone projects beyond the conical zone, there are
established height limits sloping seven feet outward for each foot upward
beginning at the sides of and at the same elevation as the approach surface and
extending a horizontal distance of 5,000 feet measured at 90-degree angles to
the extended runway centerline.
         (d)   Horizontal Zone. Established at 150 feet above the airport
elevation.
         (e)   Conical Zone. Slopes 20 feet outward for each foot upward (20:1)
beginning at the periphery of the horizontal zone and at 150 feet above the
airport elevations and extending to a height of 350 feet above the airport
elevations.
      (2)   Runway Approach Minimum Zones. The slopes established by the
minimum ceiling or visibility under which an aircraft may be landed with the
use of a published approach procedure; and by the planned non-precision or
precision instrument approach minimums so indicated on an approved airport
layout plan or any other planning document.
      (3)   Terminal Instrument Procedures (TERPS) Surface Zones. The slopes
established by the electronic signals transmitted by ground and space based air
navigation electronic equipment, which instrument procedures aircraft pilots
use to fly between airports and land on runways, and as referenced on the State
Department of Transportation’s (KDOT) “Kansas Airspace Awareness Tool” http:/
www.ksdot.org/airspacetool.
      (4)   Vertically Guided Approach Surfaces (VGAS) Zones. The slopes
established by the VGAS approach surfaces longitudinally centered on the
extended runway centerline beginning at the runway threshold and extending
outward and upward at a slope of 40 feet outward for each foot upward (40:1)
(2.5%) for a horizontal distance of 20,200 feet. The surface is 2,000 feet wide
(1,000 feet either side of centerline) at the runway threshold and expands to a
width of 8,000 feet at 10,200 feet from threshold. From 10,200 to 20,200 feet
the surface is 8,000 feet wide (4,000 feet either side) and parallel to the
runway centerline extended and referenced on the State Department of
Transportation’s (KDOT) “Kansas Airspace Awareness Tool” http:/www.ksdot.org/
airspacetool.
(Prior Code, § 8.36.040) (Ord. 3415, passed 7-17-2012)
§ 157.005 CONSTRUCTION REQUIREMENTS; SUPPLEMENTAL NOTICE.
   In order to comply with §§
157.003 and
157.004 of this chapter, as well as relevant FAA Advisory Circulars, including
hazardous wildlife attractants on or near airports, AC No: 150/5200-33B, this
section is established to require notice of construction or alteration to any
object(s) that potentially affects the navigable airspace of the Fort Scott
Municipal Airport. Any application for a permit directed to the City Commission
that potentially affects the navigable airspace of the Fort Scott Municipal
Airport must be supplemented by a completed and submitted Federal Aviation
Administration (FAA) Form 7460-1 (2-99), Notice of Proposed Construction or
Alteration. Any application to construct or alter any wildlife attractant or a
proposed municipal solid waste landfill shall be supplemented by a completed
and submitted FAA Form 7460-1 (2-99).
(Prior Code, § 8.36.050) (Ord. 3415, passed 7-17-2012)
§ 157.006 USE RESTRICTIONS.
   Notwithstanding any other provisions of this chapter, no use may be made of
land or water within any zone established by this chapter in such a manner as
to create electrical interference with navigational signals or radio
communication between the airport and aircraft, make it difficult for pilots to
distinguish between airport lights and others, result in glare in the eyes of
pilots using the airport, impair visibility in the vicinity of the airport,
create wildlife attractants or habitat for hazardous wildlife or otherwise in
any way endanger or interfere with the landing, takeoff or maneuvering of
aircraft intending to use the airport. Furthermore, no use may be made of the
land or water within any zone established by this chapter that would result in
permanently raising the published or planned approach minimums.
(Prior Code, § 8.36.060) (Ord. 3415, passed 7-17-2012)
§ 157.007 NON-CONFORMING USES.
   The regulations prescribed in this chapter shall not be construed to require
the removal, lowering or other change or alteration of any structure or tree
not conforming to the regulations as of the effective date of the ordinance
from which this chapter derives, or otherwise interfere with the continuance of
a legal non-conforming use.
   (A)   Regulations not retroactive. Nothing contained herein shall require
any change in the construction, alteration or intended use of any structure,
the construction or alteration of which was begun prior to the effective date
of the ordinance from which this chapter derives and is diligently prosecuted.
   (B)   Marking and lighting and tree trimming. Notwithstanding the preceding
provision of this section, the owner of any existing non-conforming structure
or tree is hereby required to permit the installation operation, and
maintenance thereon of such markers and lights as shall be deemed necessary by
the airport manager to indicate to the operators of aircraft in the vicinity of
the airport the presence of such airport obstruction. Such markers and lights
shall be installed, operated and maintained at the expense of the Fort Scott
Airport and, hence, the City Commission. In the event of a non-conforming tree,
the tree shall be topped, cropped or trimmed at the expense of the Fort Scott
Airport and, hence, the City Commission.
   (C)   Non-conforming uses abandoned or destroyed. Whenever the Fort Scott
Airport manager determines that a non-conforming tree or structure has been
abandoned or more than 51% torn down, physically deteriorated or decayed, no
permit shall be granted that would allow the reconstruction of such abandoned
or destroyed non-conforming structure or tree to exceed the applicable height
limit or otherwise deviate from these height and hazard regulations.
(Prior Code, § 8.36.070) (Ord. 3415, passed 7-17-2012)
§ 157.008 PERMITS.
   No permit shall be granted for a use inconsistent with the provisions of
this chapter, or for a use that would allow the establishment or creation of an
airport hazard or permit a non-conforming structure to be made or become higher
or become a greater hazard to air navigation than it was when the applicable
regulation was adopted or when the application for a permit is made; unless a
variance has been approved.
   (A)   Permits required when. Permit applications shall be submitted for
proposed structures in any of the zones or surfaces, except as specifically
provided in division (B) below. Each permit application shall be submitted on
forms required by the city to determine whether the resulting use, structure or
tree would conform to these regulations. If such determination is in the
affirmative, the permit shall be granted.
   (B)   No permit required when. No permit shall be required for structures
shorter than 75 feet; provided that, in an approach or transitional zone the
proposed structure or tree is more than 4,200 feet from the end of a runway;
provided further that, this permit exception shall not be construed as
permitting any construction, or alteration of any structure, or growth of any
tree in excess of any of the height limits established by this chapter.
   (C)   Permit conditioned on FAA Form 7460-1, “Notice of Construction”. Any
proposed construction, alteration or repair requiring a permit must have filed
an FAA Form 7460-1 (FAA 77.13, Construction or Alteration Requiring Notice)
with the appropriate FAA regional office. No construction, alteration or repair
work may commence until the determination is received from the FAA and said
determination is reviewed by the airport manager in consultation with the City
Commission.
   (D)   Permit may be conditioned on marking or lighting and tree trimming.
Any permit or variance granted may be conditioned on a requirement that the
owner of the structure or tree in question install, operate and maintain, at
the owner’s expense, such markings and lights or tree trimming as may be deemed
reasonably necessary to effectuate the purpose of this chapter. If deemed
proper by the City Commission, this condition may be modified to require the
owner to permit the City Commission, at its own expense, to top, crop or trim
the tree or install, operate and maintain the necessary markings and lights.
(Prior Code, § 8.36.080) (Ord. 3415, passed 7-17-2012)
§ 157.009 ADMINISTRATION AND ENFORCEMENT.
   It shall be the duty of the airport manager of the Fort Scott Airport to
administer the regulations prescribed herein in consultation with the City
Commission. Applications for permits and variances shall be made to the airport
manager upon a form published for that purpose. Applications required by this
chapter to be submitted to the airport manager shall be promptly granted or
denied. In the event the applying party receives what they determine to be an
adverse decision, the party shall have the right to apply to the Airport Board
of Appeals for review of the decision.
(Prior Code, § 8.36.090) (Ord. 3415, passed 7-17-2012)
§ 157.010 APPEALS.
   (A)   Who may appeal. Any person aggrieved, or any taxpayer affected, by any
decision of the airport manager of the Fort Scott Airport, made in the
administration of the chapter, may appeal to the Airport Board of Appeals.
   (B)   Powers of the Airport Board of Appeals.
      (1)   The Airport Board of Appeals shall have the following powers:
         (a)   To hear and decide appeals from any order, requirement, decision
or determination made by the airport manager in the enforcement of this
chapter;
         (b)   To hear and decide special exceptions to the terms of this
chapter upon which such Airport Board of Appeals under such regulations may be
required to pass; and
         (c)   To hear and decide specific variances.
      (2)   The Board may, in conformity with the provisions of this chapter,
reverse or affirm, in whole or in part, or modify the order, requirement,
decision or determination appealed from and may make such order, requirement,
decision or determination as may be appropriate under the circumstances.
   (C)   Variances. Any person desiring to erect or increase the height of any
structure, or permit the growth of any tree, or use of property, not in
accordance with the regulations prescribed in this chapter, may apply to
Airport Board of Appeals for a variance from such regulations. The application
for variance shall be accompanied by a determination from the Federal Aviation
Administration as to the effect of the proposal on the published or planned
approach minimums, operation of air navigation facilities and the safe,
efficient use of navigable airspace. Such variances shall be allowed where it
is duly found that a literal application or enforcement of the regulations will
result in necessary hardship and relief granted, will not be contrary to the
public interest, will not create a hazard to air navigation, will do
substantial justice, and will be in accordance with the spirit of this chapter.
Additionally, no application for variance to the requirements of this chapter
may be considered by the Airport Board of Appeals unless a copy of the
application has been furnished to the City Commission for review and comment;
and, for appeals in unincorporated territory of a county, to the respective
county commission for same. If the Fort Scott Airport manager does not respond
to the application for a variance within 14 days after receipt, the Airport
Board of Appeals may act on its own to grant or deny said variance application.
   (D)   Meetings of the Board of Appeals. Meetings of the Airport Board of
Appeals for the purposes of this chapter and the review of any decision by the
airport manager shall be at the regularly scheduled Airport Advisory Board
meeting time, unless a special meeting is called. The Airport Board of Appeals
shall keep minutes of its proceedings showing the vote of each member upon each
question; or, if absent or failing to vote, indicating such fact, and shall
keep records of its examinations and other official actions, all of which shall
immediately be filed in the office of the City Clerk and on due cause shown.
   (E)   Reasonable time for rulings. The Airport Board of Appeals shall fix a
reasonable time for hearing appeals, give public notice and due notice to the
parties in interest and decide the same within a reasonable time. Upon the
hearing, any party may appear in person or by agent or by attorney. The airport
manager shall transmit to the Airport Board of Appeals all the papers
constituting the record upon which the action appealed from was taken.
   (F)   Appeal to stay all proceedings. An appeal shall stay all proceedings
in furtherance of the action appealed from unless the airport manager certifies
to the Airport Board of Appeals, after the notice of appeal has been filed with
it, that by reason of the facts stated in the certificate a stay would in the
opinion of the airport manager cause imminent peril to life or property. In
such case, proceedings shall not be stayed, except by order of the Airport
Board of Appeals or notice to the airport manager and on due cause shown.
(Prior Code, § 8.36.100) (Ord. 3415, passed 7-17-2012)
§ 157.011 AIRPORT BOARD OF APPEALS; REVIEW.
   The Airport Board of Appeals shall make written findings of facts and
conclusions of law giving the facts upon which it acted and its legal
conclusions from such facts in reversing, affirming or modifying any order,
requirement, decision or determination which comes before it under the
provisions of this chapter. The concurring vote of a majority of the members of
the Airport Board of Appeals shall be sufficient to reverse any order,
requirement, decision or determination of the airport manager or decide in
favor of the applicant on any matter upon which it is required to pass under
this chapter or to effect variation to this chapter. Any person aggrieved, or
any taxpayer affected, by any decision of the Airport Board of Appeals shall be
entitled to a judicial review of said decision as set forth below.
(Prior Code, § 8.36.110) (Ord. 3415, passed 7-17-2012)
§ 157.012 JUDICIAL REVIEW.
   Any person aggrieved, or any taxpayer affected, by any decision of the
Airport Board of Appeals may appeal to the District Court of the county,
pursuant to state law.
(Prior Code, § 8.36.120) (Ord. 3415, passed 7-17-2012)
§ 157.013 CONFLICTING REGULATIONS.
   Where there exists a conflict between any of the regulations or limitations
prescribed in this chapter and any other regulations applicable to the same
area, whether the conflict exists with respect to the height of structures or
trees, and the use of land, or any other matter, the more stringent limitation
or requirement shall govern and prevail.
(Prior Code, § 8.36.140) (Ord. 3415, passed 7-17-2012)
§ 157.999 PENALTY.
   Each violation of this chapter or of any regulation, order or ruling
promulgated thereunder shall constitute a misdemeanor and shall be punishable
by a fine as set by the City Commission; and each day a violation continues to
exist shall constitute a separate offense.
(Prior Code, § 8.36.130) (Ord. 3415, passed 7-17-2012)

§ 158.001 ZONING REGULATIONS ADOPTED BY REFERENCE.
   (A)   There is incorporated by reference for the purpose of prescribing
zoning and subdivision regulations, that certain code known as “Zoning
Ordinance and Subdivision Regulations of the City of Fort Scott, Kansas 1988-
89”, prepared for the city and published in 1989 by Professional Engineering
Consultants; three copies of which code have been filed in the office of the
Clerk of the city, having been marked “Official Copy as Incorporated by
Ordinance No. 2992”, to which shall be attached a copy of the ordinance
codified in this section and filed with the City Clerk to be open for
inspection and available to the public at all reasonable hours. The zoning and
subdivision regulations are adopted and incorporated as fully as if set out at
length in this section, and from the date on which the ordinance codified in
this section takes effect, their provisions thereof shall be controlling within
the boundaries of the city and shall also control all real estate outside of
the city boundaries which is within three miles thereof.
   (B)   There is further adopted by the governing body of the city, for the
purpose of defining the boundaries of zone districts referred to in the zoning
regulations adopted in this section, a certain map marked “Official Copy as
Incorporated by Ordinance No. 2992”, and filed in the office of the City Clerk.
The map shall be open to inspection and available to the public at all
reasonable business hours. The map and all notations, references and other
information shown thereon shall be deemed to be a part of this section by this
reference and shall have the same force and effect as if the map and all
notations, references and other information shown thereon were fully set forth
or described in this section.

passed - -2000)
§ 158.002 H, HISTORIC DISTRICT.
   (A)   Creation. Creation of the H Historic District is intended to promote
the educational, cultural, economic and general welfare of the public through
the preservation and protection of historic buildings; through the development
of an appropriate setting for these buildings; and through the benefits
resulting to the economy of the city by developing and maintaining its vacation
travel industry through the promotion of these historic areas.
(Prior Code, § 17.08.010)
   (B)   Application for rezoning. Application for zoning classification in the
H Historic District shall be made by the property owner.
(Prior Code, § 17.08.020)
   (C)   Membership; terms. Application for a certificate of appropriateness
shall be made to the Zoning Administrator for the city. The Fort Scott Historic
Preservation and Resource Commission shall review the certificates of
appropriateness and signs and make a recommendation to the City Commission in
determining the appropriateness of design and architectural techniques to be
utilized in maintaining the historical integrity in the historic district. The
Fort Scott Historic Preservation and Resource Commission shall have no more
than 30 days from the date the application is filed to make said recommendation
the City Commission.
(Prior Code, § 17.08.030)
   (D)   Certificate of appropriateness; considerations. The City Commission
shall not consider interior arrangement, relative size of the building or
structure, or features not subject to any public view, and shall not make any
requirements, except for the purpose of preventing developments obviously
incongruous to the purposes of the Historic District within which the structure
is located. The Board of Commissioners shall consider the following in passing
upon the appropriateness of proposed architectural changes and proposed uses:
      (1)   Exterior architectural features, including all signs, which are
subject to public view from a public street, way or place;
      (2)   General design and arrangement;
      (3)   Texture, material and color;
      (4)   The extent to which the appearance of the structure would be
harmonious with or obviously inappropriate to the historic character and value
thereof;
      (5)   In case of a building proposed to be demolished or razed, a primary
consideration will be the extent to which its continued existence would tend to
protect irreplaceable historic places and/or structures and to preserve the
general historic atmosphere of the city; and
      (6)   The extent to which the proposed alteration of the structure or the
proposed use thereof will promote or injure the general welfare of the
inhabitants of the city and all citizens in promoting the preservation and
protection of historic places and areas.
(Prior Code, § 17.08.040)
   (E)   Certificate of appropriateness; approval. Decisions of the Board of
Commissioners will be incorporated in certificates of appropriateness when
approved or there shall be written reasons for disapproval. Immediately upon
approval by the Board of Commissioners of any application to erect,
reconstruct, alter, restore or raze a building, a certificate of
appropriateness signed by the Mayor and bearing the date of issuance shall be
made available to the applicant. The Building Inspector shall refuse to honor
any request for a building permit without such a certificate of
appropriateness; but a certificate of appropriateness will in no way affect the
requirement to comply with the other provisions necessary to obtain a building
permit.
(Prior Code, § 17.08.050)
   (F)   District regulations. In the H District, no land shall be used and no
structure shall be used, demolished, reconstructed, restored, painted, altered,
enlarged or erected, except in conformity to the provisions of this section.
(Prior Code, § 17.08.060)
   (G)   Uses. The H District designation shall neither enlarge or restrict any
use permitted within the zoning district within which the historical structure
was situated prior to the application of the H District designation.
(Prior Code, § 17.08.070)
   (H)   Area, setback, frontage, yard and parking regulations. Structures
within a historic district shall not be altered so as to not conform to the
general established pattern already in existence in the district insofar as
area, setback, frontage, yard and parking regulations are concerned.
(Prior Code, § 17.08.080)
   (I)   Structural alterations.
      (1)   No building within a historic district may be demolished, in whole
or in part, nor may any external architectural feature of a building be
altered, without prior approval of the Board of Commissioners. Neither shall
any building or structure hereafter be erected, reconstructed, altered or
restored within a historic district unless and until an application for a
certificate of appropriateness shows it has been approved by the aforementioned
Board of Commissioners. Review of such application by the Board of
Commissioners will include an analysis of external architectural features,
including signs which are subject to view from any public street or place.
      (2)   Nothing contained in this section shall preclude alteration or
reconstruction of the interior portion of any structure within a Historic
District, so long as the same shall in no way alter the external appearance of
such structure.
(Prior Code, § 17.08.090)
   (J)   Fees and expenses.
      (1)   A fee of $2 shall be charged to an applicant seeking a zoning
amendment which would classify property within the H Historic District.
      (2)   A fee of $1 shall be charged to an applicant seeking a certificate
of appropriateness, as provided in this section.
      (3)   Unless otherwise provided, all additional expenses of obtaining a
zoning amendment classifying property within the H Historic District,
including, but not limited to, publication costs, shall be paid by the
applicant.
(Prior Code, § 17.08.100)
   (K)   Fencing regulations.
      (1)   All fencing shall be maintained and not detract from the historical
integrity of the Fort Scott Historic District. Any fencing is considered a form
of construction and any fencing must be reviewed by the Fort Scott Historic
Preservation and Resource Commission and issued a building permit by the city.
      (2)   The following guidelines must be adhered to in the Historic
District.
         (a)   Existing historic fencing should be maintained repaired, and
restored when needed. If replacement was to become necessary, it should be in
compliance with the existing guidelines.
         (b)   The use of fencing in the historic district should be strongly
discouraged unless the existence can be documented with historic photographic
evidence, or there is a documented need for fencing to block access to property
for security, or to stop access to nuisance areas of properties such as between
buildings, around HVAC units, power boxes and exposed water and gas lines.
Fences in downtown areas were typically behind buildings if they were used and
generally not viewable from public streets, so this needs to be kept in mind if
considering a fence in the Historic District.
         (c)   New fences are subject to review and recommendation by the Fort
Scott Historic Preservation and Resource Commission and approval by the City
Commission. New fences may be added to historic district properties provided
there is a documented need and they are appropriate to the style and type of
property in terms of design, style and size. The following building materials
are prohibited for use in fencing in the Downtown Historic District:
            1.   Any type of gridded metal material such as chainlink, wire
netting, wire mesh, hog wire, barbed wire, farm wire, chicken wire, two-by-four
wire and the like;
            2.   Light gauge metal tins, plastic, wood split rail and cinder or
concrete blocks;
            3.   Bricks and native stone to the area could be considered if
there is documented use of them with a particular building and they match the
existing structure; and
            4.   Solid wood fences like stockade or vertical board fences are
discouraged, but may be allowed if minimally visible from a public street or do
not obscure the view of historic buildings.
         (d)   Any fencing should not exceed six feet tall.
         (e)   Fencing shall not impede the flow of water across the property.
         (f)   These guidelines should supersede commercial zoning regulations
regarding fences in a commercial area if the property falls within the Historic
District.
(Prior Code, § 17.08.110)
(Ord. 2607, passed - -1976; Ord. 3016, passed - -1990; Ord. 3495, passed 3-28-
2017; Ord. 3538, passed 8-21-2018; Ord. 3798, passed 4-7-2026)

   Historic Preservation, see
Chapter 152
§ 158.003 COUNTY GOVERNMENT ZONING DISTRICT.
   (A)   Intent and purpose. The County Government Zoning District is intended
for the purpose of allowing the Board of County Commissioners the authority to
construct and erect all buildings, parking lots and other improvements
necessary for said county for the county to carry out its governmental
functions.
(Prior Code, § 17.12.010)
   (B)   District regulations. In the County Government Zoning District, no
structure or land shall be used, constructed, erected, altered or enlarged
without the express written consent of the Board of County Commissioners.
(Prior Code, § 17.12.020)
   (C)   Uses allowed. The County Government Zoning District shall include uses
that are necessary for the county government of the county to carry out its
political function.
(Prior Code, § 17.12.030)
   (D)   Intensity of use regulations. All intensity of use regulations shall
be established by the Board of County Commissioners.
(Prior Code, § 17.12.040)
   (E)   Height, yard, sign and parking regulations. All height, yard, sign and
parking regulations shall be established by the Board of County Commissioners.
(Prior Code, § 17.12.050)
   (F)   Plumbing, Electrical and Fire Code regulations. The city shall
maintain control and authority over all improvements constructed in the County
Government Zoning District, which will require plumbing and electrical service.
Control over these two areas as well as ensuring compliance with the Fire Code
of the city are specifically reserved to the city for public safety purposes.
(Prior Code, § 17.12.060)
(Ord. 2779, passed - -1981)
